# Epitaph — Development History

Epitaph is a Minecraft Bedrock Edition add-on built as a toolkit for realm and server operators — land plots with real, layered permissions, generators and spawners with a full upgrade economy, an NPC market and player-run shops, PvP systems, and a long tail of quality-of-life and admin tooling built up over hundreds of iterations.

This document is the complete, unedited development log kept alongside the project's own codebase — every feature, bug fix, and piece of research recorded as it happened, including the mistakes and the dead ends, not just the highlights. It is published here as a look behind the curtain, not as documentation for players.

**This repository intentionally contains no source code and no downloadable add-on file** — just this history, and a page summarizing the most recent changes (see `recent-changes.html`).

One deliberate exception: a handful of entries describe an extremely rare, unnamed item added to the game. Those entries are kept intentionally vague — no name, no mechanic — so it stays a genuine surprise for anyone who finds one in-game.

---

```
  still exist and still run — it silently degrades the CLIENT-side
  autocomplete/param-hint UI for enum and selector-typed parameters, which
  matches the reported symptom exactly (commands work, but show zero

  `min_engine_version: [1, 26, 0]` (matching the main pack, which is the one
  ground-truth value for what this whole addon actually assumes) and
  bumping every satellite's version by one more patch so Bedrock can't skip
  re-import via the same-UUID-same-version dedup. If a satellite pack is
  ever rebuilt from an archived/recovered source again, cross-check
  min_engine_version against the main pack's manifest specifically —
  `node --check` alone will not catch this class of bug.
  **This min_engine_version fix was real (the mismatch existed and is
  correctly fixed) but turned out NOT to be the actual cause of the
  reported symptom** — reported again as still broken ("commands still
  don't register, like '/plot home'"), which prompted a full re-audit of

  **Real root cause, confirmed by direct doc research**: 5 of the 9
  satellites (Plot/Kit/Shop/Market/Raid Commands — every satellite whose
  bare command dispatches to a subcommand word, e.g. "home"/"claim"/
  "browse") registered their subcommand parameter as plain
  `CustomCommandParamType.String`, not a real registered
  `CustomCommandParamType.Enum`. A String parameter has never shown
  autocomplete on ANY engine version, ever — that's inherent to how
  Bedrock string params work, confirmed directly against Microsoft's own
  Custom Commands reference and the Bedrock Wiki's practical writeup, not
  something `min_engine_version` affects at all. The main pack's own
  consolidated `/epitaph` command was never affected because it already
  uses a real `enumName`/`CustomCommandParamType.Enum` pair for its own
  subcommand slot (`registerBuiltinCommands`, ~line 820) — the satellites
  had just silently drifted onto (or in 5 cases, been reconstructed onto)
  plain String instead, the whole time they existed as subcommand
  dispatchers, all the way back to the stale 0.8.64-era archives. Not a
  regression introduced this session — a pre-existing gap this pass
  finally closed. Also confirmed independently and worth recording since
  it corrects a standing (wrong) assumption baked into this file's own
  history: the "alias trick" (`namespace:namespace` → bare `/namespace`)
  works because Bedrock auto-registers EVERY custom command's non-
  namespaced form by dropping the `namespace:` prefix entirely (e.g.
  `wiki:goto` becomes reachable as both `/wiki:goto` and bare `/goto`) —
  there is no "colon becomes space" behavior anywhere. This means a
  command registered as, say, `plot:claim` (a different word than its own
  namespace) is NOT reachable as `/plot claim` — its own auto-alias would
  be bare `/claim` instead. Every subcommand this addon wants reachable as
  "/plot <word>" MUST live as an enum value inside the self-referential
  `plot:plot` registration, not as its own separate `plot:<word>`
  registration — confirmed this is exactly how the current `plot:plot`
  enum (home/info/sethome/delhome/permissions/border/list) is structured,
  and confirmed `plot:claim`/`plot:delete`/`plot:setmaxradius`/
  `plot:debuggen`/`plot:debugclear` are INTENTIONALLY separate admin-only
  registrations reachable via their own bare aliases (`/claim`, `/delete`,
  etc.) or the full colon form (`/plot:claim`, `/plot:setmaxradius`), never
  via "/plot claim" — matching how `/plot:setmaxradius` was already
  documented elsewhere in this file as a deliberate colon-form command.
  Fixed by adding `reg.registerEnum(...)` + switching the subcommand
  parameter to `CustomCommandParamType.Enum` in all 5 affected satellites
  (`plot:sub`, `kit:sub`, `shop:sub`, `market:sub`, `raid:sub`), matching
  the main pack's own established pattern exactly, and bumping all 5
  satellites' versions again.

  - **KitCommands, ShopCommands, RaidCommands matched the main pack's

  **Satellite count cut 10 → 9 (BP 0.9.159), on direct instruction ("is there ANY way we can cut down on satellites").** The hard constraint is real and doesn't go away: `CustomCommandRegistry` allows exactly one namespace per behavior pack (`NamespaceMismatch` otherwise), so every distinct namespace genuinely needs its own pack — this isn't a design choice that can be refactored away. Two options were presented: (1) delete `Epitaph-SellAllCommand` outright, since it only ever relayed to the existing `market:sellall` scriptevent id (the same handler `/market sellall` already triggers) — a free cut, costing only the bare `/sellall` alias in favor of typing `/market sellall`; (2) fold the four single-purpose bare commands (`/pay`, `/guide`, `/bounty`, `/levelshop`) into one new shared satellite as subcommands, cutting 4 packs to 1 at the cost of losing their clean muscle-memory bare-word form. **User chose option 1 only, explicitly skipped option 2** — `/pay`, `/guide`, `/bounty`, `/levelshop` all remain their own bare top-level commands. Every player-facing mention of the bare `/sellall` string was updated to `/market sellall` for accuracy (two guide book pages — page 10 "NPC MARKET" 222→229 bytes, page 18 "QUICK REFERENCE" 219→226 bytes, both re-verified via `Buffer.byteLength` against the 256-byte cap — and one rotating tip in `TIPS_MODULE`); the one non-command mention (the Stats HUD admin panel's summary line naming "sell/sellall" as a general feature, not a typed command) was left alone since it was never advertising a syntax that just stopped existing.

  Every satellite pack above now ships its own pack_icon.png (256x256, sourced

Epitaph-Complete.mcaddon   ← distributable; rebuild after every change (see below)
```

TypeScript source is NOT present; `main.js` is the pre-built esbuild bundle and is the primary edit target. `flight_legacy.js` and `flight_ability.js` are the exception — plain, hand-written (not esbuild-bundled) ES modules sitting alongside `main.js` in the same `scripts\` folder, implementing the two swappable Plot Flight systems (see Plot Flight below for which is currently active). Bedrock loads whatever `main.js` (the manifest's declared entry point) transitively imports, so whichever of the two isn't currently imported is just inert cargo in the pack until something actually imports it.

### Architecture

**One namespace per behavior pack.** Bedrock's `CustomCommandRegistry` throws `NamespaceMismatch` if you try to register two namespaces in a single pack. The main BP owns the `epitaph` namespace; every other namespace lives in its own satellite pack.

**Alias trick.** Registering a command as `namespace:namespace` (same word both sides) gives a space-based alias — players type `/plot claim` not `/plot:claim`. All player-facing commands use this pattern.

**Satellite relay pattern.** Satellite packs contain zero logic: every command fires `player.runCommand("scriptevent <id> <args>")`. The main `main.js` listens on `world.afterEvents.scriptEventReceive` and dispatches all real work there.

**Commands class (`main.js` line ~145).** `registerNow(registry, def)` registers to Bedrock. If `def.enumName`/`def.enumValues` are set it pre-registers the enum for autocomplete. `dispatch(def, origin, args)` gates on `isAdmin()` then defers to `system.run()`.

**Consolidated `epitaph:epitaph` command.** All 16 admin/player subcommands (`menu`, `ann`, `ann_fire`, `clock`, `cmd`, `cmd_fire`, `cmd_toggle`, `spawn`, `warp`, `struct`, `struct_place`, `portal_scan`, `balance`, `item_clear`, `setworldborder`, `help`) route through one registration in `registerBuiltinCommands()`. Note there is no `generators` subcommand here (despite earlier drift in this doc saying otherwise) — generator browsing lives under `/market` (`market:generators`), not `/epitaph`. `grant` and `giveadv` remain separate because they use `PlayerSelector`/`ItemType` typed params needed by command blocks. **`pay`, `guide`, and `bounty` used to live here but were moved out to their own bare top-level commands** (`Epitaph-PayCommand`, `Epitaph-GuideCommand`, `Epitaph-BountyCommand` satellite packs — `/pay`, `/guide`, `/bounty`) since they're genuinely player-facing economy/PvP actions, not admin tooling, and shouldn't read as such just for living under the `/epitaph` namespace; `/epitaph` itself is now admin-only apart from `spawn`/`balance`/`help`. Each satellite forwards via `scriptevent` to `pay:pay`/`guide:open`/`bounty:bounty`, which the main pack's `scriptEventReceive` listener dispatches to the same `handlePayCommand`/`giveGuideBook`/`handleBountyList`+`handleBountyPlace` logic that used to run under `/epitaph` — no game logic moved, only the command surface. Player-accessible without `isAdmin()` under `/epitaph` itself: `spawn`, `balance`, `help`. `warp` has an *additional*, more restrictive gate on top of `adminOnly()` — it shares `handleUse2` with the physical Admin Pearl item, which is deliberately tag-only (`cfg2.adminTag`, `"epitaph.admin"`) rather than `isAdmin()` (Operator status alone doesn't grant pearl access, per `validate2`'s own comment) — a genuine Operator without that exact tag gets a clear "requires the tag specifically" message rather than silently failing inside `handleUse2` with a confusing pearl-flavored one.

**Enum registration (stable `@minecraft/server@2.8.0`):**
- Enum name must be namespaced: `"plot:sub"` not `"PlotSubcmd"`.
- `type` field must be the `CustomCommandParamType.Enum` constant, not a string.
- `enumName` (to separate display label from ID) is beta-only — not available on stable. `name` must equal the registered enum ID exactly.

**`CustomCommandParamType` aliases.** The bundle has multiple imports of the same binding (esbuild assigns `CustomCommandParamType3`, `4`, `5`, `7` from different source sections). All are the same object; use whichever is in scope. ES module imports are hoisted so any alias is accessible anywhere in `main.js`.

**Version bumping.** Every functional change requires a manifest patch-version bump or Bedrock silently skips re-import (same UUID+version = already installed). Bump `header.version` and all `modules[].version` together; do NOT touch SDK dependency versions.

**`/pay` takes a permanent 7% tax (`PAY_TAX_RATE`, `handlePayCommand`) and confirms via `showConfirm` before sending** — a money sink (the 7% just vanishes, isn't routed anywhere) rather than a pure transfer, since `/pay` was the one economy path that didn't already pull money out of circulation the way market sell prices and generator upgrade costs do. `handlePayCommand` is `async` now (awaits the confirm dialog) — the balance check runs twice, once before showing the confirm prompt and once again right after it closes, since the player could spend the money elsewhere (a shop purchase, another `/pay`) while the dialog was open.

**Guide book pages (`giveGuideBook`, the `pages` array) have a real, enforced 256-byte-per-page cap — bytes, not JS string `.length`.** `book.setContents()` validates by UTF-8 *byte* length, not UTF-16 code units — got this wrong twice before it stuck: a first fix trimmed one page from 314 to 232 by `.length` and it *still* failed in production, because `§` (U+00A7) is 2 bytes in UTF-8 and any em dash (—) or curly apostrophe (') is 3 bytes, all counted as 1 by `.length`. Fixed for good this time with three layers: (1) every em dash/curly apostrophe in the guide replaced with plain ASCII (`-`/`'`, 1 byte each), (2) each page now sits in its own `// PAGE N — TITLE` banner comment inside the array so any single page can be replaced by swapping just its one string, without touching neighbors, and (3) `giveGuideBook()` now runs every page through a hand-rolled `utf8ByteLength()` (real UTF-8 byte counting using only `codePointAt` — deliberately not `TextEncoder`, since Bedrock's runtime is QuickJS-based and that API's availability there isn't confirmed) *before* calling `setContents()`, and will name the exact page number and byte count in `Log.error` if one is ever over again, instead of the engine's generic unlocatable error. **When editing a page here, the banner comments mark safe replacement boundaries, and the runtime check will catch a mistake immediately with a specific page number** — but still verify by eye with real byte counting after any edit, not `.length`.

**Guide book was fully rewritten to 15 pages (targeting ~250 bytes each, hard cap 256) to reflect real game mechanics and fix drifted/incomplete wording** — page order: Intro, Starter Kits, Your Plot, Plot Access, Plot Permissions, Generators, Upgrades, Plot Flight, Market, Player Shops, Economy, PvP & EXP, Bounties, Quick Reference, Server Rules. Every claim was re-verified against actual code before writing (e.g. the Generator Hub opens via *either* right-clicking the generator block itself *or* shift-right-clicking the block above it — the old text only documented the shift-click path). `/epitaph balance` and `/epitaph spawn` are deliberately omitted from the book even though they're real, unmigrated player commands — the user's instruction was that nothing reading `/epitaph <anything>` belongs in player-facing docs, full stop, not just the three that got migrated.

**Guide book is now auto-granted exactly once, on a player's genuine first-ever join** — `giveGuideBook(player, preferredSlot)` takes an optional slot argument now; the first-join hook (`main.js`, inside the `world.afterEvents.playerSpawn` handler, piggybacking on the exact same `if (!getOwnedPlot(player))` check that already drives one-time plot auto-claim) calls `giveGuideBook(player, 4)` — slot 4 is the center hotbar slot. Only claims that exact slot if it's genuinely empty (via `container.getItem`/`setItem`); falls back to `addItem()` otherwise, same as a manual `/guide` re-run (which still calls `giveGuideBook(player)` with no slot argument, unchanged).

**`keepOnDeath` on the Spawn Ball and Admin Pearl (`buildBall`/`buildPearl`) used to fail completely silently** — a bare `try { stack.keepOnDeath = true; } catch (_) {}` swallowed any failure with zero logging, so if the assignment ever didn't take, the item would just disappear on death with nothing anywhere explaining why. Both now verify the read-back (`stack.keepOnDeath === true`, the same pattern `buildGiveItem`/`executeGiveAdv` already used for admin-granted items) and call `Log.warn`/`Log.error` — the codebase's always-on logger, not the per-module `dbg`/`dbg2`/`dbg3` console logging that's gated behind each module's own debug toggle — so a real failure is now visible instead of invisible. Both items already had an independent, identical respawn-restore safety net before this fix (`validate`/`validate2`, triggered off `playerSpawn` ~5 ticks after every respawn, not just initial join) that replaces a missing item regardless of whether `keepOnDeath` actually worked — this fix is about surfacing the failure, not the only thing standing between a death and a lost item.

**The `C` color-constant object (`main.js`, "COLOR CONSTANTS" section) had four bright colors silently aliased to the wrong dark ones** — `green`/`aqua`/`red`/`yellow` were literal copies of `darkGreen`/`darkAqua`/`darkRed`/`gold`'s codes (§2/§3/§4/§6) instead of the actual bright codes (§a/§b/§c/§e). Every message built from `C.green`/`C.red`/etc. — shop, market, kit, and the main `/epitaph` command, hundreds of call sites — rendered visibly duller than the `/plot` family and the raid/admin scriptevent switch, which both use raw literal `"§a"`/`"§c"`/`"§e"` strings instead of the `C` object and were never affected. Fixed by correcting the four constants directly; no call site needed touching.

**`PLOT_Y = 128`.** Grass surface level. `ISLAND_GROUND_OFFSET = 39` makes island grass land at Y=128. `findSafeLandingY` for plot home must scan from `PLOT_Y` downward (depth 10), never from suspension height, to avoid landing on trees.

**`ISLA2.mcstructure` has no `minecraft:air` in its palette anymore, and — critically — no *named* `minecraft:structure_void` palette entry either.** The empty 88.5% of the bounding box (85,207 of 96,320 positions) is encoded as the raw **`-1` sentinel value directly in `block_indices`**, Bedrock's actual native "skip this position, don't touch the world" marker. This distinction matters and bit the addon once already: an earlier pass "fixed" air by pointing those `block_indices` entries at an *explicit palette entry* named `minecraft:structure_void` instead of using raw `-1`. That looked identical in an NBT viewer (same name string) but is a completely different thing at runtime — `minecraft:structure_void` referenced as a normal palette entry is still just a real, placeable block like any other, so `/structure load` placed it physically (visible, ghost-like blocks appearing around every claimed island) *and* it still overwrote whatever terrain was there, exactly the destructive behavior this was supposed to prevent. Only the raw `-1` value in `block_indices` (no palette entry involved at all) is treated as a true non-destructive skip. Fixed via a direct NBT edit converting every `block_indices` entry pointing at either `minecraft:air` or the named `minecraft:structure_void` entry to raw `-1` (little-endian, uncompressed — `nbtlib.load(path, gzipped=False, byteorder='little')`; note `block_indices` layers parse as `List[Int]` in nbtlib, not a numpy-backed `IntArray`, so mutate element-by-element rather than via array/boolean indexing, which raises `AttributeError`/`IncompatibleItemType`). Two backups sit alongside the fixed file in `structures/`: `ISLA2.mcstructure.bak` (the true original, real air) and `ISLA2.mcstructure.namedvoid.bak` (the intermediate, wrongly-"fixed" named-structure_void version — kept only as a reference for what *not* to do, never restore from this one). A world that already has ISLA2 imported caches it by name in LevelDB, so this edit won't visibly change anything for a plot claimed before the update; only new claims after reimport see it. **Any island already placed under the buggy named-structure_void version has real, physical `structure_void` blocks sitting in the world right now around it — those are already-placed blocks and won't self-correct just because the source file and manifest version were fixed; an admin needs to manually clear them (e.g. `/fill ... air` over the affected area, or have the player re-claim on a fresh plot) since retroactive world cleanup isn't something a structure file or version bump can do.**

**Island placement now rotates randomly per plot (`placeIsland(cx, cz)`, ~line 4404).** Picks uniformly from `0_degrees`/`90_degrees`/`180_degrees`/`270_degrees` and passes it as the `structure load` command's rotation argument. `/structure load` always anchors its `<to>` coordinate at the lower northwest (min X, min Z) corner of the placed footprint regardless of rotation (confirmed against Microsoft's own command reference) — only the effective width/depth swap for 90°/270° (X and Z trade places, 35×32 becomes 32×35). `islandLoadOffset(rotation)` computes the recentered anchor for whichever axis holds which dimension; `Math.ceil` on half of each dimension reproduces the original, empirically-tuned 0°-case offsets exactly (35→18, 32→16), so it's one formula for all four rotations, not special-cased per direction. Both the main claim flow and `/plot debuggen` route through this single function now — no other call site constructs its own `structure load` string.

**Plot Trust is now layered: base trust (`isAuthorized`) + five per-plot toggles + a custom home point.** Plain trust used to mean unconditional full access to everything; `PLOT_PERM_DEFAULTS`/`getPlotPerm`/`setPlotPerm` (`main.js`, right after `isAuthorized`) add `build`/`break`/`interact`/`generators`/`trustmgmt` toggles on top, each checked via `canBuild`/`canBreak`/`canInteract`/`canUseGenerators`/`canManageTrust` — all five short-circuit true for the plot owner (`isPlotOwner`) so a toggle can never lock the owner out of their own plot. **Four of the five default to `true`** (build/break/interact/generators — exactly what plain trust already granted, so an untouched plot behaves identically to before this shipped); **`trustmgmt` defaults to `false`** since letting a trusted member manage *other* people's trust is a genuinely new capability that never existed at all before, not a narrowing of one that did. `verifyPermission` (build) and `verifyRaidablePermission` (now takes a `capability` param, `"break"` or `"interact"`, defaulting to `"break"`) are the two enforcement seams these route through — `playerBreakBlock` passes `"break"`, `playerInteractWithBlock` passes `"interact"` explicitly, so an owner can grant one without the other (e.g. chest access without mining rights). `/plot trust` and `/plot untrust` now also work for a non-owner with `trustmgmt` on, targeting whichever plot they're physically standing on (`getOwnedPlot(player) || getPlotAtBlock(...)`) since the command has no target-plot argument to disambiguate otherwise; `getPlotOwnerUUID(id)` blocks anyone (owner included) from untrusting the actual owner. `/plot permissions` opens a `showModal` toggle form (`openPlotPermissionsMenu`) for all five.

**2026-07-21/22 bug-hunt session (BP 0.9.113→0.9.116) — several real gaps found in the plot-protection and generator systems, some fixed, some still open:**
- **Fixed: shop chest scanner gave the wrong reason for "no chest found."** `/shop setup`'s crosshair-scan (`main.js`, Path B of the setup flow) silently `continue`d past any chest not on an authorized plot, so a chest that genuinely existed but belonged to someone else, or sat outside any claimed plot, produced the exact same generic "No chest near your crosshair" message as there being no chest at all. Now tracks the closest chest *regardless* of authorization separately, and reports the real reason ("not on any claimed plot" / "on someone else's plot, not trusted") when that's what actually happened.
- **Fixed: generator Hub interact-permission denial didn't cancel the event.** The `playerInteractWithBlock` handler that opens the Generator Hub checked ownership/trust (`canUseGenerators`) correctly, but on denial it did a bare `return` instead of `event.cancel = true` — meaning a non-owner/non-trusted player's held item could still place/use against the block, and they got zero feedback (no message at all). Now cancels unconditionally the moment the interaction is recognized as generator-related, then gates the Hub opening, with an actual denial message.
- **Fixed: mob generators ignored player-built containment boxes.** `tickMobGenerator`'s spawn-position picker always used a flat, fixed `±4` block square around the generator with no wall-awareness at all — a "crasher/grinder" box smaller than 8×8 did nothing to contain spawns. Replaced with `findMobSpawnSpots()`, a bounded flood-fill from the generator's own floor level that stops expanding at solid blocks — this naturally respects whatever size/shape box a player actually built (a wall now genuinely blocks the search), while reproducing the exact same fixed-square behavior when there's no box at all (open ground → nothing stops the flood-fill until the outer radius cap).
- **Fixed (probable, high-confidence): general block placement was unprotected by plot permissions for non-owners.** `world.beforeEvents.playerPlaceBlock` is called via optional chaining because it may not fire on a world without the right experimental toggle enabled; the intended stable-world fallback (an after-event `setblock ... air destroy` undo) was gated behind `if (!world.beforeEvents.playerPlaceBlock)` — checking whether the *property exists on the API surface*, not whether it actually *fires*. If the property is present-but-inert on a non-experimental world, that condition is false and the fallback never registers, leaving placement completely unenforced — while generator placement stayed correctly enforced the whole time because it's gated by its own always-on `afterEvents.playerPlaceBlock` subscriber, unrelated to this fallback. Fixed by registering the fallback unconditionally; redundant with a working before-event is harmless since a cancelled placement never reaches the after-event at all. **Confirmed ruled out as the explanation:** `isAdmin()`'s Operator-permission-level fallback treating every player as admin — this realm's default player permission is Member/Visitor, only the owner has Operator, so that specific theory doesn't apply here (though it remains a real fragility worth remembering if a *different* realm's default permission is ever set to Operator).
- **Fixed (BP 0.9.118): general block *breaking* bypassed by non-trusted strangers.** Reported as reproducible even for a player never added via `/plot trust` at all. The `permcheck` diagnostic from BP 0.9.116 was never actually needed to root-cause this — rather than wait on a live repro, added a second, unconditional `world.afterEvents.playerBreakBlock` subscriber right after the existing before-event handler: it re-runs `verifyRaidablePermission` and, if it should have been denied, restores the exact original block via `e.brokenBlockPermutation` (the after-event's built-in "what was destroyed" field). This closes the gap regardless of whatever specific mechanism was letting the before-event's `e.cancel = true` not take effect — a break that's genuinely already-cancelled never fires this after-event at all (the block was never removed), so the redundant check is a no-op in the normal case. One known imperfection: if a break already dropped loot before this fires, those drops aren't retroactively removed — still strictly better than the block staying gone outright. The `permcheck` logging from 0.9.116 is left in place regardless, in case this specific fix doesn't fully close it.
- **Fixed (BP 0.9.118, best-effort): EXP only ever accrued on a player's first kill, same player/same conditions on every later kill.** Couldn't pin an exact root cause even after this fix (no live repro data was available), so rather than leave it at "logs the failure," `setExp` now actively retries: `writeExpWithVerification(player, value, attempt)` re-attempts the write (and re-checks the read-back) up to `EXP_WRITE_MAX_RETRIES` (3) times, spaced 2 ticks apart, and only calls `Log.error("exp", ...)` once every retry has also failed. This turns a transient write/read-back mismatch into a self-healing one instead of a silently lost update; if the underlying issue is instead a genuinely persistent, structural one, the eventual log line still fires with the same detail as before. Deliberately did NOT wrap the `addExp(killer, gained)` call site itself in `system.run()` — the bounty-payout code three lines below it in the same `entityDie` handler does direct, unwrapped `addMoneyBig`/`pushMoneyMirror` calls and is not reported broken, which is evidence against a same-tick/event-context restriction being the cause here.
- **Shipped (BP 0.9.117): per-player plot permissions.** `/plot permissions` now opens a player picker (`openPlotPermissionsMenu`, DDUI `showSearchableActionMenu` — search box auto-appears once the trust list is long) instead of jumping straight to one blanket 5-toggle screen. Selecting a trusted player opens a small chooser (`openPlayerPermissionsMenu`: Edit / Reset to Default / Back) then the same 5 toggles (`openPlayerPermissionsEditor`) scoped to just that player. Storage is layered, not replaced: `getPlayerPlotPerm(id, uuid, key)` reads a per-player override (`plot_permover_${key}_${id}_${uuid}`) if one's been set, else falls through to the existing plot-wide `getPlotPerm(id, key)` (now called "Default (New Members)" in the UI, still edited via the original 5-toggle screen, renamed `openPlotDefaultPermissionsMenu`), which itself falls through to `PLOT_PERM_DEFAULTS`. `canBuild`/`canBreak`/`canInteract`/`canUseGenerators`/`canManageTrust` all route through `getPlayerPlotPerm` now instead of the plot-wide getter directly. `/plot untrust` calls `clearPlayerPlotPermOverrides(id, uuid)` so a departed member's custom settings don't silently resurface if re-trusted later. **No per-player avatar/face icon** — confirmed impossible on Bedrock in either forms API (DDUI's `CustomForm.button` has zero icon support at all per Microsoft's own changelog, already noted above the `showModal` definition; the older `ActionFormData.button(label, icon)` only takes a static resource-pack texture path, never a specific player's actual skin) — chose the searchable-list tradeoff over a generic identical-for-everyone icon, per direct instruction.
- **Fixed (BP 0.9.119): "whitewashed" generator/spawner UI button TEXT.** The earlier "blank white icon" theory was wrong — the user clarified it's the button *text* rendering plain white regardless of its intended color, on the Market's generator-purchase screens. That's the exact, already-documented DDUI limitation noted above `showModal`'s definition: `CustomForm.button` has **zero color support** confirmed against Microsoft's own changelog — every §-coded button label built through DDUI (`showActionMenu`/`showSearchableActionMenu`) silently renders as flat default white text, not the intended color. Four generator/market screens were built on DDUI and had heavily color-coded labels (afford = gold, can't-afford = grey, tier state, etc.): `openGeneratorCategoryMenu` ("Market"), `openPlotUpgradesMenu`, `openGeneratorItemList` (Ore/Block Generators listing), `openGeneratorQuantityMenu` (×1/×2/×4 purchase). All four converted from `showActionMenu` to plain `ActionFormData` — the same API every OTHER generator screen (Hub, Upgrades, Prestige) already used and was never affected. `ActionFormData` has no `disabled` option (unlike DDUI's `ButtonOptions.disabled`, used here for unaffordable purchases) — buttons that used to be disabled now stay clickable, but `purchasePlotBorderUpgrade`/`purchasePlotFlight`/`attemptPurchase` all already independently re-validate affordability and send their own error message, the same safety net `openGeneratorItemList`'s own listing (never disabled, color-only) already relied on. `openGeneratorCustomQuantityModal` (a text-input modal, no color-coded button labels to lose) and the actual generator Hub/Upgrade/Prestige/wild-spawner-pickup screens were untouched — they were already on `ActionFormData` and never had this bug.

**Per-line upgrade color coding (BP 0.9.120).** `GEN_NODES` entries now carry a `line` field, distinct from `category` — `category` is just which tab a node lives under, `line` is the actual tiered progression a player tracks visually. This distinction matters because two categories each secretly contain two unrelated lines: "mob" has Pack Size *and* Looting, "utility" has Display Tag (standalone) *and* the Alert line — coloring by category alone would blur those together in the same list. `GEN_LINE_COLORS` maps each of the 7 real multi-tier lines to one fixed color: `speed`→aqua, `storage`→blue, `yield`→gold, `quality`→lightPurple, `alert`→yellow, `pack_size`→darkAqua, `looting`→darkGreen; `genLineColor(node)` falls back to white for the 3 standalone one-off upgrades (Auto-Collect, Fuel Boost, Display Tag) — deliberately no dedicated color for those, since there's no second instance of them to visually link, and forcing an arbitrary hue onto a single button adds noise without conveying anything. Color choices avoid green/red (already mean "owned"/"can't afford" elsewhere in the same button and would read as a false error/success state if reused as line identity), darkPurple (already the addon-wide "this is an EXP amount" color, see `formatExp` call sites), and black/dark_blue (both low-contrast against Minecraft's dark dialog background). Applied in `openGeneratorUpgradeCategoryScreen`: ownership is signaled by a "✓" symbol (colorblind-safe on its own) with the line color on the name; locked nodes stay fully gray regardless of line, only "lighting up" into their line's color once reachable (a soft reveal cue); the prereq-chain hint on a locked node colors each ancestor by *that ancestor's own* line (not necessarily the current node's line — Buffer Alert's chain correctly shows Buffer I in storage's blue, not alert's yellow, since that's a real cross-line dependency). The category screen's own "◀ Back" button was switched from its usual aqua to plain gray specifically on this screen, since aqua and dark_aqua are both claimed as line colors here (Speed, Pack Size) and Back would otherwise blend into whichever category's showing.

**Fixed (BP 0.9.121): kit disparity (the gear-gap PvP block, `DisparityModule`) had two real bugs, both in how `player_kit_score_${id}` — a player's registered "floor" score from claiming a kit — got read and written.** Reported as "doesn't handle reverse order kits" — confirmed and root-caused as: `registerKitScore(player, score)` blindly *overwrote* the stored score on every kit claim, so claiming kits out of tier order (e.g. a starter kit grabbed for its food/tools, claimed *after* an earlier veteran-tier kit) silently lowered a player's gear-gap floor back down, even though they still had every item the stronger kit gave them. Fixed by merging with `Math.max(prev, score)` instead of overwriting — a player is never worse protected than the single best kit they've ever claimed. Second, related bug found in the same system: `getKitScore()` treated the stored kit score as the *sole* source of truth once one existed at all, completely ignoring current live gear — meaning a player's disparity score froze permanently at kit-claim time and never reflected any real progression (loot, purchases, upgrades) afterward. Fixed by taking `Math.max(stored, gearScore(player))` at read time, which still preserves the original anti-cheat intent (a stored floor can't be bypassed by temporarily unequipping — "gear-swap gaming," per the function's own pre-existing comment) while letting genuine improvement actually count. Third bug, found while reading the surrounding code, not from the original report: `world.afterEvents.playerLeave` was unconditionally wiping `player_kit_score_${id}` on every logout — with no comment explaining why — meaning the "persistent" floor a kit claim is supposed to grant didn't actually survive a single relog; `getKitScore()` would silently fall back to live-gear-only until the player claimed another kit. Removed that wipe entirely; the score now persists exactly like every other per-player stat (money, EXP), keyed the same stable way. Both admin-facing diagnostics (`/scriptevent disparity:score` and the `Disparity — Admin` control panel screen) were duplicating the old "prefer stored, else live" logic inline for their "effective" reading — both now call `getKitScore()` directly instead, so they can't drift from the real gate logic again.

**Added (BP 0.9.122): PvP Zone (`PvpZoneModule`) — one admin-configured area where the gear-gap disparity check is switched off entirely, and kills pay bonus EXP + a flat cash reward.** Square horizontal region (center X/Z + radius), unbounded vertically — same simplification the pre-existing Spawn Zone (`SPAWN_ZONE_RADIUS`) already makes, not a true cuboid. One zone at a time; configured via Control Panel → World → PvP Zone (`openPvpZoneMenu`/`setPvpZoneFlow`), same config/menu shape as Spawn Ball/Admin Pearl (`DEFAULT_PVP_ZONE_CONFIG`, `pvpZoneCoreRef.store`). `inPvpZone(loc, dimensionId)` is the single check both effects route through:
- **Disparity bypass**: `DisparityModule`'s `entityHurt` handler now returns early (skips the score-gap comparison entirely) if `inPvpZone(defender.location, defender.dimension.id)` — checked against the defender, matching the existing Spawn Zone PvP block's own convention of checking `entity.location` rather than the attacker's.
- **Bonus EXP**: `zoneBonusPct` (from `pvpZoneCfg.expBonusPct`, default 50) folds into the *same* multiplication chain as looting/event/streak/wanted in `StatsModule`'s kill-EXP formula (`(1 + (sBonus + wBonus + zoneBonusPct) / 100)`) — stacks with all of them rather than being a separate, competing bonus.
- **Bonus cash**: a flat `pvpZoneCfg.cashBonus` (default 500, plain number in config — deliberately NOT BigInt, since `core.store.set` JSON-serializes the config and `JSON.stringify` throws on BigInt; converted to BigInt only at the point of calling `addMoneyBig`) paid on every zone kill via the same `pullShopSpending`/`addMoneyBig`/`pushMoneyMirror` sequence bounty payouts already use. This is a genuinely new reward, not a multiplier — there was no baseline cash-per-kill anywhere in the addon before this (only bounty, and only if one was placed); zone kills, bounty payout (if any), and EXP all pay independently on the same kill.

**Replaced (BP 0.9.123): the trust-list access gate is gone — plot access is now a real permission system, not a binary "on the list or not."** Two separate user requests drove this in the same pass: (1) "the plot permissions UI is terrible, redo it" (a screenshot showed a flat white-text DDUI dialog), and (2) "replace the trust system entirely with permissions — everyone should have access by default, zero permissions beyond walking in, and per-player grants shouldn't fight a Basic-permissions lockout." Both landed together since the redesigned UI needed the new data model anyway (a "Default (New Members)" screen only made sense when there was a membership step to have joined).
- **Data model**: `PLOT_PERM_DEFAULTS` gained a 6th key, `entry` (walk onto the plot), defaulting to `true` for everyone — the one deliberate exception to "zero permissions by default." `build`/`break`/`interact`/`generators`/`trustmgmt` all flipped from defaulting `true` (for trusted members) to defaulting `false` (for everyone). `canBuild`/`canBreak`/`canInteract`/`canUseGenerators` dropped the `isAuthorized(player, id) &&` gate they used to have in front of `getPlayerPlotPerm(...)` — the layered override → Basic → default fallback chain (unchanged from the 0.9.117 per-player permissions work) *is* the access control now, there's nothing above it to pass through first. `canManageTrust` renamed `canManagePerms` (same `trustmgmt` storage key, just no longer literally about a trust list). A new `canEnter(player, id)` (`isPlotOwner || getPlayerPlotPerm(id, player.id, "entry")`) is the physical-presence gate.
- **`isAuthorized` deleted outright** — every call site swapped to whichever check actually applies there: `enforceBoundary`'s hard clamp and `/plot home <name>` both now gate on `canEnter` (this is the actual mechanism behind "everyone can walk onto everyone's plot by default, unless Basic Entry is turned off or that specific player is blocked"); the guest-notice HUD sweep also switched to `canEnter`, so it now stays silent for the vast majority of visitors instead of flagging every non-owner the way it used to; the shop chest scanner, `shopResolveTarget`, and the sign-auto-register handler all switched to `canInteract` (container permission is the actually-relevant check for "can you register a shop on this chest," not raw plot access).
- **Trust list repurposed, not deleted.** The old `getTrustList`/`setTrustList` (backed by `plot_trust_*` dynamic properties) are renamed `getPermRoster`/`setPermRoster` — same storage, same chunking, zero data migration needed for the list itself, but a different meaning: it's no longer an access gate, it's the index of "who currently has at least one explicit permission override on this plot," used purely to populate the permissions UI's player list. `addToPermRoster`/`removeFromPermRoster` are new — a player joins the roster the moment an override is set for them (via the new player-search flow, not a separate trust command), and falls off it when "Reset to Basic" clears their overrides.
- **Schema migration (`EPITAPH_SCHEMA` 1→2, `runSchemaMigrations`)**: since `build`/`break`/`interact`/`generators` flipped their *default* from true to false, every already-trusted player on every existing plot would have silently lost those four permissions on update — they never needed an explicit override before, trust list membership alone used to be enough. The migration walks every claimed plot's existing roster once and writes explicit `true` overrides for those four keys (skipping the owner, and skipping anyone who already has a `build` override so a genuinely-customized player's settings aren't clobbered) — after this runs once, every previously-trusted player's access is bit-for-bit unchanged, and it's a no-op on any plot claimed after 0.9.123 ships.
- **`/plot trust` and `/plot untrust` removed entirely**, both the command handlers in `main.js`'s `dispatchPlotCommand` and the `plot:trust`/`plot:untrust` entries in the scriptevent relay map. Granting or restricting a specific player now happens entirely inside `/plot permissions` — there's no "add to trust list" step separate from "set their permissions" anymore, so a dedicated command for it had nothing left to do. `Epitaph-PlotCommands` (the satellite pack that exposes `/plot:*` as real slash commands) had its `trust`/`untrust` enum entries and switch cases removed too, version bumped 1.1.0 → 1.2.0. `/plot info` and `/plot list` updated to talk about "custom permissions"/"special permissions" instead of "trusted."
- **UI redesign** (this is the part that actually fixes "the UI is terrible"): `openPlotPermissionsMenu` and its three sub-screens were rebuilt on plain `ActionFormData`/`ModalFormData` instead of DDUI's `CustomForm` (`showActionMenu`/`showSearchableActionMenu`/`showModal`) — the same class of fix as the 0.9.119 generator-Market whitewashed-button fix, for the identical root cause: DDUI's `CustomForm.button` still has zero § color support (see `stripFormatCodes`'s own header note), so every button in the old picker rendered as flat gray-on-white regardless of what color code was in the label. The new `openPlotPermissionsMenu` is an `ActionFormData` list: a gold "Basic Permissions (Everyone)" button, a "Search / Add Player..." button (`textures/ui/magnifyingGlass`), then one row per roster member with a generic `textures/ui/icon_steve` icon (the same generic-person stand-in Player Shops already uses for seller listings — a real player avatar remains impossible on Bedrock's forms API, confirmed back in the 0.9.117 per-player-permissions work) and a live one-line summary of exactly which permissions they have overridden, colored green/red per key (`permSummary`). This does give up DDUI's live search-as-you-type filtering (which only ever mattered past ~8 roster entries), a deliberate trade of a rarely-relevant feature for the visual quality that was actually reported broken. `openPlotPlayerSearch` (new) is a `ModalFormData` text field that resolves any typed name via `lookupUUID` — online or not, previously-granted-access or not — straight into the per-player editor, which is how "search for them in the permissions UI" now replaces `/plot trust <name>` as the only way to grant someone something. `openPlotDefaultPermissionsMenu` renamed `openBasicPermissionsMenu` (same `getPlotPerm`/`setPlotPerm` storage, now with the 6th Entry toggle and a name that doesn't imply a membership step that no longer exists). `openPlayerPermissionsMenu`'s Edit/Reset/Back chooser also moved to `ActionFormData`; since it has no `disabled`-button concept (unlike DDUI's `ButtonOptions.disabled`, used here before), clicking Reset when nothing is customized just re-opens the same screen instead of doing anything, rather than being greyed out.

**Addon-wide command autocomplete audit (BP 0.9.124) — went through every registered command (main pack + all 9 satellite packs), verified param types against Microsoft's own scripting docs rather than assuming, and fixed what was actually fixable.** Prompted by direct instruction not to just "believe or think it works." Key finding up front: `CustomCommandRegistry` fixes ONE parameter list per command name — it cannot type an argument differently depending on which enum value an earlier parameter resolved to, which is why the big consolidated `/epitaph <sub> [arg1] [arg2]` command (16 subcommands sharing two generic String slots) could never give any of them real autocomplete without splitting the ones that needed a different type out into their own registration. Confirmed via Microsoft Learn's own Custom Commands docs: `PlayerSelector` resolves to `Player[]` (a real target-selector match against currently-connected entities, same client autocomplete `/tp` gets) and there is no documented mechanism to update a `registerEnum`'d list after its one, synchronous, startup-time call — both facts drove every decision below.
- **Real bug found and fixed, not just an autocomplete gap: `/plot claim <name>` has never actually worked via the space-form slash command.** It shared its optional `playerName` slot with `/plot home <name>` inside the single `plot:plot` registration, but the `"claim"` case in `Epitaph-PlotCommands` relayed `[]` (hardcoded empty args) to the main pack regardless of what was typed — so `/plot claim Steve` silently behaved exactly like `/plot claim` with no name, always failing with "/plot claim <player>". Fixed by pulling `claim` out of the `plot:sub` enum entirely into its own `plot:claim` registration with a real `PlayerSelector` parameter (target must already be online — `dispatchPlotCommand`'s claim case has no offline fallback — so nothing was lost switching types), which happens to fix the bug (the target now actually reaches the main pack) as a side effect of fixing the autocomplete.
- **Second real bug: `/plot:delete <player>` and `/plot:setmaxradius <r>` were documented in `PLOT_HELP` but never registered as real commands at all.** `Epitaph-PlotCommands` only ever registered `plot:plot`/`plot:debuggen`/`plot:debugclear` — the exact same class of gap the 0.8.9x fix already found once for `/plot border`/`/plot list` (a command handled on the main pack's scriptevent-relay side with no client-facing registration to actually reach it). Both are now real registrations: `plot:delete` keeps a plain String target (the main-pack handler explicitly supports deleting an *offline* player's plot via a stored name→UUID lookup, which `PlayerSelector` can't resolve), `plot:setmaxradius` gets a real `Integer` field. A couple of internal messages/alerts that had been (incorrectly) telling admins to type these with a space instead of the colon they actually need were fixed to match.
- **`/pay <player> <amount>` → `playerName` switched from String to PlayerSelector** in `Epitaph-PayCommand` — real client autocomplete of online names, zero capability lost, since `handlePayCommand` already requires the recipient to be online ("Pick one online player.") before this change. The satellite pack extracts `.name` from the resolved `Player[]` before relaying, since a scriptevent message is plain text and can't carry an object — `handlePayCommand` on the main-pack side needed no changes at all.
- **`/epitaph balance [player]` removed outright, not fixed** — direct instruction: the Stats HUD board already shows a player's live cash balance on-screen at all times, so a command whose entire job was "print your own balance in chat" was pure redundancy. `handleBalanceCommand`/`balanceStats` deleted (nothing else called either one); every hint/tip/help-text/admin-panel line mentioning it updated to point at the HUD board instead.
- **`/epitaph:setworldborder <radius>` split into its own dedicated registration** with a real `Integer` parameter, replacing the hand-parsed `parseInt(a1, 10)` on the shared String slot — on direct instruction, accepting the colon-form syntax change (`/epitaph:setworldborder`, not `/epitaph setworldborder`) as the trade for a real typed field, since `CustomCommandRegistry` has no way to type just this one subcommand's argument differently from the dozen others sharing `/epitaph`'s registration. Admin-only, infrequently run, so the syntax change was judged an acceptable cost — this was an explicit three-way choice put to the user (new bare satellite pack for `/balance` + colon for setworldborder / colon-form for both / leave both as-is) rather than decided unilaterally, since it's a real breaking-syntax tradeoff.
- **Three cases audited and deliberately left as String, each for a different, documented reason — not oversights:**
  - `/bounty [player] [amount]` — `handleBountyPlace` explicitly supports placing a bounty on an *offline* player via a stored name→UUID lookup ("they need to have joined... at least once," not "be online now"); `PlayerSelector` can only ever resolve currently-connected entities, so converting this would silently break a real, working feature. (This is the exact same shape of parameter as `/pay`'s, which made the opposite call — the difference is entirely in whether the underlying handler requires the target online.)
  - `/kit claim <name>` — kit names live in the MAIN pack's dynamic properties, fully admin-editable at runtime with no restart (`createKitFlow`/`editKitFlow`); dynamic properties are namespaced per writing-pack UUID in LevelDB, so the separate Kit Commands satellite pack cannot read that list at all, at any time, and even a same-pack enum can only be registered once, synchronously, at startup, with no documented update mechanism. A live-accurate enum here is structurally impossible without abandoning live-editable kits. `/kit list` already exists to cover the gap.
  - `/epitaph ann_fire <id>` / `cmd_fire <id>` / `cmd_toggle <id>` — same-pack (unlike kits), so the cross-pack namespacing issue doesn't apply, but these IDs are admin-created at any time via the control panel, and the single most common workflow is create → immediately test-fire by the ID just typed. A startup-snapshotted enum would be missing exactly that ID until the next restart — actively worse than plain text for the case that matters most, not just unhelpful.
  - `/epitaph struct_place <name>` — pack structure IDs actually ARE static (baked in at build time via `.mcstructure` files, never runtime-editable), so the staleness problem above doesn't apply here, but whether `structureManager.getPackStructureIds()` is safely callable at the synchronous point `registerEnum` must run (`system.beforeEvents.startup`, before the world is guaranteed loaded) is unconfirmed — and an exception there risks aborting the rest of `registerBuiltinCommands`' registration pass for every other `/epitaph` subcommand, not just this one. Judged not worth that risk for a rarely-used admin debug command that already has a working substitute (`/epitaph struct` prints the exact valid IDs to copy from).
- **Everything else was already correct** — `/epitaph:grant`/`/epitaph:giveadv` already use `PlayerSelector`/`ItemType` (real autocomplete for both players and item IDs); `/shop`, `/market`, `/raid`, `/sellall`, `/guide` have no freeform player/name arguments at all, and every one of their `Enum`-typed subcommand selectors was cross-checked against its actual switch/dispatch logic for drift (none found beyond what's listed above).

**Added (BP 0.9.125): generator idle placeholder (bedrock + black smoke) and a spawner debug bounding-box overlay for grinder building.** Two small, unrelated QOL requests landed together.
- **Generator idle placeholder.** A block/ore generator's output slot (`y+1` above the generator) used to sit as bare, invisible air the entire time between a player harvesting it and the generator's own next scheduled production tick — which can be many seconds on a slow generator — and *permanently* for any generator with Auto-Collect purchased, since that path routes straight to a chest and never places a physical block at all (`tickGenerator`'s `auto_collect` branch). A bare air gap reads as "broken," not "idle." Fixed with a single self-healing `system.runInterval` (20 ticks) added to `ShopModule.onWorldLoad`, right after the existing mob-generator flame loop: for every active non-mob generator, if the output block is air, set it to `minecraft:bedrock`; if it's already bedrock, spawn two `minecraft:basic_smoke_particle` puffs just above it. No event listener or extra per-generator state needed — it just re-checks the live block each pass, so it self-heals from *any* cause of the slot going empty (manual mining, admin creative-mode removal, anything), and it works for Auto-Collect generators too since it doesn't care why the slot is air, only that it is. `tickGenerator`'s own "output slot occupied" check (`main.js`, right before the buffer-routing branch) was updated to treat `minecraft:bedrock` identically to air — bedrock isn't a real generator product (confirmed against `BASE_INTERVALS`/`REFINEMENT_TABLE` — no generator type or refinement tier ever produces it), so the moment a generator's real schedule fires, `outputBlock.setType(blockId)` overwrites the placeholder directly, exactly like it would overwrite air.
- **Spawner debug bounding box.** Mob generators ("Spawners") gained a "Show Spawn Radius" / "Hide Spawn Radius" toggle in the Generator Hub (owner/admin only, `isMobGen` branch), so building an efficient grinder (a walled containment box around a spawner) no longer requires guessing or reading source to find `MOB_SPAWN_MAX_RADIUS`. Toggling on renders a full 3D cage — bottom rim, top rim (3 blocks up), and four vertical corner posts, using `minecraft:end_rod` (deliberately different from `BORDER_HINT_PARTICLE`'s plot-boundary particle so the two overlays never read as the same thing) — spanning exactly the ±`MOB_SPAWN_MAX_RADIUS` square `findMobSpawnSpots`' flood-fill can ever reach. This is the *outer cap*, not the exact walkable shape inside whatever containment box a player has already built (that shape depends on walls that don't exist yet at the point this is useful — showing the maximum reachable extent is the whole point, so a player knows exactly where they can safely place walls). `genRadiusDebugPlayers` (`playerId -> genId`, one spawner at a time per player, same simplicity as `/plot border`'s `persistentBorderPlayers`) drives a `system.runInterval` (4 ticks, same cadence as the plot-border loop) added right after the idle-placeholder loop; self-heals if the generator is deactivated/removed (checked live every pass, same pattern as the plot-border loop's own self-cleanup) and is cleared on player logout (`persistentBorderPlayers.delete`'s existing `playerLeave` handler gained a matching `genRadiusDebugPlayers.delete` line right next to it).

**Fixed (BP 0.9.126): the 0.9.125 spawn-radius debug box never actually rendered, and mob generator names were wrong/ugly.** Both reported live, same session as the 0.9.125 feature shipped.
- **Root cause of the invisible box: `GEN_RADIUS_DEBUG_PARTICLE` was set to `"minecraft:end_rod"` — that identifier doesn't exist.** The real vanilla particle has no underscore: `"minecraft:endrod"`. Confirmed this time against the actual vanilla particle list instead of guessing again (the same category of mistake this project has been burned by before with icon paths, just for particles instead — except particles apparently throw on an invalid id rather than silently no-op the way a bad `ActionFormData` icon path does, and the render loop's own `try {} catch (_) {}` swallowed that throw completely, producing the exact reported symptom: no error anywhere, just nothing on screen). Also tightened the loop's mob-generator guard from a strict `!== true` equality check to a plain truthy check (`!gw(...)`), matching how every other `gen_is_mob_*` read in this file already does it — not confirmed as a second bug, but a real inconsistency worth closing while already in this code.
- **Mob generator naming was wrong in three places, all fixed together:** (1) the Generator Hub's title bar always said "X Generator" even for spawners — fixed to say "Spawner" when `isMobGen`, matching the body line just below it which already did this correctly (`${genTypeLabel(type)} ${isMobGen ? "Spawner" : "Generator"}`) — the title bar had simply never been updated to match when that body-line convention was established; (2) the "Remove Generator?" confirm dialog had the exact same hardcoded "Generator" wording, in both its title and body, now driven by the same `kindLabel` pattern; (3) the actual **item** a wild spawner pick-up gives out (`epitaph:generator_spider`, etc.) never had a display name set at all — this resource pack has no lang file (`en_US.lang`) anywhere, and no block/item entry in `blocks.json` supplies one either, so with nothing overriding it, Bedrock was showing whatever it auto-derives from the raw identifier in the hotbar/inventory, not a clean name. `openWildSpawnerMenu` now sets `genItem.nameTag = \`${chosen.label} Spawner\`` (e.g. "Spider Spawner", "Cave Spider Spawner") — the exact short names asked for. `attemptPurchase` (ore/block generators bought from `/market`) had the identical gap and got the same fix for consistency (`${item.label} Generator`, e.g. "Diamond Ore Generator"), applied via a small `makeGenStack()` helper so the nameTag is set at all three `ItemStack` construction points (direct inventory give, and both mail-overflow fallback paths) rather than just the common case. Confirmed the nameTag survives the mailbox round-trip too — `mailSnapshotFromStack`/`mailStackFromSnapshot` already explicitly preserve `nameTag` through the JSON-snapshot storage, no changes needed there.

**Fixed for real this time (BP/RP 0.9.127): generator/spawner item names were showing the literal, untranslated `tile.epitaph:generator_<type>.name` key on-screen.** The 0.9.126 fix (runtime `nameTag` set in `attemptPurchase`/`openWildSpawnerMenu`) only ever covered items created through those two exact code paths — it never addressed the actual root cause, which the user confirmed directly by reporting the literal raw key text. Root cause: this resource pack had **no `texts/` directory at all** — no `languages.json`, no `en_US.lang`, ever, for the whole addon's history. Every `epitaph:generator_*` block (all 53 of them — 46 ore/block types plus the 7 mob/spawner types) has no lang entry to resolve its name from, so Bedrock falls back to showing the raw translation key literally in any context that doesn't go through an item's own `nameTag` override (i.e. `/give`, creative inventory, command blocks, admin tools, or simply looking at the placed block) — the actual, universal fix is a lang file, not more `nameTag` call sites. Added `resource_pack/texts/languages.json` (`["en_US"]`) and `resource_pack/texts/en_US.lang` with both `tile.<id>.name` and `item.<id>.name` entries for every key in `blocks.json` — generated directly from that file's own key list using the exact same title-casing `genTypeLabel()` already applies in `main.js`, suffixed `" Generator"` for ore/block types or `" Spawner"` for the 7 mob types (`Zombie Spawner`, `Cave Spider Spawner`, `Magma Cube Spawner`, etc. — the exact short names asked for). Nothing regenerates this automatically; keep it in sync by hand if a new generator type is ever added to `blocks.json`. The 0.9.126 `nameTag` sets are harmless and left in place (redundant with the lang default now, but a real per-instance override still takes priority if ever wanted). Since this is the **first-ever RP asset change** for this addon (previously only touched for spawn-egg icons — see the RP module's own description), both `resource_pack/manifest.json`'s header AND module versions were bumped (0.9.14→0.9.15, 0.9.10→0.9.11), and the main BP manifest's own dependency entry pointing at the RP's UUID was updated to match (`0.9.14`→`0.9.15`) — a stale dependency version pin here is exactly the kind of thing that silently breaks re-import, per this file's own standing version-bump rule.

**Fixed (BP 0.9.128): Prestige was completely non-functional for every spawner, showing a nonsensical "Trade Zombie blocks" screen the user asked about directly.** `openPrestigeMenu` builds its trade-in item as `` `minecraft:${gen_type}` `` — correct for a block generator (`gen_type` is a real block, e.g. `"diamond_ore"` → `minecraft:diamond_ore`), but for a mob generator `gen_type` is the *entity* id (`"zombie"`, `"spider"`, ...), which is not an item at all — nothing can ever be "held" of it, so `held` was permanently `0`, `canAfford` permanently `false`, and the Hub's Prestige button was permanently stuck reading "Not enough Zombie" (or Spider, or whichever mob) for every single spawner, forever. Not a display bug — no spawner has ever been prestige-able. Given the choice between inventing a real per-mob trade-item economy (Rotten Flesh for Zombie, Bone for Skeleton, String for Spider/Cave Spider, Blaze Rod for Blaze, Magma Cream for Magma Cube, with Silverfish needing a substitute since it has no natural vanilla drop — real design and balancing work) versus simply not offering it, the user chose to disable Prestige for spawners outright. The Hub's "✦ Prestige" button is now gated behind `!isMobGen`, matching the exact same exclusion pattern `GEN_NODES`' own `blockOnly` flag already applies to Yield/Quality/Auto-Collect/Fuel Boost for the identical underlying reason (no output-block concept exists for mob generators). `openPrestigeMenu` itself also gained a defense-in-depth guard (sends "Prestige isn't available for spawners." and returns immediately if ever called on a mob generator directly) in case anything else ever reaches it. Guide book page 9 updated to say "Block/ore generators only" up front (197 bytes, well under the 256 cap) rather than implying every generator can Prestige.

**Added (BP 0.9.129): four market/shop education features, all on direct instruction, in priority order — "players don't know what's sellable or the rates" and "players confuse `/market sell` with `/shop`."**
- **`/sellall` and `/market sell` itemized receipts.** Both used to reply with a flat "Sold N stack(s) for $X." — no breakdown, no framing, no nudge toward the better option. New shared `formatSellReceipt(breakdown, total, mktBonus)` (`main.js`, right above `sellAllInventory`) builds a genuine per-item receipt (`Sold 64x Diamond Ore  →  $1,280`, one line per distinct item type actually sold), a Total line explicitly labeled `(NPC Market flat rate)`, and a standing closing line — `Player Bazaar shops pay more — /shop browse` — on *every single sell*, not just a one-time tip. Both `sellAllInventory` and `finalizeMarketChest` now build a `Map<typeId, {amount, subtotal}>` while iterating instead of just a stack counter, and call the shared formatter instead of their own inline message.
- **`/market rates`** — a new subcommand (`market:sub` enum gained `"rates"`, same `market:market` registration, no new satellite pack needed since it's a sibling of the existing `sell` subcommand). Opens `openMarketRatesMenu` — a plain `ActionFormData` list (deliberately *not* DDUI's `showSearchableActionMenu` — DDUI's `CustomForm.button` still has zero color support, the exact "whitewashed buttons" issue already fixed twice elsewhere in this file, and a price list is the worst possible place to lose color) showing every one of the 12 `MARKET_SELL_PRICES` entries with its icon, name, and price, each explicitly labeled "(NPC floor price)". Also added as a 5th button ("NPC Market Rates") directly in the main `/market` menu (`openGeneratorCategoryMenu`) for players who find it browsing rather than typing the command.
- **Pre-sell preview.** `/market sell` (and the "Sell Items" button, both routes) no longer places the temporary chest immediately — `openMarketSellPreview` first scans the player's current inventory (read-only, nothing is touched or removed) and shows a `showConfirm` dialog: an itemized "what's sellable right now" breakdown, a note that unsellable items get handed straight back, and a `~$` total explicitly framed as an *estimate* (not a locked-in quote, since the physical chest's real contents at close-time could differ from what's in the inventory at preview-time) before the chest is ever placed. Confirming calls `openMarketSellChest` exactly as before; cancelling places nothing.
- **"NPC Market" vs "Player Bazaar" — consistent naming pass.** Applied across the guide book (Page 10 retitled "NPC MARKET", Page 11 retitled "PLAYER BAZAAR", both content rewritten around the new framing; a stale page-range cross-reference in one of the rotating hints — "Guide pg. 9–10" — was actually wrong even before this pass, corrected to the real 10–11), the two tip-rotation arrays (`PLAYER_HINTS`/`TIPS`, several entries reworded, two new ones added for `/market rates`), the `/shop browse` screen's title (`"Browse Shops"` → `"Player Bazaar"`), the `/market` menu's title (`"Market"` → `"NPC Market"`), and the buyer/seller/offline-payout chat messages for a Bazaar purchase (`"...from Steve's shop."` → `"...from Steve's Bazaar stall."`, `"from shop sales while you were offline"` → `"from Bazaar sales while you were offline"`). The distinction is now baked into the vocabulary itself rather than left to be inferred from behavior.
- **Not built this pass** (raised by the same request, explicitly lower priority than the four above): a `/shop browse` "vs NPC Market rate" column showing whether a seller's price beats/matches/undercuts the flat rate, and a more detailed per-item "your shop sold X while you were away" join notification (today's offline-payout notice already exists and already fires, just as a lump cash total rather than itemized — see `shopClaimPendingPayout`'s `playerSpawn` handler). A physical price-board build at spawn was explicitly scoped as a manual, zero-code task for the user, not something to script.

**`/plot sethome`/`delhome` let the owner pin `/plot home` to an exact spot** (`plot_home_x/y/z_${id}`, `getPlotHome`/`setPlotHome`/`clearPlotHome`) instead of always the plot's dead center with an auto-detected safe Y. This exists because the center+auto-scan-down-from-`PLOT_Y` approach (`findSafeLandingY`) assumes a solid island is still there — once an owner's actual build sits somewhere else on the plot, or they've torn the starter island down entirely, that assumption breaks. `teleportToPlot`/`suspendPlayer`/`releaseSuspendedPlayer` all take an optional `landPoint`: when set, it's used verbatim (no block-centering, no safe-Y scan — the owner chose that exact spot) and the "is the chunk loaded yet" check uses the same any-successful-`getBlock`-call idiom `waitForChunkLoad` already uses, rather than testing for a specific non-air block — a demolished island legitimately has nothing solid to test for. `sethome` validates the target point is within the owner's own plot bounds (`getPlotBoundsBlocks`); visiting `/plot home <name>` uses the *target's* configured home, not always their center.

**Satellite pack gotcha found and fixed while touching this: `/plot border` and `/plot list` were never actually reachable as slash commands.** The main pack's scriptevent relay map already handled both, but `Epitaph-PlotCommands`' own `registerEnum("plot:sub", [...])` — the only thing that ever exposes a subcommand to the client — had stopped at the original five (`claim`/`home`/`info`/`trust`/`untrust`) and was never updated when border/list were added on the main-pack side. Folded both into the enum alongside the three genuinely new subcommands (`sethome`/`delhome`/`permissions`) rather than leaving that gap in place.

**`toGiveTargets(value)` (`main.js` line ~2932).** Accepts `PlayerSelector` array or single entity, and now also plain strings (looks up by `player.name` via `world3.getPlayers()`). Required for `/epitaph balance` and `/epitaph pay` which pass string args.

**Plot Flight — now real vanilla `/ability mayfly`, not a custom system (BP 0.9.130).** Every earlier note in this file (and in the addon's own code comments, until this pass) stated flatly that `/ability` requires Education Edition and is unusable on this realm. That was incomplete, per direct correction: Mojang's own current `/ability` documentation confirms the command still requires Education Edition *features*, but that's now a per-world toggle reachable from a normal Bedrock world's own settings (Experiments) rather than needing the separate Education Edition application — and this realm runs with it enabled. `grantPlotFlight`/`revokePlotFlight` (`main.js`, PLOT FLIGHT section, ~line 4023) now just run `player.runCommand("ability @s mayfly true")`/`"...false"` at the exact same plot entry/exit points as before (`enforceBoundary`'s authorized/raid-bypass/clamp branches, unchanged) and message the player once via a `plotFlyActive` Set guard (same "don't spam every tick" role `plotFlyEligible` used to play). Everything else the old system needed — gravity component-group swapping, double-tap-jump gesture detection, per-tick `applyImpulse` movement convergence, fall-damage cancellation — is gone from the active path entirely, because real `/ability mayfly true` hands the double-tap-Jump toggle and all movement to Bedrock's own client natively, exactly like Creative mode or an Operator-granted mayfly player; fall damage while actually flying is vanilla's own problem now too. `hasPlotFlightPerk`/`purchasePlotFlight` (the perk-ownership flag and its one-time purchase flow) are **completely unchanged** — only the activation mechanism changed, not whether or how the perk is bought.

**Fixed, then fixed again (BP 0.9.131 → 0.9.132): revoking `mayfly` didn't stop an already-active flying session.** Reported directly: a player leaving their plot kept floating in place, only actually falling once they double-tapped Jump themselves. Root cause, confirmed by testing rather than assumed: `/ability mayfly false` revokes the *permission* to fly, but does nothing to an already-in-progress flying *session* — `Player.isFlying` (a real, stable, read-only property) stays `true` and the player keeps hovering until the client's own double-tap-Jump gesture (its "stop flying" toggle) fires, independent of whatever the current mayfly value is. There is no second `/ability` toggle for the live flying state — `mayfly` is the only settable flight-related ability that exists at all (confirmed against Microsoft's own docs).

The 0.9.131 first attempt (`forceStopFlying`: every single tick, a small `applyImpulse` downward, escalating to a small `teleport` nudge, checking `isFlying` each time) was itself reported buggy — "slowly and buggily drop a few blocks, and then fly again." Two real problems, not one: (1) fighting the client's own flying-prediction with a tiny correction *every tick, forever* just repeatedly loses to it for a few frames before the next correction lands — that back-and-forth *is* the stutter; (2) far more importantly, neither the impulse nor the Y-only teleport ever touched the player's existing **horizontal velocity** — a player crossing their own plot boundary while flying at full creative speed carries that momentum right past the edge, which one tick later can carry them right back *inside* their own plot's footprint. `enforceBoundary` has no idea a forced fall is in progress; it just sees "standing on your own plot again" and calls `grantPlotFlight`, which re-enables mayfly and cancels the fall via `cancelForceStopFlying`. That's the "then fly again" — the direct consequence of the first fix never clearing the momentum that caused it, not a second, unrelated bug.

0.9.132 rewrote `forceStopFlying` to do far less, more decisively: `player.clearVelocity()` **first** (kills the exact horizontal momentum that was carrying players back onto their own plot — and matches `flight_legacy.js`'s own `deactivateFlight`, which learned this identical lesson for the old system: "leftover flight velocity kept coasting... a hard stop matches what toggling flight off should feel like"), then **one** real, decisive 1-block downward `teleport` instead of a stream of tiny ones — a single clean correction the client can't just re-absorb into its existing flying prediction the way a string of small nudges can. Re-checks `isFlying` every **4 ticks** now (not every tick), retrying up to **4 attempts** (~1s total) — deliberately slow so it never reads as fighting the player frame by frame — with a 2-tick head start before the very first correction so the `ability mayfly false` command has a moment to actually reach the client first. `grantPlotFlight` still cancels any in-progress `forceStopFlying` for that player (genuine re-entry — flying right back onto the plot — should still immediately restore flight, not keep getting pushed down); that's now safe rather than the bug's own trigger, since velocity gets cleared before every correction. `forceStopFlyingHandles` gets the same `playerLeave` cleanup as `plotFlyActive`.

**Fixed (BP 0.9.131): Plot Upgrades menu's border/flight button text rendered whitewashed, visible only on hover.** Not a DDUI issue this time — `openPlotUpgradesMenu` was already plain `ActionFormData`, which does support § color on buttons. Root cause: the border button's second line carried both the price *and* "Expands chunk-aligned border" (Flight's carried "One-time unlock" alongside its price) — a long two-line `ActionFormData` button label overflows the button's fixed on-screen width, and Bedrock silently truncates the visible label while moving the full text into an auto-generated hover tooltip, which renders in the client's own default *uncolored* style regardless of any § codes in the string — exactly the reported symptom. Fixed by moving the full border-tier progression (`64×64 → 96×96 → 128×128`) and the "one-time unlock" clarification into the form's body text instead (no per-button width limit there) and trimming both button labels down to just route + price, short enough to never need to truncate again.

**The old custom implementation is deprecated, not deleted** — preserved complete and working, exactly as it shipped, in a new file: `behavior_pack/scripts/flight_legacy.js`. This is a second script file alongside `main.js` in the same pack, imported nowhere right now (`main.js` does not `import` it), so it is fully inert. It's kept for exactly two scenarios: this realm's Education Edition features toggle ever gets turned off or becomes unavailable, or Mojang removes `/ability`'s mayfly ability outright. The file's own header comment has the exact re-enable steps (`initLegacyPlotFlight()` + swap `grantPlotFlight`/`revokePlotFlight`'s call sites to `legacyGrantPlotFlight`/`legacyRevokePlotFlight`). `behavior_pack/entities/player.json`'s `epitaph:flying`/`epitaph:grounded` component groups — which only the legacy system's gravity toggle ever used — were deliberately left in place, unused but intact, so re-enabling the legacy file doesn't also require restoring that file from scratch. (The gotcha about that file's vanilla source needing to come from a retail/stable-tagged `bedrock-samples` snapshot, not `preview`, still applies if it's ever refreshed — see the file itself, which still carries that history in its own header even though it's dormant.)

**Reverted back to `flight_legacy.js` as the active Plot Flight system (BP 0.9.133) — the `/ability mayfly` detour above lasted exactly 0.9.130→0.9.132.** Even the 0.9.132 `clearVelocity()` + decisive-teleport rewrite (previous paragraph) was reported still failing. Rather than attempt a third round of tuning the same driving logic, did actual web research into whether the underlying premise — `/ability mayfly` itself — was ever sound, and found it wasn't: four separate, still-open Mojang bug tracker (Mojira) entries confirm `/ability mayfly` is unreliable specifically for **non-operator** players — MCPE-158180 ("stops working after very brief time for non-OP"), MCPE-158606 ("Players without op cannot use the ability 'Mayfly'"), MCPE-150246 ("player takes fall damage and seems to be jumping" — non-host/non-op specifically), MCPE-158571 ("Fly Issue when Using /Ability"). Every Plot Flight grant in this addon goes to a non-operator (only the realm owner has Operator) — squarely the affected population. No amount of client-reconciliation-forcing script logic can reliably compensate for an ability that's flaky at the engine level for the exact players using it, which is why two consecutive, carefully-reasoned fixes to `forceStopFlying` both still failed in practice.

Rather than delete either implementation, swapped which one is dormant: `hasPlotFlightPerk`/`grantPlotFlight`/`revokePlotFlight` (`main.js`, PLOT FLIGHT section, ~line 4023 — same function names, so none of their call sites in `enforceBoundary`, `purchasePlotFlight`, or `openPlotUpgradesMenu` needed to change) are now thin one-line delegations to `flight_legacy.js`'s `legacyHasPlotFlightPerk`/`legacyGrantPlotFlight`/`legacyRevokePlotFlight`, imported via a plain ES import at the top of that section (ES imports are hoisted, so placement doesn't matter — same convention this bundle already relies on elsewhere). `onWorldLoad()` now calls `initLegacyPlotFlight()` (wires up the double-tap-jump gesture loop, per-tick movement, and fall-damage guard) instead of the ability system's `initAbilityPlotFlight()`. The entire 0.9.130-0.9.132 `/ability`-based implementation — `plotFlyActive`, `forceStopFlying`, `cancelForceStopFlying`, the `entityHurt` fall-damage guard, all of it — was extracted verbatim into a new file, `behavior_pack/scripts/flight_ability.js` (exported as `hasPlotFlightPerkAbility`/`grantPlotFlightAbility`/`revokePlotFlightAbility`/`initAbilityPlotFlight`, mirroring `flight_legacy.js`'s own established dormant-module pattern exactly — an inert ES module, no top-level side effects, all subscriptions gated behind an exported `init...()` nobody calls). Its header documents the same four Mojang bugs and the exact re-enable steps, for exactly one scenario: Mojang actually fixes them. A leftover `plotFlyActive.delete(e.playerId)`/`cancelForceStopFlying(e.playerId)` pair in `main.js`'s own top-level `playerLeave` handler (dead references to symbols that no longer exist in that scope, now that the block defining them was replaced) was removed — `flight_legacy.js`'s own `initLegacyPlotFlight()` already registers its own independent `playerLeave` cleanup for `plotFlyEligible`/`plotFlyPlayers`, so nothing needed to be added in its place.

**Fixed in the same pass: Plot Upgrades body text still unreadable after the 0.9.131 whitewash fix.** The 0.9.131 fix (previous section) correctly moved the border-tier progression text off the buttons and into the form body to dodge the button-label-overflow/tooltip bug — but the body text itself used `C.darkGray`, which per direct testing reads too dark/low-contrast against Minecraft's dialog background to be legible, not just dim.
