Changed to `C.gray` in `openPlotUpgradesMenu`'s `.body(...)` call — matching the convention already used everywhere else in this codebase for secondary/descriptive body text (e.g. every "Balance $X" line).

**Third flip: back to the real `/ability mayfly` system (BP 0.9.134) — the actual fix for the 0.9.131/0.9.132 stuck-flying-after-revoke bug turned out to be a game mode round trip, found by direct in-game testing rather than another guess at the velocity/teleport level.** Revoking mayfly (`ability @s mayfly false`) still doesn't stop an already-active flying session by itself — confirmed repeatedly, that's the entire reason `forceStopFlying` existed in the first place across both 0.9.131 and 0.9.132. But following the revoke with a switch to a *different* game mode and immediately back to whatever the player actually was in forces the client to fully resync its ability/movement state instead of coasting on its stale "still flying" prediction — no velocity clearing, no teleport nudge, no multi-attempt retry loop needed at all. `flight_ability.js`'s `bounceGameMode(player)` (new) captures `player.getGameMode()`, round-trips through Adventure (or Survival, if the player's real mode already is Adventure) for exactly one tick via two `gamemode` commands, then restores the original — `gameModeCommandName()` maps the `GameMode` enum's capitalized display strings (`"Survival"`, `"Adventure"`, ...) to the lowercase command-argument text `/gamemode` actually accepts, since those are two different string sets confirmed against Microsoft's own `GameMode` enum reference rather than assumed. `revokePlotFlightAbility` now calls `bounceGameMode` in place of the old `forceStopFlying`; the entire `forceStopFlying`/`cancelForceStopFlying`/`forceStopFlyingHandles` retry-loop machinery (0.9.132's clearVelocity-then-teleport approach) was deleted outright as dead code, along with `grantPlotFlightAbility`'s now-pointless `cancelForceStopFlying` call — there's no persistent multi-tick correction running anymore for a re-entry to need to cancel.

Stated plainly rather than overclaimed (matching how the 0.9.133 revert itself was reasoned about): this fixes the one concrete symptom this addon actually hit and documented twice — flight not stopping when revoked. It has **not** been specifically retested against the other three Mojang bug-tracker reports cited in `flight_ability.js`'s own header (MCPE-158180 mayfly self-dropping mid-flight for non-op, MCPE-158606 mayfly failing to grant at all for some non-op players, MCPE-150246 fall-damage/jump artifacts while mayfly is active) — those are different failure modes than "won't turn off," and this fix wasn't built to address them. If any of those three show up in play, that's a separate, still-open problem, not evidence against this fix.

Swapped which file is dormant, same pattern as every flip before it, nothing deleted: `main.js`'s PLOT FLIGHT section (~line 4023) now imports `hasPlotFlightPerkAbility`/`grantPlotFlightAbility`/`revokePlotFlightAbility`/`initAbilityPlotFlight` from `flight_ability.js` instead of `flight_legacy.js`'s legacy equivalents — same delegation-function names (`hasPlotFlightPerk`/`grantPlotFlight`/`revokePlotFlight`), so no call site outside the PLOT FLIGHT section itself needed to change. `onWorldLoad()` now calls `initAbilityPlotFlight()` instead of `initLegacyPlotFlight()`. `flight_legacy.js` is untouched and fully preserved, dormant again, for exactly one scenario: this fix doesn't hold up under wider testing after all — its own header has the exact steps to swap back.

**Addon-wide button-label-truncation audit and prevention (BP 0.9.135), prompted by the Plot Border button reproducing the exact same "invisible until hover, turns green" symptom the 0.9.131 fix was supposed to have already closed.** Root cause confirmed to still be button-label overflow: 0.9.131 removed the *third* tier and the "chunk-aligned"/"one-time" clarifications from the border/flight buttons, but the border button still embedded `"Plot Border  64×64 → 96×96"` (26 visible characters) on its own first line — still long enough to overflow and truncate into Bedrock's auto-generated, always-uncolored hover tooltip. There's no engine-level feedback for "this label truncated" to catch this programmatically at the point it happens, so the fix has two parts: closing this specific instance for good, and auditing every other button in the file for the same latent risk rather than waiting for each one to be reported individually.

- **`warnIfButtonLabelRisky(context, label)`** (`main.js`, right after `stripFormatCodes`) — measures the visible (§-code-stripped) length of the longest line in a button label and `Log.warn`s if it exceeds `BUTTON_LABEL_SAFE_CHARS` (22, chosen with margin below the 26/27-char lines that actually broke in production). This can't prevent truncation — Bedrock gives no API for the true per-button pixel width — but it turns "silently ships broken, discovered by a player report" into "flagged in the log the moment the risky label is ever constructed," and it's now called at every dynamic `form.button(...)` call site in the file (wrapped once inside each screen's local `addBtn` helper where one exists, called inline where it doesn't).
- **Plot Border button (`openPlotUpgradesMenu`)** — stripped ALL size/tier text from the button itself, down to just `"Plot Border"` + price, exactly matching the Flight button's pattern (which was never reported broken specifically because it never carried this kind of embedded detail). The border/current-tier/full-progression info moved entirely into the body text, which has no per-button width limit — and while there, the body was upgraded to also show the player's *current* tier (`"Current border: 96×96. Full progression: 64×64 → 96×96 → 128×128..."`), which the body-only text had room for all along but never used.
- **Plot Permissions roster button (`permSummary`, `openPlotPermissionsMenu`)** — used to list every overridden permission by name, color-coded, joined with `", "` (up to all 6 keys on one line — worst case ~52 visible characters, a severe latent overflow that just hadn't been reported yet). Collapsed to a fixed-width count (`"3/6 customized"`) — the full per-key breakdown is still one click away on `openPlayerPermissionsMenu`, so nothing was actually lost, just moved to where there's room for it.
- **`/market rates` per-item button (`openMarketRatesMenu`)** — dropped the trailing `"(NPC floor price)"` from every line; it was pure redundancy (the screen's own title/body already establish that framing) and was the difference between a safe line and a risky one.
- **Generator category button (`openGeneratorUpgradeCategorySelect`)** — dropped the trailing word "owned" from `"Pack & Looting  3/5 owned"` (25 visible chars) down to `"Pack & Looting  3/5"` (19) — the fraction reads fine without it given the screen's own "Pick a category" body text.
- Also wrapped, as a safety net rather than because a concrete overflow was found: the generator Hub's `addBtn` helper (Stats/Upgrades/Prestige buttons), the per-node upgrade-tree button in `openGeneratorUpgradeCategoryScreen`, and the item/quantity buttons in `openGeneratorItemList`/`openGeneratorQuantityMenu`.

Deliberately not pursued: migrating any of this off `ActionFormData`/DDUI to hand-authored resource-pack JSON UI. Raw JSON UI has no documented mechanism for a custom screen to report a button click back to a behavior-pack script at all (confirmed against the Bedrock Wiki's own JSON UI and Buttons/Toggles docs — buttons there are described as "primarily used in hardcoded instances... navigating to a screen or opening a dialog," nothing about firing a scriptevent or notifying script code) — DDUI's `CustomForm` *is* the officially-supported bridge for exactly this ("Data-Driven UI": Mojang generates the JSON UI live from data the script supplies). Rebuilding on raw JSON UI to gain arbitrary button-text color would mean losing that click-to-script pipeline and rebuilding it through unsupported tricks, to fix a problem the `ActionFormData` fallback (already proven, three times now) already solves for free.

**Level Shop (BP 0.9.136) — first piece of a broader "big-server generator economy" research pass** (Hypixel Skyblock Minions + spawner-plugin ecosystems like ATSpawners/AxSpawners were researched directly to inform this — tiered progression, cosmetic-only slots with zero power creep, timed consumable fuel, area-wide buffs, and deliberately holding real rewards back for late-game were the load-bearing patterns pulled from that research). Full planned order: Level Shop → extended Storage tier (Buffer V/VI) → Plot Crystal (plot-wide generator buff) → Auto-Sell routing (explicitly deprioritized, possibly cut — "spin it or not add it" was the direct instruction). Only Level Shop is built so far.

- **A genuinely third, separate currency.** Spends real vanilla Minecraft XP (`Player.getTotalXp()`/`Player.addExperience()`, both stable in `@minecraft/server` 2.8.0 — `addExperience` accepts a negative amount and returns the new total, which is both the spend mechanism and the post-spend balance read) — explicitly NOT this addon's own EXP (the PvP-kill-earned currency that unlocks generator upgrades, see `StatsModule`/`addExp`). Every player-facing string in the Level Shop says "Levels," never bare "EXP" — direct instruction, since the whole point was to keep the two economies from blurring together. Buying something here has a genuine opportunity cost against enchanting, since it's real XP, not a virtual counter skinned to look like one.
- **Unlock gate:** `LEVEL_SHOP_UNLOCK_LEVEL = 30` — vanilla's own long-standing "max useful enchanting level" milestone, chosen specifically because it's already familiar to every player rather than an arbitrary addon-specific number. Permanent once reached (`levelshop_unlocked_<id>` dynamic property flag) — without permanence, spending Levels back down below 30 immediately after unlocking would re-lock the store, which would be a confusing, punishing way for a reward to behave. `initLevelShop()`'s first `system.runInterval` (5s cadence) proactively watches for the crossing and sends a one-time unlock notice + `SOUND_SUCCESS` (`random.levelup` — already this file's existing level-up sound constant), rather than only surfacing the unlock the next time a player happens to run `/levelshop`.
- **Catalog, on direct instruction ("cosmetics + consumable boosts"):** four cosmetics (`LEVEL_SHOP_COSMETICS` — two nameplate recolors, two particle auras), each a one-time purchase then a free equip/unequip toggle afterward — zero power, mirroring Hypixel Minions' own cosmetic-skin-slot philosophy exactly. Particle ids (`minecraft:totem_particle`, `minecraft:mob_portal`) were checked against the Bedrock Wiki's own vanilla-particles list rather than guessed, and deliberately avoid every particle id already carrying a specific meaning elsewhere in this file (`basic_flame_particle`/`electric_spark_particle`/`endrod`/`villager_happy` already mean raid warnings, warp teleport, debug overlays, or plot-approach hints — reusing any of them here would make a cosmetic read as one of those instead). Four tonics (`LEVEL_SHOP_TONICS`) are instant one-shot consumables — no ownership, just an immediate balance check, `addExperience(-cost)`, and a timed `Entity.addEffect(effect, ticks, {amplifier})` call (Haste/Speed/Regeneration/Luck) — deliberately player-only effects, never anything touching generator rates or Market prices, since that would blur into the EXP-gated generator economy this currency is supposed to stay separate from.
- **Command surface:** new bare `/levelshop` (no arguments, always opens the menu), via a new `Epitaph-LevelShopCommand` satellite pack — same `namespace:namespace` alias-trick template as `Epitaph-BountyCommand`/`Epitaph-PayCommand`, relaying to `levelshop:open` in the main pack's `scriptEventReceive` switch. This pack does **not** yet have a `pack_icon.png` — it was created after the icon-sourcing pass covered the other 9 satellites, so it currently shows Bedrock's generic default; add one later if a matching icon is ever sourced.
- **Menu built on plain `ActionFormData`**, not DDUI, matching every other screen in this file that was previously burned by DDUI's zero-button-color-support bug — every dynamic label also runs through `warnIfButtonLabelRisky()` from the start, rather than needing a later audit pass to retrofit it the way several other screens did.

**Extended generator Storage tier — Buffer V/VI (BP 0.9.137), second piece of the big-server generator-economy plan (Level Shop → Storage → Plot Crystal → Auto-Sell, the last one still undecided).** `GEN_NODES.storage_5`/`storage_6` continue the existing `storage` line's `hasNode`-based prereq chain (`storage_5` requires `storage_4`, `storage_6` requires `storage_5` — the same sequential-prereq mechanism every other line already uses, no new gating concept introduced). Capacity keeps the exact 4x-per-tier progression tiers I–IV already established (16→64→256→1024) running straight through to 4096→16384, rather than tapering off — the whole point, per the Hypixel Skyblock research behind this plan, is that late storage tiers should read as a genuinely big jump, not a routine linear step. Cost repeats the 3x ratio storage_3→storage_4 already used (30,000 → 90,000 → 270,000), specifically so the EXP cost alone — not an invented cross-line requirement — is what keeps these naturally late-game, matching how Hypixel's own large minion storage is one of the last things worth crafting without needing an artificial gate to enforce it. `getBufferCap(id)` (`main.js`, right after `getBuffer`/`setBuffer`) gained two more `hasNode` checks ahead of the existing four; every other buffer-reading call site in the file (`collectBuffer`, `tickGenerator`'s several routing branches, the Hub/Stats/Upgrade-category screens' buffer display lines) already reads capacity exclusively through this one function, so none of them needed to change. `getPrereqChain`/`canPurchaseNode` are both fully generic over `GEN_NODES` with no hardcoded tier count, so the longer six-deep storage chain displays and purchases correctly with no changes needed there either.

**Plot Crystal (BP 0.9.138), third piece of the generator-economy plan (Level Shop → Storage → Crystal → Auto-Sell, last one still undecided).** A one-time, plot-wide purchase — third slot in `/market` → Plot Upgrades, alongside Border and Flight — giving a flat `PLOT_CRYSTAL_SPEED_BONUS_PCT` (10%) speed boost to **every** generator standing on that plot at once, rather than requiring each one upgraded individually. Directly mirrors Hypixel Skyblock's own area-wide "Crystal" buffs (Farm/Mithril/Woodcutting Crystal, +10% to every minion of a category) from the same research pass behind Level Shop and the extended Storage tier. Priced at `PLOT_CRYSTAL_COST` (20,000,000,000 — between Flight's 5B and Border's 50B/200B tiers, since its value scales with however many generators a player owns rather than being a flat single unlock like the other two). Storage is a single `plot_crystal_${plotId}` dynamic property, checked via `hasPlotCrystal(plotId)`/`hasPlotCrystalForGen(genId)` (the latter resolves a generator's owning plot through `gen_plot_${genId}`, already stamped onto every generator at placement time — a cheap direct lookup, not a scan). The bonus itself folds into `computeSpeedMultiplier(id)`'s existing additive base (alongside Speed I–IV) rather than a separate multiplicative layer, so a flat +10% means the same thing regardless of how many Speed nodes a given generator already owns — the same additive-stacking convention the PvP Zone's EXP bonus already established elsewhere in this file. Every existing consumer of `computeSpeedMultiplier` (the Hub's rate display, `computeInterval`) automatically picks up the bonus with no changes needed there — single source of truth. `purchasePlotCrystal` mirrors `purchasePlotFlight`'s exact structure (ownership guard → already-owned guard → balance check → deduct → set flag → confirm message).

**Sanity-check pass over all three new systems (BP 0.9.139) — three real bugs found and fixed, none of them theoretical.** Requested directly rather than assumed clean; all three would have shipped silently broken:

1. **`LEVEL_SHOP_TONICS.luck_tonic` used `effect: "luck"` — not a real Bedrock effect.** Confirmed against Microsoft's own `/effect` command reference (the authoritative Effect enum): Bedrock has no "luck" effect at all, unlike Java Edition. `player.addEffect("luck", ...)` would throw every time, silently swallowed by the tonic handler's own `try/catch` — a player would pay 80 Levels, see "Drank Luck Tonic!", and get nothing whatsoever. Replaced with a real, confirmed effect: `vision_tonic` (Night Vision), same cost tier, same "4th distinct-flavor utility tonic" role.
2. **Plot Crystal didn't actually speed up any already-placed generator.** `computeSpeedMultiplier`/`computeInterval` are only ever evaluated once, at `scheduleGenerator` time — the resulting interval gets baked directly into a `system.runInterval` call and is never re-checked live. Every existing speed-affecting node purchase already knows this and calls `rescheduleGenerator(genId)` immediately after (see the `["speed_1",...,"fuel_boost"]` check in the upgrade-purchase commit step) — `purchasePlotCrystal` was missing the equivalent step entirely. Fixed by scanning every generator (`gen_count`/`gen_plot_${genId}` — the same linear-scan idiom already used elsewhere for "every generator on plot X") and calling `rescheduleGenerator` on each one belonging to the purchasing plot, immediately on purchase.
3. **Level Shop's nameplate-color cosmetic silently fought an existing, independent system over `player.nameTag`.** The pre-existing STAFF TAG MODULE (`syncStaffNameTag`, further down the file) already writes `player.nameTag = STAFF_PREFIX + player.name` on a periodic sweep (`STAFF_SWEEP_INTERVAL_SEC`, every admin, every 10s) and on initial spawn — completely independently of the new Level Shop code, which also writes `player.nameTag` directly. An admin equipping a Level Shop nameplate color would have seen it silently reverted within 10 seconds by the staff sweep; gaining or losing admin would have dropped whichever half (staff prefix or color) was written most recently. Fixed with one shared composer, `rebuildPlayerNameTag(player)` (LEVEL SHOP section, right after `levelShopActiveAura`) that rebuilds the *entire* nameTag from every currently-active source (`isAdmin` → `STAFF_PREFIX`, plus the Level Shop color) every time either system needs to write it — `applyLevelShopNameColor` and `syncStaffNameTag` both delegate to it now, so neither can ever clobber the other's half again. `syncStaffNameTag` keeps its original "only write on an actual state change" gate (unchanged perf characteristic, still only touches `nameTag` when admin status flips), just delegates the write itself instead of hand-building the string.

Also fixed while checking, lower severity: two Level Shop menu button icons (`textures/ui/icon_recipe_nature`, `textures/ui/icon_potion`) were never confirmed against a real vanilla resource-pack path — unlike every other icon path already in this file (`icon_steve`, `magnifyingGlass`, `back_button_default`), all previously verified. Removed rather than left as an unconfirmed guess; an unset icon just means no icon renders, which is a safe fallback. Guide book page 6 (Plot Upgrades) also got a Crystal mention added, rewritten to fit back under the 256-byte cap (248 bytes, verified via `Buffer.byteLength`, not hand-counted) — it had no mention of the new upgrade at all until this pass.

---

**Plot-claim latency fix (BP 0.9.140), reported directly: first-join auto-claims "sometimes over 2 minutes."** Root-caused, not just patched: `claimPlot`'s strict/lenient terrain-candidate search was strictly sequential — one candidate at a time, each paying `forceLoadAndCheckTerrain`'s full force-load wait before the next even started. `MAX_TERRAIN_CANDIDATES` + `MAX_LENIENT_CANDIDATES` (60 + 20 = 80) sequential candidates × the old flat 30-tick (~1.5s) wait is a genuine 120s worst case — exactly matching the report. Two independent fixes, both preserving every existing reliability property (the 0.8.32/0.8.34 fixes' whole reason for existing):
- **`forceLoadAndCheckTerrain` now polls instead of blindly sleeping the full duration** — `TERRAIN_LOAD_POLL_TICKS` (5) apart, same idiom `waitForChunkLoad()` already used elsewhere, up to the exact same `TERRAIN_LOAD_POLL_MAX_TICKS` (30) ceiling the flat wait already used. This only changes *when* the check fires within that ceiling — a candidate near already-generated terrain (common once nearby cells have been claimed before) often resolves well under 30 ticks; a genuinely new/far-out chunk still gets the full budget the 0.8.34 fix found necessary.
- **Candidates are now checked in concurrent batches of `TERRAIN_CANDIDATE_BATCH_SIZE` (4)** via a new `tryTerrainBatch(budget, lenient, callback)`, replacing the old `tryNextCandidate`/`tryLenientCandidate` recursion. Each candidate is already its own independent, uniquely-named ticking area (the exact 0.8.34 fix for a different bug), so nothing about running several concurrently conflicts with another. Picks the first-suitable-by-spiral-index result within a resolved batch — not "whichever resolves first" — so the nearest-cell-in-spiral-order guarantee the old strictly-sequential version had is unchanged; only wall-clock timing improves (up to ~4x on the worst case), never which cell ends up chosen. `claimUnconditionally` (the zero-terrain-check last resort) is untouched — it never used `forceLoadAndCheckTerrain` at all, so there was nothing to batch there.

**Stats HUD leaderboard qualifying threshold (BP 0.9.140), reported directly: the board showed a full "Top Kills"/"Top Cash" leaderboard from the moment the world started, every online player still at 0 — a real eyesore, not a leaderboard yet.** `STATS_VIEWS` (`main.js`, ~line 8147) gained a `qualifies(r)` predicate per view, a deliberately separate concept from `key` (the player's own on/off toggle for that view, set via `/hud config` → Leaderboards, storage in `hud_prefs_*`) — `qualifies` controls whether the view has anything worth showing *right now*, `key` controls whether the player wants to see it *at all*, and the fix never touches the latter in either direction (explicit instruction: never overwrite or ignore a player's own HUD configuration). Thresholds mirror the two examples given directly (`kills >= 1`, `money >= 1000n` — note the BigInt literal, since `getMoneyBig` returns BigInt and comparing against a bare `1000` would throw); EXP and K/D extend the same spirit (`exp >= 1`; K/D gated on `kills >= 1` too, since a 0/0 K/D board is equally meaningless before anyone's fought). `computeLeaderboardViews` now returns `null` for a view when `!roster.some(view.qualifies)` — which naturally subsumes the old "(nobody online)" placeholder too, since `.some` on an empty roster is always false — and the render loop's existing `if (prefs[STATS_VIEWS[i].key])` gained a second, independent `&& cachedLeaderboardViews[i]` check rather than being restructured, so the two conditions (player wants it / it currently has something to show) stay visibly separate and neither can be mistaken for controlling the other.

**Plot pre-scan — background terrain pre-vetting (BP 0.9.141), following up on the 0.9.140 claim-latency fix with a request to help even later players (e.g. "the 1000th player's plot") as the spiral pushes farther from spawn over time.** An earlier proposal (an invisible, unremovable, respawning marker entity suspended above each plot, meant to "help scan more chunks") was floated first and explicitly turned down after analysis, not built: an entity's own chunk has to already be loading for the entity to exist at all, so it can't drive chunk loading, only ride along with it — it wouldn't have pre-warmed anything. Using it to store a plot's unique ID would also have been strictly worse than what already exists (dynamic properties, readable regardless of chunk-load state, zero tick cost) for the exact same purpose. And at "1000th plot" scale, one persistent entity per plot plus a respawn/anti-grief validation loop per entity is a real, compounding tick cost working against the goal, not toward it.

What shipped instead reuses the exact terrain-check code already in place, just runs it proactively:
- **`advanceCursorAt(cursorKey)`** — `advanceSpiralCursor()`'s distance/exclusion/already-claimed filtering logic, extracted into a shared helper parameterized by which dynamic-property key holds the cursor position. `advanceSpiralCursor()` itself is now a thin wrapper (unchanged external behavior, keeps the 80%-band-exhaustion-warning logic that's specific to the live cursor) around `advanceCursorAt("plot_spiral_cursor")`; a new `advancePrescanCursor()` calls the same helper with `"plot_prescan_cursor"` — a fully independent cursor position that never touches (or is touched by) the one real claims use.
- **`initPlotPrescan()`** runs a slow background loop (`PLOT_PRESCAN_INTERVAL_TICKS`, one candidate every 5s — deliberately paced, since this has no deadline and shouldn't compete with anything time-sensitive) that advances the pre-scan cursor and vets whatever cell it lands on via `prescanCell()` — the same force-load-then-check pattern `forceLoadAndCheckTerrain` already uses, checking both strict and lenient suitability off a single force-load and caching the result as `plot_prescan_verdict_${col}_${row}`: `"S"` (passes strict), `"L"` (fails strict, passes lenient), or `"R"` (fails even lenient). Self-limiting via `PLOT_PRESCAN_BUFFER` (200) — the loop goes idle once that many cells beyond the live cursor are already cached, and resumes automatically as real claims consume the buffer; if a burst of claims ever outruns the trickle and the live cursor overtakes the pre-scan cursor, the pre-scan cursor is simply snapped forward to match (nothing to retroactively catch up on — those cells just get vetted live, exactly like before this feature existed).
- **`claimPlot`'s `tryTerrainBatch`** checks `plot_prescan_verdict_${col}_${row}` before calling `forceLoadAndCheckTerrain` for each candidate in a batch — a cached verdict resolves synchronously with zero wait at all (an "S" satisfies either pass, "L" only satisfies lenient, "R" fails both); no cache entry falls through to the exact same live check as before. If an entire batch happens to already be cached, the whole batch (and any recursive continuation) can resolve within the same tick — the more of the pre-scan buffer a claim lands inside, the closer to instant it gets. This changes nothing about *which* cell ends up chosen (same nearest-suitable-in-spiral-order guarantee as always) — only how fast the answer arrives.
- **`claimCell`** now clears a cell's `plot_prescan_verdict_${col}_${row}` the moment it's actually claimed — the verdict has no further use once a cell is taken (never looked up again, since `advanceCursorAt` already filters out claimed cells before returning them as candidates), so this is just hygiene against unbounded dynamic-property growth over a long-lived server. `freeCell` (plot deletion) deliberately does NOT re-vet or clear anything — the cached terrain verdict is still accurate if that cell is ever reclaimed (terrain doesn't change), so leaving it in place means a freed cell's *next* claim gets the instant-cache benefit too.

**Lag audit, round 1 (BP 0.9.142), reported directly: a beta tester felt real, literal lag — delay, teleport stutter, general slowness — testing on a LAN world.** Initially (and **incorrectly**) attributed to the Plot Pre-Scan system shipped one turn earlier (0.9.141) — retuned it much more conservatively regardless (`PLOT_PRESCAN_BUFFER` 200 → 30, `PLOT_PRESCAN_INTERVAL_TICKS` 100 → 200, new `PLOT_PRESCAN_STARTUP_DELAY_TICKS` 6000 delaying the loop's start by 5 minutes), which is still a legitimate improvement on its own terms, but **the user corrected directly afterward: the reported lag symptoms were observed *before* pre-scan was ever implemented**, so it could not have been the cause. Recorded here so the mistake isn't silently lost — the retune stands (real reasons of its own, see the PLOT PRE-SCAN section above), but it did not fix the actual reported problem. Also fixed in this same pass, and unaffected by the misattribution: the persistent plot-border visualization loop (`/plot border`, `main.js` ~line 7279) was, for every toggled-on player, spreading the *entire* online player list into a fresh array and linearly searching it every 4 ticks — flipped to iterate `world.getPlayers()` once and check `persistentBorderPlayers.has(p.id)` (O(1)) instead, identical output, no repeated allocation.

**Lag audit, round 2 (BP 0.9.143) — the actual pre-existing cause, confirmed by the user to have been present (and directly involved: at least one spawner was active) before pre-scan existed.** `findMobSpawnSpots` (the mob-generator/spawner spawn-position flood-fill, added by the 0.9.116 containment-box fix — well before this session, matching the corrected timeline) had two real, compounding costs:
1. **No early exit.** An unboxed generator (open air on every side — the default state right after buying one, before a containment box gets built) always flood-filled its *entire* radius — up to 81 cells × 2 `getBlock` calls each — every single production cycle, regardless of how many valid spots it had already found. Fixed with `MOB_SPAWN_SPOT_CAP` (24) — the fill now stops the moment it has enough candidates to pick from; random selection among 24 spots looks identical to random selection among 81.
2. **Recomputed from scratch every cycle.** A fully-upgraded fast spawner can produce as often as every ~4s, and each mob generator runs its own independent scheduled loop — so this flood-fill could fire that often, per active spawner, continuously. `getMobSpawnSpotsCached(dim, id, x, y, z, maxRadius)` now wraps `findMobSpawnSpots` with a per-generator cache (`mobSpawnSpotCache`) reused for `MOB_SPAWN_CACHE_TICKS` (400 — 20s) before recomputing. One accepted tradeoff: a player who just finished building a containment box sees it take effect within that same ~20s window, not instantly — the cache explicitly refuses to reuse an empty result forever (a briefly-fully-enclosed generator that gets a wall removed recovers on the very next cycle, not stuck for the full TTL). `clearGenCaches(id)` (called from `removeGenerator`) also clears this cache now, alongside the other per-generator in-memory caches it already cleared, so a removed generator's stale spot list doesn't linger.
- **Checked and ruled out as contributors, same pass**: `enforceWorldBorderVelocity` (every tick, but a genuinely cheap early return for the overwhelming common case of "well inside the border"); `enforceWorldBorderVisuals` (only does any real work — a particle wall — when a player is actually near the world border, not by default); `enforceBoundary`'s `getPlotAtBlock` (O(1) chunk-keyed cache lookup, doesn't degrade with plot count); the Stats HUD board refresh (`buildRosterData()` computed once per refresh cycle and shared, not recomputed per player); the spawner debug radius box and Level Shop aura particle loops (real particle volume while active, but both narrow, self-limited opt-ins, not always-on).
- **Noted, not yet changed**: the 0.9.140 batched-claim system (4 concurrent 9×9-chunk force-loads) is heavier per join than the old sequential version at the instant it happens, but it's a one-time cost exactly when a new player claims, not a sustained drain — lower priority, revisit only if it's ever reported as its own felt hitch specifically at join time.

**Upgrade-effect audit (BP 0.9.143 verification pass) — confirmed every generator/spawner upgrade actually changes tick behavior, not just the purchase UI.** Requested directly ("we need to ensure upgrades ACTUALLY do affect all Generators and Spawners, not just UI changes"). Yield/Quality/Storage/Alerts/Pack Size/Looting/Auto-Collect are all read live via `hasNode(id, ...)`/`getBufferCap(id)` on every production tick — no caching staleness is possible since `addNode()` writes through to both the dynamic property and the in-memory `genNodeCache` synchronously in the same call. Speed I-IV, Fuel Boost, and Plot Crystal are the one real exception — `computeInterval` is baked once into `system.runInterval` at schedule time and never re-read live — but both purchase paths (`handleNodePurchase` for the four speed nodes + fuel boost, `purchasePlotCrystal`'s per-plot generator scan) already call `rescheduleGenerator` on commit, verified by reading the live code rather than trusting prior notes. No bug found; no changes made.

**Generator Leveling re-enabled (BP 0.9.144)** — on direct instruction to build something real rather than "a bunch of small, almost-useful features," and with server stability treated as the first constraint. `GEN_LEVELS`/`getLevelData`/`checkLevelUp` had been fully built (a 10-tier XP curve topping out at +18% passive yield at level 10) but every `awardXP(...)` call site sat commented out `// disabled — future update`, making the whole system permanently unreachable dead code — no player could ever level a generator or see the bonus apply. Re-enabling this properly required solving the actual reason it was probably left off: a naive `awardXP` would call `setDynamicProperty` on every single production tick, and a fully-upgraded generator can produce every ~4-5 ticks, with every active generator running its own independent scheduled loop — precisely the same "cheap per-call, expensive at scale" shape the 0.9.143 mob-spawn-cache fix had just diagnosed and fixed one turn earlier.
- **Batched, not live, persistence.** `awardXP` now only touches an in-memory `genXpCache`/`genXpDirty` pair (the same established per-tick-hot-read caching pattern as `genNodeCache`/`genPosCache`) — the actual `setDynamicProperty` write and level-up check happen in one periodic sweep, `flushGenXp()`, wired into `ShopModule.onWorldLoad` at `GEN_XP_FLUSH_INTERVAL_TICKS` (200 ticks / 10s) and iterating only the dirty set, never a scan of every generator that exists. Worst case, ~10 seconds of XP is lost on an ungraceful crash — an accepted tradeoff for a cosmetic-flavor passive bonus, unlike money or plot state, which are never handled this way. `getLevelData`/the Hub and Stats screens' own XP display both read through `getGenXp(id)` (the in-memory value) rather than the raw dynamic property, so the UI never shows stale XP just because a flush hasn't happened yet — only the persisted-to-disk copy lags, invisibly.
- **Extended to mob generators/spawners, which the original disabled stub never actually covered at all** — every pre-existing `awardXP` call site lived inside `tickGenerator`'s block-generator-only code paths, past the point where mob generators already branch off and return. The level *yield bonus roll* itself was already computed before that branch (shared with the yield/prestige rolls) so uncommenting it automatically benefits both generator types for free — a spawner's `baseSpawn` passed into `tickMobGenerator` already includes the chance-based level bonus. XP awarding for spawners needed a new, explicit call (`awardXP(id, yieldCount)` right after the `tickMobGenerator` call, before the branch's `return`) since nothing there awarded XP before.
- **Fixed an inconsistency in the original stub while re-enabling it, not just uncommented it as-is** — the buffer-store code path's disabled line was `awardXP(id, 1)`, a flat amount regardless of how much (if anything) actually got stored, and it sat inside the `hasBuffer(id)` guard so a generator with no Storage purchased got zero XP for a blocked cycle even though nothing was produced either way (a wasted cycle correctly earning nothing). Moved the call inside the `toStore > 0` check and changed the amount to `toStore` (the real quantity credited to the buffer) so XP is now proportional to genuine production in every code path (buffer-store, auto-collect, normal placement, and the new mob-generator path) — no path over- or under-credits relative to what the generator actually did that cycle.

**Beta-test bug-fix pass (BP 0.9.146), from a real 3+ player LAN test — a direct error log plus a list of felt-in-play symptoms, cross-referenced against each other rather than treated as separate reports.** Nine real, confirmed bugs found and fixed, several of them severe:

1. **`sleep(ms)` used browser/Node's `setTimeout`, which doesn't exist anywhere in Bedrock's scripting runtime** — every call threw `ReferenceError: 'setTimeout' is not defined` as an unhandled promise rejection, confirmed directly in the error log (repeated dozens of times). `sleep()` is the retry-delay used by `showFormWithRetry`/`showMessageBoxWithRetry`/`showActionFormWithRetry` — the three functions nearly every menu in the addon routes through — specifically on the "player already has a form open" busy-retry path, a routine occurrence with 3+ players active. When that path threw, the calling menu function's own `await` never resolved, silently aborting whatever it was doing with zero further code running — this is the most likely explanation for the separately-reported "you search someone's name [in Plot Permissions] and it exits." Fixed by switching to `system.runTimeout` (the exact tick-based pattern `sleepTicks()` already used correctly elsewhere in the file), converting the existing millisecond value to ticks.
2. **`GameMode.Creative` (capital C) doesn't exist on the real enum — it's `GameMode.creative` (lowercase)** — confirmed by cross-referencing `flight_ability.js`/`flight_legacy.js`, both of which already use the correct lowercase form and explicitly document having verified it against Microsoft's own reference. Every comparison against the capitalized (wrong) form silently evaluated to `undefined`, meaning the check could never match. This hit 5 separate call sites in `main.js`: the Kit Disparity gear-gap Creative-mode exemption (`DisparityModule`'s `entityHurt` handler — directly matches "Kit disparity seems to be failing"), the Creative-mode kill-EXP exemption (meaning non-admin Creative-mode players could earn PvP EXP, a real farming hole), and both `sellAllInventory`/`openMarketSellChest`'s "can't sell in Creative" guards (meaning Creative players could actually sell items, an unintended economy bypass). All 5 fixed together — same repeated typo, not five independent bugs.
3. **Block-tier generators (Netherite Block and 6 others) had no entry in `BASE_INTERVALS` at all** — only the 5 "ore" category items did; every "block" category item (Amethyst, Quartz, Lapis, Gold, Emerald, Diamond, Netherite Block) silently fell back to `computeInterval`'s emergency default of a bare 20 ticks (1 second), confirmed by direct report ("Netherite Blocks are at 1/s") and matching "all generators have the wrong rates." These 7 values were never actually defined anywhere in the code before — not a restored original, a new addition, chosen to roughly track the existing ore progression's own price-to-interval scaling (see the constant's own comment for the exact numbers and reasoning) — worth a manual balance pass since nobody ever actually tuned these before.
4. **The wild-spawner pickup menu's mob-type dropdown threw on every single attempt** — `ModalFormData.dropdown()`'s third argument used to be a bare default-index number in an older SDK; this version requires a `ModalFormDataDropdownOptions` object instead, confirmed directly from the live error text ("argument [2] expected type: ModalFormDataDropdownOptions | undefined"). Directly matches "Terrain Generated/Wild spawners can not be picked up" — the picker form never even reached the player. Fixed by wrapping the index as `{ defaultValueIndex: 0 }`.
5. **Illegally placed blocks were undone with `/setblock ... air destroy`** — `destroy` mode makes the command behave like a real survival break, dropping the removed block as an actual item entity. Since this handler exists specifically to reverse a placement a player already paid an inventory item for, the "destroy" mode meant the block vanished from the world AND a duplicate copy dropped on the ground — a genuine, confirmed item-duplication exploit, matching "blocks placed on other's islands break and duplicate" exactly. Fixed at all 3 call sites (portal zone, spawn zone, general plot-permission denial) by switching to plain `air` (silent removal, no drop) — nothing should ever drop from undoing a placement that was never legitimate in the first place.
6. **A generator/spawner destroyed by non-standard means (commands, WorldEdit, an admin creative-mode edit) ran forever with no way to stop it** — the only removal path wired up anywhere was the in-Hub "Remove Generator?" confirm flow; anything that changed the block without going through that UI left `gen_active_` true and its scheduled interval running indefinitely, exactly as reported. Fixed with a self-healing check at the top of every `tickGenerator` cycle: if the chunk is loaded and the generator's own physical block no longer matches what was placed there, a new `cleanupOrphanedGenerator()` cancels the schedule and clears its dynamic properties automatically — never fires on a merely-unloaded chunk, which must keep crediting the buffer for legitimate offline production exactly as before.
7. **Plot terrain suitability only ever sampled 5 fixed points (center + 4 corners) across a footprint up to 4,096 blocks** — far too sparse to reliably catch a tree or hillside sitting anywhere else in that area, confirmed as the direct cause of "plots can generate within terrain like trees and sometimes partially within mountains." Widened `isTerrainSuitable` to a proper 3x3 grid (adds the 4 edge midpoints) — meaningfully denser at negligible extra cost, since the actual expensive part of a terrain check is the chunk force-load, not a handful of extra block/biome reads. Stated plainly: this substantially reduces both risks but doesn't mathematically guarantee zero — a lone small tree can still in principle sit between grid points on a very large footprint; a true guarantee would mean scanning every column in the footprint, a much heavier check not implemented here.
8. **Reported entity-death crash** (`InvalidEntityError: Failed to call function 'hasTag'`, dozens of repeats in the log) — the mob-generator Looting-bonus handler on `world.afterEvents.entityDie` read tags off `ev.deadEntity` with no guard at all; that event fires for every death in the world (not just generator mobs), and by the time an after-event runs the entity is already dead/removing, so any property access on it can throw at any point, not just one line. This silently broke the Looting bonus-drop roll on every single kill server-wide, not just whichever death happened to trigger it. Wrapped the whole handler body in try/catch rather than just the specific `hasTag` calls, since the location/dimension reads a few lines down are exactly as vulnerable.
9. **`/epitaph struct_place` with no argument sent an empty string straight into the native structure-load command** — surfaced as a confusing raw engine error ("Structure '' must be prefixed by a namespace") instead of ever explaining what went wrong; low severity (admin-only debug tool, `/epitaph struct` already exists to list valid IDs) but a real, confirmed one-off in the log. Added an explicit empty-check with a proper usage message.

**"Bazaar" renamed to "Player Shops" (still BP 0.9.146), following up directly on the "tips say Bazaar... will never be a feature intended to be added" note above.** Confirmed via a direct question rather than guessed: the underlying player-shop system (`/shop setup`/`/shop browse`, everything the old "Player Bazaar" name referred to) is real, intentional, and stays exactly as-is — the objection was specifically to the Hypixel-flavored *name*, not the feature. Renamed every occurrence of "Bazaar"/"Player Bazaar" to "Player Shops" (or contextually "shop"/"player shop" for singular references): guide book page 11 (rewritten, re-verified under the 256-byte cap — 223 bytes), both tip-rotation arrays, the `/shop browse` screen title and its new Guide explanation, the NPC Market screen's own Guide text and rates-screen nudge, the buy-confirmation and offline-payout chat messages, and the Stats HUD admin panel's system-summary line. Nothing about the feature's mechanics, pricing, or tax changed — this was a naming pass only.

**Flagged, not fixed — genuinely needs your call, not mine:**
- **"We need a working list of players, and also the working search bar."** The current Plot Permissions screen doesn't have a live-filtering search field at all (that was deliberately replaced at 0.9.123 with a plain type-a-name modal, to fix a DDUI button-color bug) — this reads as a feature request for a browsable, filterable player list back, not a bug in existing code. Not built without confirming that's actually wanted.

**Resolved by direct confirmation, no code change needed:** "When I debug plot claims, they never actually claim and they stay unowned/vacant" — confirmed this is `/plot:debuggen`'s actual designed behavior (synthetic `DEBUG_<id>` test plots deliberately not tied to a real player UUID), not a bug. Dropped from the open list.

**Plot Permissions overhauled (BP 0.9.154)** — the last remaining open item, on direct instruction to overhaul permissions, player list, player search, and per-player permissions together rather than bolt on a minimal search fix.
- **`getAllKnownPlayers()`** (new, right after `lookupUUID`) — reconstructs the full list of every player who has ever joined by scanning `World.getDynamicPropertyIds()` (a real, stable, foundational method — as old as `getDynamicProperty`/`setDynamicProperty` themselves) for the `plot_name_by_uuid_` prefix every join already writes. No new registry to maintain; only ever called from an explicit player action (search, browsing a list), never a hot path, so scanning the full key set once per call is an acceptable bounded cost.
- **`/plot permissions` gained a genuine "Online Players" entry** (`openPlotOnlinePlayers`) — a directly browsable, zero-typing list of everyone currently online (except the owner), each row showing their live permission summary, tapping straight into their editor. This is the actual "player list" that was missing — the old roster only ever showed players who already had overrides set.
- **Search overhauled from strict-exact-name to real search** (`openPlotPlayerSearch`) — partial, case-insensitive substring matching now, against a union of online players and `getAllKnownPlayers()` (covers offline names too, de-duplicated by uuid), not just an exact spelling. A unique match still jumps straight to the editor (the old fast path, preserved); multiple matches now show a genuine **`openPlotSearchResults`** picker screen (capped at 20 shown, with an explicit "narrow your search" note beyond that) instead of just failing — "Search Again" carries the previous query back in as a prefill so refining doesn't mean retyping from scratch.
- **Prefill implementation note:** the prefill needed a text field with a working `defaultValue` — raw `ModalFormData.textField()`'s third-argument shape was never actually proven anywhere else in this file (every other raw-ModalFormData text field only ever passes label+placeholder), and this file has been burned before by exactly this class of guessed form-option shape (`ModalFormData.dropdown`'s own 3rd-arg change, fixed at 0.9.146). Used `showModal` instead — this codebase's own DDUI-based field-array wrapper, whose `defaultValue` handling is directly confirmed in its own header comment ("confirmed against TextFieldOptions/SliderOptions/ToggleOptions/DropdownOptions directly") and already proven working for the *exact* same "previous search query" prefill pattern in `openShopSearch`.
- **Per-player permissions screen (`openPlayerPermissionsMenu`) now shows the real per-key breakdown**, not just a "3/6 customized" count — a new **`permDetail(id, uuid)`** helper (green ✓ / red ✘ per permission, "(basic)" tag for anything not personally overridden) replaces `permSummary` in this screen's body text specifically. The collapsed count form was only ever necessary to dodge button-label truncation (`permSummary`'s own header comment); a screen's `.body()` text has no such width limit, so there was no real reason the fuller detail wasn't already there. `permSummary` itself is untouched and still used everywhere a permission summary needs to fit on a *button* (roster, Online Players, Search Results) — the truncation risk that motivated collapsing it in the first place still applies there.

**Permission Groups added (BP 0.9.155)**, on direct instruction, modeled on how this is actually done elsewhere rather than invented from scratch: WorldGuard's per-region groups (named group + member list + its own flag set, resolved player-override > group > region default) and the player > group > default layering every serious Minecraft permission plugin (LuckPerms, PermissionsEx) uses. Deliberately **not** LuckPerms' own multi-group-with-weight model — a single plot managed casually by one owner doesn't need simultaneous group memberships with priority resolution, so a player belongs to **at most one group at a time**, the same simpler model smaller/older Bukkit permission plugins used. This was chosen specifically to keep the mental model simple for a non-technical plot owner ("Steve is in my Builders group"), not because the richer model is wrong in general.
- **Resolution order, end to end: personal override > group's own toggle > Basic.** `getPlayerPlotPerm` (the single function every permission check in the file already routes through — `canBuild`/`canBreak`/`canEnter`/etc.) gained exactly one new tier in the middle; every existing call site picked this up automatically with no changes needed anywhere else.
- **Storage**: `plot_group_names_<id>` is the *only* JSON-array property in this whole system — it's the one place an ordered, enumerable list is actually needed (the other per-plot/per-player permission data is all flat, one-property-per-fact, matching the existing convention). A group's own 6 toggles (`plot_group_perm_<key>_<id>_<name>`) and player→group membership (`plot_player_group_<id>_<uuid>`) both follow that same flat style. Membership has no separate per-group member-list array to keep in sync with the reverse lookup — `getGroupMembers` derives it live by scanning `plot_player_group_<id>_` keys via `World.getDynamicPropertyIds()` (the same technique `getAllKnownPlayers` already established), so there is exactly one place membership can ever be recorded, never two sources that could drift apart.
- **UI**: a new "Groups" entry on the main Plot Permissions screen opens `openPlotGroupsMenu` (list existing groups + Create Group + a Guide button of its own, since this is a genuinely distinct concept from basic per-player editing) → `openPlotGroupDetail` (permission breakdown, Edit / Manage Members / Delete, all confirm-gated where destructive) → `openPlotGroupMembers` (member list with tap-to-remove, Add Member). Adding a member reuses the exact same partial/case-insensitive search this session already built for Permissions' own player search (`searchKnownPlayers`, extracted out of `openPlotPlayerSearch` specifically so this didn't need a second copy of the online+ever-joined union/filter logic) — routed to "add to this group" instead of "edit this player," with an identical single-match-fast-path / multi-match-results-screen split. `openPlayerPermissionsMenu` (a specific player's own screen) gained a "Group" button and a corrected header line — it used to always say "using Basic permissions" whenever there was no personal override, which became actively wrong the moment a player could be in a group instead; now distinguishes personal override / group / plain Basic explicitly.
- **Editor de-duplication**: Basic, per-player, and per-group permission editing all need the identical 6 toggles with identical labels — adding groups would have made this the third near-identical hand-copied screen, so a shared `editPlotPermToggles(player, title, bodyLabel, getter, setter)` was factored out and all three screens now call it. It matches `openBasicPermissionsMenu`/`openPlayerPermissionsEditor`'s own already-proven raw `ModalFormData.toggle(label, {defaultValue})` shape exactly — parametrizing an existing, working pattern rather than introducing a new one.
- **Caught one guessed icon path before it shipped**: `"textures/ui/color_plus"` for the Create Group button was never confirmed anywhere else in this file (unlike `icon_steve`/`icon_multiplayer`/`magnifyingGlass`, all already proven). Removed rather than risk it — an unset icon just means no icon renders, the same call made for `icon_recipe_nature`/`icon_potion` earlier in this file's history.
- Guide book page 5 (Plot Permissions) updated to mention Groups, re-verified under the byte cap (215 bytes).

**Groups threaded through the surrounding systems it touches (BP 0.9.156)**, on "do as you wish, continue" — a deliberate audit pass over everything adjacent to Groups rather than treating the feature as done the moment its own screens worked, since a new permission tier that only the Permissions menu itself knows about is exactly the kind of thing that quietly goes stale elsewhere.
- **Verified (no change needed): a non-owner with "Manage Permissions" granted via a group, not just a personal override, can already reach and fully use the entire Groups UI.** `/plot permissions`'s own access gate (`canManagePerms`) and every one of the new Groups functions all route through `id` (the target plot) rather than ever assuming the acting player owns it — confirmed by re-reading the actual command dispatch, which already resolves `id` to "the plot you're standing on" for a non-owner, exactly as it did before Groups existed.
- **Fixed: `/plot info` never mentioned Groups at all** — the per-plot summary showed "Custom permissions" (personal overrides) and Basic, with no indication groups existed or who was in them. Added a `Groups: name (N), name (N)` line.
- **Fixed a real, previously-invisible gap: `/plot list` (which shows every OTHER plot the calling player has special access to) only ever checked the roster** — personal overrides only. A player who's *only* a group member on a friend's plot (no personal override) was invisible to this command entirely, and would see "no special permissions on any other plots" while actually having real access somewhere via their group. Now checks group membership too, and tags the line with which group when that's the reason (`[Builders]`).
- `PLOT_HELP` and guide book page 5 both already mentioned groups from the original 0.9.155 pass — confirmed page 4 ("Plot Access," the entry-level overview) correctly defers detail to page 5 rather than needing its own mention.

**In-menu "Guide" buttons added across every player-facing top-level menu (BP 0.9.145)**, on direct instruction: one Guide button per *parent* menu (not its sub-screens, unless a sub-screen represents a genuinely distinct feature in its own right), explaining what that feature is and how it works — deliberately player-facing only, no version numbers, dynamic-property names, or internal mechanics. A shared `openFeatureGuide(player, title, bodyText, onBack)` helper (right after `showConfirm`) renders the explanation as a plain `ActionFormData` screen with a single Back button that re-opens whichever menu the player came from — built on `ActionFormData` rather than `showActionMenu`'s DDUI `CustomForm` specifically so the explanation text keeps its § color regardless of which style the calling menu itself uses (DDUI's zero-button-color-support limitation is documented extensively elsewhere in this file; explanatory body text is exactly the kind of content that shouldn't render as flat white). Wired into: NPC Market (`openGeneratorCategoryMenu`), Plot Upgrades (`openPlotUpgradesMenu` — treated as a distinct feature from the Market despite being reached through it, since Border/Flight/Crystal are plot economy, not generator shopping), Player Shops (`openShopBrowseMenu`), Mailbox (`openMailboxMenu` — distinct feature from browsing), the Generator Hub (`openGeneratorHubMenu`), Prestige (`openPrestigeMenu` — distinct trade-in mechanic), Level Shop (`openLevelShopMenu`) plus its two sub-screens Cosmetics and Tonics (`openLevelShopCosmetics`/`openLevelShopTonics` — kept separate since one is a permanent one-time-buy-then-free-toggle and the other is a pay-again-each-time consumable, different enough mechanics to need their own explanation rather than sharing the parent's), Plot Permissions (`openPlotPermissionsMenu`), and HUD Config (`openHudConfigMenu`). Deliberately skipped: every admin-only control-panel screen (not player-facing at all); Kit/Bounty commands (chat-text output, no actual menu to attach a button to); `/market rates` and the Generator Stats screen (plain data displays, self-explanatory, not a "feature" with mechanics to explain); `openMyShopsMenu` (an edge-case fallback shown mid-flow when a seller hits their shop cap, not a menu a player deliberately navigates to as its own destination); the wild-spawner pickup form (a single-button confirm whose body text already says everything needed).

**Portal protection vs. a player's own plot (BP 0.9.147)**, reported directly: a player denied block placement on their own claimed, authorized plot with the message "The End Portal zone is protected," even though — per the report — the nearest known portal was 3,200 blocks away. Not a coordinate bug: `inPortalZone`'s own header is explicit that it protects **every** End Portal frame anywhere in the world, not just one known "spawn" portal, and its cache auto-registers any frame block any player ever so much as breaks/places/interacts near (`inPortalZone`'s own side effect at the direct-block-type check). Since this realm migrated off a void world to real generated terrain, a genuine vanilla stronghold — with its own real portal frames — can end up sitting under or near a claimed plot with nobody aware of it, since islands float at `PLOT_Y` and a stronghold is nowhere near visible from up there. The protection was working exactly as designed; the design just never accounted for a claimed plot taking precedence over an incidental natural feature underneath it. Fixed at all 4 portal-check call sites (before/after `playerBreakBlock`, before/after `playerPlaceBlock`) by adding `!verifyRaidablePermission(...)`/`!verifyPermission(...)` — the same functions the normal plot-permission check just below each one already calls — to the portal-block condition: a player already authorized to build/break at that exact location (owner, granted permission, or a legitimate raid-night bypass) now skips portal protection there entirely, while anywhere they're *not* already authorized (unclaimed land, someone else's plot) the full portal protection still applies unchanged. Also confirmed the accompanying duplication report ("the block destroys and duplicates") should already be resolved by 0.9.146's `air destroy` → `air` fix in the exact same handler — flagged that this report may predate reimporting that build, since no separate duplication path was found in the portal-specific code.

**Portal diagnostic added (BP 0.9.148), following a direct follow-up: "I looked around for any portal near the Plot, and there wasn't any I could find."** That pushback was worth taking seriously rather than assuming a hidden/underground stronghold explained it away — `ISLA2.mcstructure` (the actual island file this pack loads, confirmed via `ISLAND_STRUCTURE_NAME`) was checked directly with `nbtlib` against its real block palette, and it contains zero portal-related blocks of any kind — so a stray frame baked into the island template, the other obvious explanation, is ruled out. Since `_portalCache`'s only two write paths (`scanPortalFrames`'s admin-run scan, and `inPortalZone`'s own auto-register-on-direct-hit) both require a real `minecraft:end_portal_frame`/`minecraft:end_portal` block check to succeed before anything gets added, there's no mechanical bug found that would let a non-portal location get cached — but that doesn't rule out a genuinely real (if easy to miss — underground, no visible marker from a floating island) stronghold, or a stale cache entry from earlier in this world's life that's no longer easy to explain after the fact. Rather than keep guessing, added `/epitaph portal_nearest` — stand anywhere (e.g. on the affected plot) and it reports the single closest cached frame coordinate and its real distance, so the actual data can be inspected directly instead of theorized about. The 0.9.147 fix already makes this a non-issue for the player's own plot regardless of what `portal_nearest` turns up — this is purely for tracking down whether the cache itself needs a manual correction (e.g. if it turns up something that's clearly not a real portal, or one from a completely unrelated, long-abandoned scan).

**World border warning zone was wildly oversized (BP 0.9.149)**, reported directly: "at a certain distance to the world border, it permanently spams the on-screen notification indefinitely." Not a stuck/stale display — `WORLD_BORDER_WARN_DIST` was 500, completely out of scale with every other number in the same system (`ISLAND_BORDER_MARGIN` is only 100, and its own comment says even that only needs to clear a 32-block island half-size plus a 15-block push zone — tens of blocks, not hundreds). With `MAX_RADIUS` at 1,350 and the real border at ±1,500, anything past 1,000 blocks out — **over half of the entire legal claiming band** (650–1,350) — sat permanently inside that 500-block warning zone, refreshing every `WORLD_BORDER_CHECK_INTERVAL` (4 ticks) the whole time. Any player who simply lived on a legitimately far-out, perfectly safe plot saw this warning nonstop, forever, for doing nothing wrong. Dropped to 60 — comfortably past `WORLD_BORDER_PUSH_ZONE` (15) so a player actually approaching the edge still gets real advance warning, while sitting below the closest a legitimately built plot edge can ever get to the true border (worst case: a maxed 128×128 plot's own 64-block half-width subtracted from `ISLAND_BORDER_MARGIN`'s 100 leaves 36 blocks of real headroom before this could ever fire on someone's own build again).

**Deep re-audit pass (BP 0.9.150), on direct instruction to keep going over everything already flagged and genuinely improve it further, not just patch symptoms.** Went back through every fix from this session with a critical eye rather than treating any of them as closed. Found four more real things worth fixing/improving, not just cosmetic re-reads:

1. **A fourth real duplication site, wrongly left alone in the original 0.9.146 pass.** `fillClear()` (the helper that removes `/market sell`'s temporary chest after the flow finishes) was also using `air destroy` — at the time this was deliberately left as-is on the theory it might be intentionally "refunding" the chest to whoever placed it. Re-checked that assumption directly this pass: `fillIfAir()` places that chest via a bare `setblock`, never taken out of the player's own inventory at all — it's completely free to conjure. `destroy` mode on cleanup meant every single `/market sell` cycle minted one real, physical chest item for free, a genuine repeatable exploit, not a refund of anything anyone paid. Fixed to plain `air`, same as the other 3 sites.
2. **Terrain check density genuinely increased, not just previously improved.** The 0.9.146 fix (5 points → a 3x3 grid, 9 points) was explicitly flagged at the time as still not a real guarantee against trees. Went further this pass: `isTerrainSuitable` now scans a full grid stepped every `TERRAIN_SAMPLE_STEP` (8) blocks across the actual footprint — 81 points for the 64x64 starter size instead of 9. Checked this against server-stability concerns specifically (not just claim-time cost) since `prescanCell()`'s background pre-scan job also calls this function, up to twice per cell — but that job only processes one cell every 10 seconds, so even worst case this amortizes to well under one extra block read per tick on average; not the same shape of cost as the mob-spawner flood-fill bug, which scaled with active generator count.
3. **Portal protection cache never verified its own entries stayed valid.** A cached frame coordinate was trusted forever, even if the real portal it pointed to was later destroyed by any means (explosion, WorldEdit, terrain regeneration) — the protection zone around it would persist permanently with nothing to prune it. `inPortalZone` now re-verifies the actual block at a cached position is still a real frame/portal every time a cache hit would otherwise fire, and prunes it on the spot if not — conservatively keeping the entry if the chunk can't be checked right now (never guesses a real portal away just because it's temporarily unloaded). Also added `/epitaph portal_clear` (confirm-gated) as a manual "start over" tool alongside the automatic pruning, for a cache an admin has independent reason to distrust.
4. **Orphaned-generator cleanup silently said nothing if the owner was offline.** The 0.9.146 fix added a cleanup + notification for a generator destroyed by non-standard means, but the notification only fired for an owner who happened to be online at that exact moment — offline owners got no message at all, ever, and would only discover the generator was gone by noticing later. The existing `gen_pending_msg_` pattern (used for buffer alerts) couldn't be reused as-is: it's delivered by looking up the generator's own `gen_owner_` property, which cleanup itself wipes along with everything else. Added a parallel per-PLAYER notice (`gen_orphan_notice_<uuid>`) that survives independently of the now-deleted generator, delivered by the same `playerSpawn` hook via a second, independent check.

**Portal protection radius narrowed to the actual portal room (BP 0.9.151)**, on direct instruction: nothing should be blocked from placing/breaking near a portal, only inside the portal room itself. `PORTAL_PROTECT_RADIUS` was 4 — since the frame ring is already a 5x5 footprint (12 frames), a 4-block buffer around *every one* of those 12 blocks pushed the actual protected area out to roughly 13x13, easily spilling past the real portal room into surrounding stronghold corridors and rooms where a player has every reason to expect normal building/mining to work. Dropped to 1 — still gives a one-block buffer against the classic piston/dispenser-parked-against-a-frame exploit this system exists to stop, while the resulting protected footprint (roughly 7x7, since every inner ring cell sits within 1 block of some frame) actually tracks the portal room instead of a wide area around it. Noted plainly rather than glossed over: explosion-radius abuse from TNT chained in from *outside* the zone is a different problem this was never solving regardless of radius size (cancelling placement/break events doesn't stop blast damage reaching in from a legal position just outside the zone) — shrinking the radius doesn't open a new gap here, that gap was never closed by the larger radius either.

**Spawners never spawned anything, at all, ever — root cause finally found (BP 0.9.152).** Asked directly to re-verify the earlier "spawners don't work" report rather than assume the wild-spawner pickup crash fix (0.9.146) was the whole story — it wasn't. Every generator, mob or block, is placed sunk one block into the floor so its output lands at the surface the player actually clicked — `y+1` from the generator's own registered position (see the placement handler's own "sink generator one block into the floor" comment). Block generators already correctly use `y+1` for their output block. `tickMobGenerator` was being handed the raw, sunk `y` instead — the generator's own solid block position, not open surface air. `findMobSpawnSpots`' flood-fill starts by checking whether its starting cell is air; it never was (it's the generator itself), so the search died on its very first cell — the neighbor-expansion code sits *after* that check in the loop body, so it never ran at all. Every single production tick, for every spawner that has ever existed in this addon, silently returned zero spawn spots, with nothing anywhere logging it as an error since `spots.length === 0` is a handled, expected-looking case (`findMobSpawnSpots`' own comment even calls it "fully enclosed with no floor space" — a plausible, silent-by-design outcome, which is exactly why this went unnoticed instead of throwing). Fixed at the one call site in `tickGenerator`'s mob branch — passing `y + 1` instead of `y` fixes every downstream use inside `tickMobGenerator` at once (the flood-fill, the entity-cap proximity search, and the actual spawn Y) without touching that function's own internals. Also corrected the "Show Spawn Radius" debug overlay box, which read the same raw sunk Y and would have rendered its guide box one block too low, visibly misaligned with where mobs now actually land.

**Spawner mob cap made genuinely per-generator, on direct instruction: "don't get capped by normal Minecraft mob spawn cap, have our own custom spawn cap, ensures upgrades work" (BP 0.9.153).** `dim.spawnEntity()` already bypasses vanilla's own mob cap entirely — it creates an entity directly rather than going through the natural-spawn cycle that cap throttles, so that specific interaction was never actually the problem. The real bug: `tickMobGenerator`'s own cap check counted *any* entity of the matching type within 16 blocks, with zero regard for where those entities actually came from — a wild-spawned zombie wandering near a zombie spawner, or a second nearby zombie spawner's own mobs, both silently counted against this spawner's cap and throttled it even though it was nowhere near its own real capacity. That directly undermines Pack Size, which only matters if there's cap headroom for the extra mobs to spawn into. Every mob a generator produces is now tagged with that generator's own ID (`epitaph_gen_id_<id>`) unconditionally — not just when Looting happens to be owned, like the pre-existing loot tags — and the cap check filters by that tag via `hasTag()` (not `EntityQueryOptions`' own `tags` field, which isn't proven anywhere else in this file — `hasTag()` already is, in the entityDie handler and Kit Disparity). The cap is now genuinely this generator's own, unaffected by wild spawns, sibling spawners, or anything else nearby.

**Research pass: is there anything better than DDUI? (still BP 0.9.153-era, no code changed by the research itself)** Verified live against Microsoft's current documentation rather than relying on memory, since this is exactly the kind of thing that could have changed. Confirmed: `CustomForm.button()`'s `ButtonOptions` still has exactly `disabled`/`tooltip`/an experimental `imageDetails` — no color/style field at all, dated as recently as 2026-07-21, so this addon's long-documented DDUI color gap is still accurate today, not fixed by a newer API version. OreUI (Mojang's own modern, React-based UI tech) is not directly authorable by add-ons — DDUI is the sanctioned bridge into that rendering pipeline, not "one option among several." One genuinely new finding: plain `ActionFormData` now also has `.header()`/`.divider()` (confirmed via its own live reference), closing much of what used to be DDUI's other cited advantage (real section organization vs. a flat button list) — the two systems' only remaining real difference is DDUI's live Observable-bound reactivity and multi-field toggle/slider/dropdown/textField forms, which `ActionFormData` still can't do.

**Full security audit: verified no non-admin path exists into any admin-only action (still BP 0.9.157), on direct instruction to check "100% ... no way someone without Admin have any access."** Traced every entry point systematically rather than spot-checking:
- **The 4 top-level `CustomCommandRegistry.registerCommand()` calls in the main pack** (`epitaph`, `setworldborder`, `grant`, `giveadv`) — the latter 3 use `perm: "admin"`, enforced centrally inside the shared `Commands.dispatch()` method (`if (def.perm === "admin" && chatPlayer && !isAdmin(chatPlayer)) return {status: Failure, ...}`), which runs before any command's actual handler logic — confirmed by reading `dispatch()` directly, not assumed from the `perm` field's name alone. Every command is deliberately registered at the *engine* level as `CommandPermissionLevel.Any` regardless of `perm` — the real gate is this addon's own tag-based `isAdmin()` inside `dispatch()`, not Bedrock's native operator-only permission level, specifically so a tag-based admin who isn't a server Operator still passes (documented directly in the code's own comment).
- **Every one of the 16 subcommands inside `/epitaph`'s own switch** — each admin-only case (`menu`, `ann`, `ann_fire`, `clock`, `cmd`, `cmd_fire`, `cmd_toggle`, `warp`, `struct`, `struct_place`, `portal_scan`, `portal_nearest`, `portal_clear`, `item_clear`) calls the local `adminOnly()` closure as its first line; `spawn`/`help` are correctly left ungated since they're genuinely player-facing.
- **The master `scriptEventReceive` relay** (the one place every satellite pack's slash commands actually land) — confirmed a single `if (!isAdmin(player)) return;` gate covers the entire admin-only switch beneath it (`plot:debuggen`, `plot:debugclear`, `plot:delete`, `plot:setmaxradius`, `raid:halt/resume/status`, `disparity:off/on/score`) before that switch can run at all. `plot:claim` routes differently (through `dispatchPlotCommand`'s own "claim" case, since it shares a relay shortcut with player-facing plot commands) — verified separately that its own `isAdmin(player)` check is still the very first line.
- **Every satellite pack** (Plot, Raid, Shop, Market, Kit, SellAll, Guide, Pay, Bounty, LevelShop Commands) — grepped for `permissionLevel` across all of them at once: every single command in every satellite pack registers as `CommandPermissionLevel.Any` uniformly, with zero local logic of any kind (pure `scriptevent` relay, exactly as this addon's own architecture describes) — meaning the satellite packs can't accidentally under- or over-gate anything, since they never gate at all; the security boundary lives in exactly one place; a real design strength for auditability, confirmed rather than assumed.
- **The entire admin control-panel menu tree** (`/epitaph menu` → `openControlPanel` → every module's own admin screen) — grepped every admin screen function (Kits/Announcements/Autonomous Commands/Spawn Ball/Admin Pearl/Structures/Give Items admin menus) for alternate call sites; every single one is only ever reached through internal menu navigation rooted at the one gated `"menu"` case, with no standalone/ungated entry point anywhere.

No gap found anywhere in this pass.

**Admin screens migrated off DDUI onto ActionFormData, fixing the same whitewashed-button bug already fixed 3× on the player side (BP 0.9.157)** — the natural next step after the DDUI research above, and something flagged as worth doing last turn. Rather than migrate each of the 13 admin screens individually, rewrote the single shared `showActionMenu(player, title, body, buttons)` helper itself to build a plain `ActionFormData` instead of DDUI's `CustomForm` — every call site (Kits, Announcements, Autonomous Commands, Spawn Ball, Admin Pearl, Plots, Stats HUD, both Shop admin panels, Staff Tags, Disparity, PvP Zone, Playtime Rewards, plus the player-facing "My Shops" screen as a side benefit) got fixed at once, zero changes needed to any individual screen. Confirmed lossless before making the change, not assumed: grepped the whole file for `tooltip:`/`disabled:` usage on any button passed to this helper — zero hits anywhere, so nothing built on top of `showActionMenu` was actually using the two DDUI-only capabilities (hover tooltips, disabled buttons) being traded away. `rt()`'s `{text}` RawMessage wrapping was dropped too — that was specifically a DDUI requirement (its own header comment says so directly); `ActionFormData` already accepts a plain § string natively, exactly like every other `ActionFormData` screen in this file. Also confirmed via grep that `CustomForm` has exactly 2 remaining direct usages in the whole file (`showModal` and `showSearchableActionMenu`'s above-threshold live-search branch) — both genuinely need DDUI's reactivity (multi-field toggle/text forms, live search-as-you-type) and were deliberately left alone; the Structures admin screen's search list specifically still trades DDUI's live filtering for plain color when it exceeds the search threshold, the same tradeoff already made and accepted for Plot Permissions' own search this session.

**Ranks & Kits added (BP 0.9.158)**, on direct instruction: "Staff, General Staff... Admin, Ban, mute... Creator... Member (3+ hours playtime)... Beta Tester... add more ranks you think are worth adding, nothing just for filler... different Kits... only usable with /kit if owned."

- **Two independent display "slots" near a name, not one flat list.** A HIERARCHY rank (Owner > Admin > Staff — moderation authority, senior wins, at most one shown) and a BADGE rank (Creator > Beta Tester > Member — recognition/perks, at most one shown). Both can show at once (a Staff member who's also a Beta Tester displays both prefixes). **Owner is the one rank added beyond what was asked for**, and deliberately the only addition — it's derived from genuine world Operator status (`PlayerPermissionLevel.Operator`), not tag-granted, surfacing a distinction `isAdmin()` already drew internally but had never shown visually (every admin, Operator or tag-only, used to render as one identical "[Staff]" prefix). Nothing else was added "for filler," per the instruction.
- **Staff/Creator/Beta Tester are tags** (`epitaph.rank.staff`/`.creator`/`.beta`), same `hasTag()` mechanism `ADMIN_TAG` already established. `isStaffOrAbove(player)` (`isAdmin(player) || hasTag(RANK_TAG_STAFF)`) is the one new permission primitive, and it is never wired into `isAdmin()` itself — "no massive permission level" for Staff was explicit, so every existing admin-only gate in the file (Control Panel, `/epitaph`'s own `adminOnly()`, the scriptevent relay's admin check) is completely unaffected by this rank existing at all.
- **Member is auto-granted at 3+ hours of playtime, tracked by a NEW, separate persistent counter** — the pre-existing `PlaytimeModule` (session-scoped, AFK-aware, resets to zero on every relog, exists purely to pace item/cash/EXP rewards) was confirmed unsuitable for "total playtime" by reading it directly, not assumed. `getTotalPlaytimeSec`/`addTotalPlaytimeSec` are a plain per-player dynamic property, incremented +10s every 200 ticks for every online player with no AFK-awareness at all (being logged in and briefly AFK still counts, deliberately unlike the reward-pacing module). Crossing the threshold fires a one-time unlock message + sound and immediately rebuilds the player's nameTag, rather than waiting for the next unrelated nameTag write to surface it.
