- **One shared composer prevents the 3-way (hierarchy/badge/Level-Shop-color) clobbering the 0.9.139 bug-hunt already found and fixed once for staff-tag-vs-color.** `computeExpectedNameTag(player)` builds the full string from every currently-active source; `rebuildPlayerNameTag` just assigns it. `syncStaffNameTag` (the periodic sweep, still 10s) now does a full-string comparison against this instead of the old single-boolean "has the Staff prefix" check, since that check predates badges existing and can't express a partial change (e.g. badge changed, hierarchy didn't).
- **EXP bonuses fold additively into the existing kill-EXP stacking formula** (`StatsModule`'s `entityDie` handler — same term as streak/wanted/PvP-zone bonuses, not a separate multiplicative layer): Creator +15% (the direct-instruction "general bonus"), Beta Tester +8% (a smaller thank-you, not a competing perk) — only one applies if a player somehow qualifies for both, Creator taking precedence to match the badge display's own senior-wins order. Modest, deliberately not power creep (an EXP-rate nudge, not gear/stats), in line with this addon's existing bonus tiers (PvP Zone's own default is 50%).
- **Two new kits, `CONTENT` and `BETA`, gated via `permTag` to `RANK_TAG_CREATOR`/`RANK_TAG_BETA`.** `claimKit`'s existing `kit.permTag && !player.hasTag(kit.permTag)` gate already fully implements "usable via /kit only if owned" — zero new gating code was needed, only correct wiring. Every other kit in this file is built by an admin physically holding items and running the in-game "Create Kit" hotbar-snapshot flow; these two have no such live source, so `seedRankKit`/`seedRankKits` (new, right after `writeState`) insert them declaratively and idempotently on every world load (skips if a kit by that name already exists, so an admin's later edit via the normal Kits admin UI is never clobbered on the next boot). No currency/ore filler in either list, per standing project policy — Content gets filming/showcase-flavored utility (spyglass, fireworks, banners, glow ink, scaffolding); Beta gets a "thank you for testing" mix (totem of undying, golden apples, ender pearls). Both recur on a 7-day cooldown rather than being one-time — "must have some general bonuses"/"its own kit" read as an ongoing perk of holding the rank, not a single welcome gift.
- **Staff gets its own toolkit, reachable via a NEW `/epitaph staff` subcommand gated at `isStaffOrAbove()`, not `adminOnly()`** — the literal "general helpful things... no massive permission level" ask. Teleport-to-Player, Freeze/Unfreeze (in-memory `frozenPlayers` Map, a `system.runInterval` every 5 ticks does `clearVelocity()` + one decisive teleport-back rather than fighting movement every tick — same lesson Plot Flight's `forceStopFlying` rewrite already learned the hard way), and Warn Player (persistent per-player counter, `Log.warn`-logged for an audit trail).
- **Admin gets Mute and Soft-Ban, both deliberately honest about what they don't do — neither is a real kick/ban/chat-block, and both say so directly in their own in-menu Guide text.** Live-refetched Microsoft's current `WorldBeforeEvents` reference before building this to re-confirm `chatSend` is still experimental-only (it is, page dated 2026-06-11) — the addon already had this documented once (`STAFF_PREFIX`'s own header comment: "chatSend is experimental-gated and would bypass Xbox Live chat filtering"), re-verified rather than assumed since a real "Mute" was explicitly requested. **Mute is a tracked flag only** (`mute_flag_<uuid>`, dynamic-property-keyed so it can be set for an offline player too, unlike a tag which needs a live Entity) — coordination for staff, no chat suppression exists anywhere. **Soft-Ban is a continuous debuff loop** (Blindness/Slowness/Weakness/Nausea reapplied every 100 ticks while the flagged player is online) — there is no kick/ban API in stable `@minecraft/server`, so this disorients rather than removes; the Guide text explicitly says a genuine removal needs the Realm's own player management outside this addon. Both toggle via the same offline-capable search-and-pick flow (`promptPlayerSearch`, reusing `searchKnownPlayers`/`getAllKnownPlayers` from the 0.9.154 Permissions overhaul) rather than a new one.
- **Every new button label was checked against this file's own 22-char safe limit** (`BUTTON_LABEL_SAFE_CHARS`, the 0.9.135 truncation-audit convention) before shipping, not after a report — "Grant/Revoke Beta Tester" (24 chars) shortened to "Grant/Revoke Beta", "Freeze / Unfreeze Player" (24) to "Freeze / Unfreeze", a long dynamic Guide-button label collapsed to "Mute/Soft-Ban Guide".

**"Give myself cash" replaced with a real "Give" menu — Items/Cash/EXP → player picker → action (BP 0.9.160)**, on direct instruction, plus a fix for the reported "giving an enormous number makes the stat permanently stuck" bug.
- **Root cause of the "stuck" report, confirmed by reading the code rather than guessed**: the only existing tool (`openGiveCashMenu`, deleted) was ADD-only (`addMoneyBig(player, amount)`, admin-to-self only, no player picker at all) with no way to directly SET or reset a stat. Reversing a huge balance meant knowing its exact digit string to negate — but the HUD/chat only ever show it abbreviated (`formatMoneyHud`/`formatMoneyBig` compact into tiers like "1.00Qi" past a billion), so once a balance crossed into that abbreviated range there was no practical way to read the exact value back out to undo it. Not a storage-corruption bug — `getMoneyBig`/`setMoneyBig` (BigInt, uncapped) never actually lose precision at any magnitude — it was a pure missing-capability gap: no "set exact value" or "reset" action existed anywhere.
- **`openGiveAdminMenu` is now the top-level "Give" picker** (Items / Cash / EXP), replacing the old direct-to-player-list behavior — that flow moved unchanged into new `openGiveItemsMenu`. Cash and EXP both route through `openGiveResourceMenu` (pick an online player) → `openGiveResourceActions` (pick an action): **Give Amount** (the old add/subtract behavior, now available for ANY player not just yourself), **Set Exact Value** (a modal pre-filled with the target's real, full, un-abbreviated current value — clear it and type anything, including 0), and **Reset to 0** (one confirm tap, no typing). Reset is never blocked by anything — it's the guaranteed unstick path regardless of how corrupted-looking an existing balance is.
- **`GIVE_RESOURCE_CAPS` bounds what a NEW value can ever be set/added to, so the same "enormous number" scenario can't recreate itself** — Cash capped at 1 quadrillion (one tier below the "Qi"/quintillion range that triggered the report), EXP capped at 100 million (EXP is a plain JS `number`, not BigInt, so it needed its own, much smaller, cap — nowhere near `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`'s ~9×10¹⁵ ceiling where a JS number starts silently losing integer precision, a genuinely different failure mode than money's arbitrary-precision BigInt storage). Caps only gate NEW values — lowering an already-over-cap stat (via Set or Reset) is never blocked, so an already-corrupted balance from before this fix is still always fixable.
- Both Cash actions route through the exact same `pullShopSpending` → write → `pushMoneyMirror` sequence every other money-touching flow in this file already uses (`giveResourceApplySet`), so a Give can't desync from a shop purchase happening the same tick — no new mirror-sync path was invented.
- `GiveModule`'s admin menu label changed from "Give Items" to plain "Give" to match; the Control Panel's old hardcoded "Give myself cash" quick-action button (top of `openControlPanel`, above the category list) was deleted outright now that the real Give menu covers giving yourself cash as just one case of "pick a player."

**Comprehensive Admin Plot Management added, and every player-search flow in the file unified onto one inline-search pattern (BP 0.9.161)**, on direct instruction: a real per-player plot toolkit (teleport/reset/delete/permissions) reachable via a searchable player picker, plus "put the search bar at the top of the list instead of a separate button" applied everywhere it already existed.

- **Inline-search unification.** `showSearchableActionMenu` (existing DDUI helper — a live-filter search field at the TOP of the same button list, only appearing once the list is long enough to need one) was already the right shape for this; the actual work was migrating every modal-first "type a name → see a separate results screen" flow onto it instead of leaving them as one-off copies. New shared helpers, both right after `searchKnownPlayers`:
  - `allKnownPlayersList(excludeName)` — the full online+ever-joined pool, unfiltered (just `searchKnownPlayers("", excludeName)` with an obvious name).
  - `pickPlayerFromList(player, title, bodyText, candidates, onPick, labelFor?, extraButtons?)` — the one shared "player list with inline search" primitive. `labelFor` lets a screen show a richer per-row label (Plot Permissions' name + permission summary) while `search` is always set to the plain name, so filtering matches what the player actually types even when the visible label carries extra decoration. `extraButtons` appends plain `{label, run}` entries (almost always a trailing "◀ Back") since every list screen in this file adds its own explicit Back rather than relying on the form's own close/cancel.
  - `pickOnlinePlayer(admin, title, onPick, backTo)` — thin online-only wrapper (excludes self) for the four tools that need a live Entity, not just a name/uuid pair.
- **Migrated, four separate modal+results-screen flows removed entirely**: Plot Permissions' "Online Players" list + "Search Players" (modal) + "Search Results" screen collapsed into one `openPlotPlayersMenu` (and the old inline roster list at the bottom of the main Permissions screen was removed too — fully redundant with Players now showing everyone, customized or not, in the same searchable list); Plot Groups' "Add Member" modal + results screen collapsed into `openPlotGroupAddMember`; the Ranks system's `promptPlayerSearch`/`pickOnlinePlayerMenu` (used by Grant/Revoke Staff/Creator/Beta, Mute toggle, Soft-Ban toggle, and Staff Tools' Teleport/Freeze/Warn) all rewritten onto `pickPlayerFromList`/`pickOnlinePlayer`. `SEARCH_RESULTS_CAP` and all three now-dead functions it belonged to were deleted rather than left as unreferenced dead code.
- **Deliberately NOT migrated: Player Shops' item/shop search (`openShopSearch`/`openShopSearchResults`).** Structurally different from every other search here — it needs an async stock-computation pass across every open shop's container (`shopComputeStocksFor`) before there's anything to list at all, not a static known-players array that can be rendered and filtered immediately. Converting it would mean touching the shop economy's more delicate async path for a lower-value win (it's not one of the flows this session's request was actually about), so it was left as its own modal, on purpose, not missed.
- **New Admin Plot Management** (`Plots — Admin` → "Manage Player Plots"): `openAdminPlotPicker` lists every known player who actually owns a plot (via new `getOwnedPlotByUUID`) through the same searchable picker; selecting one opens `openAdminPlotActions` — a per-plot summary (owner, plot #, center, border tier via new `plotBorderTierLabel`, groups, live generator count via new `countGeneratorsOnPlot`, which scans `gen_count`/`gen_active_N`/`gen_plot_N` the same way `purchasePlotCrystal` already does) plus four actions:
  - **Teleport Here** — reuses `teleportToPlot(admin, plotId)` directly; that function has no ownership/permission gate of its own (callers are expected to check first), so an admin can always visit any plot.
  - **Manage Permissions** — reuses `openPlotPermissionsMenu(admin, plotId)` unchanged, giving an admin the exact same Basic/Groups/Players tooling an owner has, for any plot.
  - **Reset Plot** (confirm-gated, honest about scope) — re-places a fresh starter island at the plot's existing center via the same `placeIsland()` the original claim flow uses, and clears any custom `/plot sethome` point first so `/plot home` lands on the fresh island rather than a now-stale custom point. Explicitly does NOT touch anything built outside the structure's own footprint, and does not touch ownership/permissions/groups — a terrain reset, not a delete, and the confirm dialog says so plainly rather than overclaiming.
  - **Delete Plot** (confirm-gated) — calls new `adminDeletePlotByUUID`, extracted from the `plot:delete` scriptevent handler's own inline logic (free the cell, clear ownership, clear the permission roster) so the relay command and this admin UI delete a plot through exactly one code path instead of two copies that could quietly drift apart. The scriptevent handler itself was shortened to call this shared function too.
- `PlotsModule.adminMenu`'s inline body was extracted into a named `openPlotsAdminMenu(player)` (same pattern already established for the Ranks module's `openRanksAdminMenu`) specifically so the new Plot Management sub-screens have a real function to navigate back to.

**Five real, confirmed bugs found and fixed in one pass (BP 0.9.162), on direct instruction to find and fix all of them, not patch one and stop.** Reported together: can't grant/revoke own Ranks, Owner badge coming from Operator status "not wanted," Kits not claimable for self, and — the big one — "Permissions just do not work: disabling Entry for all does not kick them out, and they still have break, build, hit and open container privileges." All five traced to real root causes by reading the actual enforcement code, not guessed:

1. **Can't grant/revoke your own Rank.** `pickOnlinePlayer` (the shared online-player picker used by Grant/Revoke Staff/Creator/Beta and Staff Tools' Teleport/Freeze/Warn) unconditionally excluded the acting player from the candidate list — correct for Teleport/Freeze/Warn (targeting yourself is meaningless there), wrong for rank granting, where self-targeting is completely valid and was actively needed. Also explains "Kits cannot be given to myself" — `/kit claim CONTENT`/`BETA` are gated behind `RANK_TAG_CREATOR`/`RANK_TAG_BETA` (`claimKit`'s existing `permTag` check, working exactly as designed), so being unable to grant yourself the rank tag made the rank-gated kits unclaimable as a direct downstream effect, not a separate kit-system bug — confirmed there is no separate "admin gives a kit to a player" UI at all; kits are self-claim only via `/kit claim <name>`, and non-rank-gated kits were never affected (no `permTag` means anyone can already claim them). Fixed by adding an `includeSelf` parameter to `pickOnlinePlayer` (default `false`, so Teleport/Freeze/Warn are byte-for-byte unchanged) and passing `true` only from `openRankGrantMenu`; also deduped the confirmation message so self-granting doesn't send the same fact to yourself twice.
2. **Owner badge coming from Operator status.** `getHierarchyRank` used to treat any player with genuine world `PlayerPermissionLevel.Operator` as "Owner" — reported directly as wrong, since a Realm owner can (and did, per the report) grant Operator to other trusted players for unrelated reasons (flight, cheats), and Operator is a Realm-wide permission level, not proof of being the literal owner. There is no script API that exposes genuine Realm ownership/account identity independent of in-world permission level — confirmed by reading through `@minecraft/server`'s actual surface, nothing like a `getRealmOwner()` call exists — so this can't be detected any more precisely than a name check. Fixed to an explicit `OWNER_NAMES = ["RealmOwnerName"]` allowlist (`isRealOwner`), replacing the Operator check entirely — update that constant directly if the real owner's name or account ever changes; there's no other mechanism to keep it in sync.
3. **The actual big one — "permissions just do not work" — traced to `isAdmin()`'s bare-Operator check leaking into plot-protection enforcement.** `verifyPermission`, `verifyRaidablePermission`, and `isBoundaryBypassed` all called `isAdmin(player)` as their bypass check — and `isAdmin()` (correctly, for its OWN actual job of gating admin COMMANDS) treats any genuine Operator as an admin. On a Realm where the owner has granted Operator to other players (exactly the scenario behind bug #2), every one of those Operators silently bypassed build/break/interact/entry checks entirely, regardless of what Basic/per-player/group permissions actually said — this is confirmed, direct root cause of "they still have break, build, hit and open container privileges" despite permissions being off. `isBoundaryBypassed`'s own comment already said "Only epitaph.admin bypasses movement boundary" — the code just didn't match its own stated intent. Fixed with a new, narrower `isPlotProtectionExempt(player)` (tag-only: `player.hasTag(ADMIN_TAG)`, no Operator check at all) used in place of `isAdmin()` in all three functions, plus the two generator ownership/interact checks that had the identical pattern (`playerInteractWithBlock`'s generator-Hub gate, the generator break/removal gate), plus the guest-notice sweep (`isAdmin(p)) continue` → `isPlotProtectionExempt`, since that sweep needs to match whoever's actually subject to enforcement now, not the old broader set). **`isAdmin()` itself is deliberately unchanged** — it still gates admin commands (Control Panel, `/epitaph`'s `adminOnly()`, the scriptevent relay's admin check), a separate, already-audited concern this report never touched; every other `isAdmin()` call site in the file (PVP combat-tag exemption, spawn-protection exemption, Spawn Ball daytime bypass, kill-EXP creative/admin exemption) was individually checked and confirmed to be about a genuinely different feature, not plot protection, and left alone.
4. **"Disabling Entry for all does not kick them out."** Root cause: `enforceBoundary` (the per-tick clamp loop) deliberately skips its own real permission re-check when a player's position hasn't changed since the last tick — a real, intentional perf optimization for the common AFK/idle case, not itself a bug. But it only tracks POSITION, not permission STATE: a player standing perfectly still the instant Entry gets toggled off never re-triggers the real check at all, since nothing about where they're standing changed, only the plot's rules did. Rather than remove that optimization, added `invalidateAllBoundaryState()` (clears the `boundaryState` Map outright) and wired it into every single low-level permission-write function — `setPlotPerm`, `setPlayerPlotPerm`, `clearPlayerPlotPermOverrides`, `setGroupPerm`, `setPlayerGroup`, `clearPlayerGroup`, `deletePlotGroup` — rather than the UI screens that call them, specifically so no future permission-editing code path can forget this and silently reintroduce the bug. Clearing state for every online player (not precisely computing who's standing on the affected plot) is deliberate: permission edits are rare admin/owner actions, not a hot path, so one extra real check for everyone on the very next boundary tick (every `BOUNDARY_CHECK_INTERVAL` = 4 ticks) is a non-issue, and reuses the exact same, already-correct clamp/kick logic already in `enforceBoundary` rather than reimplementing it.
5. **Found while auditing, not originally reported, but matches "hit" in the same complaint: item frames, glow item frames, armor stands, and paintings had ZERO plot protection at all, in either direction — not a bypassed check, there was simply never a check to begin with.** These are entities, not blocks, so none of the existing break/place/interact block handlers ever reached them — a classic grief vector (punch an item out of a frame, kill an armor stand and its equipment, tear down a painting) this addon's container/build protection was never actually covering. Added a `world.beforeEvents.entityHurt` handler (the same event this file already uses elsewhere for PvP-zone bypass and flight fall-damage, a proven real API) that cancels damage to these four entity types from a player without "interact" permission on that plot — gated on the existing "interact" key (same permission a chest already uses), not a new permission category, since these read as container-adjacent decorative fixtures; ordinary PVP and mob combat are completely untouched.

**Investigated and NOT found to be a real bug, reported honestly rather than papered over:** "Unsure, but commands, especially setting up shops, may be blocked behind operator status." Read `handleShopSetupCommand`/`shopResolveTarget` directly — shop setup gates on `canInteract(player, plotID)` (`isPlotOwner(...) || getPlayerPlotPerm(..., "interact")`), with no `isAdmin`/Operator check anywhere in that path at all, in either direction. No code-level mechanism found that would require (or block based on) Operator status specifically for shop setup. The isAdmin()/Operator-bypass bug (item 3 above) runs the opposite direction — it made Operators bypass MORE restrictions, not get blocked by them — so it doesn't explain this specific worry either. Flagged rather than "fixed" with an invented explanation; if shop setup is still denied unexpectedly after this pass, it needs its own concrete repro (which plot, whose permissions, what exact message) to trace further.

**Ordinary block placement (crafting tables, etc.) on unclaimed wilderness was flatly denied and destroyed the item with no way to get it back (BP 0.9.163).** Reported directly: "stuff like crafting tables and other things outright cannot be placed in open terrain... destroys and never replaces the item." Root cause, confirmed by reading `verifyPermission` directly: `if (id === 0) return false` — placement was fail-CLOSED on unclaimed land (id 0 = no plot), while `verifyRaidablePermission` (mining/looting/containers) has always treated the identical case as fail-OPEN (`if (id === 0) return true`), since there's nothing on unclaimed land to protect. This inconsistency meant a player couldn't place ANY block anywhere off their own plot, in genuinely empty wilderness, with zero grief risk involved. The one place this strictness is a deliberate, load-bearing economy rule — not collateral — is generator/spawn-egg placement (`itemStartUseOn`), which also calls `verifyPermission`: a shop-bought generator egg working in the wild would bypass the whole plot-ownership tie-in entirely. Fixed with a new `allowUnclaimed` parameter (`verifyPermission(player, loc, allowUnclaimed = false)`) — the two general `playerPlaceBlock` handlers (before-event and the stable-world after-event fallback) pass `true`; the spawn-egg call site and both portal-zone-authorization checks (which must stay strict regardless of claimed status, or a portal sitting on unclaimed land would lose its protection entirely) were deliberately left at the default `false`. Also fixed the item-loss half of the same report: the after-event fallback's "undo via `setblock air`" (the established 0.9.146 anti-duplication choice, kept — `destroy` mode would drop a duplicate) had no way to restore the item already consumed the instant the player placed it, so every genuinely-denied placement (someone else's plot, portal zone, spawn zone) destroyed the item outright with no recourse. New `restoreDeniedPlacementItem` rebuilds a plain `ItemStack` of whatever was just placed and gives it back (inventory first, dropped at the spot as a fallback) — wired into all three denial branches of the after-event handler, not just the general one.

**Plot Entry still wasn't actually being enforced after the previous permission-enforcement pass — found and fixed a second, deeper bug in the SAME boundary-clamp function (BP 0.9.164).** Reported directly: "I have zero permission to enter a plot, yet don't get removed UNTIL I break rules like destroy." Traced to `enforceBoundary`'s teleport-grace mechanism, a real feature (a brief window after a genuine long-distance jump — an ender pearl, `/plot home` to a friend's plot — before the hard clamp kicks back in, so a legitimate teleport doesn't get immediately punished) with two compounding, separate flaws:
- **A player with no PRIOR known position (a fresh join, or literally anyone right after any permission change, since `invalidateAllBoundaryState` — the previous fix — clears this exact state) had their `jumpDist` computed as `Infinity`** (`state.lastX !== undefined ? dist2D(...) : Infinity`), which always exceeded the grace threshold — meaning simply walking onto a denied plot with NO teleport involved at all was misread as "just teleported here," granting a free multi-second grace window before any real check ever ran. Fixed: `jumpDist` is now `null` (not `Infinity`) when there's no prior position to compare against, and the grace branch requires a real, measured jump (`jumpDist !== null`) — with nothing to compare against, it now falls straight through to the real clamp check on the very first evaluation instead of stalling.
- **Even a genuine, legitimate grace period had no expiry enforcement of its own.** Every tick during grace updates `lastX`/`lastZ` to the player's current position purely to track movement — if the player didn't move AT ALL during the ~3-second window (`BOUNDARY_AUTO_GRACE_TICKS` = 60, the common case: walk in, then just stand there to look around), `lastX`/`lastZ` stayed perfectly in sync with their static position the whole time. The instant grace expired, the function's own "position unchanged since last tick" fast path (a real, deliberate perf optimization for AFK players, kept) fired and skipped the real check *forever* — functionally identical to grace never having ended. Fixed with a new `justExitedGrace` flag (`state.wasGraceActive && !graceActive`, updated every tick) that forces exactly one real check through on the tick grace transitions from active to inactive, regardless of whether position changed, then gets out of the way for the fast path to resume normally afterward.
- **Raid Night's entry bypass was verified untouched by both fixes, per direct instruction to keep it working** — `raidBypass` (`!authorized && isRaidNightActive() && isOwnerOnline(plotID)`) is checked and returns earlier in the same function, before either the grace logic or the final clamp are ever reached, so an unauthorized player during an active raid with the owner online still walks in exactly as before; nothing about this pass changes that order or logic.

**Two real NPC Market bugs fixed (BP 0.9.165): live event bonuses invisible on `/market rates`, and the price list only accepting Silk Touch ore blocks instead of what normal mining actually drops.**
- **"Market surge doesn't actually increase the values shown in `/market rates`."** Confirmed directly — `openMarketRatesMenu` always rendered the raw `MARKET_SELL_PRICES` number with zero reference to `getCcuMarketMult()` anywhere in the screen. The live event system itself (`CCU_EVENTS`, `ccuActiveEvent`, tier-based Market Surge/Profit Wave/Wealth Wave/Market Apex) and the ACTUAL sell payout in both `sellAllInventory` and `finalizeMarketChest` were both checked directly and confirmed already correct — both already compute and add the live multiplier's bonus to what a player actually receives. Only the rates *display* was ever wrong. Fixed by computing the same `getCcuMarketMult()` in `openMarketRatesMenu` and showing the effective, event-adjusted price as the primary number (with a short "(+NN%)" tag) whenever an event is active, plus a one-line banner naming the active event — nothing changes when no event is running.
- **"Most are set to ores, instead of actual ingots or items like Diamonds, Iron ingot."** Confirmed by checking what vanilla mining actually drops: `minecraft:diamond_ore`/`gold_ore`/`emerald_ore`/`lapis_ore` as *items* are only obtainable via Silk Touch — a normal pickaxe drops the gem or raw-material item instead (`diamond`, `emerald`, `lapis_lazuli`, `raw_gold`), none of which had a `MARKET_SELL_PRICES` entry at all. Iron was missing in every form, ore or ingot, despite being one of the most basic materials in the game; coal and redstone were absent too despite being two of the most commonly sold materials in any survival playthrough. Fixed by ADDING the real drop/smelted forms alongside the existing ore-block entries (not replacing them — a genuine Silk Touch seller isn't regressed): `coal`, `redstone`, `raw_iron`/`iron_ingot`/`iron_block` (iron didn't exist in this table in any form before), `raw_copper`/`copper_ingot`/`copper_block` (also entirely new), `raw_gold`/`gold_ingot` (gold's existing `gold_ore` entry effectively already priced the raw-gold tier), `lapis_lazuli`, `emerald`, `diamond` (each priced identically to its already-established `_ore` counterpart, since mining one ore without Fortune yields exactly one gem — same underlying resource, same value), and `netherite_scrap`/`netherite_ingot` (the two missing intermediate steps between the existing `ancient_debris` and `netherite_block` entries). Table grew from 12 to 25 entries; `shopItemDisplayName`'s generic "strip namespace, title-case" transform and `shopItemTexturePath`'s existing item/block fallback paths both already handle every new id correctly with no further changes needed (confirmed `iron_block`/`copper_block` already had explicit block-texture entries; `iron_ingot`/`diamond`/`netherite_ingot`/`netherite_scrap` were already proven-working typeIds elsewhere in the file).

**Kit system extended with real cost + enchantment support, plus a full PVP kit progression seeded in code (BP 0.9.166)**, on direct request ("can you actually setup kits... use /kit <name>, it gives you the kit"). Two genuine capability gaps were closed first, since the requested kits couldn't be built correctly without them:
- **Kits could never carry a money cost.** The kit data model (`{name, items, cooldownSec, oneTime, permTag}`) had no cost concept at all — every kit was either free or rank-gated, never purchasable. Added a `cost` field (plain JS `number`, never BigInt — the kit list round-trips through `JSON.stringify` via `readState`/`writeState`, which throws on BigInt; same reason the PvP Zone's own cash-bonus config stays a plain number) and wired it into `claimKit`: checked and deducted via the same `pullShopSpending`/`getMoneyBig`/`addMoneyBig`/`pushMoneyMirror` sequence every other money-touching flow in this file already uses, converted to BigInt only at the point of touching the ledger. Also exposed in the admin UI — both `createKitFlow` and `editKitFlow`'s modals gained a "Cost to claim" field — so a cost isn't something only code-seeded kits can have.
- **Kits could never carry enchantments — not even admin-created ones.** `snapshotHotbar` (what actually builds a kit's item list when an admin holds items and runs "Create Kit") only ever captured `typeId`/`amount`/`nameTag`/`lore` — an admin creating a kit while holding a genuinely enchanted sword got a kit that silently handed out a plain, unenchanted one, with no warning anywhere that anything was lost. Fixed by capturing `minecraft:enchantable`'s live enchantments the same way `buildGiveItem` already reads them for `/epitaph:giveadv`, and applying them back in `claimKit` via the identical `comp.addEnchantment({ type: new EnchantmentType(id), level })` call. This also meant `kitItemGearScore` — the Kit Disparity anti-cheese system's kit-based scoring — needed the same fix its own comment had flagged as a known limitation ("no enchant bonus since the kit storage format doesn't preserve enchantments"): added `kitItemEnchantBonus` (reads a kit-item's stored `{id, level}` pairs against the same `DISPARITY_ENCHANT_SCORE` table `disparityEnchantBonus` uses for live equipment, since kit items are plain stored objects, not real ItemStacks with `hasComponent`/`getComponent`) so a heavily-enchanted kit now correctly raises a player's tracked gear-gap floor instead of under-reporting it.
- **Both fixes share one new generic `seedKit(name, items, opts)` primitive** — idempotent by name (never overwrites an admin's later edit via the normal Kits UI), covering every field the admin UI itself can set (`cooldownSec`, `oneTime`, `permTag`, `cost`). `seedRankKit` (the existing CONTENT/BETA seeder) now delegates to it instead of duplicating the insert logic.
- **The actual kit progression, "only PVP focused" per direct instruction, cost and cooldown both scaling with gear quality**: `Starter` (free, 1-hour cooldown — basic stone sword, leather armor set, bow, shield, food), `Warrior` (cost $2,000, 3-hour cooldown — iron gear, Sharpness I sword, Protection I on all four armor pieces, Power I bow, 2 golden apples, 4 ender pearls), `Champion` (cost $15,000, 8-hour cooldown — diamond gear, Sharpness II, Protection II, Power II, 4 golden apples, 8 ender pearls), `Legend` (cost $75,000, 24-hour cooldown — netherite gear, Sharpness III, Protection II, Power II, 6 golden apples, 12 ender pearls). Enchantment levels deliberately never exceed what a real enchanting table can roll (max Sharpness III / Protection II / Power II, never V or anything table-unreachable) — these are convenient purchasable loadouts, not gear that trivializes earned progression. Every armor piece within a tier carries the same protection level (a genuine matching set — Bedrock protection stacks per-piece, so one enchanted piece alone would undersell the tier). No currency/ore filler anywhere, per standing project policy. CONTENT/BETA (the existing rank kits) were left exactly as they were — this request's "only PVP focused" scope applies to the new progression, not the rank-reward kits, which stay themed around their own rank instead.
- `/kit list` now shows each kit's cost inline (`$2,000`-style, next to the name) alongside its existing cooldown status, so a player can see what something costs before attempting to claim it.

**Combat-log hard blocks (BP 0.9.167), reported directly: "simple C-Logging like /plot home after being hit still works... 20s tag, means ZERO Teleports and C-Logging, if they leave the realm to C-Log, they get a warn on next join."** The PVP Tag system (`initPvpTagSystem`, the 20-second post-combat window from earlier in this addon's history) already blocked the obvious teleport commands (`/plot home`, `/warp`, etc. — every one of them already routes through `pvpCombatBlocked`/`isPvpTagged` checks), so the report meant something the existing gate didn't cover yet. Read every real escape route directly rather than guessing which one the user meant:
- **Ender Pearl throws were completely unblocked.** The tag system only ever tracked a pearl's *landing* (for an unrelated boundary-grace purpose) — the actual throw was never gated at all, meaning a tagged player could simply pearl away from a fight with zero restriction, the single most exploitable gap of the three found. Fixed with a new `world.beforeEvents.itemUse` subscription inside `initPvpTagSystem()` that cancels `minecraft:ender_pearl` use outright when `isPvpTagged(ev.source)` is true, calling `pvpCombatBlocked(ev.source)` for the same standard denial feedback every other blocked action already gives.
- **The World Border rescue mechanism was a second, less obvious teleport escape.** `wbRescuePlayer` (the safety net that returns a player who somehow ends up outside the world border) always teleported straight to plot home or world spawn regardless of combat state — a tagged player could deliberately run past the border to trigger a free, instant, long-distance teleport out of the fight. Fixed: `wbRescuePlayer` now checks `isPvpTagged(player)` first, and if true, does only a small local in-bounds correction (clamped to `worldBorderSize / 2 - 2` at the player's *current* x/z, no distance teleport at all) instead of the normal rescue, plus a `pvpCombatBlocked` call for feedback — an untagged player triggering the same border edge case still gets the original, full rescue behavior, unchanged.
- **Leaving the realm mid-tag ("C-Logging") now gets a persistent, running-count warning on next join**, per direct instruction. The existing `playerLeave` handler (which already recorded a combat-log event when a tagged player disconnected) now also sets a `combatlog_warn_<uuid>` dynamic-property flag and increments a permanent `combatlog_count_<uuid>` counter. A new `world.afterEvents.playerSpawn` subscription checks and clears that flag on every join, and — 3 seconds after spawn (`system.runTimeout(..., 60)`, giving the client a moment to finish loading in) — sends the player a direct warning naming their total combat-log count to date, so repeated offenses are visible to the player themselves, not just logged silently server-side.
- **Verified no other teleport-based escape exists**: swept every `.teleport(` call site in the file via a direct grep. Every remaining call site is either already gated behind the same `pvpCombatBlocked`/`isPvpTagged` checks, admin-only (moot — admins can't be PVP-tagged), an internal implementation detail of an already-gated caller (e.g. `teleportToPlot` itself, called only from already-checked entry points), or a restrictive/defensive clamp rather than a voluntary player-triggered escape (e.g. the world-border push-back, itself now fixed above). No fourth gap found.

**Plot-protection dimension leak found and fixed (BP 0.9.168)**, prompted directly: "Does the fix with Can't place here, and then force teleport work in the nether? As previously, it would teleport you near 0, 32000, 0. Similar probably with end." Investigated by reading the actual enforcement code rather than guessing at the exact number (no literal `32000` constant exists anywhere in this file, and `enforceBoundary`/`enforceWorldBorderVisuals`/`enforceWorldBorderVelocity` all already correctly bail out immediately for any non-Overworld dimension — that specific system was never the source). The real, confirmed bug was one level down, in the block/entity protection handlers themselves:

- **`getPlotAtBlock(x, z)` is a pure x/z chunk lookup with zero dimension awareness** — it has no way to know whether the coordinates it's given came from the Overworld, the Nether, or the End. Every plot-protection event handler in the file already has a portal-zone sub-check explicitly gated `dim.id === "minecraft:overworld"`, but the *general* plot-permission branch underneath each portal check — the one that actually enforces ordinary build/break/interact permissions — had no such guard in **six** separate handlers: `playerBreakBlock` (before + after event), `playerPlaceBlock` (before + after event), `playerInteractWithBlock`, and the 0.9.162 item-frame/armor-stand/painting `entityHurt` protection. A player standing in the Nether or the End at x/z coordinates that numerically happen to fall inside an existing Overworld plot's chunk grid (very achievable, since Nether coordinates are commonly close to an Overworld plot near a portal at roughly 1:8 scale) had every one of those actions checked against that unrelated Overworld plot's permissions — including the trespass-violation counter and its eventual kick-teleport, entirely spuriously, for a "plot" that has nothing to do with where the player actually is.
- **The spawn-egg/generator check (`itemStartUseOn`) had the same leak with the opposite, and arguably worse, polarity**: a coincidental x/z match against the player's *own* Overworld plot would make `verifyPermission` return `true` (allowed), meaning a generator egg could actually take root and function in the Nether/End — bypassing the entire plot-bound generator economy this addon's design depends on. Fixed by making this check `e.source.dimension.id === "minecraft:overworld" && verifyPermission(...)` — outside the Overworld, a generator egg is now always treated as invalid regardless of what the stale x/z lookup would otherwise say, with `id` forced to `0` (no plot) rather than a spurious real match when reporting/despawning.
- **All six general branches now require `dimension.id === "minecraft:overworld"` before running**, matching the pattern already established for every portal-zone check — plots simply don't exist outside the Overworld, so there is nothing there to protect, exactly the same reasoning already applied when the Ender Pearl/portal fixes were scoped to Overworld earlier in this file's history.
- **A second, independent bug in the same area**: `suspendPlayer`/`releaseSuspendedPlayer` (the "float in place, wait for chunk load, then land" mechanism behind `teleportToPlot` — used by `/plot home`, first-join plot claim, and the repeat-trespass kick) teleported using `{ dimension: player.dimension }` — the player's *current* dimension — instead of forcing the Overworld, the only dimension a plot can ever actually be in. A player triggering this while genuinely standing in the Nether or End (e.g. running `/plot home` from there, a completely legitimate thing to do) would get suspended and land-scanned *in that dimension*, at coordinates that mean nothing there, rather than actually being brought back to their Overworld plot — the mechanism this session couldn't fully explain the exact "0, 32000, 0" symptom from, but is a genuine, confirmed bug in its own right and a very plausible contributor to erratic teleport behavior when this whole pipeline fires outside the Overworld. Fixed by forcing `world.getDimension("overworld")` explicitly in both `suspendPlayer` (initial teleport and the interval's re-centering correction, which now also re-fires if the player's dimension itself has drifted, not just their Y) and `releaseSuspendedPlayer` (the landing scan and final teleport), so any call into this pipeline now always actually performs the dimension change back to the Overworld regardless of where the player started.

**Stats HUD board realm name shortened to "Elysium" (BP 0.9.169)**, on direct request - the board was showing "Elysium Skygen." `DEFAULT_STATS_CONFIG.realmName` (`main.js`, ~line 9294) changed from `"Elysium Skygen"` to `"Elysium"`. Worth flagging plainly since this value is admin-editable at runtime and persisted, not purely code-driven: `StatsModule.onWorldLoad()` does `statsCfg = { ...DEFAULT_STATS_CONFIG, ...stored }`, so this code change only takes effect if nothing has ever explicitly saved a `realmName` into the HUD Config store (`epitaph:stats:config`) - if an admin previously used HUD Config's "Realm name" field, that stored value wins over this default forever until changed again. The same field is directly editable in-game (Control Panel -> Stats HUD -> HUD Config -> Realm name) for an immediate, guaranteed fix regardless of which case applies.

**Player Shops: sign auto-register was silently broken from the moment it shipped (BP 0.9.170)**, reported directly: "Player Shops fail to display any details, like stock, item or price/item." Narrowed down (the user confirmed it's the physical in-world sign, not the /shop browse menu) and root-caused by checking `BlockSignComponent`'s actual behavior against Microsoft's own current reference rather than assuming the existing code was right: `shopGetSignText()` (`main.js`, ~line 14477 — used only by the sign-and-price auto-register flow, `world.afterEvents.playerPlaceBlock`'s sign-polling handler) read a sign's text exclusively via `signComp.getRawText()`. Per Microsoft's own docs, `getRawText()` only ever returns something if `setText()` was called with a RawMessage/RawText object - it returns `undefined` for text set as a plain string, and a player typing on a sign through the normal client sign editor produces plain-string text, not a RawMessage. This codebase's own `refreshShopSign()` also only ever calls `setText()` with a plain template string, never a RawMessage - meaning `getRawText()` was returning `undefined` for literally every sign this addon has ever touched, player-typed or script-written. The practical effect: `shopParseSignText()` always received `""`, so a player placing a sign next to their chest and typing a price (e.g. "500/ea") never triggered `shopAutoRegisterFromSign()` at all - no shop record was ever created in the backend, silently, with no error message anywhere (the poll just times out after 10 seconds). The sign kept showing exactly what the player originally typed forever, never getting upgraded to the addon's own rich seller/price/stock/item display - matching the report exactly, and worse than a display-only bug, since the shop was never actually registered (invisible to `/shop browse` too, not just visually incomplete). Fixed by trying `getText()` first (the correct read for plain-string text) with the original `getRawText()` path kept as a defensive fallback. **This does not retroactively fix a sign a player already tried and gave up on** - the auto-register poll only runs for ~10 seconds right after the sign is physically placed, so an already-placed "stuck" sign has no shop record to repair. Any shop that seemed to silently fail this way needs to be redone: either break and replace the sign (re-triggering the poll under the fixed code), or run `/shop setup` while looking at the chest directly, which registers the shop through the modal-based path instead (unaffected by this bug, since it never reads sign text at all) and immediately calls `refreshShopSign()`.

**`/plot:claim` reverted from PlayerSelector back to plain String (BP 0.9.171), plus a real-time audit across the Admin Plot UI and player-lookup pipeline**, reported directly: "The game does not update when players are online, I cant see new players plots in the Admin Plot UI. Claim command fails as it says they are offline, even when they are not. We need EVERYTHING to reflect real-time changes."
- **Audited every "who's online / who owns a plot" read path in the file** (`getAllKnownPlayers`, `searchKnownPlayers`/`allKnownPlayersList`, `getOwnedPlot`/`getOwnedPlotByUUID`, `openAdminPlotPicker`, `openPlotsAdminMenu`, `lookupUUID`) - every single one already reads `world.getPlayers()` and `world.getDynamicProperty(...)` live, fresh, on every call, with zero caching anywhere in that chain. `openAdminPlotPicker` in particular rebuilds its player-with-a-plot list from scratch every time it's opened - there was no stale cache to fix there; it already reflects real-time state.
- **The actual, confirmed root cause was narrower and more specific: `/plot:claim`'s use of `CustomCommandParamType.PlayerSelector`.** PlayerSelector resolution is Bedrock's own engine-side target-selector matching - a completely different code path from this addon's own `world.getPlayers()` reads, and the one piece of the whole claim pipeline this addon doesn't control. A freshly-joined player not yet being selector-resolvable for some window after connecting is the most plausible explanation for a genuinely-online target reporting "No matching online player found" (stated plainly: this specific engine timing quirk isn't independently documented anywhere findable, so it's a reasoned, defensive fix removing an untrusted dependency, not a confirmed-by-official-docs bug like the sign/BlockSignComponent fix above).
- **Fixed by removing PlayerSelector from `plot:claim` entirely** (`Epitaph-PlotCommands/scripts/main.js`) - back to a plain String parameter, relaying the raw typed name straight through with no engine-side pre-resolution at all. `dispatchPlotCommand`'s "claim" case (`main.js`, ~line 8161) - the one place that now does ALL the resolution - was hardened to match case-insensitively (`p.name.toLowerCase() === targetName.toLowerCase()`) so an admin's exact-case typo doesn't also read as "not online"; the online check itself was already fully live (`world.getPlayers()`, no caching), it just needed to be the ONLY check in the pipeline instead of running after an unreliable selector pre-filter.
- **Why the Admin Plot UI appeared to miss new players is explained by the same root cause, not a second bug**: a new player's plot IS auto-claimed on join independently (the `playerSpawn` handler calls `claimPlot(player)` directly off the live event's own `Player` object - no selector involved, unaffected by any of this), normally completing within a few seconds. But an admin trying to manually `/plot:claim` a player who, for any reason, didn't already have one, kept silently hitting the PlayerSelector bug above - meaning no plot ever got created that way, so there was genuinely nothing yet for the (already-live) Admin Plot Management picker to show. Once claim actually succeeds, the picker reflects it on the very next open - no refresh/reload/wait needed.
- **`/pay`, `/epitaph:grant`, and `/epitaph:giveadv` also use PlayerSelector** (added deliberately at 0.9.124 for real autocomplete) and could in principle share the exact same class of resolution risk for a just-joined target - not touched this pass since none of them were reported broken, and ripping out real autocomplete from working commands isn't a fix this addon makes speculatively (see this file's own standing design philosophy). Worth reverting the same way if any of them are ever reported with the identical "says offline, isn't" symptom.

**"Region exhausted (650-1350 band full)" alert clarified and fixed (BP 0.9.172)**, reported directly: "I keep getting alerts... What does this mean, I even raised the border to 2500." Two separate things to untangle here, both real:
- **The plot claiming band is a completely separate setting from the actual Minecraft world border.** `MAX_RADIUS` (1,350 by default) is this addon's own deliberate outer edge for where NEW plots are allowed to spiral out to - it has nothing to do with `worldBorderSize` (the real world border, changed via `/epitaph:setworldborder`). Raising the world border to 2500 does not, and was never meant to, expand the plot claiming band - that's a separate, dedicated admin override (`plot_max_radius_override`) set via `/plot:setmaxradius <r>`. If only the world border was raised, the alert was actually correct: the claiming band genuinely was still capped at 1,350 the whole time.
- **A real, confirmed bug found while checking this: the alert message itself could never reflect an override, even a correct one.** `advanceCursorAt`'s actual exhaustion check already correctly reads `plot_max_radius_override ?? MAX_RADIUS` live - so if an admin HAD already run `/plot:setmaxradius 2500`, the system really was searching out to 2500 correctly. But both `alertPlotAdmins` calls that fire on genuine exhaustion (`claimUnconditionally`'s and `noSlots`'s, `main.js` ~line 6076/6086) hardcoded the literal `MIN_RADIUS`-`MAX_RADIUS` constants directly into the message string, not the live effective value - so the alert would keep printing "650-1350" forever, regardless of what the override actually was, making it look like nothing had changed even after a correct fix. Fixed with a new `effectivePlotMaxRadius()` helper (right next to `MAX_RADIUS`'s own declaration) that both alert sites now read through instead of the bare constant, and both messages now also name `/plot:setmaxradius <r>` directly as the fix, rather than leaving an admin to guess. The exhaustion LOGIC itself was never broken - only the message describing it was.

**World border → plot band auto-sync (BP 0.9.173), plus a concrete re-verification pass**, reported directly, in frustration at a prior answer that read as "should work" reassurance rather than a checked fact: "If I use /border 2500, ALL features should reflect that change where needed... new plot slots should be available, literal border should be pushed N blocks Radius... Permission systems need to work concrete. No 'This'll work' bull." Split into what was actually broken vs. what was checked and confirmed already correct - stated plainly as which is which, not blended together:
- **Actually broken and fixed: raising the world border never actually expanded the plot claiming band on its own.** `setWorldBorderRadius` (`main.js`, ~line 4148, the function behind `/epitaph:setworldborder`) only ever sent an admin a WARNING that the plot max radius should also be raised, naming the right number to type into a second, separate `/plot:setmaxradius` call - it never did it. That's exactly the half-measure described: the border moved, the claiming band didn't, and nothing except an ignorable chat warning surfaced the gap. Fixed so `setWorldBorderRadius` now sets `plot_max_radius_override` itself, automatically, in both directions - growing it to fill the new safe space the instant the border grows (this is the literal "new plot slots should be available" behavior), and pulling it back in if the border shrinks. `/plot:setmaxradius <r>` still exists and still works exactly as before for a deliberate, later override independent of the border - this only changes what happens automatically as a direct consequence of a border change.
- **Checked and confirmed already correct, not touched because it wasn't broken: the literal border push itself.** `worldBorderSize` is a single module-level variable with exactly one write site (`setWorldBorderRadius`) and every consumer - `enforceWorldBorderVisuals`, `enforceWorldBorderVelocity`, `wbRescuePlayer`, and `advanceCursorAt`'s own border-containment check for new plot claims - reads that same live variable directly, with zero caching or snapshotting anywhere in the file (grepped every reference to confirm, not assumed). A border change takes effect for every one of those systems on the very next tick, for every online player, with no relog or reload needed. This was already real-time before this pass; saying otherwise would have been exactly the kind of unverified claim asked not to make again.
- **Checked and confirmed already correct: new-player real-time sync outside the /plot:claim command itself** (already fixed last pass) - `getAllKnownPlayers`, `searchKnownPlayers`, `getOwnedPlot`/`getOwnedPlotByUUID`, and the Admin Plot Management picker all read `world.getPlayers()` and dynamic properties live on every call, confirmed again this pass by re-reading each one rather than trusting the prior write-up.
- **Checked and confirmed already correct: the plot-protection permission chain.** Re-grepped every remaining `isAdmin(` call site in the file (17 total) and classified each one by hand: the 6 that needed to be tag-only (`verifyPermission`, `verifyRaidablePermission`, `isBoundaryBypassed`, the generator interact/break gates, the guest-notice sweep) all correctly call `isPlotProtectionExempt` instead, confirmed by reading the actual line, not the changelog claiming it. Every other `isAdmin(` site left as `isAdmin` is a genuinely different, non-protection feature (admin command gating, the PVP-tag combat exemption, spawn protection, Spawn Ball's daytime bypass, kill-EXP's creative/admin exemption, and one display-only "show buffer contents to a viewing admin" gate in the Generator Hub screen that grants visibility, not a permission bypass) - none of them are a plot-protection leak. `getPlayerPlotPerm`'s resolution order (personal override > group > Basic) was also re-read directly and matches the documented design exactly.

**Tips made fully admin-manageable, both lists (BP 0.9.174)**, on direct request after being told there was no in-game way to manage them at all: "Make tips manageable server-side, as well as Personally." Both previously hardcoded arrays - the world-broadcast rotation (`TIPS`, `TipsModule`) and the per-player sequential onboarding list (`PLAYER_HINTS`, `initHintSystem`) - replaced with one shared, in-game-editable store under `TIPS_STORE_KEY` (`{ global: [{id,text,enabled}], personal: [{id,text,enabled}], nextId, seeded }`).
- New Control Panel entry: World > Tips. `openTipsAdminMenu` splits into "Server-wide Tips" and "Personal Hints" - each opens a list (`openTipsListMenu`) of every defined tip with a live preview and Enabled/Disabled status, a "+ Add new" button, and tap-through to a per-tip editor (`openTipEditor`: edit text, toggle enable/disable, delete-with-confirm). List-button previews are deliberately short (16/20 visible chars, personal leaving room for a "#N" order prefix) and run through `warnIfButtonLabelRisky` as a safety net - this file has been burned by the button-label-truncates-into-an-uncolored-tooltip bug multiple times before (0.9.131, 0.9.135), so new dynamic list screens are built to stay under that limit from the start rather than needing their own later audit pass.
- Tip text is stored PLAIN, not pre-formatted - an admin types just the body (e.g. "Use /plot home to warp to your island."), and each list applies its own fixed color scheme automatically at render time (`formatGlobalTip`: gold "[Tip]" tag + gray body; `formatPersonalHint`: gray tag + white body, matching the two lists' original distinct visual identities exactly). An admin can still embed color codes inline for emphasis - only the repeated boilerplate prefix/suffix is automatic, exactly like every other free-text field elsewhere in this addon (announcement messages, kit item names).
- One-time migration, not a fresh start. `seedTipsIfNeeded()` (gated on a stored `seeded` flag, checked once on world load) inserts the exact same tip bodies the old hardcoded arrays used to carry - existing players see no behavior change on the very first load under the new system, they just become editable from that point on. Deleting every tip down to zero afterward is a real, permanent admin choice - the flag (not list length) is what prevents a reseed, so an intentionally emptied list stays empty across restarts.
- Personal hint progress tracks a set of SEEN HINT IDs per player, not a raw list position - a deliberate redesign, not a straight port of the old `hint_idx` counter. A position index would desync the moment an admin deleted, reordered, or inserted a hint (a player halfway through the old sequence could suddenly skip or repeat hints with no way to tell). Tracking which specific hint IDs a player has already seen stays correct no matter how the list changes afterward. `readSeenHintIds` also does a one-time migration for players who already have progress under the OLD `hint_idx` system: it synthesizes their seen-set from however many hints their old position had already covered, so nobody sees a hint repeat on this update specifically because of the storage change.
- The "veteran" fallback (a quiet repeat reminder once all personal hints are exhausted, every 90 min) now always repeats whichever hint currently sits first in the list, rather than a hardcoded index-0 string - since that first slot is admin-editable now too, this stays correct automatically if an admin ever reorders or replaces it.
- `initHintSystem()`'s call site moved from `ShopModule.onWorldLoad` into the new `TipsModule.onWorldLoad` (alongside `seedTipsIfNeeded()` and `scheduleTip()`) - both hint systems are the same underlying feature now and belong under one module with its own real admin menu, rather than one of them being wired into an unrelated module for no structural reason.

**"Server Validation" admin quick action added (BP 0.9.175)**, on direct request: "Add an Server validation button to the admin menu, to force everything to sync; I mean EVERYTHING." A real, comprehensive force-resync pass, not a token action - `/epitaph menu` now shows a `⟳ Server Validation` button at the very top, above the category list (confirm-gated via `showConfirm`, since it touches most subsystems at once), running `runServerValidation`:
- Re-runs `runSchemaMigrations()` and every idempotent seed pass (`seedRankKits`, `seedPvpKits`, `seedTipsIfNeeded`) - all safe to call any number of times, so this can recover a world where one of them never ran (a mid-session crash during world load, etc.) without needing a restart.
- Re-asserts the plot claiming band <-> world border invariant `setWorldBorderRadius` normally enforces on every border change (see BP 0.9.173) - catches any drift that happened outside that command (e.g. a direct dynamic-property edit).
- Flushes any pending in-memory generator XP to disk FIRST, then clears every generator/plot lookup cache (`genNodeCache`, `genPosCache`, `genXpCache`, `mobSpawnSpotCache`, `chunkIdCache`, `cellIdCache`, `plotCenterCache`, `plotBoundsCache`) - flush-before-clear is what makes clearing `genXpCache` safe rather than silently discarding not-yet-persisted XP.
- Rebuilds `ownerOnlinePlots` from scratch off who's actually connected right now, and calls `invalidateAllBoundaryState()` - the exact same primitive every permission-editing screen already calls after a change, forcing a real, live permission re-check for every online player on the very next boundary tick.
- Force-refreshes every shop sign whose chunk is currently loaded, using the identical guard the periodic 5s sign sweep itself uses (`dim.isChunkLoaded` before calling `refreshShopSign`) rather than force-loading every unloaded shop's chunk at once, which would risk exceeding Bedrock's ticking-area limit for no benefit - nobody's standing near an unloaded shop to see its sign anyway.
- Resyncs every online player's money mirror (`pullShopSpending`/`pushMoneyMirror`) and rebuilds their name tag (`rebuildPlayerNameTag`), then pushes one immediate Stats HUD board refresh instead of waiting for its own scheduled cycle.
- Reports back a concrete, itemized summary to the admin (exact counts - generators flushed, plots with an online owner, signs refreshed vs. skipped, players resynced) rather than a generic "Done!" message, so there's something to actually check the action against, not just trust.
- Everything it touches is either a pure read-through cache of a dynamic property (safe to clear - the next read just re-derives it) or an already-idempotent re-sync/re-seed pass, so this is safe to run as many times as wanted, at any point, with no risk of corrupting or duplicating anything.

**Shop signs still rendering completely empty after refresh, force validation, AND full break-and-replace (BP 0.9.176)** - reported directly, after the 0.9.170 fix, with "REALLY think about this." That prior fix targeted the READ path (`shopGetSignText`, used only by the sign-and-price auto-register detector) - this report is about the WRITE path (`refreshShopSign`, the function every one of those three attempts funnels through), a different bug even though it's the same class of mistake. Investigated properly rather than guessed again: verified `BlockSignComponent.setText`'s real signature and behavior directly against Microsoft's current reference (512-char throw limit, SignSide defaulting to front, waxed-sign editability) - nothing there explained a universal, always-empty result. Checked the wall-sign placement's `facing_direction` block-state values against Microsoft's own block-state reference - confirmed as a real, valid legacy state, not the bug either.
- **The actual, confirmed problem: `refreshShopSign` wrapped every step - `getBlock`, `getComponent("minecraft:sign")`, `setText`, `setWaxed` - in one bare `try { ... } catch (_) {}`, with nothing logged on failure.** If any single one of those ever failed for any reason, the whole thing would silently do nothing, indistinguishable from success - the exact same silent-catch shape that hid the 0.9.126 particle-ID bug in this same file ("works but nothing shows, no error anywhere"). There was no way to actually know which step was failing, for anyone, ever - stated plainly rather than papered over: **this pass could not get 100% certainty on the exact original root cause without a live repro**, because the diagnostic that would reveal it never existed until now.
- **Fixed the visibility gap for real**: every step now checks its own precondition and calls `Log.error` with a specific, actionable reason before giving up - no block at the stored `signLoc`, the block there isn't a sign at all (a stale `signLoc` - the sign was broken/replaced by a path that never updated the shop's record), `getComponent` returning nothing on a block that IS sign-typed, or `setText` itself throwing. The next attempt will surface exactly which of these is actually happening in the Content Log, instead of everyone continuing to guess.
- **Two defensive fixes made alongside the logging**, addressing the two most plausible silent-failure theories even without a confirmed live repro of either: `setText` now passes `SignSide.Front` explicitly instead of relying on its documented default (removes any doubt about how this specific SDK build actually handles an omitted side argument), and `setWaxed(true)` is now deferred one tick after `setText` via `system.run()` instead of firing in the same synchronous call (never confirmed as a real interaction, but a same-tick "write then immediately lock" sequence was never actually load-bearing for waxing to still apply to the committed text).
- Stated honestly rather than oversold: if signs are still blank after reimporting this build, the Content Log will now say specifically why on the very next refresh/force-validation attempt - that message is what actually pins the root cause down, not this write-up.

**Java-style chat screen added (BP dependency pin -> RP 0.9.16)**, on direct request: the user pointed at a third-party pack, "Java Chat Screen v1.0" (by @Itzriyo157, (a local copy of that pack)), and asked for "our own VERY Similar recreation" rather than bundling that pack's own files. Built as a genuinely independent implementation, not a copy:
- **New files**: `resource_pack/ui/epitaph_java_chat.json` (namespace `epitaph_javachat` - distinct from the reference pack's own `déesse_javaChat`) defines the actual look (flat black/semi-transparent icon buttons that brighten near-white on hover, a solid dark message-log panel replacing Bedrock's default translucent gradient), and `resource_pack/ui/chat_screen.json` swaps it in for vanilla's `chat_screen_content` anchor. Both registered in the RP's `ui/_ui_defs.json`.
- **Zero custom image assets needed, unlike the reference pack** - every icon is either a real vanilla stock texture (`textures/ui/copy`/`paste`/`gear`/`chat_send`/`White`/`Black`, all built into Bedrock itself) or a plain text glyph (`$icon_text`: "x", "/", "v", "^") standing in for the two icons the reference pack drew custom PNG art for (touch message-history navigation) - this was a deliberate choice specifically so nothing here reuses any of that pack's own art or files, only Mojang's public vanilla texture set plus this addon's own from-scratch JSON.
- **The vanilla anchor names referenced throughout** (`chat.text_edit_box`, `chat.auto_complete_panel`, `chat.messages_stack_panel`, `host_options.host_panel`, `chat_settings.popup_factory`, `common.scrolling_panel`, `common.button`) **are Mojang's own public JSON UI hook points, not the reference pack's original work** - any pack achieving this same visual effect has to reference these same identical vanilla anchors, since they're the actual functional building blocks Bedrock provides for the chat screen; there's no alternate/original way to wire up a custom chat screen that doesn't go through them.
- **This is the addon's first-ever chat-screen UI change**, and JSON UI failures in Bedrock are typically silent (a missing/misnamed anchor just doesn't render or function, no error surfaced anywhere) - flagged plainly rather than glossed over: this has not been visually confirmed in a live client yet, only validated for structural JSON correctness (brace/bracket/comma balance, confirmed via a comment-stripped `json.loads` parse). Test the chat screen directly after reimporting before relying on it, the same standing caution this file already applies to any UI change that can't be verified from a script context alone.
- RP header+module versions bumped (0.9.15->0.9.16, 0.9.11->0.9.12) and the main BP manifest's own RP dependency pin updated to match (0.9.15->0.9.16) - same convention established at 0.9.127 for the pack's first-ever RP asset change, since a stale dependency version pin here is exactly the kind of thing that silently breaks re-import. The main BP's own version was left untouched since no behavior-pack script content changed this pass.

**Player Shops: the actual, confirmed root cause of every sign-placement report this session (BP 0.9.177)** - reported directly, after 0.9.176's diagnostics-only pass: "A Sign never gets automatically placed on a shop chest, it doesn't place one; Nor does it affect one I place. Refresh sign fails too." This time the 0.9.176 diagnostics (Log.error calls added to refreshShopSign) pointed at the real problem instead of needing another guess - but the bug wasn't even in refreshShopSign's own write path, it was one level upstream, in how a sign gets a location to write to in the first place.

- **The actual bug: `shopAutoPlaceSign`'s `setblock` command used invalid Bedrock command syntax.** `dim.runCommand(\`setblock ... minecraft:oak_wall_sign ["facing_direction":${f.sd}]\`)` used a colon to assign the block state - that's NBT/JSON syntax, not command syntax. Bedrock's actual `/setblock` block-state array uses `=` (`["facing_direction"=2]`), confirmed against the command's real syntax. Every single call to this command has been throwing a syntax error since the day this code was written, silently swallowed by the surrounding `try/catch(_) {}` with nothing logged - meaning **no sign has ever been successfully auto-placed by this addon, at all, ever**, matching "it doesn't place one" exactly. Fixed the syntax (`=` instead of `:`) and added `Log.error` on the catch, so a future placement failure (e.g. genuinely no free space) is visible instead of silent.
- **This one bug explains "Refresh sign fails too" as a direct downstream consequence, not a separate problem.** Since auto-place always failed, every shop's `signLoc` has always fallen back to the `NO_SIGN_LOC` sentinel - and `refreshShopSign`'s very first line (`if (!shopHasSign(shop)) return;`) silently no-ops for any shop in that state. Worse, the "Refresh Sign" button's own handler unconditionally sent "Sign refreshed." regardless of whether anything actually happened - so a stuck shop reported false success on every click, which is exactly what "fails too" describes: not an error, just nothing happening while being told otherwise.
- **"Nor does it affect one I place" was a second, genuinely separate gap in the same area, not the same bug twice.** `shopFindAdjacentSign` - a function that scans the 4 blocks next to a chest for an already-placed sign - existed in the file already but was never called from anywhere (confirmed by grep: exactly one match, its own definition). `shopResolveTarget` (the function behind `/shop:setup`) only ever reused an *existing registered shop's* stored `signLoc`, or tried to auto-place a brand-new sign - it never checked whether the player had already physically placed one next to the chest themselves, so a manually-placed sign was always ignored in favor of a fresh auto-place attempt (which, per the bug above, always failed anyway). Wired `shopFindAdjacentSign` into `shopResolveTarget` ahead of the auto-place fallback, so running `/shop:setup` now finds and reuses a sign you've already placed instead of ignoring it.
- **"Refresh Sign" rebuilt to self-heal instead of just calling `refreshShopSign` directly** (new `handleRefreshSignButton`) - for any shop still stuck with `NO_SIGN_LOC` from before this fix (which is every existing shop with a sign that's ever seemed "broken"), it now first tries to acquire a sign (an already-placed adjacent one, then a fresh auto-place attempt - now that auto-place actually works), persists the new `signLoc` if one was found, and only THEN calls `refreshShopSign` - reporting honestly ("No free space next to the chest for a sign...") if it genuinely still can't find one, instead of always claiming success.
- **Any shop that predates this fix needs one of two things to actually get a sign**: run `/shop:setup` again while looking at the chest (now checks for/places a sign as part of resolving the target), or open the shop's own menu and hit "Refresh Sign" (now self-heals). Neither is automatic for an already-existing shop - the fix corrects the mechanism going forward, it doesn't retroactively sweep every past shop on its own.

**Auto-place still didn't create a sign after the 0.9.177 syntax fix (BP 0.9.178) - switched off command strings entirely, onto the real typed Script API, since a silent gap remained even with valid command syntax.** Reported directly: "refreshing the sign works, but I need to manually place it." Investigated rather than guessed a third time - had the user check the Content Log for the `shopAutoPlaceSign: setblock failed` line the 0.9.177 fix added; **nothing was logged at all**, meaning `dim.runCommand` was not throwing. Two things had to be true at once for that result to mean anything conclusive, and only one could be confirmed: the user confirmed the rebuilt pack was actually reimported, but there was no way to confirm from chat alone whether their test actually exercised a chest with zero sign anywhere nearby (the most likely alternate explanation: if a sign already existed next to the chest from earlier manual placement, `shopHasSign`/`shopFindAdjacentSign` would short-circuit before `shopAutoPlaceSign` is ever called at all, which would also produce zero log lines - the exact same, ambiguous "silence" either way).

Rather than keep reasoning about which silent-failure theory was correct, removed the ambiguity structurally: `shopAutoPlaceSign` no longer builds a `setblock` command string at all. It now calls `BlockPermutation.resolve("minecraft:oak_wall_sign", { facing_direction: f.sd })` and `Block.setPermutation(perm)` - the real, typed Script API for this, confirmed directly against Microsoft's current reference (`BlockPermutation.resolve`/`.setPermutation` both documented as "can throw errors," meaning a wrong block/state name or value now surfaces as a specific, catchable, loggable JS error instead of depending on a raw command string's own less legible failure behavior). Also added a read-back check right after `setPermutation` - re-fetches the block at the target location and confirms its `typeId` actually contains "sign" before declaring success, rather than trusting the call silently - the same "don't trust a write path without verifying it stuck" lesson this addon's own 0.9.176 `refreshShopSign` diagnostics already established. A genuine success now also logs an explicit `Log.info` line (`shopAutoPlaceSign: placed a sign at (...), facing ...`) - previously there was no positive confirmation logged anywhere, only ever a failure path, which is exactly what made the prior round's "nothing in the log" result ambiguous instead of conclusive.

Also directly relevant, found via research rather than guessed: Microsoft's own Block States and Traits reference confirms two DIFFERENT states share a similar name - the legacy, non-namespaced `facing_direction` (Integer, 0-5, `0=Down,1=Up,2=North,3=South,4=West,5=East` - what this code has always assumed and still uses) versus a newer, namespaced `minecraft:facing_direction` (String, `down/up/north/south/east/west`) added only to blocks that specifically opt into the newer `minecraft:placement_direction` block trait. This addon's existing direction math (`f.sd` values 2/3/4/5, `nameFromFd` mapping) already matches the legacy integer state's documented values exactly, so the state name/type itself was not changed - only the mechanism used to apply it (typed API instead of a hand-built command string), since that's the part with an actual confirmed history of silent failure in this codebase.

**Stated plainly**: this still hasn't been visually confirmed working in a live client - the read-back check and success log are what will finally make the very next test conclusive one way or the other, regardless of which of the two theories above (still-failing command, or a test that just never exercised the auto-place path) was actually true. If auto-place still doesn't produce a sign after this build, the Content Log will now show either a specific `Log.error` (a real, named failure) or a `Log.info` success line with a location - if it's the latter and no sign is visible at that exact spot in-game, that would point to something else entirely (e.g. a client-side render/chunk-refresh gap) rather than the placement code itself, which is a genuinely different kind of problem to chase next.

**Confirmed BP 0.9.178 still didn't place a sign - and the Content Log came back completely silent again (BP 0.9.179), which is itself the key diagnostic finding, not a dead end.** Reported directly: "No sign, has space on each face. Zero sign made, no error in log." Every single code path inside `shopAutoPlaceSign`'s per-face try block (success, the two failure branches, and the catch) now logs *something* as of 0.9.178 - there is no way to reach that block and produce zero output. Complete silence, for all 4 faces, with the user confirming visible open space around the chest, can only mean one thing: the loop's own precondition (`dim.getBlock(loc)?.typeId === "minecraft:air"`) is rejecting every candidate face before the try block is ever reached - the check itself, not the placement logic, is where this is actually failing now.

Rather than guess at why (uneven terrain sitting one block higher than the chest on some side, decorative foliage like short grass/tall grass/snow layers that read as "empty space" to a player but aren't literally the block id `minecraft:air`, or something else entirely), added ground-truth logging instead of a third blind fix: the loop now records the *actual* `typeId` found at every rejected face, and if all 4 are rejected, logs one final `shopAutoPlaceSign: no free adjacent face at chest (...) — found north=..., south=..., west=..., east=...` line naming exactly what's sitting at each of the 4 candidate positions. Confirmed real `Block.isAir` (Microsoft's own current reference) means exactly the same thing as the existing `typeId === "minecraft:air"` check - not a broader "walkable/passable" concept - so switching to it wouldn't have changed anything; what's actually needed is knowing what block is really there before deciding whether to widen what counts as placeable space (e.g. also permitting short grass/snow layers/etc.) or whether something else entirely (a terrain-height assumption) is the real gap.

**Next step is mechanical, not another guess**: reimport this build, try the same "space on each face" chest again, and check the Content Log for that one line - it will name the exact 4 block types blocking placement, which is what actually decides the next fix instead of another theory.

**Real, confirmed root cause finally obtained via a live error, plus a second, entirely separate deployment problem uncovered in the same log dump (BP 0.9.180).** The user's 0.9.179 test produced a full Content Log for the first time, and it was decisive on both fronts:

1. **`BlockPermutation.resolve("minecraft:oak_wall_sign", { facing_direction: N })` throws "Failed to resolve block ... with properties"** - a real, confirmed error, not a theory. `oak_wall_sign` does not accept the plain legacy `facing_direction` integer state that every previous attempt in this pack's history (including the 0.9.178 rewrite) assumed.
