- **`isTerrainSuitable` replaced with `evaluateTerrainCandidate(dim, cx, cz)`** — builds the existing footprint grid (`TERRAIN_SAMPLE_STEP`, unchanged) plus a new, coarser margin ring beyond the footprint edge (`TERRAIN_MARGIN_BLOCKS`=32, `TERRAIN_MARGIN_STEP`=16) that catches a large adjacent feature the footprint-only grid could never see. Every sample point — footprint or margin — now runs through one unified "bad" definition (island-height overlap, too-high/mountain, too-low/ravine, or ocean biome) applied uniformly, instead of ocean-checking being strict-only. Computes one bad-fraction from one grid walk and returns `{strictOk, lenientOk}` by comparing that single fraction against both `TERRAIN_STRICT_BAD_FRACTION` (loosened 0.10→0.15) and `TERRAIN_LENIENT_BAD_FRACTION` — no second walk, no second force-load. A margin point that throws on `getTopmostBlock` (outside the force-loaded area) is caught and skipped per-point, never aborting the whole candidate — stated honestly as best-effort, not a guarantee, since the ticking-area load radius itself wasn't changed.
- **`forceLoadAndCheckTerrain`** now calls `evaluateTerrainCandidate` once and reports `(strictOk, lenientOk, inconclusive)` to its callback, dropping its old `lenient` parameter entirely.
- **`claimPlot`'s `tryTerrainBatch` replaced with `tryTerrainCombined`** — one combined spiral pass instead of two: searches up to `MAX_TERRAIN_CANDIDATES` (60, now the one shared budget — `MAX_LENIENT_CANDIDATES` deleted, no longer needed) in batches of `TERRAIN_CANDIDATE_BATCH_SIZE` (4, unchanged), but each resolved candidate now yields both verdicts from one force-load. The first strict-passing candidate found (by spiral order) still wins immediately; if the whole budget is exhausted with no strict match, the first lenient-passing candidate seen *along the way* (a `lenientFallback` local, scoped per-claim-attempt via `claimPlot`'s own closure — free data from force-loads already paid for, no extra candidates spent) is used instead. Only if neither ever appeared does it fall through to the existing `claimUnconditionally()` last resort, unchanged. This is what actually fixes the ~1-minute timing: a lenient result no longer requires a second, independent round of fresh chunk generation after strict's full budget is spent.
- **`prescanCell`** updated to call `evaluateTerrainCandidate` once and derive its cached `"S"`/`"L"`/`"R"` verdict from the merged result — cache format/semantics unchanged, so `tryTerrainCombined`'s cache-consult logic (`"S"` satisfies either tier, `"L"` satisfies lenient only, `"R"` satisfies neither) carries over exactly as before.
- **One-time cache migration**: existing `plot_prescan_verdict_<col>_<row>` entries were computed under the OLD criteria (footprint-only, no margin, 10% strict) — reusing them as-is would silently let already-cached cells skip the new margin/ocean checks forever. Folded into the existing `EPITAPH_SCHEMA` migration system (bumped 2→3, `runSchemaMigrations`) rather than a bespoke flag — the `v < 3` block sweeps every `plot_prescan_verdict_*` key via `World.getDynamicPropertyIds()` (the same enumeration technique `getAllKnownPlayers`/`getGroupMembers` already established) and clears them once; affected cells just fall back to a live check or get re-prescanned in the background, identical to a cold cache.
- Historical header comments above `claimPlot` (0.8.32/0.8.34 notes describing the old two-pass design) and `claimUnconditionally`'s log/alert wording were updated to reflect the merged design rather than left referencing a function name (`isTerrainSuitable`) and a two-pass structure that no longer exist.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed after every stage. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.199 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack or satellite changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed (268 entries). In-game verification (no local Bedrock harness to run this from here): claim several new plots and check the Content Log's `sysLog("CLAIM", ...)` lines to confirm strict actually succeeds sometimes now (not "always" falling to lenient), confirm overall wall-clock claim time drops noticeably, and confirm no new claim lands in open ocean or with a large hill/mountain immediately touching the plot edge.

**Strict terrain checks still lost every time on a live test right after 0.9.199 shipped — the actual over-strict setting found via real research, not another fraction bump (BP 0.9.200).** Reported directly, with the Content Log showing a fresh world's very first claim landing on "no strict candidate within budget — using the best lenient match found." 0.9.199 had already loosened `TERRAIN_STRICT_BAD_FRACTION` once (0.10→0.15) without fixing this, so rather than loosen the tolerance percentage again (a blunt lever already shown not to be the real problem), looked up actual Minecraft terrain-height data rather than guessed a second time: confirmed against the [Minecraft Wiki's Windswept Hills page](https://minecraft.wiki/w/Windswept_Hills) that this biome routinely peaks around **Y=140** as its ordinary, ambient shape — not an exceptional spike — and confirmed against the [Minecraft Wiki's Mountains page](https://minecraft.wiki/w/Mountains) that Bedrock's default "Mountains" biome caps around **Y=127**. Both sit at or above `PLOT_Y` (128) as their *normal* terrain height.

- **The real bug: `TERRAIN_CLEARANCE_BLOCKS` (15) rejected any sample point above Y=113** — given the research above, that threshold catches a large share of perfectly ordinary rolling-hills terrain across an entire biome, not specifically "a massive mountain right next to the plot" (the actual reported symptom). Genuine overlap was already caught unconditionally by the separate `y >= PLOT_Y` check sitting right next to it — clearance only ever needed to add a *small* buffer on top of that so an island doesn't end up floating uncomfortably close above an ordinary hill's summit, not reject every hill that merely approaches island height. Dropped `TERRAIN_CLEARANCE_BLOCKS` 15→5 (bad now means y>123, not y>113) — genuine mountain/hill overlap and near-overlap are both still caught (a real Windswept Hills peak at ~140 or a Mountains-biome peak at ~127 both still trip `y >= PLOT_Y` or the new smaller buffer), while ordinary terrain sitting comfortably 6-15 blocks below island height no longer counts against a candidate at all.
- **Added a permanent diagnostic breakdown, not just a fix — so the next report has hard numbers instead of a third guess.** `evaluateTerrainCandidate` now tracks *why* each sample point was bad (`heightBad`/`ravineBad`/`oceanBad`, alongside the existing `sampled`/`badCount`) and returns it; `forceLoadAndCheckTerrain`'s callback gained a 4th, purely additive `diag` argument (existing 3-argument callers are unaffected — JS ignores extra arguments) carrying this breakdown through. `claimPlot`'s `tryTerrainCombined` aggregates it across every live-checked candidate in a claim attempt (`terrainDiagTotals` — cache hits contribute nothing, since a cached verdict has no breakdown to add) and logs it via `sysLog("TERRAIN", ...)` whenever an attempt falls back to a lenient match or bypasses terrain entirely — e.g. "40 sample points bad — height:38 ravine:0 ocean:2." If strict is still losing after this fix, the exact cause is now visible directly in the Content Log rather than requiring another round of research and a blind constant change.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.200 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack or satellite changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed (268 entries). In-game verification (no local Bedrock harness to run this from here): claim several new plots and confirm strict actually wins some of the time now; if it still doesn't, check the Content Log for the new `[PlotSystem | TERRAIN] diag for ...` line, which will name the exact bad-point category driving the rejection.

**Three direct requests landed together (BP 0.9.201): a "Claiming Plot..." title HUD, a hard ocean veto for plot claiming, and real double-chest support for Player Shops.**

**1. "Claiming Plot..." title HUD.** A real, green, centered vanilla title (`§aClaiming Plot...`) now shows on the claiming player's own screen for the whole duration of a claim attempt — `startClaimingTitle`/`stopClaimingTitle` (new, right before `claimPlot`) use non-zero fade/stay durations (unlike WAILA/the Stats board, which deliberately use all-zero durations to suppress vanilla's own title UI and render only through a custom resource-pack panel instead) specifically so this renders through Bedrock's native title system. Kept alive via a `system.runInterval` re-issuing the same text every `CLAIMING_TITLE_REFRESH_TICKS` (10) with a `stayDuration` (16 ticks) comfortably longer than the refresh gap, so it never visibly flickers — the same "re-issue before it fades" idiom already established for WAILA and the Stats board. `stopClaimingTitle` always finishes with one explicit blank, zero-duration write, called from every real exit path (`finalizeClaim`, `noSlots`, the ocean-avoidance last resort below) plus a dedicated `playerLeave` cleanup for a genuine mid-claim disconnect — "never seen afterwards" is enforced at every one of those, not just the success path. `claimPlot(player)` is keyed off whichever `Player` object is doing the claiming regardless of who *triggered* it (self-claim, first-join auto-claim, or an admin's `/plot:claim <name>`), so this correctly shows on the actual claiming player's screen in every case with no call-site changes needed. **Cross-system fix found while wiring this up, not left as a new bug**: WAILA and this title HUD write to the same underlying vanilla title channel — `wailaTick` now skips any player present in `claimingTitleHandles` (the new interval-tracking Map) for the few seconds a claim is in progress, since a freshly-joined player mid-claim has no urgent need for a live look-at readout and the two systems would otherwise visibly fight over the screen.

**2. Plot claiming: a hard, unconditional ocean veto, on top of the existing fractional tolerance.** Direct instruction: plots should ALWAYS refrain from being in the middle of an ocean — the existing fractional bad-point tolerance (strict 15% / lenient 30%, see the 0.9.200 entry above) already catches most large oceans in practice (nearly every sampled point would be ocean), but a footprint that's mostly land with an ocean/bay/pond sitting exactly at the candidate's own *center* would only ever be one bad point out of 370+ sampled — nowhere near failing either tolerance tier, yet that IS "the middle of an ocean," the literal case reported. `evaluateTerrainCandidate` now checks the candidate's exact center biome unconditionally, before the fractional grid walk even runs: if it's ocean, the candidate hard-fails both `strictOk` and `lenientOk` regardless of the rest of the footprint — separate from, and stronger than, the tolerance system. A biome-lookup failure here fails OPEN (matches this function's own established philosophy for every other biome check) rather than vetoing on an inconclusive read. **Extended to the true last-resort tier too**, since otherwise "ALWAYS" would have one real loophole: `claimUnconditionally` (the "every strict and lenient candidate in the budget failed, take literally anything" bypass) is now `tryOceanAvoidingFallback(attemptsLeft)`, a bounded (`OCEAN_AVOIDANCE_MAX_ATTEMPTS` = 20), cheap, synchronous center-biome-only check with no force-load at all — if the chunk isn't loaded the check just throws and fails open (take the cell), so this never becomes a second async wait pipeline; once the bound is exhausted, the very next cell is taken regardless, preserving the existing "a claim can never be a total dead end" guarantee.

**3. Player Shops: real double-chest support, not assumed to already work.** Reported directly as a request to "properly ensure" this, so it was investigated rather than assumed either way: researched Microsoft's own `BlockInventoryComponent` reference directly, which is silent on double-chest behavior — no confirmation either way whether querying either half's own `minecraft:inventory` component already reports the combined 54-slot container, or only that half's independent 27 slots. Rather than guess (this project has been burned by exactly that class of assumption before, most extensively in the sign-placement saga), built to handle both possibilities. New `shopChestContainers(dim, chestLoc)` is now the one shared path every shop container read/write goes through: if the primary chest half already reports 54 slots, it's already the whole double chest and nothing more is read; only if it reports fewer does the function look for a paired chest half and read that too. Pairing is detected via `shopFindPairedChestLoc` — confirmed real vanilla `minecraft:chest` blocks use the `minecraft:cardinal_direction` string state (north/south/east/west), a genuinely different shape than the legacy integer `facing_direction` state signs use elsewhere in this file — and confirmed via the Minecraft Wiki that two chests fuse into a real double chest only when adjacent along the axis *perpendicular* to a **matching** facing direction; matching direction is checked, not just adjacency, since Bedrock explicitly supports two independent single chests sitting right next to each other by giving them different facings, and treating any adjacent chest as automatically "the same shop" would silently merge stock with (and break-protect) a completely unrelated neighboring chest.
- `shopComputeStock`, `executeShopPurchase` (both the stock scan AND the actual sold-item removal — previously read/removed from only the primary half directly, bypassing `shopComputeStock` entirely), and `disassembleShop` (drains and returns a chest item from every physical half, not just the registered one) were all switched onto `shopChestContainers`.
- **A real correctness/protection gap found and fixed in the same pass, not just a stock-display nicety**: `shopIdAtChest` (the lookup break-protection, the sign-close restock notice, and every setup/pricing flow all use to resolve "which shop owns this chest") only ever checked the exact registered `chestLoc` — meaning a griefer (or the owner themselves) breaking the *other*, unregistered half of a double-chest shop was **not protected at all**, since that exact location was never in the `shop_by_chest_` index. Fixed by giving `shopIdAtChest` an optional second `dim` parameter: when the direct lookup misses and a dimension is available, it now also checks whether the location is one half of a registered double chest and resolves through the paired half instead. This is a read-time fix (no write-time dual-registration, no migration needed) — it also correctly and automatically covers a chest that becomes double *after* a shop was already set up (a player placing a second chest later to expand storage), since the pairing is recomputed live on every lookup rather than cached at setup time. All ~10 call sites updated to pass a reachable dimension (`player.dimension`, `event.dimension`, `block.dimension`, or a local `dim`, whichever was already in scope).
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.201 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack or satellite changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed (268 entries). In-game verification (no local Bedrock harness to run this from here): confirm the green title shows and clears cleanly on a fresh claim; claim several plots and confirm none land with an ocean at the exact center; set up a shop on a double chest, stock both halves, confirm `/shop browse` shows the combined stock, a purchase can pull from either half, and breaking either half is blocked identically.

**Java Combat: a real crosshair cooldown bar, recreated from the reference addon's actual technique (BP/RP 0.9.202) — reported directly: "no visual feedback at all," the original had "on-screen full swing cooldowns under the crosshair, accurate, changes with weapon."** The 0.9.198 launch shipped only a plain gray action-bar percentage, deliberately chosen at the time over recreating the reference addon's exact crosshair art. Re-investigated per direct instruction to "check more within the Java PVP addon" rather than guess at what was missing.

- **What the reference addon's HUD actually does, confirmed by re-reading its real script (`hud.js`) rather than trusting the first JSON UI control found.** An initial look at that addon's `hud_screen.json` turned up a `"cooldown_display"` control bound to `"#cooldown_glyph"` that looked like it might be a native engine binding — but it sits in the file surrounded by oddly-named "abcdave"/"test" scaffolding controls, and its own script never references `#cooldown_glyph` or that control anywhere. Concluded (and treated as the working theory, not over-claimed as certain) that it's dead/leftover code, not the real mechanism. The **actual, wired-up** technique: cooldown is tracked with the exact same plain-timestamp approach this addon's own Java Combat module already uses (confirmed — no native Bedrock cooldown API involved at all) — the visual is a **custom bitmap font glyph "flipbook"**: a 33-frame image strip mapped onto unused Unicode Private-Use codepoints (confirmed against the Bedrock Wiki's font docs and an independent community glyph-sheet generator: 16×16 grid, custom cell size, filename convention `font/glyph_XX.png` with codepoints starting at `U+XX00`, no separate JSON registration needed), with one frame character dropped into a `setTitle`/`setActionBar` string every tick based on the current cooldown percentage.
- **Recreated with the same technique, not ported.** New `resource_pack/font/glyph_E5.png` (1024×1024, 16×16 grid, 64px cells, generated via a Pillow script rather than hand-drawn) holds 17 original frames (`U+E500`-`U+E510`, half the reference's 33 for a smaller, more reviewable asset) depicting a Java-style horizontal bar filling left-to-right with charge — a dark semi-transparent track, white fill, thin black outline for contrast against any background. Verified visually before shipping by rendering a composite preview strip of several frames and viewing it directly, not just trusting the generator script ran without error.
- **Wired into `epitaph_hud.json`/`hud_screen.json` using the exact marker-and-panel pattern this addon's own WAILA feature already established (BP 0.9.192)** — proven working infrastructure, extended rather than reinvented. New `JAVA_COMBAT_MARKER` (`main.js`, two Private-Use-Area codepoints `U+E003`/`U+E004` — the next free pair in the same already-blank `glyph_E0.png` sheet `WAILA_MARKER` uses) tags a cooldown-bar write; a new `combat_cooldown_panel` (screen-center anchored, small downward offset so it sits just below the crosshair reticle rather than directly on top of it) captures ONLY marker-tagged text and strips the marker before display, mirroring `waila_panel`'s own structure exactly. The Stats board's own `board_data_control` exclusion filter was extended to also exclude the new marker (previously only excluded `$waila_marker`) so the cooldown bar can't get captured and briefly rendered in the Stats board's top-right corner the way an unmarked title write would. `initJavaCombat`'s cooldown loop now writes `JAVA_COMBAT_MARKER + <frame character>` with all-zero fade/stay/fadeOut durations every 2 ticks (matching WAILA's own "suppress vanilla's native title animation, render only through the custom panel" technique) instead of the old `setActionBarP` percentage call; a new `javaCombatCooldownBarShown` Set ensures the one-time "clear" write (blank, unmarked) fires exactly once when charge crosses back to fully charged, not repeatedly forever at rest.
- **Real, minor cross-system conflict found and closed while wiring this up**: WAILA and the new cooldown bar both write to the same underlying title channel, and a player actively swinging is very likely also looking at their target — exactly when WAILA would otherwise also want to write. `wailaTick` now skips any player present in `javaCombatCooldownBarShown` (added right next to the existing skip for `claimingTitleHandles` from the plot-claiming title work earlier this session), letting combat win the shared channel during a fight rather than the two visibly fighting over it every tick.
- **A real, self-inflicted mistake caught and fixed during implementation, not shipped**: an early draft of the `epitaph_hud.json`/`main.js` edits tried to type the actual invisible Private-Use-Area marker characters directly — this silently produced a genuinely empty string in one case (breaking the *pre-existing, already-shipped* `$waila_marker` value in the same edit) rather than the intended codepoints. Caught by directly inspecting the written file's raw bytes in Python (`[hex(ord(c)) for c in val]`) rather than trusting the file looked right, and fixed by switching to standard JSON/JS `\uXXXX` escape sequences (plain ASCII, unambiguous, verified via `json.loads`/reading the bytes back) for every marker value instead of raw literal characters — safer and fully equivalent, not just a workaround.
- **Stated honestly, not oversold**: this is this addon's first-ever custom font glyph asset — a genuinely new risk category this project hasn't tackled before, and (per this project's own repeated experience with resource-pack-level tricks, most extensively the 0.9.176→0.9.183 sign-placement saga) not something that can be fully trusted without a live client test. Specifically unverified: whether the bar actually renders at the crosshair instead of blank/tofu, whether `font_scale_factor` (currently `2.5`, chosen without being able to see real on-screen output) renders a reasonably-sized bar rather than one that's too small or too large, and whether the Stats board's corner panel stays clean during combat. First things to check after reimporting: swing a weapon and confirm a bar appears under the crosshair, watch it fill smoothly as the weapon recharges, confirm it disappears entirely once fully charged, and confirm the Stats board's own corner readout is undisturbed throughout.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; both RP UI JSON files re-validated with `json.loads` after every edit. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.202 (header + both modules); RP manifest bumped 0.9.17→0.9.18 (header) / 0.9.13→0.9.14 (module) — first RP change since the WAILA panel work — and the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match (0.9.17→0.9.18), per this project's own standing rule about stale dependency pins silently breaking re-import. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed, 269 entries (268 + the one new `glyph_E5.png` file).

**Java Combat cooldown bar: the REAL glyph system, ported directly from the reference addon rather than recreated (BP/RP 0.9.203)** — on direct instruction: "take the cooldown/glyph system from the original pack, but edit the code a bit to suit our codebase and needs." The 0.9.202 pass had already reverse-engineered the *technique* (a custom bitmap font glyph flipbook dropped into the title channel) but hand-drew its own 17-frame bar rather than reusing the original's actual, already-polished art.

- **Ported the real asset.** `resource_pack/font/glyph_E5.png` (the hand-drawn 0.9.202 sheet) was deleted; `resource_pack/font/glyph_E9.png` — a verbatim copy of Blurry's Java PvP's own glyph sheet — was added instead. Confirmed its real dimensions before trusting the copy (512×512, 16×16 grid, 32px cells — different from the 64px cells the 0.9.202 sheet used) and rendered a cropped, upscaled preview strip of several actual frames to visually confirm it's genuinely a left-to-right cooldown bar before wiring anything to it, rather than assuming the copy was correct.
- **Ported the real frame data, not retyped by hand.** `JAVA_COMBAT_COOLDOWN_GLYPHS` (`main.js`) is the reference addon's own `glyphMap` array — its 32 real codepoints (page E9: `0xE910`, `0xE920`, `0xE911`, `0xE921`, ... interleaved exactly as the original defined them) extracted by reading `hud.js`'s raw bytes directly in Python (`[hex(ord(c)) for c in ...]`), then written into `main.js` as plain numeric literals rather than retyped Unicode characters — deliberately avoiding the exact mistake made once already this session (see below).
- **Adapted, not ported verbatim, per the instruction to fit this codebase and its needs:**
  - The original's second glyph map (`entityGlyphMap`, swapped in when a target is in view via its own bespoke view-cone entity scan) was **not** ported — this addon's WAILA module already does a more precise version of "what is the player looking at" for a different purpose, and a second, looser entity-scan just for a cosmetic glyph swap wasn't judged worth the extra per-tick cost for every fighting player.
  - **A real, narrow bug found while porting, fixed rather than reproduced.** The original computes `round(percent/100*32)` clamped to a *maximum* of 32 — but its own `glyphMap` only has 32 entries (indices 0-31), so any charge rounding up to exactly index 32 (roughly 98.4%+, just before the separate "fully charged, hide entirely" branch takes over) silently looked up `undefined` and fell back to a blank title for a brief instant right before completion. `javaCombatCooldownFrameIndex` now clamps to 31, the real array bound.
  - **Cadence matched to the original's real behavior, once actually checked rather than assumed**: confirmed the original's `system.runInterval` call in `hud.js` has no period argument at all (fires every tick), so `initJavaCombat`'s cooldown loop was switched from the 0.9.202 pass's 2-tick throttle to a real per-tick refresh, while keeping this module's own more efficient iteration (only players with a live `javaCombatCooldown` entry, never a scan of every online player the way the original's own loop does).
  - **A write-throttle the original didn't have, added on top**: `javaCombatCooldownLastFrame` (renamed from the 0.9.202 pass's `javaCombatCooldownBarShown` Set — now a Map that also caches the last-written frame index) skips the `setTitle` call entirely on any tick where the frame index hasn't actually changed, rather than writing every single tick regardless like the original does.
- **A second instance of the exact same self-inflicted mistake from the 0.9.202 pass, caught before it shipped this time.** Editing the large comment block ahead of `JAVA_COMBAT_MARKER` via the normal Edit tool repeatedly failed to match — traced to the same root cause as before (typing invisible Private-Use-Area characters by hand is unreliable and had already silently corrupted a marker value once this session). Rather than keep fighting it, switched approach entirely: used a small Python script to locate safe, plain-ASCII anchor text on either side of the risky line and splice the new content in directly, verifying the untouched `JAVA_COMBAT_MARKER` line's actual codepoints were still intact via the same raw-byte inspection technique afterward. No hand-typed invisible characters were needed anywhere in this pass — every codepoint in `JAVA_COMBAT_COOLDOWN_GLYPHS` is a plain hex number, immune to this whole class of mistake.
- **`font_scale_factor` adjusted from `2.5` to `3.5`** for the new, smaller 32px-cell source (the 0.9.202 value was tuned, without a live test, against the old 64px-cell sheet) — a reasoned compensating adjustment, not a confirmed-correct value; still flagged as needing an in-game check like everything else about this feature's on-screen sizing.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; `epitaph_hud.json` re-validated with `json.loads` after the `font_scale_factor` change. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.203 (header + both modules); RP manifest bumped 0.9.18→0.9.19 (header) / 0.9.14→0.9.15 (module) and the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed, 269 entries (glyph_E5.png removed, glyph_E9.png added — net unchanged from the 0.9.202 count). Stated honestly: reusing the reference addon's own proven art removes the "is this custom sheet valid at all" risk the 0.9.202 pass carried, but the on-screen SIZE, crosshair positioning, and Stats-board isolation are all still unverified in a live client — same first-things-to-check list as the 0.9.202 entry above applies here too.

**Cooldown bar: fixed for real, both reported bugs (too large; only ever showed once) traced back to picking the wrong JSON UI pattern for this specific case, and to a guessed size never checked live (BP/RP 0.9.204)** — reported directly after actually testing 0.9.203 in-game: "the glyph is too large, and ONLY appears for the first time once I hit something," with a direct instruction to go back and check how the original pack itself handled both. Re-read the reference addon's `hud_screen.json` a second time, more carefully this time, specifically for how it displays its OWN cooldown glyph through the title channel — and found it does NOT use the manual "preserved_text + visibility_changed" capture pattern this addon's `combat_cooldown_panel` had copied from WAILA at all. It uses a completely different, simpler, real Bedrock mechanism: a `hud_title_text_factory` registration (`abcdave_factory` in the reference addon's own file), which hands a fresh `$title_text` variable to its child control on every single `setTitle()` call — no manually-preserved snapshot, no state that can go stale.

- **Root cause of "only shows once," reasoned through rather than left as an unexplained mystery**: the WAILA-style pattern only re-captures `#preserved_text` at the exact moment its own `#visible` property *transitions* (via `binding_condition: "visibility_changed"`). Once the cooldown bar's marker-tagged title is visible and STAYS visible across many rapid, differently-charged writes in the same combat session (which is exactly what a smoothly filling bar does — write after write, all marked, never reverting to unmarked in between), `#visible` never flips back to false and back to true again, so the "visibility_changed" capture never re-fires — the panel freezes on whatever the very first captured frame was. This is a real structural mismatch between the pattern (built for "occasionally show/hide a fixed value," which is exactly what WAILA's own signature-dedup'd info display and the Stats board's slower refresh cycle both are) and this specific use case (a value that changes on nearly every single write while staying continuously visible) — not a bug in WAILA's own already-shipped behavior, which was never exposed to this failure mode in the first place.
- **Fixed by switching to the reference addon's own real mechanism**: `combat_cooldown_panel` (the old preserved-text-based control) was removed entirely and replaced with `combat_cooldown_display` + `combat_cooldown_factory` — a `hud_title_text_factory` registration mirroring the reference addon's `abcdave`/`abcdave_factory` pair exactly, mirrored down to using the same prefix-slice-and-compare visibility check style (`(('%.2s' * $title_text) = $combat_marker)`, matching the reference's own `('%.13s' * $title_text) = 'notificatiom.'` pattern) instead of the subtraction-based marker check WAILA's pattern used. Every `setTitle()` call now drives a genuinely fresh display with zero staleness risk, by construction — not by another guess. `hud_screen.json`'s `root_panel` insertion was updated to reference the new factory control. No `main.js` changes were needed for this half of the fix — `initJavaCombat`'s loop already calls plain `setTitle()`, exactly the same call the factory mechanism is built to pick up.
- **Fixed the size using the reference addon's own real, already-tuned value instead of guessing again**: its `abcdave_promo_text` control (the one that actually displays marked title content, including its own cooldown glyph) uses `font_scale_factor: 0.5` — not derived or guessed, read directly off the file. The first pass's own `3.5` had been chosen blindly (compensating for the glyph sheet's cell-size difference with no way to check the result), which is exactly what produced an oversized bar. Set to `0.5`, matching the reference addon's own proven value for displaying this same glyph sheet through the title channel.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; both RP UI JSON files re-validated with `json.loads` after every edit. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.204 (header + both modules); RP manifest bumped 0.9.19→0.9.20 (header) / 0.9.15→0.9.16 (module) and the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed, 269 entries (no asset count change — this was a pure logic/wiring fix, no new files). In-game verification is still the user's to run next: confirm the bar now updates continuously through a full swing cycle (not just once), confirm it's a reasonable size at `font_scale_factor: 0.5` (adjust further if still off), and re-confirm the Stats board's own corner panel still stays clean during combat now that the cooldown panel's underlying mechanism has changed.

**Java Combat Module rebuilt end to end to match Blurry's Java PvP's REAL mechanics, following a full stop-and-analyze pass (BP 0.9.205)** — on direct instruction: "Stop. Analyze the entire system from Blurry's PVP. As we are currently failing in replicating/migrating systems." Every file actually wired into that addon's own `main.js` import list was read in full this time (`system.js`, `itemDamages.js`, `itemSystem.js`, `shield.js`, `config.js` — not just `hud.js`, which every prior pass had focused on) and compared line-by-line against this module's own numbers/logic. Findings written to `Java-PvP-System-Analysis.md` at the addon root; the decisions below were made directly by the user in response to that analysis, not assumed.

- **Confirmed architecture was already correct, unchanged**: Blurry's addon works by silently swapping every vanilla sword/axe for a custom `bf:`-namespaced reskin (so vanilla's own automatic damage is negligible) and layering fully custom damage on top via `world.afterEvents.entityHitEntity`. Epitaph deliberately never adopted custom items, so `world.beforeEvents.entityHurt` + `e.cancel = true` + one deferred `applyDamage()` remains the correct adaptation for real vanilla items — confirmed, not changed.
- **Damage table replaced with Blurry's real in-combat numbers** (user's explicit choice, over the authentic-Java values this module had before): `JAVA_COMBAT_BASE_DAMAGE` now matches `itemDamages.js` exactly — sword/axe values are Blurry's own (consistently 1 higher than real Java; the analysis found Blurry's OWN addon disagrees with itself here, its lore text shows the real-Java number but its actual combat code deals one more — the user chose to match what actually executes in combat, bug or not). Pickaxe/shovel/hoe were never part of that sword/axe swap-and-relabel system in Blurry's addon, so their numbers were never in question — hoe is now tiered by material (it wasn't before). Trident is the one deliberate exception: Blurry's `itemDamages.js` defines no trident entry at all (silently falls through to a bare-hands default of 1 there) — judged an unintended gap, not a real design choice, so it's kept at the authentic Java value (8) rather than replicating an apparent oversight that would make tridents nearly useless.
- **Damage-scale curve replaced with Blurry's real 21-point piecewise-linear table** (`JAVA_COMBAT_SCALE_THRESHOLDS`/`OUTPUTS`, ported directly from `system.js`'s `getCooldownDamageScale`, re-expressed in 0-1 charge terms), plus the three-way charge gate that table only ever partially reaches in practice: below 60% charge a swing is now completely silent (no damage, no knockback, no sound, no particle — matching Blurry's real "dam" tag mechanism); 60%-70% plays hit sound/particle feedback but still deals zero damage/knockback (matching Blurry's separate, more lenient gate); 70%+ is a full hit. Mace never gets the fully-silent treatment (Blurry's own mace branch always plays feedback, at any charge) but still deals no real damage below 70% either way — mace's own fall-smash mechanic remains deliberately un-recreated, per the original scoping decision.
- **Critical hit condition fixed, not just re-matched to Blurry** — this was flagged in the analysis as likely a genuine bug regardless of any porting decision, and fixed on that basis: the old `charge >= 0.9` requirement is gone entirely. A crit is now purely `(!onGround && isFalling && !inWater) || (inWater && !isSprinting)`, matching both Blurry's real code and real Java Edition's own actual crit condition (falling-state only — charge is a separate, independent multiplier a crit still gets scaled by, not a gate on whether a crit can happen at all).
- **Knockback rewritten to Blurry's real three-tier, armor-aware system** (user's explicit choice): a hit is either "normal" or a "knockback hit" (sprinting OR wielding any axe, regardless of sprint state — axes always count now, a real distinct mechanic this module never had), each with its own netherite-armor-dampened variant (3+ of 4 armor pieces netherite: more horizontal, less vertical knockback) via new `javaCombatIsNetheriteArmored`. The specific numbers adopted are Blurry's own "Java Knockback Hit" toggle values, taken as this module's permanent baseline since Java Combat is always-on here rather than an opt-in flavor on top of default combat the way that toggle is in Blurry's own addon. Airborne player targets now take half knockback (Blurry's own real rule, not previously implemented). One simplification made explicitly rather than silently: Blurry's own code makes crit and sprint-knockback mutually exclusive via a persisted per-tick sprint-transition tracker specific to its implementation — this module computes both conditions independently, so a sprinting, falling hit now gets both the crit multiplier and the knockback-hit tier rather than one overriding the other.
- **Sweep splash rewritten to Blurry's real flat numbers** — previously scaled at 40% of the primary hit's own damage; now a random 2.5-7.5 damage and fixed 0.6 horizontal / 0.28 vertical knockback per nearby entity, matching `system.js`'s own literal constants, independent of whatever the primary target's hit rolled.
- **Per-weapon cooldown durations adopted from Blurry's real `tagCooldownMap`**: axe now splits wooden (1.2s) / stone (1.1s) / everything else (1.0s flat) instead of the old wooden+stone-both-1.25s split; pickaxe/shovel/hoe are now all flat 0.8s (previously three different values); trident is now 1.2s (was 0.9s). Sword (0.625s) and mace (1.4s) were already correct and are unchanged. A Haste/Mining-Fatigue cooldown-duration modifier was also added (`startJavaCombatCooldown`, matching `handleCooldowns`'s real formula) — flagged honestly in its own comment as Blurry's own design choice, not a real vanilla Java mechanic (Haste/Fatigue affect block-breaking speed in real Java, not attack speed), ported for fidelity to "the original pack" rather than claimed as authentic.
- **Two entirely new mechanics added, both explicitly requested**: a weapon-switch cooldown reset (new `javaCombatLastHeldItem` Map + its own coarser-cadence tick loop scanning every online player's held item, not just those already mid-cooldown, since a fully-charged player switching weapons still needs their charge reset for the new weapon — stops tabbing a fast weapon at 100% into an instant full-charge heavy hit) and an always-on anti-reach check (new `JAVA_COMBAT_ANTI_REACH_DISTANCE_SQ`, Blurry's own real ~3.52-block threshold — voids a hit entirely if attacker/victim are calculated too far apart, though the swing still resets the cooldown timer, matching Blurry's own unconditional-`activateCooldown` behavior for every blocked tier). The full shield block/break subsystem found in the analysis (`shield.js` — custom shield items, entity component-group events, animations, scoreboards) was confirmed correctly out of scope and not built, per the original "drop complex things" instruction.
- **Visual feedback: the entity-in-view dual glyph map, explicitly requested ("Entire Visual feedback migration, exactly as is within Blurry's") after being deliberately skipped in the 0.9.203 pass.** New `JAVA_COMBAT_ENTITY_GLYPHS` (codepoints `0xe970`-`0xe98f`, same already-copied `glyph_E9.png` sheet, extracted the same safe numeric-literal way as the cooldown bar's own glyph array) and `javaCombatEntityInView(player)` — a direct port of `hud.js`'s real `getEntityInView`, its own bespoke "narrow tube along the view direction" cone check (dot-product projection against a small perpendicular radius, 0.5 horizontal / 2.0 vertical, out to 4 blocks), not WAILA's precise reticle raycast, since it's a genuinely different, more forgiving combat-relevant question with its own real algorithm worth reproducing faithfully rather than substituting. The cooldown-bar display loop now calls this every tick (for players already mid-cooldown, same efficient iteration as before) and swaps to `JAVA_COMBAT_ENTITY_GLYPHS` instead of `JAVA_COMBAT_COOLDOWN_GLYPHS` when a target is in view — a real, confirmed correctness fix was needed here too: the existing dedup cache only ever tracked frame index, but the displayed glyph also depends on entity-in-view state (the same frame index looks different between the two arrays), so the dedup key was widened to `frameIndex * 2 + (entityInView ? 1 : 0)` to avoid skipping a real, needed write when only the view-target state changes at an otherwise-unchanged frame.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed after every stage of this rewrite (built via several Python-scripted anchor-based patches rather than the Edit tool directly, both to handle the sheer size of the change and to avoid the invisible-marker-character matching issue this file has already hit twice this session). Confirmed no stale references remain to any removed constant (`JAVA_COMBAT_CRIT_MIN_CHARGE`, the old flat knockback constants, the old percentage-of-primary sweep-damage constant). BP manifest bumped to 0.9.205 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes this pass (reuses the already-copied `glyph_E9.png`, just new codepoint references into the same sheet). `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed, 269 entries (unchanged from 0.9.204 — no new assets). In-game verification is still the user's to run next: confirm hits below 60% charge are genuinely silent, 60-70% gives feedback with no damage, sweep/crit/knockback-hit all still trigger correctly and can now co-occur (sprinting+falling), netherite-armored targets take the adjusted knockback, switching weapons mid-cooldown visibly resets the bar, hits from ~3.5+ blocks away are voided, and the entity-in-view glyph swap actually changes the bar's appearance when aiming at a target mid-swing.

**Cooldown bar: found and fixed the real reason ZERO glyph ever appeared — a structural mismatch with the factory pattern's actual required shape (BP/RP 0.9.206).** Reported directly after testing 0.9.205 in a live client: "Zero on-screen cooldown glyph appears," alongside a Content Log confirming the underlying combat mechanic itself was working fine (`applyKnockback: using the 2-argument object form` — proof a real hit resolved and `startJavaCombatCooldown` ran). So the mechanic was firing; only the display was blank — pointing squarely at `epitaph_hud.json`'s `combat_cooldown_display` control, not `main.js`.

- **Root cause, found by fetching a genuine third reference point rather than re-guessing**: pulled the actual vanilla `hud_screen.json` (bedrock-dot-dev's public stable-pack mirror) to see how Mojang's own `hud_title_text_factory` registration is really shaped, since only two examples had been checked before (the reference addon's own, and this file's first attempt at copying it). Vanilla's own factory-driven `hud_title_text` control is a `stack_panel` containing nested frame panels with the actual label(s) inside — **not a bare label directly targeted by the factory**. Re-examined the reference addon's own `abcdave`/`abcdave_factory` pair with this in mind and confirmed the same shape there too: `abcdave` is a `panel` (the actual factory target), containing a nested child label `abcdave_promo_text` that carries the real `$title_text`/`visible`/`text` logic. This file's own `combat_cooldown_display` — the 0.9.204 fix — skipped that wrapper entirely and made the factory target a **bare label directly**, the one structural detail that differed from both known-working examples. Whether the factory silently fails to populate `$title_text` on a directly-targeted label, or something else entirely, wasn't confirmed by direct testing of the flattened version specifically (there was no live-tested "bare label" example anywhere to compare against, which is exactly the gap here) — but matching the two real, confirmed-working shapes exactly is the fix, not another guess at a third shape.
- **Fixed by restructuring to the real shape**: `combat_cooldown_display` is now a `panel` (the actual factory target, matching `combat_cooldown_factory`'s `control_ids` unchanged), containing one nested child label `combat_cooldown_text` that carries everything the control used to have directly — `$combat_marker`, `$title_text`, the `visible` prefix-check, the `text` extraction, `font_scale_factor: 0.5`, the screen-center-ish positioning. No `main.js` changes were needed — the underlying `setTitle()` calls were always correct, only the JSON UI shape receiving them was wrong.
- **Verification**: both RP UI JSON files re-validated with `json.loads` after the restructure; `node --check` passed on `main.js` (unchanged this pass, confirmed via a sanity re-run since the fix is RP-only). BP manifest bumped to 0.9.206 (header + both modules, no script changes) and RP manifest bumped 0.9.20→0.9.21 (header) / 0.9.16→0.9.17 (module), with the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed, 269 entries (unchanged — pure JSON restructure, no new assets). In-game verification is still the user's to run next: confirm a glyph now actually renders under the crosshair during a swing, at a reasonable size, and that it still animates/clears/swaps to the entity-in-view variant correctly.

**Cooldown bar: still zero glyph after 0.9.206 — found and fixed a second, more subtle structural difference from Blurry's real code, plus added a permanent diagnostic since this display path has now failed twice for non-obvious reasons (BP/RP 0.9.207).** Reported directly: "That still fails. Check blurry." — re-examined the reference addon's files a third time, this time going further than the scripts: checked the actual custom sword/axe item JSON definitions (`items/sword/*.json`, `items/axe/*.json`) to rule out one live theory (that `hud_screen.json`'s own unreferenced `"cooldown_display"`/`"#cooldown_glyph"` control — dismissed as dead scaffolding twice already — might actually be driven by a genuine `minecraft:cooldown` item component on those custom weapons). Confirmed it isn't: every custom sword/axe has `"minecraft:damage": 0` (confirms vanilla damage really is suppressed, as already established) but **no `minecraft:cooldown` component anywhere** — so that control really does have nothing behind it; the glyphMap-in-title-string mechanism (already ported) remains the one real, confirmed-executing display path.

- **The actual, more subtle difference found this pass**: comparing `epitaph_hud.json`'s `combat_cooldown_text` against the reference addon's `abcdave_promo_text` property-by-property (not just structurally, which was already fixed in 0.9.206) turned up one remaining mismatch — the reference's own `visible` check compares against a **literal quoted string constant** embedded directly in the Molang expression (`('%.13s' * $title_text) = 'notificatiom.'`), never a `$`-variable, for the marker comparison specifically. This file's own version used a `$combat_marker` **variable** for that same comparison (`= $combat_marker`) instead of a literal — a difference that seemed immaterial in principle (Molang variables and literals should compare equivalently) but had never actually been tested in isolation anywhere, since every other confirmed-working example of this exact factory pattern (vanilla's own, and the reference addon's) only ever uses a literal for this specific check. Changed to match exactly: `visible` now reads `(('%.2s' * $title_text) = '')` — the marker embedded as a literal (safe `\u` JSON escape, not hand-typed characters), not a variable. The `$combat_marker` variable declaration was removed from this control entirely (it's still declared separately, unaffected, on the unrelated Stats-board exclusion filter in `board_panel`, which uses a different binding mechanism — `#`-bound view bindings, not factory-supplied `$title_text` — and was not touched, since it's a different context with no evidence of the same problem).
- **A permanent diagnostic was added this time rather than another silent fix-and-hope, since this exact display path has now failed for two different non-obvious reasons in a row**: `initJavaCombat`'s cooldown-bar loop now logs once (`javaCombatCooldownBarLogged`, mirroring the established one-time-log pattern `applyCombatKnockback`'s own shape-detection already uses), naming the exact glyph codepoint and marker length actually sent through `setTitle()`, the moment the very first write happens. If the glyph still doesn't render after this fix, the Content Log will now directly confirm whether `main.js` is sending exactly the right data (in which case the remaining problem is provably JSON-UI/display-side, not the script) or whether the write itself is silently throwing (now logged explicitly, previously swallowed by a bare `catch (_) {}`).
- **Verification**: both RP UI JSON files re-validated with `json.loads`; `node --check` passed on `main.js`. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.207 (header + both modules); RP manifest bumped 0.9.21→0.9.22 (header) / 0.9.17→0.9.18 (module) and the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed, 269 entries. Stated honestly: this is the second fix attempt for the same symptom, so it's flagged plainly rather than confidently declared solved — if a glyph still doesn't appear after this build, the new one-time Content Log line (`[PlotSystem... | COMBAT] cooldown bar: first write sent, glyph codepoint ...`) is the concrete next data point to check, and its presence or absence narrows the remaining search space considerably either way.

**The actual root cause, finally found (BP/RP 0.9.208): `resource_pack/ui/_ui_defs.json` never listed `hud_screen.json` at all.** Reported directly: "It still fails crazy. We need literal code ripping at this point. Also, check to see if any other parts of our addon/rp is causing issues" — alongside the 0.9.207 diagnostic's own Content Log output, which read `cooldown bar: first write sent, glyph codepoint 0xe911, marker length 2` — **conclusive proof `main.js` was sending exactly the right data, every single time**, meaning three consecutive rounds of fixing `epitaph_hud.json`'s internal JSON UI structure could never have mattered, no matter how correct each one actually was.

- **Followed the "check any other parts" instruction literally, and found it fast**: `_ui_defs.json` is Bedrock's own real manifest of which UI namespace files actually get loaded and merged into the engine's UI tree. It listed `ui/epitaph_hud.json`, `ui/epitaph_java_chat.json`, and `ui/chat_screen.json` — **but never `ui/hud_screen.json`**, the one file whose entire job is inserting this addon's controls (`epitaph_board_panel`, `epitaph_waila_panel`, and now the combat glyph's own factory) into the real, vanilla `root_panel` via the `modifications`/`insert_front` mechanism. If a UI file is never listed here, none of its contents — however correct — are ever parsed or attached to anything. This explains the symptom completely and unconditionally: every one of the last three fix attempts changed the *content* of a control that was never actually wired into the visible UI tree at all, regardless of content.
- **This is a genuinely alarming, wider-reaching finding, stated plainly rather than downplayed**: since `hud_screen.json` was never loaded, this means the Stats board (`epitaph_board_panel`) and WAILA (`epitaph_waila_panel`) — both previously believed shipped and working, going back to BP 0.9.192 for WAILA and considerably earlier for the Stats board — **may never have actually been rendering either**, through this exact same gap, for as long as `_ui_defs.json` has been missing this entry (not confirmed how far back that goes, since this file wasn't audited before now). This wasn't something introduced by the Java Combat work — it was a pre-existing gap in the addon's own resource pack that Java Combat's new display simply became the first thing to surface, by being the first feature whose failure was investigated this thoroughly.
- **On direct instruction ("we need literal code ripping"), also replaced the hand-reconstructed `epitaph_hud.json` combat display with a genuine, property-for-property verbatim copy of the reference addon's own `abcdave`/`abcdave_factory` pair** (renamed `epitaph_javacombat_display`/`epitaph_javacombat_factory` to avoid namespace collision), using Blurry's own real literal marker string `"notificatiom."` instead of this file's own invented Private-Use-Area codepoint pair. This has a real, independent benefit beyond fidelity: a plain ASCII marker is trivially safe to type, read, and verify — removing the entire class of invisible-character mismatch that has already corrupted an edit at least twice this session. `main.js`'s `JAVA_COMBAT_MARKER` was updated to match (`"notificatiom."`), and `board_panel`'s own Stats-board exclusion filter's `$combat_marker` variable now holds the same literal string instead of PUA codepoints (its structure and the rest of its logic, including the still-separate `$waila_marker`, were left untouched).
- **Sanity-swept the rest of the addon per the direct instruction to check for other issues**: every JSON file in both the resource pack (10 files) and behavior pack (55 files) was re-parsed (comment-stripped, matching JSON UI's comment-tolerant format) and confirmed valid; both font sheets (`glyph_E0.png`, `glyph_E9.png`) were re-verified as loadable, correctly-dimensioned images; every other RP index-style file (`textures/item_texture.json`, `textures/terrain_texture.json`, `texts/languages.json`) was confirmed present and consistent with what's actually on disk. No other gap of this kind was found.
- **Verification**: both RP UI JSON files (`epitaph_hud.json`, `_ui_defs.json`) re-validated with `json.loads`; `node --check` passed on `main.js`. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.208 (header + both modules); RP manifest bumped 0.9.22→0.9.23 (header) / 0.9.18→0.9.19 (module) and the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed, 269 entries — and this time, the built `.mcaddon` was directly re-opened and `_ui_defs.json`'s actual packaged content was read back out and confirmed to contain the fix, rather than trusting the source file alone. In-game verification is still the user's to run next, but this is the first fix in this whole saga backed by a confirmed, mechanical explanation for why every previous attempt could not have worked, rather than another plausible-sounding theory.

**Real sweep particle/sound feedback added — this module never had any at all (BP/RP 0.9.209).** Reported directly: "The Sweeping Edge effects doesn't appear when swinging multiple entities." The sweep mechanic itself (splash damage + knockback to nearby entities on a clean, non-critical, non-knockback-hit sword swing) has worked since the 0.9.205 Blurry-fidelity rewrite — but `resolveJavaCombatHit`'s sweep block only ever applied damage/knockback in a bare loop, with zero particle or sound of any kind. Re-read Blurry's own `system.js` (`handleHit`) specifically for this: on every qualifying sword swing it spawns a custom `dave:sweep` particle at a position offset in front of the attacker (not the victim) and broadcasts a custom "sweep" sound — unconditionally, whether or not anything else was actually in range, matching real Java's own sweep-attack animation (which always plays on a qualifying swing, not only when it connects with extra targets).

- **Ported the real assets, not recreated**: `resource_pack/particles/epitaph_sweep.json` (Blurry's own `particles/sweep.json`, renamed to the `epitaph:sweep` identifier and `textures/particle/epitaph_sweep` texture path — the flipbook component config itself copied verbatim, since it was already correct and proven) and `resource_pack/textures/particle/epitaph_sweep.png` (a byte-for-byte copy of Blurry's real sweep flipbook texture, confirmed a valid 256×16 PNG via `file` before wiring anything to it). `resource_pack/sounds/sound_definitions.json` is a **new file** for this pack (no sound definitions existed anywhere in the RP before this) defining `epitaph_sweep` with the same 7 randomized variants Blurry's own "sweep" sound event uses, backed by `resource_pack/sounds/sweeping_edge/sweep1.ogg`-`sweep7.ogg` (also ported verbatim).
- **`main.js`**: two new constants (`JAVA_COMBAT_SWEEP_PARTICLE`, `JAVA_COMBAT_SWEEP_SOUND`) right next to the existing sweep-splash constants; the sweep block in `resolveJavaCombatHit` now spawns the particle (`dim.spawnParticle`, position computed from the already-in-scope `dirX`/`dirZ` swing direction, matching Blurry's own offset formula exactly) and plays the sound (`Dimension.playSound` — a real, stable, documented method, chosen over a raw `runCommand` string given this file's own repeated history of silent command-string failures elsewhere) **unconditionally** the moment the sweep condition is met, before the nearby-entity loop runs — so it now fires on every qualifying swing exactly like Blurry's and real Java's own sweep-attack animation, not just when a second entity happens to be caught in the splash.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed on `main.js`; both new/changed RP JSON files (`epitaph_sweep.json`, `sound_definitions.json`) validated with `json.loads`. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.209 (header + both modules); RP manifest bumped 0.9.23→0.9.24 (header) / 0.9.19→0.9.20 (module) and the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match — first RP asset addition since the 0.9.203 glyph-sheet port. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed, 279 entries (269 + the 10 new sweep-related files: 1 particle JSON, 1 sound-definitions JSON, 7 `.ogg` variants, 1 texture). In-game verification is still the user's to run next: swing a sword into empty air or at a single target and confirm the sweep particle/sound now plays on a clean, non-critical, non-sprinting hit, and confirm it doesn't fire on a critical or knockback-hit swing.

**Sweep firing on every single hit, not just deliberate full-charge swings — a real, confirmed divergence from Java (BP 0.9.210).** Asked directly: "Is there anything making our sweeping edge different to Java? It seems to happen on every single hit." Yes — real Java's sweep attack only triggers on a near-fully-charged swing (attack cooldown >= ~90%) while the attacker is on the ground; this module's sweep condition (`info.category === "sword" && !isCritical && !isKnockbackHit`, ported at 0.9.205 from Blurry's own `handleHit`) never checked charge at all. Since a "full hit" in this module starts at 70% charge, every non-critical, non-sprinting swing from 70%-100% charge swept — which reads as "every single hit" for anyone not spam-clicking. Confirmed this isn't a porting mistake on this addon's part specifically: Blurry's own real code has the identical gap (its `handleHit` sweep branch is gated only on `hasTag("is_sword") && !isCritical && !isKnockBack`, no charge check anywhere) — this is a deliberate departure from the reference addon to match actual Java behavior instead, not another Blurry-fidelity port.

- **Fixed** with a new `JAVA_COMBAT_SWEEP_MIN_CHARGE` (0.9) added to the sweep condition, plus a fresh `attacker.isOnGround` check (`attackerOnGround`) — both gates real Java applies that neither this module nor Blurry's own code had. **Stated honestly, not oversold**: real Java also requires the attacker to be genuinely stationary (no horizontal movement that exact tick) — not replicated here, since it would need its own per-tick position-delta tracker with no existing infrastructure in this file to reuse, and the charge+ground-state fix already closes the actual reported symptom ("every single hit"). Flagged as a known, accepted simplification rather than silently dropped.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP-only change (no resource-pack/satellite files touched) — manifest bumped to 0.9.210 (header + both modules). `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed, 279 entries (unchanged — no new assets). In-game verification is still the user's to run next: confirm sweep now only triggers on a slow, deliberate, fully-charged, stationary-ish swing, and no longer on a quick, repeated non-critical hit sequence.

**WAILA: favor entities through foliage/liquid, plus a real staleness bug fixed — the frozen "0 HP after it's gone" symptom traced to the Java Combat cooldown skip, not a scan-cadence lag (BP 0.9.211).** Two direct requests: "favor entities, so you can focus them through Tall grass, Sugar cane and water," and "it updates late sometimes which means the entity is dead, but it only shows 0 hp after its gone."

- **Entity-favoring fix**: `wailaScan`'s precise ray (`getEntitiesFromViewDirection`) is a pixel-perfect check — real, tall grass/sugar cane's thin visual sprite doesn't match where their actual block-space sits, and water's surface can throw off precise aim, both making the exact ray easy to miss even when a player is clearly looking at a mob. Added a forgiving cone fallback (`wailaFindEntityInCone`) between the precise ray and the block scan — the identical "narrow tube along view direction" technique Java Combat's own `javaCombatEntityInView` already uses (dot-product projection, 0.6 horizontal / 1.2 vertical tolerance), but returning the single CLOSEST candidate rather than a bare true/false. A new `wailaEntityOccluded` helper keeps this from also seeing through real solid walls: it casts `Dimension.getBlockFromRay` (the same underlying raycast `Entity.getBlockFromViewDirection` wraps, just callable with an arbitrary origin/direction instead of only the player's own current look angle) with the identical `includeLiquidBlocks:false`/`includePassableBlocks:false` options this file's own block-scan branch already relies on — tall grass, sugar cane, and water are all transparent to it, while a real solid block still registers short of the target and blocks the candidate. Stated honestly: this call shape hasn't been visually confirmed live, so it fails open (never occluded) if it doesn't behave as expected — an unconfirmed technicality shouldn't hide a real entity, worst case it's exactly as forgiving as Java Combat's own cone (which has no occlusion check at all).
- **Staleness fix, root-caused rather than just tightened**: the actual bug wasn't scan-cadence lag (WAILA already refreshes every `WAILA_TICK_INTERVAL` = 5 ticks, ~0.25s) — it was the 0.9.202 cross-system skip (`if (javaCombatCooldownLastFrame.has(player.id)) continue;`, added so Java Combat's crosshair cooldown bar "wins" the shared title channel during a swing) applying to EVERY WAILA update, including the target disappearing entirely. Since a melee kill immediately populates `javaCombatCooldown` for the attacker (cooldown durations run 0.625s-1.4s depending on weapon), WAILA was fully frozen for that whole window right after every kill — on whatever it last showed, typically the target's final "0 HP" reading — exactly matching "shows 0 hp after its gone." Fixed by moving the combat-cooldown skip to apply ONLY to the ordinary "target still exists, info merely changed" case; a target actually disappearing now always calls `wailaClear` immediately regardless of an active cooldown, since a stale corpse readout is a worse bug than briefly sharing the title channel with the combat bar.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed (both edits near the pre-existing `WAILA_MARKER` line, which carries real Private-Use-Area codepoints from the original WAILA work, were made via a safe ASCII-anchored Python patch rather than the Edit tool directly — this file's own established workaround for the invisible-character-corruption class of mistake — and the marker's own codepoints were verified byte-for-byte unchanged afterward). BP-only change (no resource-pack/satellite files touched) — manifest bumped to 0.9.211 (header + both modules). `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed, 279 entries (unchanged — no new assets). In-game verification is still the user's to run next: confirm WAILA now picks up a mob standing in tall grass/sugar cane/behind a water surface it previously missed, confirm it still can't see through an actual solid wall, and confirm a kill mid-swing clears the readout immediately instead of freezing on a stale "0 HP" line for the rest of the cooldown.

**Staff Tools expanded — "STAFF cannot enter the Staff Menu" turned out to be pure discoverability, not a bug (BP 0.9.212).** Traced the full path end to end first, since the report read as a hard failure: `/epitaph staff`'s registration (`perm: "player"`, no engine-level gate), `Commands.dispatch()` (only blocks on `def.perm === "admin"`, which this isn't), and `openStaffToolsMenu`'s own `isStaffOrAbove()` check all check out correctly — there was no code bug anywhere in the chain. Confirmed directly with the user: they simply didn't know `/epitaph staff` existed as its own command, separate from the fully admin-gated `/epitaph menu` (Control Panel) — asked, and that was it. Followed up on their actual next ask instead of forcing a fix onto a non-bug: "add more staff functionality. Nothing extreme, but more things nonetheless."

- **Three new tools added to `openStaffToolsMenu`** (still reached only via `/epitaph staff`, still gated at `isStaffOrAbove()`, not `adminOnly()` — unchanged from 0.9.158's original design):
  - **Bring Player Here** — the natural complement to the existing Teleport to Player; teleports the target to the acting staff member's own location instead of the other way around.
  - **Player Info** — a READ-ONLY lookup (hierarchy rank, badge rank, total playtime, warning count via the already-existing `getWarnCount`, and Muted/Soft-Banned status via the already-existing `isMuted`/`isSoftBanned`). Deliberately no toggle for the latter two here — granting/revoking Mute and Soft-Ban stays exclusively in Admin's own Ranks & Moderation panel; Staff can only SEE what's already set. This is the concrete answer to "ensure Staff and Admin features are separate": Staff gained real new capability without gaining any of Admin's actual moderation POWER, just visibility into it.
  - **Staff Chat** — a modal-prompted message broadcast only to currently-online players where `isStaffOrAbove()` is true, for moderation coordination. No economy/plot/generator access, matching every other tool already on this menu.
- **`/epitaph help`'s Staff line updated** to name all five tools instead of the original three, so the in-game help text itself now serves as the discoverability fix for the next person who doesn't know this command exists.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP-only change (no resource-pack/satellite files touched) — manifest bumped to 0.9.212 (header + both modules). `.mcaddon` rebuilt, all 11 packs confirmed, 279 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition). In-game verification is still the user's to run next: confirm all five Staff Tools buttons work for a Staff-tagged (non-admin) player, confirm Player Info correctly shows a target's real rank/playtime/warnings/mute/soft-ban state without offering any way to change them, and confirm Staff Chat reaches every online Staff/Admin but nobody else.

**Two new systems added: `/rtp` (random safe teleport within the world border) and `/tpa` (player-to-player teleport requests) (BP 0.9.213), on direct request.** Both new bare top-level commands, each in its own satellite pack (`Epitaph-RtpCommand`, `Epitaph-TpaCommand`) per this addon's standing one-namespace-per-pack constraint — pack count now 11 → 13.

- **`/rtp`** — teleports the calling player to a random location within the current world border. "Safe and travellable" is interpreted the same practical way this file's own plot-terrain system already does, reusing its established building blocks rather than inventing new criteria: `rtpEvaluateCandidate` requires a real solid, non-liquid surface block (`Dimension.getTopmostBlock` — actual generated terrain height, never a floating structure), two full blocks of headroom above it (so a teleport can never drop a player into a suffocating gap), and rejects any column currently inside a claimed plot (`getPlotAtBlock`, so a random roll can never land someone on another player's private island uninvited) or classified as ocean/river (`OCEAN_BIOME_IDS`, the exact same set the plot-claiming ocean veto uses) or below `TERRAIN_MIN_SAFE_Y` (a deep ravine/chasm floor). Up to `RTP_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (20) random candidates are tried, each force-loaded via `withLocationLoaded` — the same generalized ticking-area helper Player Shops and the mailbox vault already use, so this never competes with the vanilla 10-ticking-area command limit (see that function's own header for the full story). Gated identically to every other voluntary teleport in this file: `isPvpTagged` + `pvpCombatBlocked` blocks a combat-tagged player from escaping a fight this way, matching `/plot home`, the Spawn Ball, and Ender Pearl throwing. Restricted to the Overworld (the only dimension the world border and plot system apply to). A flat `RTP_COOLDOWN_SEC` (30s) cooldown is applied on every attempt regardless of outcome — a resource-safety guard, not a requested feature in its own right, since each attempt genuinely force-loads chunks and this stops repeated rapid retries from churning through ticking areas. **Stated honestly, not oversold**: this does NOT do real pathfinding back to civilization — an accepted simplification, the same tradeoff `evaluateTerrainCandidate`'s own header already makes for plot claiming (catches the real, common failure cases — ocean, ravine, suffocation, no ground at all — without claiming a mathematical guarantee of "you can definitely walk from here to anywhere else").
- **`/tpa <player>`** — sends a teleport request; the target accepts with `/tpa accept`, declines with `/tpa deny`, or the sender can back out with `/tpa cancel`. One pending INCOMING request per target at a time (`tpaRequests`, keyed by target id) — a second sender is told the target is already busy rather than silently clobbering the first request; `tpaSenderTarget` mirrors "which target does this sender currently have pending" so cancel and the sender-side dedupe check are both O(1). Accepting always moves the SENDER to the TARGET (the conventional "request to teleport TO you" direction) via `sender.teleport(player.location, ...)`. Requests expire after `TPA_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` (60s), swept every second with a notice to both sides rather than silently vanishing; cleared on `playerLeave` from either side (as a pending target or as a pending sender). **Also gated on the same combat-log discipline as every other teleport in this file** — `isPvpTagged` blocks sending a request, AND is re-checked again at accept time (not just send time, since tag state can change during the request's open window) before the sender is actually moved — closing the same class of escape route 0.9.167 already closed for Ender Pearls, `/plot home`, and the world border push-back. The single argument is deliberately a plain `String`, not `PlayerSelector`/`Enum` — it has to hold either a player name or one of the fixed words "accept"/"deny"/"cancel", and `CustomCommandRegistry` fixes one type per parameter slot with no way to type it differently per value; this mirrors the exact same reasoning (and the exact same previously-reported failure mode) that reverted `/plot:claim` and `/pay` from `PlayerSelector` back to plain `String` at 0.9.171/0.9.195 — real parsing happens entirely in `handleTpaCommand` on the main pack's side.
- **Both wired into the existing player-facing (non-admin-gated) branch of the `scriptEventReceive` switch** (`rtp:rtp` → `handleRtpCommand`, `tpa:tpa` → `handleTpaCommand`), alongside `pay:pay`/`bounty:bounty`/`levelshop:open` — same architecture, zero logic in either satellite pack. `/epitaph help`'s "See also" line updated to list `/rtp`/`/tpa` alongside `/pay`/`/guide`/`/bounty`, so both are discoverable from in-game help rather than needing to be found out about by accident (the exact gap the Staff Tools conversation earlier this session just surfaced for `/epitaph staff`).
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed on `main.js` and both new satellite scripts; both new manifests validated as JSON. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.213 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. Two new satellite packs created (`Epitaph-RtpCommand` v1.0.0, `Epitaph-TpaCommand` v1.0.0, fresh UUIDs, no `pack_icon.png` sourced yet — same acknowledged gap `Epitaph-LevelShopCommand` already has). `.mcaddon` rebuilt with both added to the build script (here and in the PowerShell snippet above), 13 packs confirmed, 283 entries. In-game verification is still the user's to run next: confirm `/rtp` lands somewhere solid, dry, and clear of any claimed plot; confirm a combat-tagged player is blocked from both `/rtp` and sending/accepting a `/tpa`; confirm `/tpa <name>` → `/tpa accept` actually moves the sender to the target; and confirm an expired or denied request notifies both sides correctly.

**All 11 satellite packs retired for real — confirmed live, then adopted (BP 0.9.214).** The 0.9.213-era "is there literally zero way to reduce satellites" research (`CustomCommandErrorReason.NamespaceMismatch`'s real doc text: "Supplied Custom Command namespace does not match previous registrations for this add-on") was tested end to end in a disposable throwaway copy of the whole addon (`Epitaph - Addon - ConsolidatedTest\`, a full folder copy, main pack untouched) before touching anything real — every satellite's command re-registered directly under `epitaph:`, satellites excluded from that test build entirely, reimported into a real test world. **Confirmed working live**: schema migrations ran, a plot claim succeeded end-to-end (terrain diagnostics logged normally), and `/plot home` fired correctly through the new consolidated registration path with zero satellite packs installed. On direct confirmation ("It seems it all works... we will go with this new method"), the exact same `registerBuiltinCommands()` block was applied to the real working copy, and the workspace was cleaned up around it.

- **What changed in `main.js`**: 20 new command registrations added to `registerBuiltinCommands()` (`plot`, `claim`, `delete`, `setmaxradius`, `debuggen`, `debugclear`, `kit`, `shop`, `market`, `raid`, `pay`, `guide`, `bounty`, `levelshop`, `rtp`, `tpa`) — all under the `epitaph:` namespace this pack already exclusively owned, all calling the real handler function directly (`dispatchPlotCommand`, `claimKit`, `handleShopSetupCommand`, `handlePayCommand`, `handleRtpCommand`, etc. — the exact same functions the retired satellites' scriptevent relays used to reach) instead of forwarding a scriptevent for this same file's own listener to catch. Subcommand enums renamed to avoid colliding with each other or the existing `epitaph:sub` (`plot:sub`→`epitaph:plot_sub`, `kit:sub`→`epitaph:kit_sub`, `shop:sub`→`epitaph:shop_sub`, `market:sub`→`epitaph:market_sub`, `raid:sub`→`epitaph:raid_sub`). The admin-only ones (`claim`/`delete`/`setmaxradius`/`debuggen`/`debugclear`/`raid`) now get `perm: "admin"`'s centralized `Commands.dispatch()` gate instead of the old manual `if (!isAdmin(player)) return;` inside the scriptevent switch — equivalent protection, one fewer bespoke gate to keep in sync. **The master `scriptEventReceive` relay map/switch was deliberately left completely untouched** — `/scriptevent plot:home ...` etc. still works exactly as it always has, for anyone/anything that still wants to call it that way directly; only the slash-command registration path changed.
- **One confirmed, permanent, unavoidable side effect** (reported directly after testing: "it shows '/epitaph:command' and '/command' for each"): checked Microsoft's actual `CustomCommand` interface — it has exactly six properties (`name`, `description`, `permissionLevel`, `mandatoryParameters`, `optionalParameters`, `cheatsRequired`), no visibility/hidden/alias-only flag of any kind — so there is no supported way to suppress the auto-generated namespaced form from autocomplete while keeping the bare one. Not a new problem this consolidation introduced (`/plot:plot` existed alongside bare `/plot` the exact same way under the old satellite architecture) — just more visually clustered now that all twenty colon-forms share the identical `epitaph:` prefix instead of being scattered across `plot:`/`shop:`/`pay:`/etc.
- **Workspace cleanup, on direct instruction**: all 11 retired satellite source folders deleted from `Epitaph-Complete\` (now an empty folder, left in place only so the rebuild script's `$sat` path still resolves to something real if a satellite is ever reintroduced); the 5 standalone `.mcpack` exports for retired satellites at the addon root (`Epitaph-KitCommands.mcpack`, `Epitaph-MarketCommands.mcpack`, `Epitaph-PlotCommands.mcpack`, `Epitaph-RaidCommands.mcpack`, `Epitaph-ShopCommands.mcpack`) deleted; the rebuild script (both the copy in this file and the one actually run) updated to zip only `Epitaph-BP`/`Epitaph-RP`; the "Structure" section's per-satellite listing above marked retired with strikethrough, kept as historical record rather than deleted outright, since the namespace research/incident history there is still real institutional knowledge worth keeping. The `Epitaph - Addon - ConsolidatedTest\` throwaway validation copy was also deleted — its only purpose was proving the theory before this pass, and it's now a pure, fully-redundant duplicate of the real copy.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.214 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt with only 2 packs (`Epitaph-BP`, `Epitaph-RP`), confirmed via the established Python zipfile check (253 entries — down from 283, matching the removed satellite content). In-game verification: already done, live, before this pass landed (see the ConsolidatedTest paragraph above) — this pass is porting a confirmed-working change into the real copy, not shipping something untested.

**Dead pre-0.8.28 generator-system assets removed outright: 84 `.mcstructure` files + 42 spawn-egg icon textures (BP/RP 0.9.215)**, on direct instruction ("We still have mcstructures and Spawn egg textures within our pack, remove them. the mcstructures consist of our OLD Generator system"). Confirmed dead before deleting anything, not assumed:
