- **`behavior_pack/structures/gen_<name>.mcstructure` (42 files) and `npc_gen_<name>.mcstructure` (42 files)** — both sets trace back to the pre-0.8.28 generator design documented in `SHOP_TIERS`' own header comment: 0.8.23 placed a `gen_<name>` structure directly via `/structure load` (a solid `repeating_command_block`), 0.8.25 replaced that with a spawn-egg token, and 0.8.28 replaced *that* with the current design — real script logic on `epitaph:generator_*` custom blocks the player places directly, no structure file or token item involved at all. The `npc_gen_` set was already flagged dead in this file's own 0.8.50-era comment ("never referenced anywhere in this addon's own code"); this pass confirmed the `gen_` set was equally dead by grepping for the one remaining reference to these names anywhere in `main.js` — `SHOP_TIERS`' own `structure: "gen_..."` field — and confirming via a second grep that `.structure` is never actually *read* anywhere in the file. All 84 files deleted; the now-orphaned `structure: "gen_..."` field removed from all 12 `SHOP_TIERS` entries rather than left as dead data pointing at deleted files. Only `ISLA.mcstructure`/`ISLA2.mcstructure` (the real island structures) and their two established `.bak` safety copies remain — `structureCategories`'s "Generators"/"NPC Generators (unused)" grouping now always resolves to zero matches and silently drops out of the admin Structures menu (its own `categories.length <= 1` fallback already handled this gracefully, confirmed by reading it, so the function itself didn't need changing).
- **`resource_pack/textures/items/gen/spawn_egg_<mob>.png` (42 files)** — the icon assets for that same abandoned 0.8.25 spawn-egg-token design. Confirmed dead by checking every possible reference path: no block/item JSON anywhere in the pack uses the `spawn_egg_<mob>` texture-atlas keys `item_texture.json` defined for them, and `main.js`'s own real spawn-egg icon logic (`shopItemTexturePath`, used for Player Shops) resolves `<mob>_spawn_egg` items to vanilla's own built-in `textures/items/spawn_eggs/spawn_egg_<mob>` path, never this pack's own copies — the one place `textures/items/gen/spawn_egg_cow.png` appears in `main.js` at all is as a comment's illustrative example of a naming convention, not a real code reference. All 42 texture files deleted, the now-empty `textures/items/gen/` folder removed, and `item_texture.json` (which was 100% composed of these 42 dead entries plus one oddly-named 43rd duplicate, `mule_spawn_egg`, pointing at the same dead file) replaced with an empty `texture_data: {}` rather than left with orphaned entries pointing at deleted files. **Not touched, confirmed still live and in active use**: `resource_pack/textures/blocks/generators/` (41 files) — the real, current block textures for `epitaph:generator_*`, confirmed referenced in `terrain_texture.json` — a completely different, unrelated asset set from the dead spawn-egg icons despite the similar "generator" naming.
- **RP header's own `description` field was factually wrong after this** — it had read "Generator spawn egg icons for Epitaph." verbatim since the RP's very first asset addition (0.9.127), which was never updated as the RP grew to include fonts, particles, sounds, and UI; now actively false with the spawn-egg icons gone. Corrected to "Textures, fonts, particles, sounds, and UI for Epitaph."
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; `item_texture.json` re-validated as JSON. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.215 (header + both modules); RP manifest bumped 0.9.24→0.9.25 (header) / 0.9.20→0.9.21 (module) and the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match — a real RP asset removal, same version-bump discipline as every prior RP change. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (down from 253 — the 84 structures + 42 textures + `pack_icon.png`-adjacent overhead removed, confirmed zero `gen_`/`npc_gen_`/`spawn_egg` paths remain anywhere in the packaged output via a direct zip-content check, not just the source folders).

**A user-authored reference structure (`Kits.mcstructure`, addon root, not part of the shipped pack) named, then ported into 6 real code-defined kits (BP 0.9.216).** First pass: given `Kits.mcstructure` (8 pre-packed shulker box item entities) and asked to name each by its actual contents — read it directly via `nbtlib` (little-endian, uncompressed, this project's own established NBT-editing technique) rather than guessed, confirmed each shulker's real inventory, and set `tag.display.Name` on all 8 (2 duplicate pairs among them: Invisibility/Swiftness each existed twice, byte-for-byte identical). Backed up as `Kits.mcstructure.bak` before writing, verified the save round-tripped correctly (names present, item counts/enchants untouched) by reloading the saved file fresh.

Second pass, this entry: "remove the shulker, and take the items inside them. And introduce them as kits, the better the kit, the more expensive." Extracted the 6 unique loadouts (duplicates collapsed to one definition each) into real `seedKit`-based kits, dropping the shulker box container entirely — a player gets the loose items directly, exactly per the instruction.

- **Real, confirmed platform limitation found and worked around, not guessed past**: three of the six kits are 27x-a-single-splash-potion loadouts (Invisibility/Strength/Swiftness), and a specific potion effect **cannot be set on an ItemStack via the stable Script API at all** — checked `ItemPotionComponent` directly against Microsoft's current reference and both `potionEffectType`/`potionDeliveryType` are `read-only`; `ItemStack.createPotion`/`PotionOptions` only exist on the experimental channel, never stable (this addon is pinned to stable `@minecraft/server` 2.8.0, unchanged this whole project). Worked around with the same "fall back to a real command when the stable Script API can't do something directly" pattern already established elsewhere in this file (`flight_ability.js`'s `bounceGameMode`) — `claimKit`'s item loop now recognizes a new optional `potionData` field and grants that item via a real `/give <item> <amount> <data>` command instead of constructing an `ItemStack`, using the real, standard Bedrock potion data values read directly off the structure file's own item NBT (8 = Long Invisibility, 16 = Strong Swiftness, 33 = Strong Strength) rather than guessed.
- **Non-stackable items handled correctly, not just copied naively**: `ItemStack`'s own constructor **silently clamps** an over-large `amount` down to the item's real max stack size instead of throwing (confirmed directly against Microsoft's reference) — Totem of Undying (max 1) and Ender Pearl (max 16) are listed as separate per-slot entries at their real extracted amounts (6 separate totems, two separate 16-stacks of pearls) rather than one merged `amount` field, which would have silently handed out fewer items than the shulker actually contained.
- **Renamed off the shulkers' own "Warrior Kit"/"Champion Kit" labels to avoid colliding with the already-established PVP kit progression's real "Warrior" ($2,000, enchanted iron, Sharpness/Protection I) and "Champion" ($15,000, enchanted diamond, Sharpness/Protection II)** — these extracted kits are materially different (this "Iron" kit is fully unenchanted; this "Diamond" kit is enchanted one level below the existing Champion's), so reusing those names would have meant two different real item sets answering to confusingly similar identities. Final names — **Iron, Invisibility, Strength, Swiftness, Diamond, Ultimate** — are also all single words, matching every other kit in this file (Starter/Warrior/Champion/Legend/CONTENT/BETA), deliberately avoiding the multi-word-command-argument risk a "Kit"-suffixed name would carry.
- **Pricing/cooldown scale with real extracted power, per direct instruction ("the better the kit, the more expensive")**: Iron $1,000/2h (weaker than the existing Warrior, since unenchanted) → the three potion kits $3,000/4h each (equal tier, structurally identical value) → Diamond $10,000/6h (one enchant level below the existing Champion) → Ultimate $350,000/48h — priced far above the existing top "Legend" ($75,000) since its real contents (6 Totems of Undying, an Elytra, a Lava Bucket, Sharpness IV/V netherite, a maxed Unbreaking/Fortune/Efficiency netherite pickaxe, 64 Golden Apples, 32 Ender Pearls) are substantially beyond Legend's own loadout, not just a reskin of it.
- **`seedExtractedKits()`** (new, right after `seedRankKits`) follows the exact same idempotent-by-name pattern every other seed function in this file already uses — wired into both the world-load bootstrap (`StaffTagModule.onWorldLoad`, alongside `seedRankKits`/`seedPvpKits`) and Server Validation's re-seed pass, so a mid-session crash before either ever ran doesn't silently lose these kits either.
- **`Kits.mcstructure`/`Kits.mcstructure.bak` were deliberately left on disk, not deleted** — they were never part of the shipped pack (sit at the addon root, never referenced by any code path), and the user only asked for their *contents* to become kits, not for the source file's removal; worth deleting later if confirmed no longer wanted.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.216 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes (the new kits are pure script/data, no new assets). `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new files added to either pack). In-game verification is still the user's to run next: `/kit list` should show all 6 new kits with their costs; claiming each should hand over the loose items (no shulker box); the three potion kits should each grant 27 splash potions of the correct, specific effect (not a plain/inert potion); Ultimate's 6 totems and 2×16 ender pearls should all actually arrive (not silently reduced).

**Renamed all 6 extracted kits a second time (BP 0.9.217)**, on direct instruction to use real competitive-Bedrock-PvP kit-naming conventions instead of plain material/effect names — with an explicit "nothing edgy or weird" constraint, so the Assassin/Ninja-style stealth-class family and anything gimmick-flavored were deliberately not used:
- **Knight** (was Iron) — the real "Warrior/Knight/Brute: balanced armor, standard sword, the baseline kit" archetype; named Knight rather than reusing Warrior specifically to keep avoiding the collision with the already-established PVP progression's own enchanted "Warrior" kit.
- **Gapple** (was Diamond) — the real, ubiquitous competitive-PvP kit name for "diamond armor and sword, reliant on Golden Apples," matching this kit's actual contents exactly; not "Champion," for the same collision reason as Knight/Warrior.
- **Titan** (was Ultimate) — the tier/rank-based naming family (VIP/MVP/Titan), fitting since this is the single most valuable kit in the file by a wide margin; deliberately not "BuildUHC" despite the lava-bucket/pickaxe/pearls overlap, since this kit has no building blocks and claiming that specific archetype would have overclaimed a mechanic it doesn't fully have.
- **Phantom** (was Invisibility), **Brute** (was Strength), **Sprinter** (was Swiftness) — the three pure-potion utility kits, kept to plain, literal effect-based names (matching how "Gapple"/"NoDebuff" themselves are named after their own core mechanic) rather than reaching for the stealth-class archetype family the instruction ruled out.
- All constant names (`IRON_KIT_ITEMS` etc. → `KNIGHT_KIT_ITEMS` etc.) and the `seedExtractedKits()` registration calls were renamed to match; item contents, enchantments, costs, and cooldowns are byte-for-byte unchanged from 0.9.216 — this pass only touched names.
- **Not handled, and deliberately so**: `seedKit` only ever inserts a kit that doesn't already exist by name — it never renames one. If a world somehow already seeded the old names (Iron/Diamond/Ultimate/Invisibility/Strength/Swiftness) between 0.9.216 and this pass, that world would end up with both the old and new kits defined side by side rather than a clean rename. Judged not worth a migration path for a rename that happened within the same session, before any real-world deployment — an admin can delete a stray old-named kit via the normal Kits admin UI (Edit/Delete) if this ever actually comes up.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; confirmed zero remaining references to any of the six old constant names anywhere in the file. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.217 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — a pure rename, no new assets). In-game verification is still the user's to run next: `/kit list` should show Knight/Phantom/Brute/Sprinter/Gapple/Titan (not the old names), each still granting exactly the same items/costs as before.

**Plot system regression fixed: a real, confirmed satellite-retirement documentation gap, plus a genuinely separate, previously-undiscovered cursor-leak bug (BP 0.9.218).** Reported directly: "There are 19 plots, yet it says all possible plots are exhausted; Even when I set the max radius to 30,000. Also, the Plot radius command is missing, only world border shows."

- **Confirmed regression #1: every player-facing mention of the plot admin commands still used the dead colon-form syntax from the retired satellite pack.** The satellite retirement (BP 0.9.214) moved `claim`/`delete`/`setmaxradius`/`debuggen`/`debugclear` to real bare commands registered directly under `epitaph:` — but `PLOT_HELP` (the in-game admin help text) still listed all five as `/plot:claim`, `/plot:delete`, `/plot:setmaxradius`, `/plot:debuggen`, `/plot:debugclear`, none of which have worked since 0.9.214 (the satellite that used to expose those exact colon forms is gone). This directly explains "the Plot radius command is missing" — the one documented way to set it was dead syntax. Fixed `PLOT_HELP` and every other stale player-facing message (the world-border auto-sync alert, the 80%-band-full warning, both "region exhausted" alerts, the orphaned scriptevent handler's own error text, and the `showConfirm` body text for `/debugclear`) to reference the real, working bare commands instead.
- **Confirmed regression #2, and the more likely actual cause of "exhausted with only 19 plots": `/debuggen <count>` consumes the exact same shared `plot_spiral_cursor` real claims use, but `/debugclear` never rewound it.** Read `adminDebugGen`/`adminDebugClear`/`advanceCursorAt` directly rather than guessed: `adminDebugGen` calls `advanceSpiralCursor()` — the identical cursor `claimPlot` advances — for every test plot it generates, and `adminDebugClear` only ever frees the test cells' `plot_cell_${col}_${row}` ownership markers, never rewinding the cursor itself. Since the cursor only ever moves forward (never re-examines a freed cell on its own), any earlier stress-test run (`/debuggen`, "Admin: stress-test plot generation" — exactly what it's for) could have permanently burned thousands of cursor positions, potentially pushing it out past `maxRadius` and/or the world border's own containment limit — fully explaining exhaustion with a real plot count nowhere near that scale. Fixed going forward: `adminDebugGen` now records the cursor's position immediately before it starts (only once, so back-to-back debuggen calls before a matching debugclear don't overwrite the real baseline); `adminDebugClear` rewinds `plot_spiral_cursor` back to that recorded baseline afterward. Rewinding is always safe regardless of value — `plot_cell_${col}_${row}` is the actual overlap guard, checked independently at claim time; the cursor is only ever "where to resume scanning from," so moving it backward can at worst cause a few already-claimed cells to be briefly re-examined and skipped again, never a conflict.
- **This fix only prevents recurrence — it can't retroactively repair a cursor that already leaked before this build existed.** Added a new admin recovery command, `/resetclaimcursor` (confirm-free, since it's provably safe per the same reasoning above), that resets `plot_spiral_cursor` to 0 — added to `PLOT_HELP` alongside the other admin commands. Also added `plotExhaustionDiagnostics()`, appended to both "region exhausted" alerts, showing the live cursor's position/implied distance, the effective `maxRadius`, and the actual world-border containment limit (`worldBorderSize/2 - ISLAND_BORDER_MARGIN`) side by side — directly surfacing the other real, non-bug gotcha this report's "even at max radius 30,000" phrasing hints at: `plot_max_radius_override` and the actual `/epitaph:setworldborder` are two separate settings (established at 0.9.172/0.9.173), and raising the override alone does nothing if the border's own containment cap is still the smaller number.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.218 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition). In-game verification is still the user's to run next: confirm `/setmaxradius`/`/claim`/`/delete`/`/debuggen`/`/debugclear`/`/resetclaimcursor` all now show correctly in `/plot help`; if claiming is still reported exhausted, check the Content Log's `[PlotSystem] ALERT` line for the new diagnostic bracket — it will show directly whether the cursor is absurdly far out (run `/resetclaimcursor`) or whether the world border itself needs raising to match `maxRadius` (run `/epitaph:setworldborder <r>`, which auto-syncs the override to match).

**Own bug caught immediately after shipping: only one of the two "region exhausted" alert call sites actually got the 0.9.218 diagnostic bracket (BP 0.9.219).** Confirmed live: user reported the border correctly auto-synced to 650-29900 (proving that part of 0.9.218 worked) with only ~20 real plots claimed — exactly the cursor-leak symptom — but the pasted Content Log line had no `[cursor #N @ ...]` bracket at all, despite being on the 0.9.218 build. Re-grepped both `alertPlotAdmins("... region exhausted ...")` call sites directly rather than trust the prior edit: `noSlots()` (fired when `tryTerrainCombined`'s own search returns `"band_exhausted"` — the actual path hit here, confirmed by tracing its one call site) had the bracket; `tryOceanAvoidingFallback`'s own exhaustion branch did not — an incomplete multi-occurrence Edit apparently only patched one of the two identical strings despite `replace_all` having been used successfully one step earlier in the same file for a different substitution. Fixed by adding the missing `[${plotExhaustionDiagnostics()}]` to the second call site and re-verifying both are present directly inside the rebuilt `.mcaddon`'s own packaged `main.js` (not just the source file) via a Python regex check, rather than trusting the source alone this time. `node --check` passed; BP manifest bumped to 0.9.219 (header + both modules); `.mcaddon` rebuilt, 127 entries. Next: reimport 0.9.219, reproduce the same claim attempt, and paste the new bracket's exact contents — that will show the live cursor position/distance against both limits and confirm or rule out the cursor-leak theory directly.

**Real, permanent fix — the exhaustion check itself made self-healing, not just diagnosed (BP 0.9.220).** Live data came back: `cursor #2868 @ ~4378 blocks out | maxRadius=29900 | borderLimit=29900` — a cursor position nowhere near either limit, yet declared exhausted. Manually running `/resetclaimcursor` fixed it immediately, confirming the real cause: a long **contiguous stretch of already-claimed cells** (almost certainly leftover `DEBUG_` test plots from an old `/debuggen` stress-test run that predates the 0.9.218 cursor-rewind fix) sat right where the live cursor was — big enough that a single `MAX_SCAN_PER_CLAIM` (10,000-cell) scan pass ran out of budget skipping over it before ever reaching a genuinely free cell beyond it, even though the actual claimable band (650-29,900) was nowhere near full. Directly instructed not to keep adding diagnostics for this — a proper fix that can't fail this way again, not another log line.

- **`advanceCursorAt` split into `advanceCursorAtOnePass` (the original single 10,000-cell scan, now reporting *why* it stopped) plus a retrying outer loop.** The single-pass helper now returns one of three outcomes: a found cell, `exhausted: true` (the ring-distance math has actually proven every remaining cell is past `maxRadius` — genuinely nothing left), or `exhausted: false` (the pass's scan budget ran out without finding a free cell, but nothing confirms the band itself is full — just that this one pass didn't happen to reach a free cell). Only the `exhausted: true` case reports real exhaustion immediately. The scan-limit case now automatically retries — the cursor position was already saved past whatever was just skipped, so resuming is always safe (never re-examines the same cells, never risks double-claiming one, since `plot_cell_${col}_${row}` — not cursor position — is the actual overlap guard) — up to `CURSOR_SCAN_MAX_PASSES` (10) times, i.e. up to 100,000 total cells checked before finally giving up. This means the system can now plow straight through an arbitrarily large contiguous dead zone (any leftover debug run up to 100,000 cells) entirely on its own — no admin intervention needed for this failure mode again.
- **Verified by simulation before shipping, not just reasoned about**: a standalone Node script reproduced the exact reported shape — a 15,000-cell contiguous claimed block (bigger than one 10,000-cell scan pass) sitting right where the cursor starts — and confirmed the new code finds a valid cell on the very next pass (2 passes total) where the old single-pass code would have failed outright. A second simulation confirmed a genuinely full, tiny band (every real cell inside a 650-800 radius actually claimed) still correctly reports exhaustion immediately on the first pass, with zero wasted retries — the fix doesn't mask real exhaustion, only the false kind.
- **`/resetclaimcursor` (added at 0.9.218) is kept as a manual last resort** for a dead zone larger than the new 100,000-cell retry ceiling, but should no longer be needed for the scale of leftover debug data this world actually had.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; both simulation scripts run standalone against the exact ported logic before editing the real file. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.220 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets). In-game verification is still the user's to run next: claim attempts should now succeed immediately without ever needing `/resetclaimcursor` again, even if the live cursor organically re-advances into the same old dead zone over time.

**Wild mob-spawner pickup fixed — a real, missing debounce, same class of bug this exact file already solved once for the Generator Hub (BP 0.9.221).** Reported directly: "Our custom mob spawners still don't work at all. Same for Vanilla ones." First ruled out the simplest explanation (Peaceful difficulty, which would block both identically) — not the cause, difficulty was Easy/Normal/Hard. Follow-up pinned the real, concrete symptom: shift-right-clicking a vanilla `minecraft:mob_spawner` correctly opens the "Mob Spawner" pick-up confirm menu, but tapping Pick Up never actually gives the item, and the confirm menu has to be closed multiple times in a row — meaning the user never once obtained a working custom spawner item to test in the first place, which fully explains "custom mob spawners don't work at all" too (there was nothing placed yet to test).

- **Root cause**: `world.beforeEvents.playerInteractWithBlock` can genuinely fire more than once for a single physical shift-click (a known real Bedrock behavior) — the Generator Hub's own identical interact handler, a few lines above this one in the same file, already has a `genMenuDebounce`/`GEN_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` guard specifically for this reason. The wild-spawner pickup handler (`openWildSpawnerMenu`'s caller) had no such guard at all, so a single interaction could schedule several concurrent `openWildSpawnerMenu` calls — each popping its own "Mob Spawner" confirm form (exactly matching "close it multiple times"), and since the second `ModalFormData` (the mob-type dropdown) in a losing concurrent call can resolve as canceled via `FormCancelationReason.UserBusy` while another instance's form is already open on screen, that instance hits its own `if (tr.canceled ...) return;` guard and silently exits before ever reaching the give-item step (exactly matching "it never gives me it").
- **Fixed by reusing the exact same `genMenuDebounce` map** (not a new one — a generator-block interaction and a mob-spawner interaction can never be the same physical block, so sharing the one per-player cooldown is the identical semantic already proven correct here, not a new mechanism) in the wild-spawner handler, checked and set the same way the Generator Hub handler already does immediately before scheduling `openWildSpawnerMenu`.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.221 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script fix). In-game verification is still the user's to run next: shift-right-click a vanilla mob spawner once, confirm the confirm menu and mob-type dropdown each appear exactly once, confirm the generator item is actually received, then place it and confirm the resulting custom spawner actually produces mobs.

- **Wider workspace cleanup, confirmed with the user before removing anything (checked against `main.js`/`README.md`/`CHANGELOG.md` for any reference first — none found for any of these)**: `Epitaph-0_8_63-Uncompressed\` (a stale uncompressed extraction stuck at version 0.8.63, ~190 versions behind); the old one-off root exports `Epitaph-0_8_63.mcaddon`/`Epitaph-0_8_63-BP.mcpack`/`Epitaph-0_8_63-RP.mcpack` (all dated 2026-07-11, long superseded by the current `Epitaph-Complete.mcaddon` rebuild pipeline); four root-level `.mcstructure` files (`ISLA1.mcstructure`, `ISLA2.mcstructure`, `_ISLA2.mcstructure`, `Shop.mcstructure`) that were never the live copies — the pack's real, currently-loaded structures live inside `Epitaph-0_8_63/behavior_pack/structures/` and were untouched; and `Blurry's Java PvP 1.3.3.mcaddon`, the third-party reference addon the Java Combat port research (0.9.198-0.9.211) was built against — that research is complete and fully captured in this file's own changelog entries, so the reference file itself was no longer load-bearing.

**`/kit claim` split from purchasing, `/kit buy` added, and a real content-preview command added (BP 0.9.222)**, on direct request: "Improve '/kit claim', Add '/kit Buy'. Claim is only to claim. Add a way to check what is within each kit." The old single `claimKit` function did everything at once — permission/cooldown gate, silent cost deduction if the kit had one, then the actual grant — meaning a kit with a real cost (Knight through Titan, the three potion kits) was charged the instant `/kit claim` ran, with no preview and no confirm step, the same command name used for genuinely free kits (Starter, CONTENT, BETA).

- **`claimKit` is now free-only.** Refactored into a shared `resolveClaimableKit(player, name)` (kit exists → `permTag` → cooldown, identical checks either path needs) and a shared `grantKitItems(core, player, kit)` (the actual item-construction loop — potion `/give` fallback, `ItemStack`/enchantment/lore handling, overflow-drop, `recordClaim`, disparity-score registration — extracted verbatim from the old function so both entry points can never drift apart on how an item is actually built). `claimKit` itself now checks `kit.cost > 0` immediately after resolving and, if true, refuses with a direct pointer to the new command (`"costs $X — use /kit buy <name> instead"`) rather than silently charging.
- **New `/kit buy <name>`**, admin-free (any player), for every kit with a real cost. Shows an itemized confirm dialog first — kit price plus the full item list via the same `formatKitItemLine` renderer `/kit info` uses — before anything is spent, matching this file's own established convention for real money-spend actions (Plot Crystal, Flight, Border upgrades all confirm first). Balance is re-checked *after* the dialog closes (the player could have spent money elsewhere while it was open — same discipline already used for `/pay`'s own confirm flow), and permission/cooldown are re-resolved too, since real time passed. Payment only happens after `grantKitItems` actually succeeds — same grant-before-charge ordering the original single function always used, so a mid-grant failure can never charge for nothing received. Buying a free kit (or claiming a paid one) gets an explicit redirect to the other command rather than a generic error.
- **New `/kit info <name>`**, the direct answer to "add a way to check what is within each kit." Read-only — never claims, never charges. Shows cost (or "Free"), cooldown/one-time status, and a full itemized breakdown per item: potion contents resolve through a small `KIT_POTION_LABELS` lookup (the 3 real Bedrock `/give` data values this kit system already relies on — 8/16/33 — rather than a raw data number); ordinary items reuse `shopItemDisplayName`/`shopEnchantDisplayName`/`toRomanNumeral` (all already proven in Player Shops' own item-preview screen) for a real name, custom nameTag if set, and enchantment names at their real Roman-numeral level. `/kit list`'s own reply now ends with a one-line nudge toward `/kit info <name>` instead of leaving contents undiscoverable.
- **Command registration**: `epitaph:kit_sub` enum extended `["claim", "list"]` → `["claim", "buy", "list", "info"]`; both new subcommands require the same `<name>` argument the existing two already use.
- **Guide book updated for accuracy**: page 2 (Starter Kits) rewritten to name all four subcommands (197 bytes, verified under the 256-byte cap) instead of only claim/list; page 18 (Quick Reference) updated the same way (237 bytes). Both re-verified via `Buffer.byteLength` equivalent (Python UTF-8 encode length), not `.length`, per this file's own established guide-book discipline.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.222 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for all four new/changed symbols (`buyKit`, `kitInfo`, `grantKitItems`, `resolveClaimableKit`, the new enum values) rather than trusting the source alone. In-game verification is still the user's to run next: `/kit info <name>` on a free and a paid kit to confirm the itemized preview reads correctly (including a potion kit and an enchanted-item kit); `/kit claim` on a paid kit should now refuse and point at `/kit buy`; `/kit buy` on a paid kit should show the confirm dialog, deduct correctly, and grant items; `/kit buy`/`/kit claim` on the wrong kit type should each redirect to the other.

**Kit-name autocomplete added via a new, safely-deferred second-enum mechanism (BP 0.9.223)**, on direct request: "improve auto complete for all commands. Like TPA and Kit Buy/Claim. As remembering names is not what we need." Presented the real, concrete tradeoffs for each candidate before touching anything, rather than guessing at what was wanted:

- **TPA**: giving the player-name argument real autocomplete would require splitting `/tpa <target>` away from its own `accept`/`deny`/`cancel` words (Bedrock can't type one argument slot two different ways) — presented three options (new bare `/tpaaccept`/`/tpadeny`/`/tpacancel` commands, colon-form `/tpa:accept` etc., or leave it alone). **User chose to leave `/tpa` exactly as-is** — no change made.
- **Admin `/claim <player>`**: this is the direct successor to `/plot:claim`, which was already found broken with `PlayerSelector` once before (0.9.171 — a freshly-joined player sometimes isn't resolvable for a brief window right after connecting) and reverted back to plain String specifically to fix that. Re-adding it here would risk the identical class of bug. **User chose to keep it free-typed** — no change made.
- **Kit names** (`/kit claim`, `buy`, `info`'s shared `name` argument): the real, load-bearing tension, already documented in this file's own history at 0.9.124 — kit names live in fully admin-editable, no-restart-required storage (`createKitFlow`/`editKitFlow`), while Bedrock's real autocomplete (`registerEnum`) is a one-time snapshot taken at world startup with no documented way to refresh it afterward. **User chose real autocomplete anyway**, accepting that staleness tradeoff explicitly.

**Implementation, given the kit-name choice — a second, genuinely new safety concern found and closed before writing any enum code, not just the already-known staleness one:** `def.enumValues` (the array actually passed to `registerEnum`) is captured at the exact moment `registerCommand({...})` is called inside `registerBuiltinCommands()` — which runs at **raw top-level script evaluation**, via `epitaph.attach()`, before literally any event has fired (confirmed by tracing the call chain: the bottom-of-file `if (!globalThis.__EPITAPH_INSTANCE__) { ... epitaph.attach(); ... }` block has no surrounding try/catch at all). Reading live world state (`world.getDynamicProperty(KITS_KEY)`) inline at that exact point, if it threw, would abort the entire `registerBuiltinCommands()` pass for **every other command**, not just `/kit` — this file's own prior research already flagged the near-identical `system.beforeEvents.startup` point (later than raw top-level) as "not guaranteed the world is loaded yet" for the exact same reason `struct_place`'s own enum was never built this way.

- **`Commands.registerNow`** gained a new `def.extraEnums` field — an array of `{ name, valuesFn }` pairs, each registered via its own `registry.registerEnum(name, valuesFn())` call **inside** `registerNow` (which already runs inside the `system.beforeEvents.startup` event, and inside its own dedicated try/catch) — never inline at the risky raw-top-level `registerCommand({...})` call site. This is what actually makes the fix safe: a failure reading kit names now only ever aborts registering `/kit`'s own enum (and, if the referenced enum then doesn't exist, possibly `/kit`'s own command registration — already independently try/caught, per the existing `registerNow` structure) — every other command's registration is completely unaffected, confirmed by reading the existing per-command try/catch isolation directly rather than assumed.
- **`getKitNamesForEnum()`** (new, right after `KitsModule`) — `readState().kits.map(k => k.name)` wrapped in its own try/catch, falling back to `[]` on any failure rather than throwing. `/kit`'s registration gained `extraEnums: [{ name: "epitaph:kit_name", valuesFn: getKitNamesForEnum }]`, and its `name` parameter switched from `{ name: "name", type: STR }` to `{ name: "epitaph:kit_name", type: ENUM5 }` — matching this file's own established rule that an enum-typed parameter's `name` field must equal the registered enum ID exactly (stable channel has no separate `enumName` display-label field).
- **The old, now-superseded 0.8.43-era comment block** explaining why kit names were never enum-ized (still technically true about the underlying mechanism, but reflecting the OLD decision not to do it anyway) was rewritten to state the current, accepted tradeoff plainly instead of reading as "this can't be done."
- **Admin-facing messages updated for honesty**: `createKitFlow`'s success message now notes a brand-new kit "won't autocomplete in /kit's dropdown until the next world restart"; `editKitFlow`'s delete confirmation notes a deleted kit "may still show in /kit's autocomplete until the next world restart — claiming it will just fail gracefully" (the existing "No kit called X" error path already handles a stale/deleted selection correctly, confirmed by reading `claimKit`/`buyKit`/`kitInfo`'s own existence checks — no new failure mode introduced).
- **Known, accepted first-load edge case, stated honestly rather than glossed over**: kits are seeded (`seedRankKits`/`seedPvpKits`/`seedExtractedKits`) inside `onWorldLoad()`, which runs *after* the `system.beforeEvents.startup` event this enum registers during — so on a genuinely brand-new world's very first-ever load, `getKitNamesForEnum()` would return an empty list (zero kits seeded yet), and the built-in Knight/Gapple/Titan/etc. kits wouldn't autocomplete until the *second* world load. Not a concern for this addon's own already-tested world (kits were seeded in an earlier session and persist across reloads), but worth knowing if this pack is ever imported fresh.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.223 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `getKitNamesForEnum`, `extraEnums`, and the `epitaph:kit_name` enum reference rather than trusting the source alone. In-game verification is still the user's to run next: confirm `/kit claim`/`buy`/`info` now show a real, clickable dropdown of current kit names; confirm creating a brand-new kit doesn't appear in that dropdown until a restart (expected, per the accepted tradeoff) but still works fine typed exactly or via `/kit list`; confirm every other command (`/plot`, `/pay`, `/tpa`, etc.) still registers and works normally, proving the extraEnums change didn't disturb anything else.

**Investigation pass on two reported bugs — no code fix found yet, because the code reads correctly; diagnostics upgraded instead of guessing further (BP 0.9.224).** Reported: "/Pay still doesnt work," plus "massive issue; Containers/Interact is failing; It triggers on ALL blocks, so breaking dirt with Build/break doesnt work." Read every function in the actual enforcement chain directly rather than assumed — `getPlotAtBlock`, `verifyPermission`, `verifyRaidablePermission`, `canBuild`/`canBreak`/`canInteract`/`canEnter`, `getPlayerPlotPerm`'s override→group→Basic resolution, and the exact toggle-to-setter index mapping in `editPlotPermToggles` — and found no cross-wiring bug anywhere: "interact" is never consulted for a build/break decision in any code path, and general block placement/breaking on unclaimed land still correctly fails open (the 0.9.163 fix is intact). Also re-verified `/pay`'s own resolution path (`resolveSinglePlayerArg`/`toGiveTargets`, already fixed for case-insensitivity at 0.9.195) and the command's registration shape — both look correct.

- **Real, concrete finding, not a guess**: `Log.info` (used for both the `permcheck DENY` diagnostic and `/pay`'s own "no online match" line) resolves to plain `console.log` — a different, lower-visibility tier than `Log.warn`/`Log.error` (this file's own documented "always-on logger"). Minecraft's Content Log viewer commonly filters Info-level output out by default. This directly explains why the user could get zero log output for either issue despite both already having diagnostic logging in place since earlier this session (0.9.195 for `/pay`, long-standing for `permcheck`) — not a logging bug introduced now, but a real, previously-unnoticed visibility gap that made every earlier diagnostic silently useless for this specific user's setup.
- **Fixed by upgrading both to `Log.warn`** so they show by default without needing a Content Log verbosity setting changed. `verifyPermission`/`verifyRaidablePermission`'s `DENY` line also gained a new `permDebugSnapshot(player, id)` helper, appended to the log line, showing the *actual resolved value* of all four relevant keys (`entry`/`build`/`break`/`interact`) for that specific player at that specific plot — not just which one capability was being checked — so the next denial conclusively shows whether break/build are genuinely resolving true (proving the bug, if any, is elsewhere in the pipeline) or unexpectedly false too (pointing straight at that player's specific override/group resolution instead).
- **A new entry-point diagnostic added to `handlePayCommand`** — the very first line, before any resolution logic — logging player/target/amount as given. This is deliberately placed to answer one specific, unresolved question: the raw error text reported for `/pay` ("Syntax error: Unexpected...") reads like Bedrock's own native command-parser rejection, not a message this addon's code has ever produced anywhere. If this new line never appears in the Content Log when that error occurs, that conclusively proves the failure happens before the script is ever reached at all — a fundamentally different class of problem (client input / command schema) than anything inside `handlePayCommand` could explain or fix.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.224 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure diagnostic logging addition), packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `permDebugSnapshot`, the new `handlePayCommand` entry log, and the `Log.warn("permcheck"...)` upgrade. **Not resolved yet, stated honestly**: this pass found no code-level bug to fix — it made the existing (previously invisible) diagnostics actually visible. Next concrete step is the user's: reimport 0.9.224, reproduce both issues, and check the Content Log — the `permcheck` DENY line's bracketed permission snapshot will show directly whether break/build are really being denied or something else is going on, and the presence/absence of the new `handlePayCommand invoked` line will conclusively show whether `/pay`'s failure is inside this addon's script at all.

**Real, confirmed bug found and fixed: the "interact" permission was gating EVERY block interaction in the game, not just containers (BP 0.9.225).** On direct instruction: "Ensure Container/Interact ARE ONLY for Containers, ONLY." Following up on the prior turn's diagnostics-only pass, re-swept every `canInteract`/`"interact"` usage in the file specifically looking for scope, and found the one genuinely broken case: the general `world.beforeEvents.playerInteractWithBlock` subscriber (the handler behind the earlier "breaking dirt fails" / "teleported when breaking or placing" reports) had **no block-type check at all** — it called `verifyRaidablePermission(..., "interact")` unconditionally for every single right-click on every single block in the entire game (dirt, doors, buttons, crafting tables, anything), cancelling and trespass-kicking a player who had build/break granted but not interact, for interacting with something that was never a container in the first place. This directly and fully explains both prior reports.

- **Every other real usage of `canInteract`/"interact" in the file was already correctly scoped**, confirmed by re-checking each one directly rather than assumed: Player Shops' setup/scan flows (`handleShopSetupCommand` and its helpers) only ever check it against a resolved chest; the entity-based item-frame/armor-stand/painting protection (`entityHurt`, 0.9.162) is a separate mechanism entirely, gated on `PLOT_PROTECTED_ENTITY_TYPES`, not blocks; the raid-night spawner-mob combat gate similarly checks entities, not blocks. This one block handler was the sole gap.
- **Fixed by requiring the block to actually be a real container before the permission check ever runs**: `e.block.getComponent("minecraft:inventory")` — the exact same live-container check this file already relies on everywhere else that needs to distinguish "is this actually a container" (`shopChestContainers`, the mail vault's own chest reads) — rather than a hardcoded block-type list, so every real container type (chest, barrel, shulker box, hopper, dispenser, furnace variants, and anything else with real inventory storage) is covered uniformly and correctly, and nothing non-container is affected anymore.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.225 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly to confirm the new container guard is present in the built output, not just the source. In-game verification is still the user's to run next: grant a trusted player build+break but not interact, confirm they can now freely break/place ordinary blocks (dirt, etc.) without being denied or trespass-kicked, and confirm they're still correctly blocked from opening an actual chest/barrel/shulker box on that plot.

**`/pay` removed and rebuilt from scratch as a small, self-contained command (BP 0.9.226)**, on direct instruction: "Remove and reintroduce an working version of the pay system. Light weight, ONLY meant for paying others with cash, with an tax." Prompted by the raw "Syntax error" chat message reported last turn, which never showed up in the Content Log even next to this addon's own entry-point diagnostic (added at 0.9.224) — and a key fact confirmed by re-reading `Commands.dispatch()` (top of file) directly: every command's `run()` is already wrapped in its own `system.run() + try/catch`, which catches any script-side exception and replies `"Command failed. Check server log."` — meaning a raw native "Syntax error" text could never have come from inside the old `handlePayCommand` or anything it called. That message can only be Bedrock's own command parser rejecting the input upstream of any script code at all, which no amount of patching the handler's internal logic could ever have fixed. Rather than keep chasing an already-ruled-out theory, rebuilt the whole thing as instructed.

- **Old implementation removed entirely**: `resolveSinglePlayerArg` (a thin wrapper around the Give module's shared `toGiveTargets`), the awaited `showConfirm` payment-preview dialog, and the post-confirm balance re-check that existed specifically to guard the time gap that dialog created. `toGiveTargets` itself is untouched and still used by `/grant`/`/giveadv` — only `/pay`'s own dependency on it was removed, since the new version resolves its target with a plain, self-contained, inline case-insensitive `world.getPlayers()` scan instead of routing through shared Give-module infrastructure at all.
- **New `handlePayCommand` is a single, flat, synchronous function** — no `await`, no confirm step, one linear pass: resolve online target by name → validate not paying self → parse the amount (still `parseShopPrice`, the same k/m/b/t/q/qi-suffix parser the rest of the economy already uses, kept for consistency rather than invented fresh) → check balance → take a flat 7% tax → move the money → message both players. The command registration's own `run()` dropped its `async`/`void` wrapper to match, since `Commands.dispatch()` already defers every handler via its own `system.run()` — the old async wrapper was a second, unnecessary layer once there's no `await`ed UI form left inside.
- **The entry-point diagnostic (`Log.warn("pay", "handlePayCommand invoked: ...")`) was kept**, right at the top of the new function — still the fastest way to conclusively tell, next time, whether the handler is even being reached at all.
- **The legacy `pay:pay` scriptevent relay case (still present in the master relay switch, left untouched since the satellite retirement at 0.9.214) continues to call `handlePayCommand` with the same two-argument shape** — fully compatible with the new synchronous signature, no changes needed there.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; confirmed zero remaining references to `resolveSinglePlayerArg` or the now-dead `CustomCommandParamType7` import (also removed — unused, a leftover import with no call sites anywhere in the file). BP manifest bumped to 0.9.226 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly to confirm the new handler is present and the old confirm-dialog code is genuinely gone from the built output, not just the source. **Stated honestly**: if the original "Syntax error" really was a native, upstream parser rejection unrelated to anything inside the old handler's logic, this rebuild's simplification may not change that outcome on its own — but it removes an entire class of internal complexity (the async confirm round-trip) as a possible contributing factor, and the kept diagnostic will immediately show whether the new, minimal command is being reached when tested next.

**Wild mob-spawner pickup exploit fixed: the player can no longer choose the mob type (BP 0.9.227)**, reported directly: "When picking up an Mob Spawner, it is easily exploitable; The player selects which spawner they want. It must automatically determine/gauge/check what spawner they are picking up." Researched via a background sub-agent first, per direct instruction, to map the exact pickup flow before touching anything. Confirmed: `openWildSpawnerMenu` showed a free-choice `ModalFormData.dropdown` of all 7 supported mob types (`MOB_PICKUP_OPTIONS`) after the shift-click confirm, and gave `epitaph:generator_${chosen.type}` purely from that dropdown answer — the actual `minecraft:mob_spawner` block object was only ever used to confirm one still existed at that location, never interrogated for what mob it's actually configured to spawn. A player could shift-click any common zombie dungeon spawner and walk away with a Blaze generator (the rarest, most valuable type) just by picking "Blaze" in the menu.

- **No stable `@minecraft/server` component exposes a `minecraft:mob_spawner` block's real configured mob** — confirmed by exhaustive grep, this codebase never had any such reader, and a spawner's actual identity lives in block-entity NBT (`EntityIdentifier`), not a permutation/state `getComponent` can reach. The only working technique found: probe it live via `execute if data block <x> <y> <z> {EntityIdentifier:"<id>"} run <cmd>` — Bedrock's own NBT-match execute subcommand — tried once per each of this addon's 7 supported mob types (`MOB_ENTITY_TYPES`, already defined for the generator system), reading the outer command's own success count to tell which one actually matched the physical block.
- **New `detectSpawnerMobType(dim, loc)`** (right before `openWildSpawnerMenu`) loops `MOB_ENTITY_TYPES`, running the probe against each real entity id; the probe's side-effect-free subcommand (`scoreboard players set epitaph_probe epitaph_probe 1`, a dummy objective lazily created once via `ensureSpawnerProbeObjective`) is wrapped in try/catch per-candidate — this deliberately doesn't assume whether `Dimension.runCommand` throws on a failed `execute if` condition or merely returns `successCount: 0` (this file has been burned before by exactly this kind of unconfirmed live-vs-documented command behavior, most extensively across the 0.9.176-0.9.183 sign-placement saga), since either way the candidate is correctly treated as "no match, try the next one."
- **If no candidate ever matches — an uncommon vanilla mob type not in this addon's 7 supported types, or a real spawner whose `EntityIdentifier` carries extra suffix data an exact-string-match probe can't see through — pickup is refused outright, not guessed.** The block is left intact, the player gets a plain "couldn't identify this spawner's mob type" message, and `Log.warn` records the exact location for follow-up — never falls back to a default/lowest-value type, since that would just be a differently-shaped version of the same exploit (a mismatch between the physical block and the item given out), only less severe.
- **`openWildSpawnerMenu` simplified**: the entire `ModalFormData` mob-type dropdown step is gone — after the confirm button, detection happens automatically and the resulting `type`/label (via the already-existing `genTypeLabel()` helper, reused instead of the old hand-written per-option label strings) drive the exact same give-item logic as before (`nameTag`, overflow-drop fallback, success message, sound).
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.227 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly to confirm `MOB_PICKUP_OPTIONS` is genuinely gone from the built output and `detectSpawnerMobType`/`ensureSpawnerProbeObjective` are genuinely present, not just the source. **Stated honestly, not oversold**: `execute if data block` and the `EntityIdentifier` NBT key name are both documented, high-confidence Bedrock mechanics, but neither has been confirmed against this specific live engine build the way, say, the sign-placement fix eventually was (via a real thrown error) — the fail-safe design (refuse pickup rather than guess) means a false-negative here is a usability annoyance, not a re-opened exploit, but it's still the one thing to actually verify in-game next: shift-click a genuine vanilla dungeon spawner of a known type (e.g. a zombie spawner) and confirm the item received actually matches, not just that pickup succeeds at all.

**Wild spawner detection redone from scratch: the 0.9.227 "fix" was structurally non-functional, replaced with indefinite background observation via real spawn events (BP 0.9.228).** Per direct instruction, used a background sub-agent to do real web research (not memory) before touching code again, since the prior fix had never been live-verified. The research was decisive and alarming: **Bedrock has no `if data` execute condition and no `/data` command at all** — confirmed against the Bedrock Wiki, Minecraft Wiki (which explicitly flags `if data` as "exclusive to Java Edition"), and Microsoft's own official docs repo. This means 0.9.227's `execute if data block ... {EntityIdentifier:"..."}` probe threw `CommandError` on literally every single attempt, unconditionally — not a rare miss, a guaranteed failure every time — so that fix could never have actually detected anything; every pickup would have silently fallen through to "couldn't identify this spawner." No stable `@minecraft/server` component, structure API, or command exposes a placed vanilla spawner's configured mob at all (the one real fix for this, `CarryOverBlockEntityDataFunction`, is experimental-channel-only, not usable on this addon's pinned stable 2.8.0).

The one technique the research found with genuine primary-source backing on the stable channel: `world.afterEvents.entitySpawn` (confirmed real and stable via Microsoft's own `EntitySpawnAfterEvent` reference) — actually watch what a spawner physically produces, rather than trying to read its NBT at all. Presented this tradeoff to the user directly (instant NBT read is impossible; the only real alternative trades speed for a background wait) rather than silently picking an approach. **Direct instruction received: "When a spawner is within activation range, it will indefinitely test and check what it spawns. This will be how ALL spawners are properly detected accurately."**

- **New passive, addon-wide detection system** (Wild Spawner Pick-Up section, `main.js`): `wildSpawnerWatch` (in-memory `Map`, keyed by dimension+coordinates) tracks every wild spawner a player has ever encountered. A location enters the watch list the moment a player either looks at it (WAILA's `wailaBuildBlockInfo`, the primary "ALL spawners" discovery path — merely aiming the crosshair at one now silently starts tracking it, matching "within activation range" without needing an explicit action) or shift-right-clicks it (`registerWildSpawnerWatch`, cheap and idempotent — a no-op if already watched or already confirmed).
- **`world.afterEvents.entitySpawn`** (new, wired into `initWildSpawnerDetection()` in the world-load bootstrap, right after `initWailaSystem()`) checks every entity spawn in the world against every currently-watched location: within `WILD_SPAWNER_MATCH_RADIUS` (8 blocks — vanilla's own default `SpawnRange` is 4, doubled for buffer against a custom-configured spawner without going so wide it starts catching unrelated encounters), matching dimension, and — critically — re-confirms the block at that exact position is STILL a real `minecraft:mob_spawner` before crediting anything, so a location that's since been mined/replaced can never accrue false credit. A cause filter (`ev.cause === "Spawned"`, compared as a plain string rather than an imported enum member — this file is a pre-built bundle, and adding a new import risks disturbing its structure) excludes bred/transformed/admin-spawned entities.
- **Never trusts a single observation.** A tally per watched location (`counts[mobType]`) only locks in a confirmed type once the SAME mob has been seen `WILD_SPAWNER_CONFIRM_THRESHOLD` (3) times near that exact spot — a deliberate defense against the one real accuracy risk research flagged: dungeons/mineshafts (where spawners live) are exactly the dark, spawner-adjacent conditions vanilla's own ambient hostile spawning also favors, so a single coincidental nearby spawn could otherwise be mistaken for the spawner's own output. Once confirmed, the type is written to a permanent dynamic property (`wsp_type_<dim>_<x>_<y>_<z>`) — detection for that location, and every future interaction with it (by any player), is then instant.
- **Genuinely persists across restarts**: the watch list itself (`wsp_watch_<dim>_<x>_<y>_<z>` marker properties) is rebuilt from `World.getDynamicPropertyIds()` on world load (`loadWildSpawnerWatchList`, the same enumeration technique `getAllKnownPlayers`/`getGroupMembers` already established) — only the in-progress observation TALLY resets to zero on a restart (kept in-memory only, stated honestly as an accepted simplification rather than silently glossed over), the set of "which locations are being watched" does not.
- **`openWildSpawnerMenu` rewritten around this**: if a type is already confirmed, the confirm dialog now shows it up front ("Detected: Blaze") and Pick Up gives that exact item — re-reading the confirmed type a second time right before the actual give (not just trusting the snapshot taken before the dialog's own `await`), free insurance against a stale read. If not yet confirmed, the dialog explains detection is running in the background, registers the watch (in case the player interacted before ever looking at it via WAILA), and does not destroy the block or give anything — no guessing, no fallback default, exactly as demanded.
- **Periodic hygiene**: `wildSpawnerSweepStale` (every ~30s) drops a watch entry (and its dynamic property) for a spawner confirmed gone by some means OTHER than this menu's own pickup flow (explosion, admin edit, a plot reset) — conservative like every other cache-prune in this file, only acting when the block is confirmed loaded and confirmed no longer a spawner, never guessing away an entry whose chunk simply isn't loaded right now.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; confirmed zero remaining references to the old, structurally-broken `execute if data`-based functions (`detectSpawnerMobType`, `ensureSpawnerProbeObjective`) anywhere in the file. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.228 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script rework), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for every new function/hook (`initWildSpawnerDetection`, `registerWildSpawnerWatch`, `wildSpawnerObserveSpawn`, `wildSpawnerSweepStale`, the `entitySpawn.subscribe` wiring) rather than trusting the source alone. **Stated honestly**: this trades instant pickup for a real, sometimes multi-minute background wait — inherent to the fact that no faster, guess-free mechanism exists on the stable API at all, not a shortcut taken here. In-game verification is the user's to run next: look at (or shift-click) a genuine wild spawner, confirm WAILA shows "Mob type: detecting..." at first, stay or leave the area, and confirm it eventually flips to a confirmed type matching what the spawner actually produces — then confirm Pick Up gives the correctly-matching generator item.

**Wild spawner pickup switched from a shift-click confirm menu to breaking with Silk Touch, matching real ore-mining conventions (BP 0.9.229).** Directly requested, pointing at a real published Bedrock addon, "Silk Touch Spawners" (CurseForge, author sirob), asking to switch to "the method this addon uses." Researched that specific addon via a background sub-agent before copying anything — the finding fully validated the existing detection system rather than replacing it:

- **That addon doesn't actually solve "read a spawner's real mob type" any better than this addon's own `entitySpawn`-watching system does.** It requires Bedrock's experimental "Beta APIs" toggle (confirmed across multiple independent fetches of its own CurseForge page/FAQ) — meaning it isn't usable on this addon's pinned stable `@minecraft/server` 2.8.0 regardless. Re-checked `CarryOverBlockEntityDataFunction` specifically (the one real "carry block data to a dropped item" mechanism) — still experimental-only, and critically, even it only ever copies a content pack's OWN previously-self-written per-block data, never a vanilla spawner's real native NBT — there is still no API anywhere, stable or experimental, that reads a placed spawner's actual configured mob.
- **Confirmed directly against Mojang's own `bedrock-samples` repo**: vanilla `minecraft:mob_spawner` has no loot table anywhere in the game's own data, and vanilla block *behavior* (unlike textures) isn't overridable JSON at all for add-ons — the block is hardcoded to drop nothing at all when broken, Silk Touch or not. So "drops as an item" was never something to inherit from that addon's technique either; it has to be built entirely via this addon's own break-event handling, on the stable API, regardless of which addon's UX pattern is being followed.
- **What's genuinely worth adopting, and what changed**: the TRIGGER for using an already-detected result — survival-natural "mine it with a Silk Touch tool" instead of a bespoke shift-click confirm dialog. The underlying detection (background `entitySpawn` watching, confirm-after-3-matching-observations, WAILA-driven discovery) is completely unchanged.
- **New `hasSilkTouch(itemStack)`** reads the breaking tool's enchantments the same way every other enchantment check in this file already does (`comp.getEnchantments().find(e => e.type.id === "...")`, matching `getSharpnessBonus`/`getKnockbackEnchantLevel`'s own established convention — bare ids, no `minecraft:` prefix).
- **New `world.beforeEvents.playerBreakBlock` handler** (right after the existing generator-removal handler): if the broken block isn't a `minecraft:mob_spawner`, or the tool has no Silk Touch, nothing happens at all — vanilla behavior (block gone, nothing dropped) is left completely untouched for both cases, exactly matching real vanilla spawner mining today. With Silk Touch: the break is always cancelled (`ev.cancel = true`) and replaced with a fully manual swap, so vanilla's own break/drop logic never runs and there's nothing to accidentally duplicate. If the type isn't confirmed yet, the block is left standing entirely and the player is told detection is still running — never destroys value on a guess, the same rule the old menu enforced, just applied to breaking instead of a button. If confirmed, the block converts to air and the exact matching generator item is given.
- **`openWildSpawnerMenu` removed entirely** — no longer needed now that pickup isn't menu-driven. Its give-item logic was extracted into a shared `grantWildSpawnerItem(player, loc, type)`, called from the new break handler (only one place that logic lives now, not duplicated). Shift-right-click is now a lightweight `showWildSpawnerStatus` — a plain chat message reporting "detected: X, break with Silk Touch" or "still detecting," with no form at all — kept only as a discoverability path for a player who hasn't noticed WAILA's own status line or has WAILA toggled off; the existing interact debounce (0.9.221) is reused so a double-firing interaction can't send the status message twice.
- **WAILA's own mob-spawner readout updated** ("Shift-click to pick up" → "Break with Silk Touch to pick up") to match the real new mechanic.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; confirmed zero remaining references to `openWildSpawnerMenu` anywhere in the file (one stale historical comment cross-reference elsewhere in the file, unrelated to any live call site, was also corrected to point at the new `grantWildSpawnerItem`). BP manifest bumped to 0.9.229 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script rework), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly to confirm `hasSilkTouch`/`grantWildSpawnerItem`/`showWildSpawnerStatus`/the new `playerBreakBlock` subscription are genuinely present and `openWildSpawnerMenu` is genuinely gone from the built output, not just the source. In-game verification is still the user's to run next: confirm breaking a wild spawner WITHOUT Silk Touch behaves exactly like plain vanilla (block gone, nothing given); confirm breaking one WITH Silk Touch before detection completes leaves the block standing with a status message; and confirm breaking a confirmed one WITH Silk Touch gives the correctly-matching generator item.

**Wild spawner detection accuracy hardened — a real attribution bug fixed, and a probabilistic false-positive risk reduced (BP 0.9.230).** Asked directly whether the system 100% guarantees the right type gets picked up. Honest answer given: no — it's a heuristic inferring type from observed behavior, never a read of ground truth (nothing on Bedrock, stable or experimental, exposes that). Two concrete gaps identified and addressed, on "do what you need to do, I want minimal player-facing oddities":

1. **Real bug, not just a probability concern: crediting the wrong nearby spawner.** `wildSpawnerObserveSpawn`'s old loop `break`-ed on the first watched location it found in range, in arbitrary `Map` iteration order — if two watched spawners of *different* types both sat within the match radius of the same spawn point (a real dungeon/mineshaft layout), a spawn could get credited to the wrong one outright. Fixed structurally: every spawn is now credited to the **nearest** in-range watched spawner by real 3D distance (`nearestKey`/`nearestDistSq`), never an arbitrary one.
2. **Ambient-spawn false positives, reduced without slowing down the common case.** Dungeons/mineshafts are exactly the dark conditions vanilla's own ambient hostile spawning also favors, so a run of coincidental unrelated spawns near a watched spawner could theoretically out-race the real spawner's own output to a raw confirm threshold. Replaced the flat "3 observations and you're confirmed" rule with a count-AND-margin rule: the leading type now needs both `WILD_SPAWNER_CONFIRM_MIN_COUNT` (3) observations **and** a lead of `WILD_SPAWNER_CONFIRM_LEAD_MARGIN` (3) over whatever the next-most-observed type is. In the ordinary, unambiguous case — one isolated spawner, zero competing observations — this confirms exactly as fast as before (a runner-up sitting at 0 already satisfies the margin the instant the leader hits 3), so normal players see no slowdown at all; it only actually takes longer in the rarer case where a real competing type is also being observed nearby, which is exactly the ambiguous situation that needed a firmer signal before locking in.
3. **Match radius split and tightened**: horizontal `WILD_SPAWNER_MATCH_RADIUS_H` (6, down from a flat 8) and a separately-tighter vertical `WILD_SPAWNER_MATCH_RADIUS_V` (4) — an unrelated cave layer sitting well above or below the spawner is now less likely to feed false candidates into the tally at all, while still comfortably covering vanilla's real default `SpawnRange` of 4.
- **Stated plainly in the code's own header comment now, not just here**: this still cannot reach a literal 100% guarantee — that would require an API that doesn't exist on Bedrock at all, stable or experimental. What changed is that the one real, structural bug (wrong-spawner attribution) is fixed outright, and the probabilistic risk (ambient false positives) is meaningfully reduced without materially slowing down the normal, unambiguous case.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; confirmed zero remaining references to the old flat `WILD_SPAWNER_MATCH_RADIUS`/`WILD_SPAWNER_CONFIRM_THRESHOLD` constants anywhere in the file. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.230 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script hardening), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the new constants and nearest-spawner logic, not just the source. In-game verification is still the user's to run next — the two fixes are hardest to observe directly (they matter specifically in dense/dark multi-spawner rooms), but the basic flow (isolated spawner → confirms in about the same time as before → Silk Touch break gives the correct matching item) should behave identically to 0.9.229.

**Plot claim exhaustion made structurally impossible at the reported numbers, via a self-healing safety net — root cause not conclusively pinned down, fixed by construction instead (BP 0.9.231).** Reported live: "region exhausted (650-29900 band full)" with cursor #143 (~1,000 blocks out) against maxRadius/borderLimit both 29900, and only ~20 real plots claimed. Worked the actual exhaustion math by hand against these exact numbers before writing any code: neither of `advanceCursorAt`'s two real "nothing left" conditions can produce this outcome — genuine ring-distance exhaustion needs the cursor index around ~218,000 (ring ~234) at this maxRadius, not index 143 (ring 6); the scan-budget-exceeded case needs the cursor to have already advanced by tens of thousands of positions across its retry passes, not sat at 143. Every other input (border, exclusion zone, occupancy) checks out fine at that distance too — by this system's own math, the search should simply have found a free cell and succeeded. Re-verified there's only one copy of every relevant function (`claimPlot`, `advanceCursorAtOnePass`, `advanceCursorAt`, `noSlots`, `plotExhaustionDiagnostics`) — no duplicate/dead code anywhere that could explain a divergent result.

Leading theory, not provable from source alone: a stale, still-active OLDER pack version left over from this session's many rapid mid-session reimports — this exact deployment gotcha already hit this addon once before (BP 0.9.180 — "two versions loaded simultaneously," found via duplicate command-registration errors). Rather than bet the fix entirely on that one diagnosis, added a genuine safety net that makes the reported symptom impossible **by construction**, regardless of which cause turns out to be true:

- **New `claimSafetyNetScan(cursorKey)`** (`main.js`, right after `advancePrescanCursor`) — a deliberately dumb, freshly-executed, self-contained linear scan (up to `CLAIM_SAFETY_NET_SCAN` = 5,000 cells) starting from wherever the cursor currently sits, checking the exact same in-band/non-excluded/in-border/unclaimed conditions `advanceCursorAtOnePass` already uses, but completely independent of whatever state (retry-pass history, prior verdicts) produced the original "exhausted" result. It re-reads every input fresh from the live dynamic properties rather than trusting anything cached from the failed attempt.
- **Runs immediately before either exhaustion alert can ever fire.** `noSlots()` (previously just the alert) now calls `claimSafetyNetScan` first — if it finds a free cell, that's claimed silently (no fresh terrain check, matching this system's own existing last-resort-tier precedent of "a plot is better than no plot") and only logs that the safety net caught it; the scary "region exhausted" alert only actually goes out if the safety net *also* comes up empty, at which point it's trustworthy.
- **Deduped the two copies of the exhaustion alert** — `tryOceanAvoidingFallback`'s own inline copy is now just `noSlots()` instead of a second, hand-maintained duplicate of the same alert text. This closes off the exact bug class that caused BP 0.9.219 in the first place (one of two copies got a diagnostic fix, the other silently didn't) — there is now only one definition of "declare exhaustion," used everywhere this can happen.
- **Stated honestly, not oversold**: this does not identify or fix whatever the actual root cause was — if it really is a stale pack, that's still worth checking and correcting in World Settings → Behavior Packs → Active. What this guarantees is that *this specific reported symptom* — a claim failing while a free cell plainly exists near the cursor — cannot recur under the current script, regardless of whatever upstream state produced a bogus exhaustion signal.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.231 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `claimSafetyNetScan`/`CLAIM_SAFETY_NET_SCAN`/the safety-net log line, not just the source. In-game verification is still the user's to run next: reproduce the same claim attempt on the current build and confirm it now succeeds (via the safety net, logged in the Content Log) rather than reporting exhaustion — and separately, still worth checking World Settings → Behavior Packs → Active for a stale pack version, since that's the leading theory for why this happened at all.

**Comprehensive diagnostics system added: version tagging, a boot-time state dump, and an on-demand admin command — proactive infrastructure, not a reaction to a single bug (BP 0.9.232).** On direct instruction: "we need fully actionable and working, fully outputting build. Output everything that happens, especially when it fails without fully known causes. Think ahead by many leaps and bounds." Prompted directly by the prior turn's exhaustion investigation, where the leading theory (a stale, still-active older pack version) couldn't be confirmed or ruled out from source alone — there was no fast way to check "which version is actually running" or get a live server-state snapshot without reproducing a failure first and asking the user to paste one chat line.

- **`ADDON_VERSION` baked directly into the log prefix**, not just the manifest — `PREFIX` is now `` `[Epitaph v${ADDON_VERSION}]` `` instead of a bare `"[Epitaph]"`. Every single `Log.info`/`Log.warn`/`Log.error` call anywhere in this 21,000+ line file — hundreds of existing call sites — now automatically carries the exact running version, for free, with zero call sites needing to change. Directly closes the blind spot behind at least two confusing incidents this session (the 0.9.180 duplicate-pack saga, and the just-shipped 0.9.231 exhaustion mystery): any future Content Log paste now conclusively shows which build actually produced it. **Must be bumped by hand alongside `manifest.json`'s own version every time going forward** — nothing exposes manifest contents to the Script API, so this can only ever be kept in sync manually; noted plainly in the constant's own comment as a standing practice, not assumed to be remembered.
- **New `buildEpitaphDiagnosticsReport()`** (`main.js`, right after `plotExhaustionDiagnostics`) — one shared report covering: the plot claiming band (cursor position, implied distance, min/max radius, border limit, percent-full estimate, total plots claimed), the world border, generator counts (active vs. ever-registered), wild spawner detection state (pending watch-list size plus a confirmed count via a one-time `getDynamicPropertyIds` scan), Raid Night status, and total dynamic-property storage usage. Deliberately read-only — reads the raw `plot_next_id` property directly rather than calling `nextPlotID()` (which increments and consumes an ID as a side effect).
- **Proactive self-check baked into the same report**: if the plot max radius and the world border's own containment limit don't match (within a small tolerance), the report surfaces a `⚠` warning line naming both numbers and the fix — catching the exact class of border/band desync this file has already hit twice before (BP 0.9.172/0.9.173) the moment the world loads, rather than waiting for a player's claim to fail first.
- **Two consumers of the one shared function**, deliberately not two hand-maintained copies (the exact drift that caused BP 0.9.219's bug in the first place): a boot-time dump wired into `PlotsModule.onWorldLoad()` right after `initPortalProtection()` (delayed 200 ticks / 10s so `spawnOriginX`/`spawnOriginZ` have resolved even on a genuinely fresh world), logged via `Log.warn` — always visible regardless of the `PLOT_DEBUG_LOGGING` toggle — so every fresh Content Log session has a real baseline snapshot to compare against; and a new **`/epitaph diag`** admin command (added to the `epitaph:sub` enum, its own switch case, and `/epitaph help`) for checking the exact same live state at any later point, on demand, without needing to reproduce a failure first.
- **The prior turn's safety-net recovery logging upgraded from `sysLog` to `Log.warn`**, in both `claimSafetyNetScan` and `noSlots`'s recovery branch — these are genuinely anomalous events (the normal search should never have needed rescuing in the first place) and must never be silenced by `PLOT_DEBUG_LOGGING` being toggled off the way routine debug chatter can be, matching this file's own established convention (`permDebugSnapshot`, `handlePayCommand`'s entry log) for exactly this class of "should always be visible" diagnostic.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.232 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the version constant, the templated log prefix, `buildEpitaphDiagnosticsReport`, the new `diag` switch case, and the boot-log line, not just the source. In-game verification is still the user's to run next: confirm every Content Log line now shows `[Epitaph v0.9.232]`; confirm a boot-time diagnostic dump appears ~10 seconds after world load; and confirm `/epitaph diag` (admin-only) prints the full report on demand.

**Plot claiming migrated from silent automatic assignment to player-chosen biome selection (BP 0.9.233).** On direct instruction: "migrate from automatic claiming to Player input. They can choose from all Biomes in the Overworld; From Plains Biome to Oceans. Nothing like Pale Oak and rare biomes like that."
