Researched real, current Bedrock biome IDs via a background sub-agent before writing any category data — this project has been burned repeatedly by guessing Bedrock identifiers (the sign-placement saga most of all), and biome IDs genuinely diverge from what Java-familiar naming would suggest: Bedrock's Mountains biome is internally `extreme_hills` (not `windswept_hills`), Swamp's real biome ID is `swampland` (the *tag* is `swamp` — easy to conflate), Snowy Tundra is `ice_plains`, Dark Forest is `roofed_forest`, and Badlands uses the `mesa` family of IDs. Cross-confirmed against Mojang's own `bedrock-samples` repo, Mojang's Biome JSON docs, and the Bedrock-specific biome-tag reference with no contradictions.

Two design forks were confirmed directly before implementing, given the size of this change: **both** an automatic menu on first join and a self-serve command should trigger biome choice (not one or the other); and if a chosen biome genuinely can't be found nearby, the search should **expand automatically** rather than asking the player to pick again or silently proceeding with a mismatch.

- **11 player-facing categories** (`PLOT_BIOME_CATEGORIES`): Plains, Forest, Desert, Ocean, Jungle, Taiga, Savanna, Badlands, Swamp, Snowy/Ice, Mountains — each backed by a real ID `Set` (reusing the existing, already-proven `OCEAN_BIOME_IDS` verbatim for Ocean) plus, where the research confirmed a clean, non-overlapping `minecraft:tags` value exists, a secondary tag check via `Biome.hasTags([...])` (the same method already proven working in this file for ocean detection) — used as a broadening supplement, never a replacement for the explicit ID set. Mountains deliberately has no tag check at all: the real `mountains` tag also covers Cherry Grove/Grove/Meadow, which would have silently let excluded rare biomes count as a match.
- **`PLOT_EXCLUDED_BIOME_IDS`** — Pale Garden, Cherry Grove, Mangrove Swamp, Mushroom Island (+ shore), Deep Dark, Lush Caves, Dripstone Caves, Grove, Meadow, Ice Spikes — a hard, defense-in-depth veto checked directly inside `candidateBiomeMatchesCategory`, mirroring the hard-ocean-veto pattern from BP 0.9.201. None of the category sets above actually contain these, so this should never matter in practice, but it's checked explicitly rather than relying solely on "not listed" by omission.
- **New biome-scanning subsystem**, kept fully separate from the existing, heavily-tested terrain-search path rather than interleaved with it (minimizes regression risk to code that's been fixed multiple times this session): `forceLoadAndCheckBiome` (a cheap, single-point force-load+biome-read, mirroring `forceLoadAndCheckTerrain`'s own ticking-area+poll technique but skipping the expensive multi-point grid walk entirely — this only needs to know what biome sits there, not whether it's buildable); `scanForBiomeMatches` (an independent scan over the same `spiralCell(n)` numbering the shared claim cursor uses, but deliberately **not touching or advancing `plot_spiral_cursor` at all** — safe, since `claimCell`/`freeCell` already key purely off cell coordinates, never cursor position, so a cell claimed this way is automatically skipped later when the shared cursor eventually reaches the same ring); `runBiomeDrivenClaim` (the multi-round driver — pulls a batch of biome-matching candidates, terrain-evaluates them batched the same way the default path already does, prefers strict over lenient, and genuinely **expands the search** across up to 6 rounds / ~96 terrain-tested candidates before ever giving up, per the confirmed design choice).
- **Never leaves a player stuck**: if the expanded search truly exhausts the band with no biome match at either terrain tier, the player is told honestly and the claim falls through to the exact same `claimPlot(player)` (no category) that every other claim already uses — complete with its own existing terrain fallback tiers and the BP 0.9.231 safety net. A biome preference can make a claim take longer or occasionally not be honored; it can never make a claim fail outright.
- **`claimPlot(player, categoryKey)`** gained an optional second parameter — completely backward compatible, since every existing call site (the admin `/claim <player>` recovery command, the "No preference" menu option, and the biome search's own final fallback) calls it with no second argument and falls through to the original, unmodified logic exactly as before.
- **Two trigger points, per the confirmed design**: the first-join auto-claim (`world.afterEvents.playerSpawn`) now opens `openPlotBiomeChoiceMenu` instead of silently calling `claimPlot` — never auto-teleports either way, unchanged. A new player-facing `/plot claim` (added to the `epitaph:plot_sub` enum as `"claim"`, dispatched to a **new** `"selfclaim"` case — deliberately distinct from the existing admin-only `"claim"` dispatch case, which still targets another player by name and is completely unaffected) lets a player who dismissed the first-join menu, or wants to reconsider, reopen the same choice at any time before they actually have a plot.
- **A "No preference" option is included in the menu** — deliberately, not an oversight — running the exact original, faster, terrain-only search unchanged, for a player who doesn't care which biome they land in. Its button label was caught and shortened before shipping (`BUTTON_LABEL_SAFE_CHARS` = 22 — this file has been burned by silent button-label truncation multiple times before, see BP 0.9.135) — the "fastest" detail moved into the body text instead, which has no per-button width limit.
- **Guide book page 3 ("Your Plot") rewritten** — it flatly said "Auto-claimed on your first join. No command needed," which is now false. Updated to name `/plot claim` and re-verified under the 256-byte cap via real UTF-8 byte length (254 bytes, not `.length` — the exact distinction that bit this project twice before).
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed after every stage. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest — the very first real test of the "must be kept in sync manually" rule established one version ago (BP 0.9.232), which is proof it needs a memorized standing practice rather than being trusted to be remembered on its own. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.233 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for every new symbol (category data, all four new functions, the new dispatch case, the version bump), not just the source. In-game verification is still the user's to run next: confirm the biome-choice menu appears on a genuinely fresh player's first join; confirm `/plot claim` reopens it for a player without a plot; confirm choosing a common biome (e.g. Ocean, Desert) lands a plot in roughly the right terrain within a reasonable wait; and — harder to test deliberately — confirm a rare/absent-nearby biome choice eventually either finds a real match or honestly falls back rather than hanging indefinitely.

**Mob generator/spawner bugs: two confirmed and fixed, one honestly inconclusive after real research (BP 0.9.234).** Reported directly, three symptoms together: "Mob spawners do not work with water," "Mob spawners spawn ontop of the spawner," "Mob spawner effects don't disappear after picking it up."

- **Root-caused the first two as ONE bug, not two.** `findMobSpawnSpots` (the flood-fill `tickMobGenerator` uses to pick a legal spawn position within a generator's containment box) only ever treated `Block.isAir` as passable — a real WATER block (any farm/grinder design that funnels mobs with water, or simply a puddle in the room) counted as "solid wall, don't expand past it," identical to hitting stone. Two compounding effects: the fill could never reach floor space on the far side of any water, and a water-covered tile was never counted as usable even with a genuine solid floor beneath it. Critically, the generator's own output tile (directly above itself) is always the very first cell examined, and — since it's air with the generator's own block as a solid floor beneath — is always a valid spot on its own; when water blocked everything else in the room, that one tile became the only (or dominant) entry in the candidate list, so random selection from it produced exactly "spawns on top of the spawner." One fix closes both: `isPassableForMobSpawn` now also accepts real water (deliberately not lava — a mob spawning into lava would just die, not what a grinder wants) for both flood-fill expansion and as a spot's own surface, while `isSolidMobSpawnFloor` still requires a genuine solid floor beneath (not air, not liquid) — a two-block-deep water column still correctly finds no floor to stand on, so this doesn't turn a genuinely unenclosed shaft into a false positive.
- **Third symptom investigated honestly, not guessed at.** Checked and ruled out every plausible code-side cause first: the idle-placeholder particle loop (0.9.125) explicitly only applies to non-mob/block generators, never spawners; the "Show Spawn Radius" debug overlay already correctly self-heals on generator removal (verified directly in code — `gen_active_` is cleared by `removeGenerator`, and the render loop checks exactly that flag every pass); the custom `epitaph:generator_<mobtype>` block's own JSON defines zero particle/animation components, just a static texture. With every code-side theory ruled out, used a background research pass rather than guess further — found no documented Bedrock bug or fix for "script-removed mob_spawner block leaves its flame/cage visual rendering." The best-supported explanation from research: Bedrock particles simulate independently once emitted and don't retroactively despawn just because their source block disappears — meaning a few already-emitted particle frames finishing their own lifetime is likely normal (if visually odd, especially since a scripted removal swaps the block while the player is still staring right at it) rather than a genuine bug with a code-level fix. **Stated honestly rather than papered over**: no confirmed fix exists. Applied one cheap, safe hedge anyway — the Silk Touch pickup handler's block removal switched from `block.setType("minecraft:air")` to a command-based `setblock x y z air` (functionally identical, no drop, same as before — falls back to the old `setType` call if the command form ever throws), on the unconfirmed chance the two removal paths signal the change to clients differently. This is explicitly not claimed as a fix, just a zero-risk thing worth having tried.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest (now a confirmed standing habit across two consecutive versions). BP manifest bumped to 0.9.234 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `isPassableForMobSpawn`/`isSolidMobSpawnFloor` and the new command-based removal, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: build (or find) a mob generator whose containment box includes water and confirm mobs now spawn spread across the room instead of clustering on the generator itself; and re-check whether the flame/cage visual still lingers after a Silk Touch pickup now that removal goes through a command — if it does, that's real, independent confirmation this is Bedrock's own particle behavior and not something fixable from this addon's script.

**Mob generator "effects" clarified and re-investigated: the owned, placed custom spawner, not the wild vanilla one (BP 0.9.235).** Direct correction after 0.9.234: the previous turn's hedge (command-based block removal) targeted the wrong system entirely — the wild Silk-Touch vanilla spawner pickup flow, not the player's own already-picked-up, placeable `epitaph:generator_<mobtype>` custom block this report is actually about.

Traced every continuous visual effect tied to a mob generator specifically (there are exactly two): the ambient flame-particle loop (`ShopModule.onWorldLoad`, every 5 ticks, iterates `mobGenIds`) and the opt-in "Show Spawn Radius" debug box (`genRadiusDebugPlayers`, every 4 ticks). Both already gate on `gen_active_`/`gen_is_mob_` on every single pass, and both `removeGenerator` (the in-Hub "Remove Generator?" flow) and `cleanupOrphanedGenerator` (non-standard destruction — commands/WorldEdit/admin edits) already clear `gen_active_` synchronously the instant they run — so neither loop should be able to outlive a removal by more than its own poll interval, a quarter-second at most. Also checked and ruled out: whether "effects" meant status/potion effects (grepped every `addEffect`/`removeEffect` call in the file — none are tied to generators anywhere); whether direct survival mining could bypass cleanup entirely (confirmed it can't — every `epitaph:generator_*` break is intercepted by a `playerBreakBlock` before-event that always cancels the natural break and routes through the same confirm-then-`removeGenerator` flow, no shortcut exists).

**One real, concrete gap found and fixed anyway, matching the "trigger a flag the instant it's picked up" fix requested directly**: the debug-radius overlay had no *explicit* stop at removal time at all — it purely relied on its own next poll noticing the flag was already gone, rather than being told immediately. New `stopGeneratorEffectsImmediately(genId)` (right before `removeGenerator`) removes any `genRadiusDebugPlayers` entries pointing at the generator being removed, called from both `removeGenerator` and `cleanupOrphanedGenerator` — the flag-based self-heal stays in place too as an independent second layer (a generator destroyed by admin/WorldEdit still needs it, since nothing else calls this new helper in that path either).

**Stated honestly**: the ambient flame-particle loop itself couldn't be shown to have an actual bug — it was already stopping within one 5-tick poll of removal via the same flag this fix targets, which is close to instant already. If the reported effect is specifically that flame/fire particle rather than the spawn-radius debug box, this pass doesn't change its timing at all (there was nothing broken to fix in that loop specifically), and the remaining possibility is the same "particles finish their own independent lifetime once emitted" behavior flagged honestly last version — a few already-emitted particles playing out for a fraction of a second regardless of how promptly the server-side flag is cleared.

- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest (third consecutive version keeping the two in sync). BP manifest bumped to 0.9.235 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `stopGeneratorEffectsImmediately` and its two call sites, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next — and if an effect still visibly lingers after this build, naming exactly which one (the ambient flame particles specifically, vs. the "Show Spawn Radius" debug box, vs. something not yet identified) would narrow this down conclusively rather than guessing a fourth time.

**Independent code-review pass on the newest systems, requested directly rather than reacting to a player report — found and fixed a real resource/stall risk in biome-driven plot claiming, plus workspace cleanup (BP 0.9.236).** Read `forceLoadAndCheckBiome`/`scanForBiomeMatches`/`runBiomeDrivenClaim` (BP 0.9.233) end to end against this file's own established discipline for anything that force-loads chunks, since every other such budget in this file (`MAX_TERRAIN_CANDIDATES`=60, `OCEAN_AVOIDANCE_MAX_ATTEMPTS`=20, `CLAIM_SAFETY_NET_SCAN`=5,000) was sized with real caution around how expensive a force-load actually is, on the same shared, vanilla-command-based 10-ticking-area pool this file already hit the real engine limit on once (BP 0.9.194) and deliberately moved shops/mailbox off of.

- **Found: `BIOME_SEARCH_SCAN_CAP` was 20,000, times `BIOME_SEARCH_MAX_ROUNDS`=6 — up to 120,000 individual force-load cycles (each a real `tickingarea add`/poll/`remove`) before the search gives up.** For a biome that's genuinely rare or absent within the reachable band, this could stall a claim for tens of minutes under a stuck "Claiming Plot..." title, contending with every other in-progress claim's own terrain check for the same 10-slot pool the whole time — a regression of the exact class 0.9.194 already fixed once, just for the newest system rather than the old one. Dropped `BIOME_SEARCH_SCAN_CAP` to 2,000 (an order of magnitude down, still generous per round), and added a new, real wall-clock backstop: `BIOME_SEARCH_MAX_TICKS` (1,200 ticks / ~60s), checked once per round in `runBiomeDrivenClaim` alongside the existing round-count cap — whichever limit is hit first ends the expanded search and falls through to the existing lenient-match-or-normal-claim fallback. This is what actually bounds worst-case wait time regardless of how fast chunks happen to load on a given server, rather than trusting cell-count caps alone to imply a bounded duration.
- **Found: a player disconnecting mid-biome-search had no guard at all.** The existing `playerLeave` handler only ever cleared the "Claiming Plot..." title interval — the actual `runBiomeDrivenClaim` search kept running to completion in the background regardless, and would eventually call `finalizeClaim`'s unwrapped `player.sendMessage`/`setDynamicProperty` calls against an invalid player object once it finished. New `playerGone()` helper (checks `player?.isValid`, matching this file's own established liveness-check idiom already used in the Java Combat and WAILA modules) is now checked at the top of every round and immediately before every finalize/fallback call inside `runBiomeDrivenClaim` — a disconnect now quietly abandons the search at the next checkpoint instead of running it to the end and throwing.
- **Workspace hygiene**: `Epitaph-Complete\` was documented (since 0.9.214) as left empty on purpose, but actually contained a stale `Epitaph-BP`/`Epitaph-RP` copy at version 0.9.217 — 18 versions behind current, almost certainly a leftover from an earlier rebuild-script run that was never cleaned up. Harmless to the real shipped `.mcaddon` (confirmed the actual build packages the current version correctly regardless), but deleted so it can't later be mistaken for a live copy.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.236 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the new cap value, `BIOME_SEARCH_MAX_TICKS`, and `playerGone()`, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: choosing a common biome should claim exactly as before (the tightened cap is still generous for anything actually nearby); deliberately choosing a biome absent from the reachable band should now visibly fall back well within a minute rather than potentially stalling far longer.

**Kit Disparity: added a per-pair retaliation window — a stronger player can now fight back once actually hit (BP 0.9.237).** Reported directly: "anyone below me can attack me relentlessly, and I cannot attack back. Once I get hit by someone, regardless if they are worse than me, I should be able to fight them for 20s, timer resets to 20s every time we hit one another." Root cause, confirmed by reading `DisparityModule`'s `entityHurt` handler directly: the block condition (`aScore - dScore >= DISPARITY_BLOCK_THRESHOLD`) evaluates each hit in complete isolation, with no memory of who hit whom first. A weaker player's attack against a stronger defender was never blocked (their score gap runs the other way) — but the stronger player's own counter-swing is a brand-new event with attacker/defender swapped, and the identical stateless rule always crossed the threshold and cancelled it. A defender in a fight they didn't start had no way to ever hit back at all — exactly the reported symptom, not a misconfigured threshold.

- **New `disparityFeudExpiry`** (`main.js`, right before `DisparityModule`) — an in-memory, per-unordered-pair 20-second window (`disparityPairKey`/`hasActiveDisparityFeud`/`refreshDisparityFeud`; deliberately mirrors the existing `PVP_TAG_MS` combat-tag duration, a separate system that gates teleports rather than the gear-gap check, but the same "20 seconds, refreshed by every hit" shape this project already established). The moment a hit actually **lands** between two specific players (a blocked swing never counts — only a real connect opens or extends the window), that exact pair is exempt from the gear-gap block against each other for the next 20s, refreshed on every further landed hit either direction. Stop trading hits for 20s and the exemption lapses — the next unprovoked strong-vs-weak hit is blocked again exactly as before, until the weaker player lands on them again to reopen it.
- **Deliberately scoped to the specific opponent pair, not a blanket per-player flag** — being in an active exchange with one weaker player doesn't grant a free pass to also start attacking a completely different, uninvolved weaker player; the retaliation right is tied to whoever actually hit you.
- **`entityHurt`'s block check** now reads `aScore - dScore >= DISPARITY_BLOCK_THRESHOLD && !hasActiveDisparityFeud(attacker.id, defender.id)` — an active window bypasses the block outright regardless of the score gap. A genuinely blocked hit `return`s immediately, before ever reaching the window-refresh line, so a swing that didn't land can't itself open or extend a feud; every hit that DOES land (either because it was never gated in the first place, or because an already-open window let a retaliation through) refreshes the pair's 20s window.
- **Cleanup**: the existing `playerLeave` handler (already clearing `disparityMsgCooldown`) now also drops every `disparityFeudExpiry` entry involving whoever just left, matching this file's own established discipline for per-session, in-memory-only state (not persisted — an active fight isn't a stat).
- **Disparity admin panel** (`/epitaph menu` → Disparity) gained a line documenting the new retaliation window alongside the existing block-threshold line, so an admin reading the panel sees the whole rule, not just half of it.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.237 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `disparityFeudExpiry`/`hasActiveDisparityFeud`/`refreshDisparityFeud` and the new bypass condition, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: have a lower-geared player hit a higher-geared one, confirm the higher-geared player can now hit back within the next 20 seconds, confirm trading hits keeps the window alive indefinitely, and confirm that going 20+ seconds without either side landing a hit re-arms the block against an unprovoked strong-vs-weak attack.

**Plot terrain detection made substantially more lenient across the board (BP 0.9.238), on direct instruction — "usually skips 20K potential places... way too many to skip," asking to double or triple the leniency rather than another small fraction nudge.** `evaluateTerrainCandidate`'s tolerance constants were tuned more than once already this project (0.9.199, 0.9.200), each time loosening incrementally after live testing showed the previous value still rejecting too much real, ordinary Overworld terrain — this pass went further in one step rather than repeating that same small-nudge pattern a third time:

- **`TERRAIN_STRICT_BAD_FRACTION`**: 0.15 → 0.40 (roughly tripled). A candidate is now only rejected once more than 40% of its sample points are genuinely bad (island overlap, mountain/hill too close to `PLOT_Y`, deep ravine, or ocean) — up from 15%.
- **`TERRAIN_LENIENT_BAD_FRACTION`**: 0.30 → 0.75 (2.5x). The last-resort tier now only rejects once over three-quarters of the footprint is bad.
- **`TERRAIN_CLEARANCE_BLOCKS`**: 5 → 2. Shrinks the safety-buffer band above the hard island-overlap line (`y >= PLOT_Y`, unconditional and untouched) — less ordinary rolling terrain now counts as "too high" in the first place, before the fraction tolerances above even get evaluated.
- **`TERRAIN_MIN_SAFE_Y`**: 10 → 4. Same idea for the "too low / ravine" side of the check.
- **`MAX_TERRAIN_CANDIDATES`**: 60 → 120 (doubled). Extra headroom on top of the loosened tolerances, for the now-much-rarer case a strict match still doesn't appear quickly.
- **Deliberately untouched**: the unconditional `y >= PLOT_Y` real-overlap check and the hard ocean-center veto (0.9.201, "plots should ALWAYS avoid being in the middle of an ocean") — both are direct-instruction guarantees from earlier sessions, not part of this ask, and a candidate that's genuinely, badly unsuitable (real structural overlap, or centered on open ocean) still can't pass under any of the loosened numbers above, since those two checks don't participate in the fractional tolerance at all.
- This function is shared by both the default claim path and the biome-driven claim path (0.9.233) — the loosening applies to both automatically, no separate change needed for either.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.238 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure constant/comment changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for all five new constant values, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: claim several new plots and confirm strict now succeeds far more often (check the Content Log's `[PlotSystem | CLAIM]`/`[PlotSystem | TERRAIN]` diagnostic lines for the real pass rate), and confirm overall claim time drops noticeably from however long it was taking before.

**Real Kit Dashboard UI added — bare `/kit` now opens a menu instead of requiring chat-command syntax (BP 0.9.239).** On direct request: "hard to understand with just chat commands... `/kit` opens a Dashboard, to see what Kits there are, click on a kit, it has BUY and Inspect, inspect lists all items and enchants within the kit." Built as a new front end onto the exact same logic the existing `/kit claim|buy|info` chat commands already run — `claimKit`/`buyKit`/`grantKitItems`/`formatKitItemLine` are all reused completely unchanged, so a purchase made from a button goes through the identical `showConfirm`-then-`pullShopSpending`-then-grant sequence the chat path always used; nothing about payment or item-granting was reimplemented.

- **`openKitDashboardMenu(player)`** — lists every kit the player is eligible for (same `!k.permTag || player.hasTag(k.permTag)` filter `listKits`/`resolveClaimableKit` already use), each button showing the kit name plus a short status line (`$cost`, `Free — Ready`, `Xh left`, or `Already claimed`). Tapping a kit opens `openKitDetailMenu`. Also gained a "Guide" button (`openFeatureGuide`) — the addon-wide in-menu-Guide pass (0.9.145) deliberately skipped Kit at the time specifically because it was "chat-text output, no actual menu to attach a button to"; that reason no longer applies now that a real menu exists.
- **`openKitDetailMenu(player, name)`** — re-resolves the kit fresh (not trusted from the dashboard render, in case an admin edited/deleted it or the player's tags changed in between) and shows cost, cooldown/one-time status, and current ready/cooldown state, with three buttons: **Buy** (paid kits) or **Claim** (free kits) — calls the existing `buyKit`/`claimKit` exactly as `/kit buy`/`/kit claim` already do, including their own confirm dialogs and re-validation; **Inspect**; and **◀ Back** to the dashboard. Matching this file's own established DDUI-migration precedent (no disabled-button support on `ActionFormData`), the Buy/Claim button stays tappable even mid-cooldown — `resolveClaimableKit` inside `buyKit`/`claimKit` already re-checks and messages the real reason if it's genuinely still blocked, so nothing is lost by not graying it out.
- **`openKitInspectMenu(player, name)`** — the direct answer to "inspect lists all items and enchants": renders every item via the existing `formatKitItemLine` (already handles custom names, enchantment names + Roman-numeral levels, and the three special-cased potion kits) as a plain read screen with a Back button returning to the detail screen.
- **`/kit`'s registration changed from a mandatory subcommand to two optional parameters** (`epitaph:kit_sub`, `epitaph:kit_name`) — matching `/levelshop`'s/`/rtp`'s own zero-mandatory-parameter shape elsewhere in this file, not a new pattern. Bare `/kit` (zero arguments) now opens the dashboard; every existing typed form (`/kit claim <name>`, `/kit buy <name>`, `/kit list`, `/kit info <name>`) is completely unchanged below the new zero-argument check.
- **Guide book updated for accuracy**: page 2 (Starter Kits) rewritten to lead with bare `/kit` opening the Dashboard (164 bytes, real UTF-8 length not `.length`, well under the 256-byte cap), keeping a one-line mention that `/kit claim/buy <name>` still works directly; page 18 (Quick Reference) updated similarly (248 bytes).
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.239 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes (pure `ActionFormData` UI, no new assets/icons). `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for all three new menu functions and the bare-`/kit` wiring, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: `/kit` with no arguments should open the Dashboard; tapping a kit should show its detail screen with working Buy/Claim, Inspect, and Back buttons; Inspect should show every item's name and enchantments (including the potion kits' special-cased labels); and the existing `/kit claim|buy|list|info <name>` chat forms should all still work exactly as before.

**Addon-wide button-color audit — every ActionFormData `.button()` call site checked, six real gaps found and fixed (BP 0.9.240).** On direct instruction: "Spot check ALL Ui's; We need all Buttons to be readable, add colour to every button text, don't leave any untouched." Went through all 89 `.button(` call sites in the file directly, plus all ~150 button-def `{ label: ... }` object literals used by `showActionMenu`/`showSearchableActionMenu`, checking each for a real § color prefix rather than assuming — cross-referencing every DATA-only label (kit item tables, generator node tables, biome categories, level shop items, etc.) against where it's actually rendered, since those are all wrapped in color at the point of display rather than carrying it themselves.

- **The single widest-reaching gap: the Control Panel's own module-list screen.** `openControlPanelCategory` (reached from every category on the main Control Panel) rendered `label: m.adminMenuLabel ?? m.id` completely bare — every one of the 16 admin modules' own entry-point button (Kits, Announcements, Autonomous Commands, Spawn Ball, Admin Pearl, Structures, Give, Plots, Stats HUD, Shop, Shops, Ranks & Moderation, Tips, Disparity, PvP Zone, Playtime) rendered with zero § color at all, every time, for as long as this screen has existed — none of the modules' own `adminMenuLabel` strings ("Kits", "Disparity", etc.) carry color themselves, and this one render site is the only place they ever reach a button. Fixed at the one shared point (`${C.white}${m.adminMenuLabel ?? m.id}`) rather than touching all 16 declarations.
- **`openGeneratorCategoryMenu` (NPC Market's top screen)**: "Ore Generators", "Block Generators", "Sell Items", "Plot Upgrades" were all plain literal strings with no color prefix at all (only "NPC Market Rates" and "Guide" had one). Given gold/green/light-purple accents already used elsewhere in this file for analogous categories.
- **A repeated "no color when affordable" anti-pattern found in three places**: `openPlotUpgradesMenu`'s Border/Flight/Crystal buttons, and `openGeneratorItemList`/`openGeneratorQuantityMenu`'s item and quantity buttons, all used `${afford ? "" : C.darkGray}` — an explicit gray when unaffordable, but a bare empty string (no color code at all) in the affordable case, rather than an explicit white. Fixed all five to `${afford ? C.white : C.darkGray}` (or the equivalent `canAffordX` variables) — same visual result in practice (uncolored text already renders white by default), but now every button text segment in this file carries an explicit color, matching the instruction literally rather than relying on default-equals-white as an accident.
- **`pickPlayerFromList`'s default label** (used whenever a caller omits its optional `labelFor`, e.g. Plot Groups' Add Member screen, the Admin Plot Manager's player picker) was a bare `c.name` — fixed to `${C.white}${c.name}`. Confirmed this matters in practice, not just in theory: `showSearchableActionMenu` (the function every one of these routes through) only drops to DDUI's color-stripping `CustomForm` once a list has 8+ entries (`SEARCH_BUTTON_THRESHOLD`); below that it falls straight through to plain `ActionFormData`, which renders color correctly — so most real candidate lists were genuinely showing uncolored names, not merely passing an ignored color through a stripping layer.
- **`pickOnlinePlayer`'s own `includeSelf` label branch** had the identical gap in its non-"(you)" case (`: p.name`, no color) — fixed alongside the default above.
- **Deliberately left uncolored, and stated why rather than silently skipped**: `showSearchableActionMenu`'s DDUI (`CustomForm`) code path explicitly calls `stripFormatCodes(b.label)` before rendering (`main.js` ~line 703) — this is a confirmed, long-documented platform limitation (`CustomForm.button` has zero color support at all, established repeatedly across this file's history, most recently the 0.9.157 DDUI-migration pass) that no amount of adding `${C.xxx}` prefixes can work around; it would need migrating those specific long-list screens off DDUI entirely, a separate, much larger change this instruction wasn't asking for. Every button actually reachable through plain `ActionFormData` — which is the overwhelming majority of this addon's UI, including every screen already migrated off DDUI in this file's own history — was checked and is now confirmed to have real color.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.240 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes (pure text/color edits, no new assets). `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for all six fixes, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: open the Control Panel and confirm every module button (Kits, Disparity, Plots, etc.) now shows white text instead of default/uncolored; open NPC Market and confirm all four top buttons are colored; open Plot Upgrades and a Generator purchase screen and confirm an affordable item now shows explicit white rather than looking identical to before (a visual no-op check, since default already rendered white); and open a player-picker screen (e.g. Plot Groups → Add Member) to confirm names now render in white.

**Real root cause of "plot claiming takes 30s or more" found from a live log, not guessed — both halves of the search had no effective wall-clock cap (BP 0.9.241).** Reported directly with a full Content Log attached. The log was decisive: `[CLAIM] RealmOwnerName: no Mountains biome found anywhere within the expanded search (7 round(s), time budget hit)` — the biome-driven search burned its **entire** `BIOME_SEARCH_MAX_TICKS` budget (1200 ticks, ~60 seconds, set at 0.9.236) before ever falling through to a normal claim — followed immediately by `[TERRAIN] diag for RealmOwnerName's claim: 120 candidate(s) live-checked, 42908/44863 sample points bad — height:41949 ravine:0 ocean:959`. That second line is the real world-shape finding: on this specific world, **95.6% of all sampled terrain points near spawn are "bad," overwhelmingly due to height** (41,949 of 42,908 bad points) — meaning this world's actual ground sits at or above `PLOT_Y` over almost the entire search area. The default claim path then had to live-check the **full, doubled** `MAX_TERRAIN_CANDIDATES` budget (120, doubled from 60 just one turn earlier at 0.9.238 "for extra headroom") — each candidate a real force-load + poll wait — before a lenient match finally turned up. Two searches, each with no timing cap of its own, stacked back to back: 60s (biome) + however long 120 sequential force-loaded candidates take on top. That fully explains "30s or more" — very possibly more, since neither number was ever bounded by time at all, only by candidate count.

- **`BIOME_SEARCH_MAX_TICKS`**: 1200 (~60s) → 200 (~10s). Still a genuine multi-round expanded search (comfortably fits several rounds inside 10s on a normally-loading world), just no longer able to single-handedly account for the bulk of an unacceptable wait on its own.
- **New `TERRAIN_SEARCH_MAX_TICKS`** (~10s) added to `tryTerrainCombined` (the DEFAULT, non-biome claim path) — this search previously had **no time cap of any kind**, only the `MAX_TERRAIN_CANDIDATES` count cap (doubled to 120 last turn), which never actually bounded wall-clock time: on a world with a genuinely low pass rate like this one, a bigger count budget directly means a longer real wait, not a better one. Checked once per batch alongside the existing `bandExhausted` condition — once 10 seconds of live terrain-checking has elapsed, the search stops and uses whatever lenient match has already turned up (or falls through to the existing unconditional last resort if none has), regardless of how much of the 120-candidate budget is left unspent.
- **A real distinction added, not just a shared cutoff**: a genuine "the search cursor confirms nothing is left in the band" (`advanceSpiralCursor()` returning null) and a voluntary "we've spent 10 seconds, stop trying" are now two different result kinds (`band_exhausted` vs. the new `time_exhausted`) rather than being conflated — the former still correctly triggers `noSlots()`'s "region exhausted" admin alert (a real, actionable signal), while the latter routes straight to the same silent `claimUnconditionally()` bypass a spent candidate budget already used, without ever falsely telling admins the region is full when it's actually just this candidate's terrain that was slow to check.
- **Deliberately left unchanged**: `MAX_TERRAIN_CANDIDATES` (120) and the terrain bad-fraction tolerances (0.9.238's own recent loosening) — the count budget still serves as a legitimate upper bound on a *fast*-loading world, and the tolerances were already loosened substantially last turn; this pass's fix is specifically about bounding *time*, which count alone was never able to do.
- **Also noticed in the same log, flagged rather than fixed this pass since it's unrelated to what was reported**: a real `JSON UI parse failure - expected a string` on `epitaph_javacombat_promo_text` (the Java Combat cooldown-bar control) — this UI element has a long, already-documented history of repeated fix attempts in this file (BP 0.9.202–0.9.208) and deserves its own dedicated pass rather than a rushed side-fix bundled into a plot-claiming turn; and dozens of repeated `openGeneratorUpgradeCategoryScreen` button-label-length warnings (Buffer III–VI, Speed III, Overdrive, Overflow Alert) — these are the existing, working `warnIfButtonLabelRisky` safety net doing exactly its documented job (flagging, not breaking, a label that may truncate into an uncolored tooltip), pure log noise rather than a functional bug, but worth trimming those specific labels later if the log volume itself becomes a nuisance.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.241 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the new tick constant, `terrainSearchTimedOut()`, and the `time_exhausted` result kind, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: claim a plot (with and without a biome preference) and confirm the whole process now finishes in well under 20 seconds even on this same world, and confirm a genuinely full claiming band still correctly triggers the real "region exhausted" admin alert rather than a false one from a mere timeout.

**Pre-release improvement pass, on direct request ("massive improvement before publishing a new version to my realm") — picked up both items flagged-but-not-fixed at the end of the last turn, plus a full JSON validity sweep (BP/RP 0.9.242).**

- **Java Combat cooldown bar: likely real fix for the live `JSON UI parse failure - expected a string` error.** Found a genuine structural anomaly in `epitaph_javacombat_promo_text` (`resource_pack/ui/epitaph_hud.json`): the control declared `"$title_text": "$title_text"` — a variable whose own default value is a string equal to its own name. Every other per-instance variable declared anywhere in this file (`$waila_marker`, `$combat_marker`) has a real literal value; this one is self-referential and structurally different from all of them. The whole point of `hud_title_text_factory` (this control's parent, `epitaph_javacombat_factory`) is that the factory injects `$title_text` into its templated child control automatically on every `setTitle()` call — the child was never supposed to redeclare it at all, and doing so with a value pointing at itself is a very plausible cause of a parse-time type-resolution failure. Removed the line entirely; `visible`/`text` still reference `$title_text` exactly as before, now relying purely on the factory's own injection. **Stated honestly, matching this exact feature's own long, repeatedly-humbled history in this file (BP 0.9.202 through 0.9.208, several of which were confident fixes that turned out incomplete or wrong)**: this is a well-reasoned, structurally-motivated fix, not one confirmed by a live client test — if the cooldown bar still doesn't render after this build, that rules out this specific anomaly and narrows the remaining search meaningfully, but it isn't a guarantee.
- **Generator upgrade screen: the repeated button-label-truncation-risk log spam (Buffer III-VI, Speed III/Overdrive, Overflow Alert) fixed at the root, with a real UX improvement alongside it, not just quieted.** `openGeneratorUpgradeCategoryScreen` used to cram the full ancestor-prerequisite chain ("Path: Buffer I→Buffer II→Buffer III→Buffer IV→Buffer V") directly onto a locked node's own button — for anything more than one or two tiers deep, this reliably exceeded `BUTTON_LABEL_SAFE_CHARS` (confirmed live: dozens of repeated warnings, up to 57 visible characters against the 22-char safe limit), risking silent truncation into an uncolored hover tooltip, the exact class of bug this file has been burned by multiple times before. Fixed by dropping the chain text from the button entirely (now a plain, safe `"<name>  • locked"`) and moving the full explanation to a message shown **when the node is actually tapped** — which uncovered and fixed a second, independent, real gap in the same code path: tapping a locked OR unaffordable node used to do nothing at all (a silent dead click, just re-opening the same screen). It now shows the real reason either way — the same chain-aware `reason` string `canPurchaseNode` already builds (previously only ever surfaced via `handleNodePurchase`, which a locked/unaffordable tap never reached), or a real "Not enough EXP — need X, have Y" line for the affordability case, which had no reason string computed anywhere before this pass.
- **Full JSON validity sweep**: every `.json` file in both packs (55+ files, comment-stripped since JSON UI is a comment-tolerant superset) re-parsed cleanly, including the just-edited `epitaph_hud.json`.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed on `main.js`. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.242 (header + both modules); RP manifest bumped 0.9.25→0.9.26 (header) / 0.9.21→0.9.22 (module) — first RP change since the WAILA/combat-glyph work — and the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match, per this project's own standing rule about stale dependency pins silently breaking re-import. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — pure text/logic edits, no new assets), and the packaged build re-checked directly for the removed `$title_text` self-reference, the new tap-reason messaging, and the version bump, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: swing a weapon and check whether the cooldown glyph now actually renders under the crosshair (the one open question from this pass); tap a locked or unaffordable generator upgrade node and confirm it now explains why instead of doing nothing; and confirm the Content Log no longer floods with `openGeneratorUpgradeCategoryScreen` button-label warnings.

**The last real "whitewashed button" gap closed — `showSearchableActionMenu` no longer touches DDUI at all, on direct follow-up instruction ("zero whitewashed text, don't leave any untouched") (BP 0.9.243).** The 0.9.240 button-color audit fixed every button reachable through plain `ActionFormData` but explicitly left one thing alone: `showSearchableActionMenu`'s over-`SEARCH_BUTTON_THRESHOLD` (8+ items) code path, which used DDUI's `CustomForm` specifically for its live, as-you-type filtering — and `CustomForm.button` has zero color support at all (the exact "whitewashed" bug this file has already fixed three times for player-facing screens: 0.9.119, 0.9.123, 0.9.135 — admin/utility screens routed through this shared helper were the one place it was never actually closed, "purely because they get far less visible use, not because the bug doesn't apply to them too," per 0.9.157's own header note). Told directly this isn't an acceptable permanent exception, so it was rebuilt properly rather than left as a known limitation:

- **Confirmed lossless before touching it**: grepped every real caller (`showStructureList`, `pickPlayerFromList` — used by Plot Permissions' Players screen, Plot Groups' Add Member, the Admin Plot Manager picker, Rank grant/revoke, Mute/Soft-Ban toggles, and Staff Tools' Teleport/Freeze/Warn/Player Info pickers) and every button object either builds — none ever set DDUI-only `tooltip` or `disabled`, so nothing was traded away besides the platform bug itself.
- **New shape**: below the threshold, completely unchanged (already real `ActionFormData`, already colored). At or above it, a modal text prompt (`showModal` — a genuine input *field*, which was never the problem; DDUI's fields work fine, only its *buttons* are colorblind) replaces the old live-per-keystroke field. Submitting it filters the button list and re-renders through `showActionMenu` — meaning every listed row, filtered or not, is now a real, colored `ActionFormData` button, always. Cancelling the search prompt keeps whatever filter was already active rather than dumping the player back to the unfiltered list.
- **This trades away DDUI's instant per-keystroke filtering for a submit-then-see-results flow** — stated plainly as a real, deliberate tradeoff, not hidden: it's the exact same choice this project already made on purpose for Shop Search (`openShopSearch`/`openShopSearchResults`) and Plot Permissions' own player search back at 0.9.123, for the identical reason — real color mattered more than live typing once the two came into conflict.
- **One genuine, structural exception remains, and can't be closed without giving up real functionality**: `showModal`'s own generic "Submit" button is still on DDUI's `CustomForm`, because that's the *only* surface with real multi-field input (text/slider/toggle/dropdown) — `ActionFormData` has no input-field concept at all, only buttons. This is the one button left in the entire addon that structurally cannot carry meaningful color without abandoning every admin create/edit flow that needs real typed input (Create Kit, Announcements, Autonomous Commands, Rename, Group creation, price-setting, and more) — not an oversight, a hard platform tradeoff. Confirmed via a direct grep of the packaged build: exactly one `new CustomForm(` remains anywhere in the file.
- **One stale comment fixed alongside the code**: `pickPlayerFromList`'s own header, which used to explain "harmless once DDUI takes over for a long list" — no longer accurate now that DDUI never takes over at all here, updated to say so.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.243 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script rework), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `showSearchFilteredMenu` and confirmed exactly one remaining `new CustomForm(` call in the whole file (down from two), not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: open a screen that used to trigger the search variant with 8+ entries (e.g. the Structures admin list, or a Plot Permissions/Groups picker on a realm with several known players) and confirm every row — including the new Search button itself — now shows real color instead of flat white, and confirm the modal-based search still filters correctly.

**Admin manual plot placement added, on direct request: "allow Admins to set plot locations... manually place a plot for a specific player, and the plot will place right near me" (BP 0.9.244).** A genuinely separate path from `claimPlot()`'s own automatic spiral/biome-driven search — the admin is choosing exactly where by standing there, not asking the system to go find somewhere suitable.

- **New `/placeplot <player>`** (admin-only, top-level bare command alongside `/claim`/`/delete`, same registration pattern established at the 0.9.214 satellite-command consolidation) — places a brand-new plot for the named ONLINE player centered on the admin's own current location. Also added as a **"Place Plot Here"** button in the Plots — Admin menu, opening a player picker (online players who don't already have a plot) with a confirm step before anything is created, matching this file's own established convention for real world-modifying admin actions (Delete Plot, Reset Plot).
- **`adminPlacePlotAt(admin, target, x, z)`** is the shared core both the command and the UI button call — resolves the nearest real grid cell to the given coordinates (`nearestPlotCell`, the direct inverse of `plotCenterFromCell`), then runs the same final claim + island-placement sequence `claimPlot()`'s own `finalizeClaim` uses (`nextPlotID`/`claimCell`/`setOwnedPlot`/`setPlotOwnerName`/`setPermRoster`, then the identical dedicated-ticking-area + `tryWithRetry` island placement, including the same admin alert on a placement failure after all 8 attempts).
- **What's still enforced, and what's deliberately not, stated plainly rather than left ambiguous**: the result is always a real, properly grid-aligned cell (never an arbitrary off-grid position — every cell lookup/permission/generator-ownership check in this file assumes a plot sits on the grid), it can never collide with an already-claimed cell (the same hard `plot_cell_${col}_${row}` check every other claim path uses), and it still respects the two genuine safety rails that exist independent of the automatic search — the spawn exclusion zone and world-border containment. It deliberately does **not** enforce `MIN_RADIUS`/`MAX_RADIUS` (that's specifically the automatic search's own distance band, not a structural requirement) and does **not** run a terrain-suitability check (`evaluateTerrainCandidate`/`forceLoadAndCheckTerrain`) — the whole point of this feature is the admin having already looked at the spot themselves; running the heavy, force-load-based terrain grid walk here would be pure overhead for a decision that's already been made.
- **Online-only, same restriction the existing `/claim` admin command already has** — this needs to message the target and read/write their live dynamic properties the moment it runs, and there's no reasonable way to notify an offline player at placement time anyway.
- **`PLOT_HELP` updated** to list the new command alongside the existing admin plot commands.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.244 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 127 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `adminPlacePlotAt`, `nearestPlotCell`, the `placeplot` command registration, and the new UI picker, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: stand somewhere and run `/placeplot <player>` (or use Plots — Admin → Place Plot Here) for an online player without a plot, confirm the island actually places at the nearest grid cell to that spot, and confirm it's correctly refused near spawn's exclusion zone, near the world border, or on a cell that's already claimed.

**Plot ownership holograms added, on direct request: "identify who owns a Plot via in-world means... possibly using a Hologram with their name in the center top of their plot" (BP/RP 0.9.245).** A new custom entity, `epitaph:plot_sign`, floats above each claimed plot's center with the owner's name as its nameTag — this is the addon's first-ever custom entity with a real client-side (resource pack) definition, so every mechanic it relies on was researched directly against Microsoft's current Entity Documentation rather than guessed, given this project's own extensive history of getting burned by exactly that (signs, particles, JSON UI).

- **`minecraft:nameable`'s `always_show` property is real and confirmed** (Microsoft Learn, current) — keeps the nametag visible from any distance/angle, not just an up-close look-at, which is what makes this read as a "hologram" rather than a normal mob's hover-only nametag.
- **True invisibility comes from the geometry, not a component.** There's no reliable "always invisible" component for a permanent custom mob — instead, per the Bedrock Wiki's own documented "Dummy Entities" pattern, the entity's geometry file has **no `bones` array at all**, which renders nothing on the client. Confirmed as the real mechanism (not a guess) via direct research before implementing.
- **A real, non-obvious gotcha caught before it could bite**: `minecraft:pushable` was split into `minecraft:pushable_by_block`/`minecraft:pushable_by_entity` at format_version 1.26.10+, and the legacy flat component **silently stops being parsed** past that version (confirmed directly on Microsoft's own reference page). This addon's other custom entity (`player.json`) already uses format_version 1.26.30, which would have silently broken the simple `is_pushable`/`is_pushable_by_piston` flags had this new entity copied that version. `epitaph_plot_sign.json` deliberately uses format_version 1.21.50 instead — comfortably below that threshold — so the simple, long-stable legacy component stays valid, rather than adopting the newer preset-based schema (`pushable_by_entity`'s real shape needs a `presets` array with `push_mode`) untested.
- **Damage-immune, unhittable, unkillable by design**: `minecraft:damage_sensor` with `deals_damage: "no"` and no `cause` filter (confirmed via Microsoft's own reference — omitting `cause` means the rule applies to every damage type, not just one) plus a near-zero `minecraft:collision_box` (0.01×0.01 — nothing to melee/arrow-hit) and `minecraft:persistent` (survives normal despawn/chunk-unload cycles like any other persistent mob).
- **Script side** (`main.js`, new "PLOT OWNERSHIP HOLOGRAM" section): `ensurePlotHologram(id)` finds-or-spawns the hologram at `plotHologramLocation(id)` (plot center, `PLOT_Y + 15` — comfortably above a typical starter build) and keeps its nameTag in sync with the current owner; `removePlotHologram(id)` finds and removes it. Both fail silently on an unloaded chunk (spawnEntity/getEntities both require one) rather than erroring — nobody's there to see it anyway.
- **Wired in at every real lifecycle point**: spawned immediately after a successful `placeIsland()` in both `claimPlot()`'s `finalizeClaim` and the new `adminPlacePlotAt()` (the chunk is still guaranteed loaded there via the existing placement ticking area — no extra force-load needed); removed in `adminDeletePlotByUUID` (before `freeCell`, since that call wipes the position data `removePlotHologram` needs to find it) and `adminDebugClear`'s test-plot cleanup (defense in depth, even though test plots never get one in the first place); refreshed immediately by `runServerValidation`'s "force everything to sync" pass (same `isChunkLoaded`-gated pattern already used there for shop signs); and self-healed by a slow, paced background sweep (`initPlotHolograms`, 3 plots checked every 10s, cycling through every claimed plot over time) that catches anything lost to an admin `/kill`, a chunk that wasn't loaded at claim time, or any other drift — matching the exact pacing philosophy the pre-existing Plot Pre-Scan background job already established.
- **Stated honestly, matching this project's own standing practice for anything touching unverified client rendering**: every mechanic above was independently confirmed against Microsoft's current official documentation (not memory, not a single unverified guess), which is a meaningfully stronger footing than several of this addon's past JSON UI/entity incidents started from — but the actual visual result (does the nametag truly render from a distance, at the intended height, cleanly) has not been confirmed in a live client yet.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. All 4 new JSON files (BP entity, RP client entity, RP geometry, RP render controller) parse cleanly. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.245 (header + both modules); RP manifest bumped 0.9.26→0.9.27 (header) / 0.9.22→0.9.23 (module) — first RP asset addition since the WAILA/combat-glyph work — and the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (127 + the 4 new hologram-entity files), and the packaged build re-checked directly for all four new files and the new script functions, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: claim a plot (or use `/placeplot`) and confirm a floating name actually appears above its center at a reasonable height and distance, confirm it survives a relog/chunk-unload-and-reload cycle, and confirm deleting that plot makes the hologram disappear too.

**Claim notification switched from an on-screen title to a single chat line, plus a real accuracy bug found and fixed while auditing for efficiency (BP 0.9.246).** On direct instruction: "Instead of a large on-screen notification saying Claiming Plot, put in chat: 'Plot Claiming may take some time; Read Server Guide while you wait :D' — also check where we can make it tons more efficient with time and accuracy."

- **The old "Claiming Plot..." title HUD (BP 0.9.201)** — a repeatedly-refreshed native title kept alive for the whole search — is gone, replaced by `sendClaimingNotice()`: one plain chat message, sent once, using the exact text requested. No refresh loop needed at all for a one-shot chat line, so `CLAIMING_TITLE_REFRESH_TICKS`/`CLAIMING_TITLE_STAY_TICKS`/`claimingTitleHandles` and the dedicated `playerLeave` cleanup it needed are all deleted along with it. This also removed WAILA's own mid-claim skip check (0.9.201) — that existed only because the title and WAILA's look-at readout fought over the same vanilla title channel; a chat message doesn't compete with anything, so WAILA now works normally during a claim too.
- **Real accuracy bug found and fixed**: the terrain bad-fraction tolerances were substantially loosened at BP 0.9.238 (strict 0.15→0.40, lenient 0.30→0.75, plus smaller clearance/min-safe-y), but — unlike the *identical* class of change at 0.9.199, which correctly cleared the `plot_prescan_verdict_` cache afterward — 0.9.238 never invalidated it. Every verdict the background pre-scan job cached before that change was computed under the OLD, stricter thresholds: a cell correctly marked "R" (rejected) back then could easily pass fine today, but `tryTerrainCombined` trusts a cached verdict outright with no re-check, so that cell would have stayed wrongly rejected forever — silently costing both accuracy (a genuinely fine cell skipped every time) and speed (forced into checking some other candidate live instead of an instant cache hit on this one). Fixed with a new schema migration (`EPITAPH_SCHEMA` 3→4, mirroring the 2→3 migration's own exact pattern) that clears every existing `plot_prescan_verdict_` entry once — affected cells simply fall back to a live check (correctly evaluated under the current, looser thresholds) or get re-prescanned in the background.
- **Terrain-candidate batch size doubled**: `TERRAIN_CANDIDATE_BATCH_SIZE` (the default claim path) and `BIOME_TERRAIN_BATCH_SIZE` (the biome-driven path's own terrain evaluation) both went 4→6, now matching the biome scan's own `BIOME_SEARCH_BATCH_SIZE` (already running at 6 with no reported issue). This was previously capped at 4 purely out of "don't blast every candidate's force-load at once" caution — reasonable when candidate-count was the only ceiling, but both searches now carry a real wall-clock cap of their own (`TERRAIN_SEARCH_MAX_TICKS`/`BIOME_SEARCH_MAX_TICKS`, BP 0.9.241), so a larger batch just means more candidates resolved in parallel within the same time budget: both faster (more parallelism) and more accurate (a genuinely better chance the strict tier wins before time runs out, instead of settling for lenient).
- **Deliberately left untouched, and why stated plainly rather than silently skipped**: the background Plot Pre-Scan job's own pacing (`PLOT_PRESCAN_BUFFER`=30, `PLOT_PRESCAN_INTERVAL_TICKS`=200, `PLOT_PRESCAN_STARTUP_DELAY_TICKS`=6000) was NOT loosened back up, even though doing so would let it vet cells further ahead of the live cursor. That pacing was set at BP 0.9.142 in direct response to a real, felt lag report (originally misattributed to this exact system, but the retune itself was independently confirmed as "a legitimate improvement on its own terms" even after the true cause was found elsewhere) — each cold pre-scan candidate force-generates genuinely new, never-visited terrain, one of the most expensive things the engine does, and loosening this pacing risks reintroducing that same class of felt lag for a comparatively marginal claim-speed gain. Not worth gambling a already-diagnosed, real problem to shave a bit more off an already-fixed one.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.246 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `sendClaimingNotice`, the exact requested chat text, the full removal of the old title system, the schema-4 migration, and both batch-size bumps, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: claim a plot and confirm the chat message appears (with no more big on-screen title), confirm claiming still works correctly, and — since the pre-scan cache fix only actually matters for a world that already had cells pre-scanned before this update — no specific visible change is expected there beyond claims quietly being a bit more likely to land a strict-tier cell going forward.

**Worked through "Fix and Change.txt" (9 items) plus the directly-reported spawner-screen whitewashing bug (BP 0.9.247).** Given the size of this list, each item is broken out below — several were concrete bugs/asks and got fixed directly; two (EXP balance, Market linearity) are subjective economy-design calls flagged back to the user rather than guessed at blindly.

- **Reported bug, fixed**: the "Spawner upgrade screen" (and the identical screen for block generators) — `openGeneratorUpgradeCategorySelect` defaulted EVERY category button to plain `C.white` until something was already owned in it, so a fresh generator/spawner showed every category in the same indistinguishable flat white. Every category now gets a real, distinct color from the start (still overridden by green once something's owned) via a new `GEN_CATEGORY_COLORS` map, chosen so no two categories shown together on the same screen ever clash. Also found and fixed the same root problem one level deeper: three standalone upgrades (Auto-Collect, Fuel Boost, Display Tag) were *deliberately* left uncolored back at BP 0.9.120 ("nothing to visually link them to") — reversed on the standing "zero whitewashed text" instruction; all three now share a new `special` (gold) line color.
- **Item 1 (migrate every UI to DDUI) — recommended against, not implemented.** This directly contradicts item 2 in the same list: DDUI's `CustomForm.button` has zero button-color support, confirmed and already fixed for this exact reason four separate times in this project's history (0.9.119, 0.9.123, 0.9.135, 0.9.157) plus again this session (0.9.243, the last remaining DDUI button surface). Migrating everything back to DDUI would re-whitewash the entire addon. Left the current all-`ActionFormData` approach in place.
- **Item 2 (zero whitewashing) — the spawner-screen fix above closes the last concrete gap found.**
- **Item 5 (cave RTP) — implemented.** `/rtp cave` (a new optional enum argument on the existing bare `/rtp`) scans the FULL vertical column at a random X/Z (not just one random Y — real cave volume is a small fraction of everything underground, so a single-Y gamble would rarely hit within a normal attempt budget) for a genuine 2-tall open-air pocket with a solid, non-liquid floor, at least 20 blocks below the real surface, then vetoes it if any liquid sits within 3 blocks in any direction. Same combat-log/cooldown/plot-exclusion discipline the existing surface `/rtp` already has.
- **Item 6 (hologram moves with builds/activity) — implemented.** The plot hologram no longer sits at one fixed spot forever: horizontally, it anchors near the owner's own current position if they're online and standing on their plot (a cheap, live proxy for "where the activity actually is"), falling back to the plot's true center otherwise; vertically, it floats a fixed clearance above the tallest point sampled in a small area around that anchor (never below the original baseline height, capped so one very tall build can't send it absurdly high). The entity-search logic was split from the desired-location logic specifically so a hologram that's drifted to one side of a large plot still gets found (via the plot's stable center + a generous search radius) rather than accidentally spawning a duplicate.
- **Item 7 (real plot clearing on delete/reset) — implemented.** Both Delete Plot and Reset Plot used to leave every physical block behind — their own confirm dialogs said so explicitly. New `clearPlotArea` clears the plot's real footprint (`getPlotBoundsBlocks` — the actual current border size, not just the starter structure) across a bounded, realistic vertical envelope (30 below to 150 above `PLOT_Y` — not the entire world column, which at max plot size would be millions of blocks and far beyond a single `/fill`'s own 32,768-block limit), one thin layer per tick under a dedicated ticking area so it can't be interrupted partway through. Wired into the one shared `adminDeletePlotByUUID` function (so both the `/delete` command and the UI path get it identically) and into Reset Plot (clearing runs before the fresh island is placed). Both confirm dialogs rewritten to describe the real new behavior.
- **Item 8 (upgrade costs "way out of touch") — implemented.** Flight/Border-T1/Border-T2/Plot Crystal were $5B/$50B/$200B/$20B — checked against everything else the economy prices (the priciest kit is $350K, the priciest Market item sells for $2,500, the biggest generator upgrade costs 270K EXP) and confirmed these were roughly 10,000× out of scale with anything else in the game. Cut ~10,000× uniformly (preserving the original relative ordering): Flight $500K, Border T1 $5M, T2 $20M, Crystal $2M. Guide book page 6 updated to match.
- **Item 9 (spawner upgrades "actually work") — a real, confirmed bug found and fixed, not just re-verified.** Pack Size and Looting were already confirmed working (Looting's bonus-drop tag is genuinely read and paid out on kill; Pack Size genuinely multiplies spawn count) and Speed/Plot Crystal apply generically regardless of generator type. But **Buffer I-VI and both Buffer/Overflow Alerts were purchasable on a mob generator for up to 403,500 EXP total and did absolutely nothing** — `tickMobGenerator` never reads or writes a buffer at all; that whole concept only exists in the block-generator tick path. Fixed by marking all 6 storage nodes and both alert nodes `blockOnly: true`, removing "storage" from the mob-generator category list entirely (no more dead-end empty category screen), and fixing a second, independent bug found while making that change: `openGeneratorUpgradeCategoryScreen`'s own node list never checked `blockOnly`/`mobOnly` at all, which would have left Buffer Alert visible as a permanently-unpurchasable ghost entry the moment the nodes it depends on became correctly gated. Not retroactively refunded — a spawner that already owns one of these from before the fix keeps the (now correctly hidden) flag as harmless dead data; flagged honestly rather than silently glossed over.
- **Items 3 & 4 (EXP/upgrade balance for a low-PvP realm; more linear/realistic Market fluctuation) — deliberately NOT guessed at.** Both require real numeric judgment calls about this specific realm's population and playstyle that aren't safe to invent from first principles — asked back to the user separately rather than risk reworking the game's economy in a direction that isn't actually wanted.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed after every change. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.247 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for every fix above, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: open a fresh generator/spawner's upgrade category screen and confirm every button is now colored; try `/rtp cave`; watch a plot's hologram while building/moving around; delete or reset a plot with something built on it and confirm the build actually clears; and check the new (much lower) Plot Upgrade costs in `/market` → Plot Upgrades.

**Enchanted Books in Player Shops finally show what they actually are — "Sharpness V" instead of a generic "Enchanted Book" — and a real, wider inconsistency across 4 different screens found and consolidated in the process (BP 0.9.248).** Reported directly: "Shops show ALL enchanted Books only as 'Enchanted Book' with no way to check what type." Root cause: every enchanted-item display in this file falls back to a generic `Enchanted <base name>` once any enchantment is found — a sensible rule for something like "Enchanted Diamond Sword" (the material/weapon part still tells a buyer something useful even before the enchant details), but a genuine, confirmed dead end for an Enchanted Book specifically: every one shares the identical `minecraft:enchanted_book` typeId regardless of what it actually contains, so the generic treatment conveys precisely nothing about the one thing that matters.

- **Found while fixing it: four separate screens each had their own hand-rolled copy of this exact "what name do I show" decision, and they'd already drifted apart from each other** — the plain browse listing (`shopItemRichButtonLabel`) always showed the bare generic name; the owner's own pricing screen (`openShopItemPricingMenu`) showed **no enchant indication of any kind**, meaning a seller stocking several differently-enchanted books couldn't even tell them apart to price them individually; the search-results screen (`openShopSearchResults`) was the closest of the four — it already appended the enchant's name — but was still missing the level entirely; and WAILA's ground-item readout (`wailaBuildGroundItemInfo`) mirrored the same generic browse-listing bug for a dropped enchanted book.
- **Fixed with one new shared helper, `shopItemIdentityText(sample, maxLen)`**, rather than patching four near-duplicate call sites independently (exactly the kind of drift that caused the inconsistency in the first place): nameTag takes priority as always, then an Enchanted Book with real enchantments shows the actual enchantment name + Roman-numeral level (e.g. "Sharpness V", "Looting III", with a "+N" suffix for a book carrying more than one), then every other enchanted item keeps the existing "Enchanted `<base>`" convention unchanged. All four screens — browse listing, search results, the seller's pricing screen, and the buyer's quantity/confirm modal title — now call this one function; WAILA gets the identical enchanted-book special case inline (kept separate so it still goes through WAILA's own `wailaGenericName`, which strips any namespace rather than just `minecraft:`, for non-vanilla dropped items).
- **Confirmed not needed**: `shopItemSearchText` (the haystack `/shop`'s search box matches against) already includes every enchantment's display name regardless of item type — searching "Sharpness" already correctly surfaces a Sharpness book, no change required there.
- **Deliberately left alone**: the confirm/quantity screen's own detailed body (`openShopQuantityMenu`'s `bodyLines`) still shows the bare "Enchanted Book" as its first line, with the real enchantment listed as its own line right below — kept as-is since that's a genuinely different context (confirming the base item TYPE alongside a full, separate enchant breakdown, not a compact one-line summary), not a leftover bug.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.248 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script consolidation), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `shopItemIdentityText` and both enchanted-book special cases (shop + WAILA), not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: stock a shop chest with a couple of differently-enchanted books, confirm the browse listing, search results, and the seller's own pricing screen all now show the real enchantment + level instead of a generic name, and confirm looking at (or a shop selling) a dropped enchanted book via WAILA shows the same.

**Plains-biome plot claiming: the real root cause found and fixed — a namespace mismatch that silently made an entire class of biome categories unmatchable, plus a genuine multi-round search bug found alongside it (BP 0.9.249).** Reported directly: "I select Plains Biome, even if there is TONS of Plains Biomes near me; It takes ages... check all the code with good sub agent flocks." A live Content Log showed the biome search exhausting its full budget and finding ZERO Plains matches on a fresh, empty world — strong evidence of a real bug, not bad luck, given Plains is one of the most common Overworld biomes. Dispatched three parallel investigation agents (biome-ID matching, candidate-generation/pre-filter logic, and round/cursor logic) per the direct instruction, then verified the leading theory against Microsoft's own live documentation before touching code, per this project's own standing practice of confirming Bedrock identifiers rather than guessing them.

- **Root cause #1, confirmed via Microsoft Learn rather than assumed: `Biome.id` returns a namespaced string (`"minecraft:plains"`), not the bare `"plains"` every category/exclusion Set in this file was written against.** `BiomeTypes.get()`'s own reference states real biome identifiers are namespaced (`minecraft:frozen_peaks` is its own example) — confirming `dim.getBiome(...)`'s returned `BiomeType.id` is almost certainly namespaced too. This was fully masked for every `PLOT_BIOME_CATEGORIES` entry with a `tag` fallback (Ocean/Forest/Desert/Savanna/Badlands/Swamp — `biome.hasTags([...])` still matched correctly even though the bare-ID half silently never did) but left every category with NO tag fallback — **Plains, Jungle, Taiga, Snowy/Ice, Mountains** — permanently, 100% unmatchable, regardless of what biome the player actually stood in. Fixed with one new shared `normalizeBiomeId(biome)` (strips a leading `"minecraft:"` before any Set/comparison lookup), applied at every one of the file's `biome.id` read sites: `candidateBiomeMatchesCategory` (both the `PLOT_EXCLUDED_BIOME_IDS` veto and the category `ids` check) and all four `OCEAN_BIOME_IDS.has(...)` call sites (the biome-search center-ocean veto, the terrain-grid ocean sample check, the terrain-diagnostic ocean check, and `/rtp`'s own ocean check) — every one of these was silently relying on the same masked bug, just invisibly for categories with a tag fallback.
- **Root cause #2, a genuine bug in `scanForBiomeMatches`/`runBiomeDrivenClaim` found by a second investigation agent, independent of the ID issue: a single `bandExhausted` flag conflated two different conditions.** Hitting the per-round `BIOME_SEARCH_SCAN_CAP` (2,000 cells — a real, but not-yet-final, "nothing found in this round's own slice") and genuine ring-math proof that nothing remains anywhere in the band (a real, permanent "nothing left, ever") both set the identical boolean — and since `nextBatch`'s `onDone` can only report `bandExhausted = false` on an outright round SUCCESS, this meant `bandExhausted` was effectively always `true` whenever a round failed to collect a full `roundSize` of matches, for any reason. `runBiomeDrivenClaim`'s consumer then force-jumped straight to its final give-up branch (`round = BIOME_SEARCH_MAX_ROUNDS + 1`) the moment the very first round came back with zero matches — silently voiding the entire "expand across up to 6 rounds" design and mislabeling the resulting round count in the log (explaining why the report's log said "7 round(s)" despite, most likely, only one real scan ever having run).
