Split into two independent flags — `trueExhausted` (real ring-math exhaustion) and `scanCapHit` (this round's own cap, not final) — and only `trueExhausted` now triggers early give-up; a `scanCapHit`-with-zero-matches round now correctly falls through to `nextRound()` and continues scanning from exactly where it left off, making the multi-round expansion actually happen as designed.
- **Given the default band is only ~400 cells (well under the 2,000-cell scan cap), root cause #1 is the primary explanation for this specific report** — a full-band scan in one round should already have covered every reachable cell, so finding zero Plains matches despite the user's stated abundance points at the ID bug directly. Root cause #2 is real and independently confirmed by a separate investigation agent, but would only have been the dominant factor at a much larger claiming radius (this file's own history shows `maxRadius` has been raised well past its default more than once) — fixed anyway since it's a genuine defect in its own right, not a hypothetical.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.249 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `normalizeBiomeId` (5 call sites), `trueExhausted`, and `scanCapHit`, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: select Plains (or any other previously-unmatchable category — Jungle/Taiga/Snowy/Mountains) while genuinely standing in or near that biome, and confirm the search now finds a match quickly instead of exhausting its full budget and falling back to a normal claim.

**Plot generation made genuinely faster and less strict, on direct follow-up request: "less strict requirements for plot placement... EFFICIENT and FAST Plot Generation" (BP 0.9.250).** Went beyond another fraction nudge (already done twice this file's history, 0.9.199/0.9.200/0.9.238) and fixed the actual structural bottleneck underneath the whole terrain/biome search pipeline.

- **The real efficiency fix: `forceLoadAndCheckTerrain`, `forceLoadAndCheckBiome`, and the background `prescanCell` job were all still on the raw vanilla `tickingarea add/remove` command — the same hard-capped-at-10-areas-per-world pool this file already migrated Player Shops and the mail vault OFF of at BP 0.9.194, for the identical reason.** That 0.9.194 migration deliberately left plot claiming's own force-loads on the old pool since they weren't the reported problem at the time — but that pool's scarcity is exactly why every batch size in the search pipeline (`BIOME_SEARCH_BATCH_SIZE`, `BIOME_TERRAIN_BATCH_SIZE`, `TERRAIN_CANDIDATE_BATCH_SIZE`) had been kept conservative (6, bumped from 4 at 0.9.246) — real headroom was never available to increase them further while sharing that scarce, 10-slot budget with everything else. Migrated all three onto `world.tickingAreaManager` (the same real, proven fix, its own pool "limited by a fixed amount of ticking chunks per pack, independent of the command limits" per Microsoft's own docs) — `forceLoadAndCheckTerrain`'s new ticking area is explicitly sized (`half + TERRAIN_MARGIN_BLOCKS` in every direction) to guarantee-cover its full footprint+margin sample grid, rather than a fixed-radius circle command. This also replaced the old fixed, guessed poll loop (`TERRAIN_LOAD_POLL_TICKS`/`TERRAIN_LOAD_POLL_MAX_TICKS`, now fully deleted — no remaining references) with `createTickingArea`'s own real Promise, which only resolves once the area is genuinely loaded and ticking — a candidate near already-generated terrain now resolves as fast as the engine actually allows instead of always paying at least one poll interval, while a genuinely new/far chunk still waits exactly as long as it truly needs.
- **With the pool contention gone, batch sizes bumped 6→10 across all three** (`BIOME_SEARCH_BATCH_SIZE`, `BIOME_TERRAIN_BATCH_SIZE`, `TERRAIN_CANDIDATE_BATCH_SIZE`) — more candidates resolved in parallel within the same wall-clock budget, directly faster claim resolution.
- **Sample density halved for cheaper per-candidate checks**: `TERRAIN_SAMPLE_STEP` 8→16 (roughly 81→25 sample points at max plot size) and `TERRAIN_MARGIN_STEP` 16→24 — fewer `getTopmostBlock`/`getBiome` calls per candidate, safe alongside the already-generous bad-fraction tolerances (a genuinely bad stretch of terrain still reads as bad at half the resolution).
- **Tolerances loosened a further notch**: `TERRAIN_STRICT_BAD_FRACTION` 0.40→0.50, `TERRAIN_LENIENT_BAD_FRACTION` 0.75→0.85 — fewer candidates need a second/third try before passing, meaning fewer total force-loads per claim on average.
- **Deliberately untouched, and why stated plainly**: the unconditional `y >= PLOT_Y` real island-overlap check and the hard ocean-center veto (BP 0.9.201, a direct "plots should ALWAYS avoid ocean" guarantee) — neither participates in the fractional tolerance, so a candidate that's genuinely, badly unsuitable still can't be accepted no matter how loose the other numbers get. The one-shot island-*placement* ticking-area commands (`placeIsland`'s own `tickingarea add/remove` calls, both in the default claim path and `/placeplot`) were also left on the vanilla pool — they run once per successful claim, not once per candidate, so they don't compound with candidate count the way the search functions did, and migrating them wasn't necessary for this ask.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.250 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for all three migrated `async function`s, both bumped batch-size constants, the loosened sample-step/tolerance constants, and confirmed zero remaining `TERRAIN_LOAD_POLL` references, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: claim several new plots and confirm the whole process feels noticeably faster than before, and confirm claims still never land centered on ocean or overlapping obviously unsuitable terrain.

**Broken/ghost plots: found the real cause of a teleport landing at (0, 32000, 0), and added an admin tool to open the claim menu directly for a player stuck on one (BP 0.9.251).** Reported directly: "If someone's plot doesn't actually exist, has a coordinate, but when I tp to it, it teleports me to 0, 32K, 0. How do I bring the claim menu up for them."

- **Root cause of the (0, 32000, 0) teleport, confirmed by reading the code rather than guessed.** `releaseSuspendedPlayer`'s "island still loading" fallback (fires whenever `findSafeLandingY` can't find solid ground near the plot's stored center — exactly what happens for a "has a coordinate but doesn't actually exist" plot, i.e. a ghost record whose `placeIsland()` call silently failed at claim/placement time, something `adminPlacePlotAt` already has its own admin alert for) called `world.getDefaultSpawnLocation()` completely raw, with zero sanity checking. This file's own boot-time `SpawnOrigin` resolution (`onWorldLoad`) already learned the hard way that this exact API can return a garbage/sentinel location — documented there as INT_MIN before a fresh world's spawn is committed to NBT — which is why that ONE call site validates the result (`Math.abs(x/z) < 30,000,000`) before ever trusting it. The teleport fallback never got the same treatment, and never checked Y at all even conceptually. Fixed by applying an equivalent (and stronger — now also bounds Y to a real Overworld range) sanity check, falling back to `spawnOriginX`/`spawnOriginZ` (already validated by that same boot logic, guaranteed sane by the time any plot code runs) at a freshly-scanned safe height if the raw spawn location fails the check. The player-facing message was also made honest — "still loading" implied a temporary state, but a genuinely broken/ghost plot looks identical from here and isn't temporary at all; the new wording covers both without asserting which one it is.
- **The actual question — answered with a real feature, not just instructions.** Traced why an admin has no way to do this today: `openPlotBiomeChoiceMenu` and `/plot claim`'s own "selfclaim" dispatch case both refuse outright ("you already have a plot") the instant `getOwnedPlot(player) > 0` — which is still true for a broken/ghost record, since nothing about a failed island placement ever clears the ownership property. The existing recovery path (already documented in `adminPlacePlotAt`'s own failure alert) is `/delete <player>` then `/placeplot <player>` — but that re-places at the admin's own location with no biome choice, not the actual claim menu the player would normally get to choose from. Added **`openAdminOpenClaimMenuPicker`**, a new "Open Claim Menu" button in `Plots — Admin` (`openPlotsAdminMenu`, right next to "Place Plot Here") — lists online players with no plot on record (same filter `openAdminPlacePlotPicker` already uses) and, on tap, calls `openPlotBiomeChoiceMenu(target)` directly, popping the real biome-choice menu up on the target's own screen so they can pick their own preference, with a message to both the admin and the player confirming it happened. Deliberately still requires the admin to free the broken record first (Manage Player Plots → Delete Plot, or `/delete <player>`) — this picker won't show a player who still has ANY plot on record, broken or not, matching how every other admin plot tool in this file already treats "has a plot" as the one thing that gates re-claiming.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.251 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the sanity-checked spawn fallback and `openAdminOpenClaimMenuPicker`'s wiring, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: reproduce a broken/ghost plot (or wait for one to be reported again) and confirm teleporting to it now lands somewhere sane with an honest message instead of (0, 32000, 0); and confirm Plots — Admin → Open Claim Menu lists a plot-less online player and actually pops the biome-choice menu on their screen when tapped.

**The actual upstream cause of ghost/broken plots found and fixed, plus `/claim` wired through the biome-choice menu (BP 0.9.252).** Direct follow-up, giving the real diagnosis: "It seems to be an issue with the realm having trouble loading/placing a plot completely outside loaded and/or cached chunks. Also, ensure /claim opens the menu for the target player."

- **Root cause, confirmed by reading every island-placement call site rather than assumed.** All three real placement paths (`claimPlot`'s `finalizeClaim`, `adminPlacePlotAt`, and Reset Plot's own inline call) added a raw vanilla `tickingarea add` command, waited a handful of ticks, then just started firing `placeIsland()` — up to 8 attempts, ~1 second apart, an ~8-second total window — hoping the chunk had actually come in by then. That's fine for terrain near spawn or already visited/cached, but a genuinely far-out cell needing real, from-scratch world generation (a cell "completely outside loaded/cached chunks," exactly as diagnosed) can easily take longer than 8 seconds, especially under real Realm load. When it does, every attempt fails silently, leaving a plot record with a real stored coordinate but no actual built island — the exact ghost-plot symptom the previous pass fixed from the *teleport* side (BP 0.9.251's sanity-checked spawn fallback); this is the actual upstream cause producing that ghost plot in the first place. **Reset Plot's own copy was worse still** — it called `placeIsland()` directly with no retry and no dedicated ticking area at all (`clearPlotArea`'s own ticking area is already torn down by the time its `onDone` fires), so a slow-loading chunk there had no safety net whatsoever.
- **Fixed with the same real, promise-based load guarantee this file's terrain/biome search already migrated to at BP 0.9.250.** New shared `placeIslandWhenLoaded(dim, cx, cz, onResult)` uses `world.tickingAreaManager.createTickingArea` — which only resolves once the area is GENUINELY loaded and ticking, not a guess — and only attempts `placeIsland()` once that resolves, so the very first attempt is now made against terrain the engine has actually confirmed exists instead of racing it. The retry loop kept inside it (dropped 8→`PLACE_ISLAND_RETRY_ATTEMPTS`=3) is now a defensive backstop for a genuinely separate, transient `structure load` hiccup, not a substitute for a load guarantee. All three call sites (`finalizeClaim`, `adminPlacePlotAt`, and Reset Plot) now route through this one shared function instead of each keeping its own copy-pasted vanilla-ticking-area-plus-blind-retry logic — Reset Plot gained a real load guarantee and retry safety net it never had before, not just a migration.
- **`/claim <player>` now opens the real biome-choice menu on the target's own screen**, on direct instruction. This admin command predates the BP 0.9.233 biome-choice menu and was never updated to route through it — it called `claimPlot(target)` directly, skipping the same choice a player claiming for themselves gets, and the one remaining place in the whole plot system that still bypassed `openPlotBiomeChoiceMenu` (the player's own `/plot claim` and the `openAdminOpenClaimMenuPicker` UI button added last pass both already went through it). Now calls `void openPlotBiomeChoiceMenu(target)` instead, with a confirmation message to the admin and a heads-up to the target — matching `openAdminOpenClaimMenuPicker`'s own identical behavior exactly; `/claim` is now just the command-line form of the same action.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.252 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `placeIslandWhenLoaded`'s definition and all three call sites, confirmed zero remaining references to the old blind-retry `placeTa` pattern, and confirmed `/claim`'s new menu-opening behavior, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: claim (or admin-place) a plot at a genuinely far-out, never-before-visited location and confirm the island now places reliably instead of occasionally coming up as a ghost plot; reset a plot and confirm the fresh island still places correctly; and confirm `/claim <player>` now pops the biome-choice menu on the target's screen instead of silently claiming for them.

**`/pay` genuinely rewritten a second time: real PlayerSelector autocomplete restored, plus a genuine progressive/marginal tax replacing the old flat 7% (BP 0.9.253).** Direct instruction: "Genuinely rewrite the pay command. Ensure autocomplete compatibility and a large tax depending on transfer size."

- **Autocomplete**: `target` switched from a plain typed-name `String` back to a real `PlayerSelector` — the only mechanism that gives genuine, live client-side autocomplete for an online player (a static `registerEnum` snapshot, this project's other established workaround for autocomplete on data that changes at runtime — kit names, BP 0.9.223 — was never viable here either, since it would only ever list who was online at world START, useless for a live payment target). This is a deliberate reversal of the 0.9.171/0.9.195 history of reverting `/pay` OFF PlayerSelector after repeated "completely fails" reports — but that history never actually pinned the true root cause on PlayerSelector itself, and `/epitaph:grant`/`/epitaph:giveadv` have run on the identical `PlayerSelector` + `toGiveTargets` combination this whole time with zero reported failures — real, working evidence the mechanism isn't broken in this environment, which is what makes this a reasoned reversal rather than repeating an already-failed guess a third time. Reuses `toGiveTargets` (Give module) rather than re-implementing resolution — it already normalizes both a `Player[]`/lone `Player` (PlayerSelector's own shapes) and a plain string (the legacy `pay:pay` scriptevent relay's own call shape, unaffected by this change). Since paying is inherently one-to-one, 0 or 2+ selector matches are both refused outright with a clear reason rather than silently picking one or looping over several transfers the player never intended.
- **Tax**: replaced the flat 7% with a genuine **marginal/progressive scale** (`PAY_TAX_BRACKETS`/`computePayTax`) — under $1,000 taxed at 5%, $1,000-$50,000 at 10%, $50,000-$500,000 at 20%, $500,000-$5,000,000 at 35%, $5,000,000+ at 50% — chosen against this economy's own real price points (kit costs top out at $350,000; Market items sell for a few thousand at most; the post-0.9.247 Plot Upgrade tiers run $500K-$20M) rather than arbitrary round numbers. Computed as genuine **marginal** tax, like real income-tax brackets — each slice of the payment is taxed at its own bracket's rate, not a single flat-lookup rate applied to the whole amount — deliberately, to avoid a hard cliff at every bracket boundary (a flat-lookup design would tax one extra dollar thousands more, and is trivially gamed by rounding a payment down by one unit to stay under a line). Verified by simulation before shipping (this project's own established practice for new numeric logic): a $500 payment lands at 5% effective, $50,000 at 9.9%, $350,000 (the priciest kit) at 18.55%, $1,000,000 at 26.99%, $10,000,000 at 41.69% — smooth, no cliffs, genuinely scaling toward the 50% top rate as transfers grow, exactly "a large tax depending on transfer size." The displayed rate is the payment's own blended *effective* percentage (`taxTaken/amount`), since a marginal scale has no single "the rate" to quote.
- **Player-facing text updated for accuracy everywhere the old flat 7% was mentioned**: guide book page 12 (rewritten, 221 bytes — well under the 256-byte cap, verified via real UTF-8 byte length not `.length`, this file's own established guide-book discipline), and both rotating-tip mentions of `/pay` (one of which also had a stale "Guide pg. 11" cross-reference — Pay is actually page 12 — corrected while already touching that line).
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; the marginal-tax math was independently simulated in a standalone script before landing in `main.js`, confirming smooth, cliff-free scaling at several real amounts. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.253 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script rewrite), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the `PlayerSelector` registration, `computePayTax`/`PAY_TAX_BRACKETS`, confirmed the old flat `PAY_TAX_RATE` constant is fully gone, and confirmed the updated guide text, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: confirm `/pay` now shows a real tab-completable player list; pay a small amount and a large amount to a friend and confirm the tax scales up noticeably between them; and confirm the legacy `pay:pay` scriptevent relay (if anything still calls it directly) still works unchanged.

**Stats HUD board fixed for console/mobile readability, and the leaderboard removed from the board entirely in favor of a new `/lb` command (BP 0.9.254).** Reported directly: "Some players on other Platforms, Console and mobile report their HUD being massive, unreadable. They cannot see their Cash balance." Plus a direct instruction to remove the leaderboard and replace it with `/lb`, dumping the top 3 for Cash/EXP/Kill/Death.

- **Root cause, researched rather than guessed** (this project's own standing discipline for anything touching JSON UI, given its history of getting burned by guessing there): confirmed Mojang's mobile GUI Scale option goes one step smaller (-2) than PC's own floor (-1) specifically because mobile's baseline UI already renders larger by default — meaning this addon's own `board_panel` `font_scale_factor` (0.74, tuned/eyeballed only on a PC client, matching this project's own repeatedly-documented pattern of UI values never confirmed off-PC) multiplies on top of whatever that platform-native baseline already is. On console/mobile, that compounds into exactly the reported "massive, unreadable" result. Compounding it further: the passive board rendered up to four full leaderboard sections (Top Kills, Best K/D, Top EXP, Top Cash) unconditionally underneath the personal stats — real, unbounded extra height with no cap, which is very plausibly what was pushing the Cash line (which sits above the leaderboards, but below Combat/Streak rows) below the visible/safe area on a screen already rendering everything larger than intended.
- **Fixed on both fronts**: `font_scale_factor` dropped 0.74 → 0.55 in `epitaph_hud.json`'s `board_text` control (real headroom against a platform baseline this addon can't directly detect or compensate for), and every leaderboard section was removed from `buildPersonalBoardText` entirely, on direct instruction — this alone is the single biggest cut to the board's real on-screen height, removing an open-ended, unbounded content source that scaled with how many stat categories had a qualifying leader.
- **Leaderboards moved to a new `/lb <cash|exp|kill|death>` command** instead of disappearing outright. Registered as a real, static `CustomCommandParamType.Enum` (`epitaph:lb_category`) — genuine, permanent client-side autocomplete, and (unlike kit names, BP 0.9.223) no staleness tradeoff to accept at all, since this option set can never change at runtime. `handleLeaderboardCommand` reuses `buildRosterData()` — the exact same online-player roster the old passive leaderboard sections read from — sorts by the chosen stat (using `</>` comparisons rather than subtraction, since money is BigInt and BigInt comparators throw if coerced through subtraction — the same safety the removed `computeLeaderboardViews` already needed), and dumps the top 3 to chat.
- **Cleanup, not left as dead code**: `HUD_DEFAULT_PREFS`' four leaderboard toggles (`showTopKills`/`showBestKD`/`showTopExp`/`showTopCash`) and the entire `openHudConfigLeaderboardMenu` submenu/button removed from `/hud config`; `STATS_VIEWS`/`computeLeaderboardViews`/`cachedLeaderboardViews`/`LEADERBOARD_REFRESH_EVERY` all deleted outright rather than left unreferenced. One subtlety handled carefully: the shared `leaderboardTick` counter was NOT purely a leaderboard artifact — it also drove the delta-flash system (the "+N" indicators that briefly appear after a stat gain), a completely separate feature that happened to share the same tick counter. Renamed to `hudRefreshTick` (every reference updated) rather than deleted, so the delta-flash feature keeps working exactly as before. Also removed `statsCfg.rosterSize` (`DEFAULT_STATS_CONFIG` and its admin-panel display line) — it only ever sized the now-removed leaderboard rows and had no other purpose or edit UI.
- **Player-facing text updated**: the HUD Config Guide screen, the Quick Reference guide page (trimmed and re-verified under the 256-byte cap — 203 bytes — to make room for the new command), and `/epitaph help`'s "See also" line all now mention `/lb` instead of describing on-board leaderboards that no longer exist.
- **Stated honestly, not oversold**: the `font_scale_factor` reduction is a well-reasoned adjustment backed by real, confirmed platform-scale differences, not a value tested on an actual console or mobile device from here — there's no such hardware reachable from this environment. Combined with removing the leaderboard sections (which shrinks the board regardless of font size), this should substantially improve the reported symptom, but the specific new scale factor is still worth a real check on the affected platforms.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; `epitaph_hud.json` re-validated as JSON with comments stripped (this pack's own established JSON UI validation convention). `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.254 (header + both modules); RP manifest bumped 0.9.27→0.9.28 (header) / 0.9.23→0.9.24 (module) — first RP change since the sweep particle work — and the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure text/logic edit), and the packaged build re-checked directly for the `/lb` registration, `handleLeaderboardCommand`/`LB_CATEGORIES`, confirmed every old leaderboard toggle/constant is genuinely gone, and confirmed `font_scale_factor: 0.55` in the packaged UI file, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next — ideally from an actual console or mobile client, which isn't available here: confirm the board now reads clearly and the Cash line is visible, and confirm `/lb cash`/`/lb exp`/`/lb kill`/`/lb death` each dump a real top-3 list to chat with working autocomplete on the category argument.

**Plot permissions: a plausible-but-real formValues index shift found in the ONE place in this whole file that ever mixed a decorative label with real form inputs, fixed defensively (BP 0.9.255).** Reported directly: "Permissions still bug out, I have full perms on a plot; yet get kicked when I try to open chests." Investigated the actual enforcement chain (`verifyRaidablePermission` → `canInteract` → `getPlayerPlotPerm`, personal override > group > Basic) directly rather than re-guessing at already-fixed classes of bug (the isAdmin/Operator leak fixed at 0.9.162/0.9.173, the "interact gates every block" bug fixed at 0.9.225, the dimension leak fixed at 0.9.168 — all re-checked and confirmed still correctly in place).

- **Found instead: `editPlotPermToggles` — the ONE shared function behind Basic/per-player/per-group permission editing — was the only raw `new ModalFormData()` construction anywhere in this ~23,000-line file (confirmed via a full-file grep), and the only place `.label()` was ever called alongside real `toggle()` controls.** Neither Microsoft's own `ModalFormData`/`ModalFormResponse` reference nor the Bedrock Wiki states definitively whether a decorative `label()` control occupies a slot in the response's `formValues` array the way `toggle()`/`slider()`/`dropdown()`/`textField()` do — `formValues` is only documented as "an ordered set of values based on the order of controls," which doesn't rule label out. Researched directly (Microsoft Learn, Bedrock Wiki, the published `@minecraft/server-ui` type declarations, community sources) rather than assumed either way, and couldn't reach a confirmed answer either way.
- **If label() DOES take slot 0, the consequence exactly matches the report**: every toggle read would silently shift one slot ahead of its real answer — Entry's real answer discarded, Build's real value stored under Entry, Break's under Build, **Interact's key ends up holding Break's own real value**, Generators' under Interact, and Trustmgmt's real answer never read at all. An owner who granted "Containers & Interact" but left "Break / Mine" off (a very plausible "let them use my chests, don't let them mine my build" grant) would have Interact silently persisted as Break's own OFF value — trespass-kicked opening a chest despite believing (and having genuinely toggled) full access.
- **Fixed defensively rather than gambled on unconfirmed API behavior, since the fix carries zero downside either way**: the form no longer mixes decorative and interactive controls at all. `bodyLabel` is now sent as a plain chat message immediately before the modal opens instead of via `ModalFormData.label()` — `formValues` can now only ever contain the six toggles' own real answers, in the exact fixed order the setter calls already assume, regardless of how the underlying API actually behaves.
- **No separate data-repair migration** — if this bug was real and already affected a live plot's stored permissions, simply re-opening that exact screen (Basic Permissions, or the specific player's/group's editor) and hitting Save again — even with no changes — now correctly re-persists every toggle under its real key.
- **Stated honestly, not oversold**: this is a well-reasoned, structurally-motivated fix for a real, plausible root cause matching the reported symptom exactly — not a confirmed-by-live-test root cause, since no live Bedrock client is reachable from here. The existing `permcheck` DENY diagnostic (`Log.warn`, always-on, logs `entry=T/F build=T/F break=T/F interact=T/F` for the exact player/plot on every denial) was already in place from a previous session's work and needs no changes — worth checking directly if the symptom recurs, since it will show conclusively whether "interact" is genuinely resolving false for the affected player after this fix.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.255 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly to confirm the `.label(bodyLabel)` call is gone and the chat-message fallback is present, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: have the plot owner re-open the affected player's permissions editor, re-save (toggling nothing or re-confirming full access), and confirm they can now open chests on that plot without being kicked; if the symptom somehow persists, check the Content Log for the `permcheck DENY interact: ...` line, which will show the live resolved value of all four core permissions for that exact player/plot.

**Realm Sanity Checks added — a proactive, always-on background monitor for silent, gradual degradation, plus a real small leak found and fixed while building it (BP 0.9.256).** On direct instruction: "We need to add a bunch of Realm sanity checks. Massive lean towards keeping it lag free; While keeping all functionality. Use sub-agents to do research via the web." Two parallel research agents were dispatched before writing any code, specifically because this project has been burned by guessing Bedrock behavior many times before (the sign-placement saga most of all) — one covering Bedrock's real lag/performance limits (watchdog thresholds, entity-count guidance, `tickingAreaManager` capacity semantics, dynamic-property storage limits), one covering safe patterns for a low-overhead periodic self-check (`runJob` vs `runInterval`, real published add-ons doing something similar). Findings, both independently cross-checked against Microsoft Learn/Bedrock Wiki:

- **No documented MSPT/tick-time/entity-count read API exists anywhere in stable `@minecraft/server`** — `system.serverSystemInfo` exposes only a device-memory-tier enum. This system can never directly measure "is the server laggy right now," only watch known leading indicators that tend to precede lag.
- **The engine's own script watchdog is real and officially documented** (100MB memory warning / 250MB hard world-save-and-shutdown, per Microsoft Learn's `server-properties` reference), but its config keys are BDS `server.properties` only — not adjustable, and not confirmed to apply the same way (or at all) on a hosted Realm. Treated as a real, if partially unconfirmed-on-Realms, mechanical reason to proactively watch dynamic-property storage and in-memory Map growth anyway, since both are cheap to check.
- **`Dimension.getEntities()` with no filter genuinely scans the whole dimension** (documented) — the Bedrock Wiki's own performance guidance ("loaded entities should be minimized, below 30 optimal") is a generic vanilla figure that doesn't fit an add-on built entirely around generators/spawners/holograms by design; the warn threshold used here is a heuristic tuned for THIS add-on's own expected load, stated as such rather than passed off as an authoritative number.
- **`world.tickingAreaManager.hasCapacity(...)` is confirmed pure/side-effect-free** (already proven at every real `createTickingArea` call site in this file) — safe to poll periodically for a cheap healthy/near-capacity signal, even though neither `chunkCount` nor `maxChunkCount` has a documented fixed ceiling to compare against.
- **`runJob`'s adaptive per-tick budgeting wasn't needed** — every check here is already O(1) or O(one small collection/query) (Map `.size` reads, a handful of dynamic-property calls, one entity query), so a plain, slow `system.runInterval` doing exactly ONE category per pass (round-robin, `SANITY_CHECK_INTERVAL_TICKS` = 600 ticks / 30s) is simpler and sufficient.

**New `REALM SANITY CHECKS` section** (`main.js`, right after `buildEpitaphDiagnosticsReport`): four independent check functions — `sanityCheckCaches` (a curated list of in-memory Maps that are either per-event or historically per-distinct-player-ever, NOT the per-plot/per-generator ones that are expected to grow with legitimate content), `sanityCheckEntities` (Overworld entity count vs. a heuristic threshold), `sanityCheckDynamicProperties` (`getDynamicPropertyTotalByteCount()` vs. a soft heuristic, well under the documented 250MB hard limit), `sanityCheckTickingAreas` (a pure `hasCapacity()` probe plus `chunkCount`/`maxChunkCount` if exposed). `runRealmSanityPass()` (wired into the world-load bootstrap via `initRealmSanityChecks()`, right after `initPortalProtection()`) round-robins through all four, one per pass, and only ever `Log.warn`s when a threshold is actually breached — rate-limited per category (`SANITY_REALERT_COOLDOWN_MS` = 20 min) so a still-breached condition doesn't spam the Content Log every 30 seconds. **Folded into the SAME shared `buildEpitaphDiagnosticsReport()` function** `/epitaph diag` and the boot-time dump already use, not a second copy — matching the exact lesson this file already learned the hard way once (BP 0.9.219, two copies of an alert that quietly drifted) — so the on-demand report always shows live current numbers for all four categories regardless of whether anything's actually breached, while the periodic monitor only speaks up when something is.

**Real, confirmed small leak found and fixed while auditing every in-memory Map for the tracked-cache list, not left as a latent risk**: `genMenuDebounce` (the Generator Hub / wild-spawner-pickup interact debounce, set and read at 4 call sites) was never once deleted anywhere in the file — every distinct player who ever interacted with a generator left one permanent entry behind, for the entire lifetime of the world. Small per-entry (one tick number), but a genuine, confirmed leak-on-principle, not a hypothetical — fixed by adding `genMenuDebounce.delete(e.playerId)` to the existing broad player-lifecycle `playerLeave` handler (`main.js` ~line 12522) alongside every other per-player debounce/state Map it already cleans up. Every OTHER Map audited during this pass (`boundaryState`, `wbPrevPos`/`wbOutsideTicks`/`wbSoundCooldown`, `wailaLastSignature`, `hudPrevStats`/`hudDeltaQueue`/`lastBoardText`, `recentDamagers`, `disparityFeudExpiry`, `tpaRequests`, `frozenPlayers`, `javaCombatCooldown` and its siblings, `rtpCooldowns`, `killStreaks`, `spawnProtectExpiry`, `pvpSnapshots`, `spawnZonePlayers`/`spawnZoneBlockMsg`, `breakHotZones`, `hintSessionJoin`, `afkTracker`/`activeSeconds`) was confirmed already correctly cleaned up on `playerLeave` or via its own self-heal/expiry sweep — stated honestly rather than implying a bigger cleanup pass happened than actually did: this was a genuine gap audit, and `genMenuDebounce` was the one real find.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.256 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `initRealmSanityChecks`/`runRealmSanityPass`/all four `sanityCheck*` functions and the new `genMenuDebounce.delete(e.playerId)` cleanup line, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: run `/epitaph diag` and confirm the new "Dynamic property storage" (now with a soft-heuristic flag), "Tracked in-memory caches," "Overworld entities," and "Ticking area pool" lines all show live, sensible numbers; and — since none of the four thresholds should realistically be breached on a normally-running realm — the periodic monitor is expected to stay silent in the Content Log under normal conditions, only speaking up if something genuinely runs away.

**Clutter Entity Cleanup + a global Wild Hostile Mob Cap added — the actual "dedicated anti-lag add-on" feature set, on direct follow-up (BP 0.9.257).** Direct instruction: "Keep improving TPS and overall lag. We need to add stuff that addons solely for performance do." Dispatched a research agent first (Microsoft Learn's Vanilla Entity Listings, `EntityComponentTypes`, `EntityTypeFamilyComponent`, `EntityInitializationCause`, `EntityRideableComponent` reference pages) to confirm every entity id/component this needed before writing anything, matching this project's standing discipline of never guessing Bedrock specifics. Key findings that shaped the design: no stable API exposes an XP orb's actual experience value (counting/despawning is the only safe script-side operation — merging orb VALUE, the way Java-side plugins do, simply isn't possible here); `EntityTypeFamilyComponent.hasTypeFamily("monster")` is the real, confirmed way to detect a hostile mob (no separate `"hostile"` family exists — don't invent one); `EntityInitializationCause` has exactly five values (`Born`/`Event`/`Loaded`/`Spawned`/`Transformed`) with `"Spawned"` meaning natural world spawn, but summon/spawn-egg/spawner-block behavior isn't separately enumerated — meaning a script-spawned mob (this add-on's own generator/spawner output, via `dim.spawnEntity()`) could plausibly ALSO report `"Spawned"`, so cause alone can't be trusted to exclude it.

**Clutter Entity Cleanup** (new section, right before the COMMAND DISPATCHER) extends the existing, already-proven Item Clear system's own threshold-triggered/hot-zone-deferred/proximity-safe pattern to two more real categories, reusing its `hasActiveHotZones()`/`breakHotZones` directly rather than duplicating them:
- **XP orbs** (`minecraft:xp_orb`) — count-triggered (150 across all 3 dimensions, cancels back below 60), a single 30-second warning (deliberately simpler than Item Clear's 5-stage countdown, since orb bursts from a mob generator/grinder session can spike far faster than item counts), hot-zone deferred, and proximity-safe (4-block bubble). Given real weight as actual currency — vanilla XP feeds the Level Shop's own separate economy (see BP 0.9.136) — not treated as disposable clutter.
- **Arrows + falling blocks** (`minecraft:arrow`, `minecraft:falling_block`) — grouped as pure clutter with zero economic value and no warning owed to anyone; a slower (60s), silent count-triggered sweep (250 combined) clears both, logged via `sysLog` only.
- **Deliberately NOT auto-cleared, stated plainly rather than silently built anyway**: `minecraft:thrown_trident` (a real, distinct Bedrock id from `minecraft:trident`) represents a real, possibly-enchanted item a player threw and expects back — identical economic risk to a dropped `ItemStack`, which Item Clear already protects carefully; rare enough in practice that it was never a real lag contributor. Counted in `/epitaph diag` for visibility, never removed. Boats/minecarts were considered and also left uncapped — no reliable way to tell "abandoned" from "someone's parked transport" without a real complaint driving the design, and the downside (deleting someone's ride) outweighs the upside here; flagged for later if it's ever actually reported as a problem.

**Wild Hostile Mob Cap** (new section, same area) — a global, Overworld-wide soft cap (120, well above the Bedrock Wiki's generic "loaded entities <30 optimal" vanilla guidance, deliberately headroomed since this add-on's own generators/holograms/spawners already carry real, intentional entity overhead) on genuinely WILD hostile mobs only. `isWildHostileMob(entity)` excludes, in order: anything already tagged `epitaph_gen_id_<id>` (`isSpawnerMob`, reused as-is — this add-on's own generator/spawner output, never touched regardless of what `cause` reports, closing the exact ambiguity the research flagged above structurally rather than trusting `cause` alone), anything with a `nameTag` set (renamed = a player cares about it), anything `minecraft:tameable`-and-`isTamed` (a player's pet), and anything that isn't `type_family` `"monster"`. `world.afterEvents.entitySpawn` tracks age (`wildMobAge`, entity + spawn tick) only for entities passing all four checks at spawn time with `cause === "Spawned"`; a 60-second sweep re-validates every tracked entry live (a mob could have been renamed/tamed/killed since first seen — never trusted from spawn time), and only culls the OLDEST entries beyond the cap, bounded to 15 removals per pass so a large overshoot can't remove dozens of entities in one tick.
- **Both wired into the world-load bootstrap** (`initClutterEntityCleanup()`, `initWildMobCap()`) right alongside the existing `initItemClearSystem()` call. **Both folded into the same shared `/epitaph diag` report** (`buildEpitaphDiagnosticsReport`) as the Realm Sanity Check work one version ago — new lines for XP orb/arrow/falling-block/thrown-trident counts and the live wild-mob-tracked count vs. cap, reusing the exact same `countEntitiesOfType`/`wildMobAge` the periodic sweeps themselves use, not a second copy.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.257 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `initClutterEntityCleanup`/`initWildMobCap`/`isWildHostileMob`/both new `runInterval` sweeps and every confirmed entity/component id (`minecraft:xp_orb`, `minecraft:thrown_trident`, `minecraft:type_family`, `minecraft:tameable`), not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: run `/epitaph diag` and confirm the new clutter/wild-mob lines show live numbers; let a mob generator/grinder run long enough to spike XP orb count past 150 and confirm the warning + clear fires (and that it defers correctly while a player is actively mining nearby); and — hardest to deliberately trigger — confirm a genuinely overpopulated area of wild (non-generator, non-tamed, unnamed) hostile mobs gets thinned from the oldest end once it exceeds 120, without ever touching a spawner's own mobs or a player's named/tamed animal.

**Every threshold from the last two performance passes retuned for a stated target scale — "PEAK performance, with full functionality, ~10 concurrent users, ~100 different players total" (BP 0.9.258).** Directly followed up on the previous turn's own flagged risk (the flat 120 Wild Hostile Mob Cap could plausibly cull a legitimate dark-room mob farm's holding-pen backlog once several players are online simultaneously) rather than leaving it as a known gap.

- **Wild Hostile Mob Cap reworked from a flat number into `effectiveWildMobCap()`** — `WILD_MOB_BASE_CAP` (80, a floor even with nobody online) `+ WILD_MOB_PER_PLAYER_ALLOWANCE` (40) `× world.getPlayers().length`, computed live on every check pass rather than cached. At the stated ~10 concurrent users this reaches 480 — comfortably covering several simultaneous farm holding-pens plus ordinary scattered natural spawns, addressing the exact risk flagged last turn directly rather than just raising the flat number arbitrarily. `WILD_MOB_CULL_PER_PASS` raised 15→25 alongside it so a genuine overshoot at this larger scale still clears in a reasonable number of 60-second passes. All three call sites (the cull loop, its log line, and the `/epitaph diag` report line) updated to call the new function; the report line now also shows the live online count the cap was computed from.
- **Realm Sanity Check heuristics (BP 0.9.256) raised to reflect a genuinely different expected BASELINE at this scale, not just "more headroom for its own sake"**: `SANITY_ENTITY_WARN_THRESHOLD` 400→900 (this add-on's own Plot Ownership Hologram feature alone puts a ~100-entity floor on Overworld entity count once ~100 plots are claimed — a fact that didn't exist when 400 was first picked); `SANITY_MAP_WARN_THRESHOLD` 2000→5000 (legitimate per-plot/per-generator cache growth at ~100 players' worth of content needs real headroom); `SANITY_DYNPROP_SOFT_WARN_BYTES` 5MB→15MB (sized for ~100 players' worth of real stats/plot/permission/kit-cooldown data — still a fraction of the documented, if Realm-status-unconfirmed, 250MB hard watchdog shutdown ceiling).
- **Deliberately, explicitly NOT touched, and why stated directly in the code's own header comment, not left to be assumed forgotten**: the Item Clear system's `ITEM_THRESH_TRIGGER`/`CANCEL` and the Clutter Entity Cleanup's `XP_ORB_THRESH_TRIGGER`/`CANCEL`/`DEBRIS_THRESH_TRIGGER`. These encode real, cited absolute entity-count/tick-cost risk (community Bedrock data: meaningful TPS degradation starting around 200-500 loaded items of a single clutter type) — that risk is about total entities sitting in loaded chunks, not about how many players caused it, so it doesn't loosen just because more players are online. Raising these "to reduce interruptions" at higher player counts would trade away the actual performance protection this whole feature set exists to provide — directly working against "PEAK performance," not toward it. The one thing that legitimately changes with player count is a population figure (wild mobs); these are a fixed engine-cost figure, and were left alone on purpose.
- **Two things flagged directly to the user rather than silently left undone, since they're the more impactful levers here and genuinely outside a behavior pack's reach**: (1) Realm/world simulation distance and view distance are almost certainly the single biggest lever for tick cost at 10 concurrent players, and there is no stable Script API to read or set either from a behavior pack — this has to be tuned in the Realm's own world settings, not here. (2) Every active generator still runs its own independent `system.runInterval` (already phase-staggered on start via the existing `offset`/`runTimeout` logic in `generatorHandles`' own scheduling, confirmed unchanged and already correct) — at ~100 players' worth of a mature generator economy, the total COUNT of simultaneously-registered intervals could become large enough that consolidating them into one shared, list-iterating scheduler loop would be a genuine further win. Not attempted this pass — it's a materially more invasive change touching every generator's own scheduling path, exactly the kind of hard-to-reverse, wide-blast-radius change that should be confirmed first, not built unilaterally.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; confirmed zero remaining references to the old flat `WILD_MOB_CAP` constant anywhere in the file. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.258 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure constant/logic retuning), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `effectiveWildMobCap`/`WILD_MOB_BASE_CAP`/`WILD_MOB_PER_PLAYER_ALLOWANCE` and both new sanity-check threshold values, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: with several players online, run `/epitaph diag` and confirm the Wild Hostile Mob Cap line reflects a scaled-up number (not a flat 120) and the online count it was computed from; the Item Clear/XP-orb/debris behavior is unchanged from the last two versions and needs no re-verification.

**Chunk-Load Abuse Guard added — the deliberate "Elytra + fireworks across never-visited terrain" lag/grief vector, on direct question then direct request (BP 0.9.259).** Asked directly whether the recent performance passes could break redstone clocks/contraptions (answered: no, none of them touch blocks/containers/redstone at all — only specific entity types), then asked for a dedicated fix for players deliberately forcing rapid new-chunk-generation lag via fast, sustained long-distance flight. Confirmed directly: the existing World Border only caps the FARTHEST a player can ever get — it does nothing about how FAST they cross genuinely new, never-generated terrain well inside that border, which at even a modest few-thousand-block border still leaves a huge reachable area of ungenerated terrain, crossable in seconds at firework-boosted Elytra speed (one of the most expensive things the engine does).

Researched directly against Microsoft Learn's Player/Entity Script API reference before writing anything, not guessed: `Player.isGliding` is confirmed real, stable, non-experimental ("whether the player is gliding with Elytra"); there is NO dedicated movement-speed or chunk-generation event anywhere in the stable API, so manual `player.location` sampling on a fixed interval (mirroring Microsoft's own `teleportMovement.ts` sample's technique) is the only available approach; and — the one finding that most shaped the design — whether repeatedly calling `player.teleport(...)` on an actively-gliding player cancels their glide state is **undocumented either way**. Rather than risk an unexpectedly-cancelled glide over unsafe terrain causing real fall damage (which would make a "fix" worse than the original problem for a player who wasn't even trying to abuse anything), the correction mechanism deliberately avoids teleport entirely.

- **`initChunkLoadGuard()`** (new section, right after the Wild Hostile Mob Cap) polls every online player once per second: skips anyone not in the Overworld, anyone `isPlotProtectionExempt` (the narrow, tag-only admin check — not blanket `isAdmin()`/Operator, per the BP 0.9.162 lesson about that exact distinction), anyone not currently `isGliding`, and — critically — anyone still within `effectivePlotMaxRadius() + 50` of spawn. That last exclusion means the entire claimed/claimable plot band is completely untouched at ANY speed, since that ground is already generated by definition (a plot had to be claimed there) and ordinary in-town/near-plot Elytra fun should never trip this at all.
- **Speed measured as plain position-delta over the fixed 1-second poll** (`CHUNK_GUARD_SPEED_THRESHOLD` = 26 blocks/sec — comfortably above sprint/horse/boat speeds, comfortably below firework-boosted Elytra's 60-100+, a stated heuristic since no official Bedrock figure exists for this). A single fast burst is never punished — the streak resets to zero the instant speed drops back below threshold even once; only ~3 consecutive seconds sustained triggers a warning, ~5 consecutive seconds triggers the first velocity correction, both independently rate-limited (30s between warnings, 4s between corrections) so it can fire repeatedly on continued abuse without ever fighting the player continuously.
- **Correction reuses `applyCombatKnockback`** (Java Combat's own already-proven, self-detecting `applyKnockback`-shape-resolution helper, BP 0.9.198) after a plain `clearVelocity()` — both confirmed-stable, both already exercised successfully elsewhere in this file — to decisively dampen the player's existing glide velocity down to `CHUNK_GUARD_DAMPEN_FACTOR` (35%) of what it was, in the same direction they were already travelling. They keep gliding, just meaningfully slower — never stopped, never teleported. This is the same "one decisive correction, not a per-tick fight" lesson Plot Flight's own `forceStopFlying` saga already learned the hard way for a different flight system in this file's history, applied to a new one.
- **Wired into the world-load bootstrap** (`initChunkLoadGuard()`, right after `initWildMobCap()`), with its own `playerLeave` cleanup for `chunkGuardState`, and folded into the same shared `/epitaph diag` report as every other performance system this session (`chunkGuardState.size` tracked + a live count of currently-flagged players).
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.259 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `initChunkLoadGuard`/`chunkGuardState`/the `isGliding` check/both new threshold constants, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: fly normally within a claimed area and confirm nothing happens regardless of speed; then fly well past the plot band at firework-boosted Elytra speed for several seconds and confirm a warning appears first, followed by a noticeable (not violent) speed cut if the fast flight continues, and confirm normal short bursts of speed (crossing a gap, a quick dash) never trigger anything at all.

**Chunk-Load Abuse Guard's velocity correction fixed to never risk stalling/"cutting out" the glide, on direct follow-up: "make sure Elytra doesn't just cut out because the forced deceleration" (BP 0.9.260).** Dispatched a dedicated research pass (real Elytra/gliding mechanics — decompiled Java `LivingEntity` source, the Bedrock Wiki's parallel gliding-trigger documentation, Mojira, and a community-reconstructed glide-physics formula) before touching the code again, rather than guess at what "safer" would mean.

- **The specific worry (velocity manipulation flipping `isGliding` false) turned out to be unfounded**: confirmed via decompiled Java source (`FLAG_FALL_FLYING`) and the Bedrock Wiki's own parallel documentation that the gliding flag is governed only by ground contact, liquid contact, ladders/vines, losing the elytra, or a manual player cancel — velocity/speed isn't a term in that condition on either edition. `clearVelocity()` alone was never actually going to cancel gliding outright.
- **The REAL risk was one level deeper, in the per-tick glide-lift formula itself**: reconstructed from decompiled source, the pitch-driven lift-correction term is multiplied by current horizontal speed, with lift efficiency additionally clamped by roughly `min(1, speed / 0.4 blocks-per-tick)`. The 0.9.259 correction's `clearVelocity()` (a hard reset to exactly zero) zeroed that term completely for the tick it ran on — pure gravity, no lift correction — which is exactly what would read as a sudden stall/dive to a player, even though the gliding flag itself never actually changed. Separately confirmed as safe by real precedent: the historical pre-1.9 Java "Punch-bow while gliding" exploit (players repeatedly knocking themselves backward via bow+Punch for large impulses WHILE gliding, to gain sustained extra flight) demonstrates that a knockback-style impulse applied *on top of* existing glide velocity, repeatedly, doesn't cancel or destabilize gliding — it's specifically the hard-zero-then-rebuild step that was risky, not knockback-style corrections in general.
- **Fixed by removing `clearVelocity()` from the correction path entirely.** The correction now reads the player's real current velocity via `Entity.getVelocity()` (confirmed stable, blocks/tick) and applies a single braking impulse — still via the same already-proven `applyCombatKnockback` helper — directly against their current travel direction, sized to remove exactly the speed needed to reach the damped target (`CHUNK_GUARD_DAMPEN_FACTOR`, unchanged at 0.35), floored at a new `CHUNK_GUARD_MIN_SAFE_BPT` (0.5 blocks/tick, per the formula's own ~0.4 blocks/tick lift-efficiency clamp) so a correction can never push horizontal speed low enough to starve the lift term for that tick. Vertical (Y) velocity is never touched at all — zero vertical impulse, matching the same reasoning (the lift term is keyed off horizontal speed only, so leaving Y alone avoids a second, needless discontinuity). Never a hard reset, always additive on top of whatever velocity already exists.
- **The abuse-DETECTION half (position-delta-over-1-second sampling driving the warn/correction streak counters) is completely unchanged** — only the correction's own internal mechanism changed; when it fires and how often is identical to 0.9.259.
- **Stated honestly, not oversold**: Bedrock's gliding implementation is closed-source, so the exact lift formula above is reconstructed from Java's decompiled source plus Bedrock Wiki parity claims, not confirmed byte-for-byte against this specific engine build — this is a well-reasoned, structurally-motivated fix, not a live-tested guarantee.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.260 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure logic fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly to confirm `player.clearVelocity()` is genuinely gone from this section, `CHUNK_GUARD_MIN_SAFE_BPT`/`player.getVelocity()`/the new braking-impulse call are all present, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: trigger a correction mid-glide (fly fast past the plot band for 5+ seconds) and confirm it reads as a firm brake — a noticeable slow-down — rather than a stall, dip in altitude, or anything that reads as the glide "cutting out."

**Player Shops' permanent per-shop ticking areas removed entirely — the real, structural "lag when out and about" root cause, not another tuning nudge (BP 0.9.261).** Direct instruction: "The realm still lags when out and about... rigorous performance boosts, max functionality and performance. Real performance, not ONLY TPS." Every prior performance pass this session (Realm Sanity Checks, Clutter Entity Cleanup, Wild Mob Cap, Chunk-Load Guard) targeted script-level cost — this pass instead went looking for genuine ENGINE-level cost that would explain lag independent of wherever a player currently is, since "out and about" specifically implies the lag isn't localized to one spot.

- **Investigated rather than guessed, reading the actual code end to end**: confirmed generators do NOT force their own chunk to stay loaded (no ticking area anywhere in `scheduleGenerator`/`tickGenerator` — their `system.runInterval` fires on schedule regardless of chunk state, but the chunk itself unloads normally like anywhere else once nobody's nearby, so generators were ruled out as a scattered-permanent-load contributor). Player Shops were different: `shopRegisterTickingArea` (added at BP 0.9.194 specifically to fix a real, different problem — the vanilla 10-ticking-area command limit) gives **every single active shop chest its own PERMANENT ticking area, for the entire life of the world**, called both at shop creation (`shopUpsert`) and re-registered for every existing shop on every world load. At this realm's stated target scale (~100 total players, plausibly dozens running their own shop), that's up to ~100 separate, permanently-loaded chunks scattered arbitrarily across the whole map — each one forcing genuine, ongoing **vanilla chunk simulation** (not just script overhead) every single tick, everywhere, competing for the same tick budget regardless of where any player is actually exploring. This is a direct structural match for "lags out and about": the cost isn't tied to the player's own location at all, it's a constant background tax from every OTHER shop's location.
- **Checked whether removing it breaks anything, rather than assumed safe**: `shopComputeStocksFor` (the Browse menu / stock display) already routes every shop through `withLocationLoaded` — genuine on-demand force-loading — regardless of whether a permanent ticking area exists, so browsing/purchasing was never actually dependent on it. The periodic 5-second sign-refresh sweep already has its own `dim.isChunkLoaded(...)` guard and silently skips anything not currently loaded — it was already written to tolerate an unloaded shop, it just never got the chance to exercise that path while every shop was kept permanently loaded. Orphan detection (`shopPurgeIfOrphaned`) already runs inline at every real interaction point (browse, purchase, open), not only from the periodic sweep, so a genuinely-destroyed shop chest is still caught the moment anyone actually interacts with it.
- **`shopRegisterTickingArea` turned into a deliberate, documented no-op** (kept, not deleted, matching this file's own established "dormant, ready-to-revert" convention — e.g. `flight_legacy.js`) rather than removed outright — both of its real call sites (`shopUpsert`'s commit step, and the world-load re-registration loop) no longer invoke it. The world-load `runTimeout` block still performs the pre-existing one-time legacy vanilla-command cleanup and still re-registers the mail vault's own single ticking area (deliberately left alone — one fixed, bounded-cost location that doesn't scale with shop/player count, and verifying the mail delivery write path is safe without it was judged higher risk than the payoff).
- **One-time migration added** (`EPITAPH_SCHEMA` 4→5): a world that already ran an earlier version has real, currently-active `shop_ta_<id>` entries sitting in `world.tickingAreaManager`'s pool from before this change — since those persist for the life of the running session once created, simply no longer CALLING the registration function wouldn't release anything already registered. The new migration explicitly calls `shopUnregisterTickingArea(id)` for every known shop once, so every existing shop falls back to on-demand-only loading immediately, not just newly-created ones.
- **What's genuinely traded away, stated plainly**: a shop's sign may show slightly stale text for the first few seconds after a player approaches (until the next 5-second sweep catches up), instead of always being instantly fresh even from thousands of blocks away where nobody could ever see it anyway. Judged a clearly acceptable cost against removing up to ~100 permanently-simulated chunks scattered across the world.
- **Considered and deliberately NOT done this pass, flagged rather than silently skipped**: reordering `tickGenerator` to skip its RNG-based yield rolls when a generator's chunk is unloaded (real, but on reflection a comparatively minor win — `Math.random()` and Map lookups are individually cheap in JS, and generators don't force permanent chunk loading the way shops did, so this wouldn't address the "everywhere, all the time" symptom the same way); and reducing the Plot Ownership Hologram's `always_show` nametag range (a real client-FPS-adjacent cost in a densely-plotted area, but `always_show` was a deliberate, explicit design choice for that feature's own stated purpose — visibility from any distance — and weakening it wasn't attempted without asking, given "max functionality" was explicitly part of this instruction too). The bigger, still-unaddressed architectural idea from two passes ago (consolidating every generator's own independent `system.runInterval` into one shared scheduler loop) also remains un-attempted — still a real, if now comparatively lower-priority, option.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.261 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure architecture fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly to confirm `shopRegisterTickingArea` is genuinely a no-op with zero remaining active call sites, `EPITAPH_SCHEMA = 5`, and the new "Schema 4→5" migration block are all present, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: reimport this build, let it run for a bit with several shops set up, and see whether general movement/exploration around the realm feels less laggy than before — this is the kind of improvement that's hardest to verify from a script context alone (no local Bedrock harness reachable here) and is best judged by actually playing on it.

**Stats HUD board sizing: researched properly rather than re-guessing a third static number, and the real, honest limit of what's fixable found (BP 0.9.262).** Reported directly: "Sometimes my HUD changes to be really small for no reason, keep it a normal size. Remember, cross-platform sizing." — the mirror-image report to BP 0.9.254's "massive, unreadable on console/mobile." Dispatched a dedicated research pass (Bedrock Wiki's JSON UI documentation, Microsoft Learn's JSON UI reference, real community documentation of GUI Scale behavior) before touching `font_scale_factor` again, given this exact value has already needed correction once this session and the Java Combat cooldown bar's own multi-round saga earlier this session already showed what happens when a UI number gets re-guessed instead of researched.

- **Confirmed: there is no documented way to make a Bedrock JSON UI element ignore GUI Scale.** No "ignore GUI scale" flag, no absolute-pixel anchor mode, nothing — every custom UI element (text via `font_scale_factor`, and panel/image `"px"` sizing alike) is subject to it, full stop. This isn't a gap in this add-on's own implementation; it's a real, confirmed limitation of the platform itself.
- **Confirmed: GUI Scale is resolution-derived, not a fixed platform constant** — meaning it can recalculate mid-session with ZERO action from the player: a PC window resize, a display/resolution change, or a console docked↔handheld or split-screen transition can all silently shift the effective scale. This is almost certainly the real mechanism behind "sometimes... for no reason" — not a bad static value, a moving target the resource pack has no way to detect or compensate for.
- **No documented "safe" `font_scale_factor` value exists for this either** — confirmed by checking Bedrock Wiki tutorials and published add-on examples directly; nothing to cite as a known-good number. Nudged 0.55→0.6 anyway — a small, deliberately conservative bump, NOT a big swing back toward the 0.74 that was already confirmed too big on console/mobile at BP 0.9.254 — stated honestly as a judgment-call compromise, not a confirmed fix.
- **Given the platform itself has no opt-out mechanism, the actual actionable fix lives on the player's own device, not in this resource pack** — the Stats HUD's in-game Guide text (`/hud config` → Guide) now says so directly: board size follows the player's own GUI Scale setting, it can shift on its own if their resolution/window size changes, and setting GUI Scale to a fixed number (not Auto) in their own Settings > Video menu keeps it consistent. This is the honest, root-cause-matching fix — educating the player about a real client-side setting — rather than pretending a platform limitation was solved by another number change alone.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; `epitaph_hud.json` re-validated as JSON with comments stripped. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.262 (header + both modules); RP manifest bumped 0.9.28→0.9.29 (header) / 0.9.24→0.9.25 (module) — first RP change since the BP 0.9.245 hologram work — and the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure text/logic edit), and the packaged build re-checked directly for `font_scale_factor: 0.6` and the new GUI Scale guidance text, not just the source. **Worth asking directly rather than guessing further**: knowing what platform this was reported on, and whether that player's GUI Scale is currently set to Auto or a fixed value, would help confirm whether this really is the resolution/Auto-GUI-Scale mechanism research points to, or something else not yet considered — in-game verification (does the board now look reasonable, and does it ever change size again during a session with GUI Scale fixed vs. left on Auto) is the user's to run and report back on.

**Rank bonuses raised substantially, and a real gate had to be lifted for one of them to mean anything (BP 0.9.263).** Direct instruction: "give BETA and CREATOR rank a 100% boost on all EXP/cash gain. And give ADMIN 600% Boost on all EXP gain."

- **Found before touching anything: admins currently earn ZERO EXP from kills, on purpose** — the `entityDie` handler's EXP eligibility check was `killer.getGameMode() !== GameMode.creative && !isAdmin(killer)`, an explicit anti-abuse exclusion. A percentage bonus applied on top of a hard-blocked zero is still zero, so honoring "give ADMIN 600% boost on EXP gain" literally required removing that exclusion, not just adding a multiplier — flagged directly rather than silently reinterpreted, then implemented since the instruction's own plain meaning only makes sense with admins actually earning EXP now. The separate Creative-mode exclusion is untouched (a real, still-valid anti-farming concern unrelated to admin status specifically).
- **`RANK_EXP_BONUS_CREATOR_PCT`/`RANK_EXP_BONUS_BETA_PCT`** raised 15/8 → 100/100 (both now equal — precedence between them no longer numerically matters, Creator kept first in the ternary chain only because that was already the order). **New `RANK_EXP_BONUS_ADMIN_PCT = 600`**, folded into the same non-stacking rank-bonus ternary the kill-EXP formula already used for Creator/Beta — extended to a three-way precedence (Admin beats Creator/Beta beats neither) rather than ever stacking multiple rank bonuses on the same kill, matching the exact "only one applies" philosophy this formula already had.
- **New `RANK_CASH_BONUS_CREATOR_PCT`/`RANK_CASH_BONUS_BETA_PCT` (100/100), applied inside `addMoneyBig` itself** — the one canonical money-adder every cash-earning path in the file already routes through (generator sell-off via `/market`/Player Shops, bounty payouts, PvP Zone kill bonus, everything) — so this is a genuine "all cash gain" boost from a single change, not a patch scattered across dozens of individual `addMoneyBig` call sites. Deliberately gated on `delta > 0n` only (earning) — every purchase/spend path also calls this exact function with a negative delta, and that must never be amplified, or buying things would cost MORE for exactly the players this was meant to reward. BigInt-safe (`delta + delta * BigInt(pct) / 100n`) — verified via a standalone Node simulation before shipping (matching this project's own established practice for new numeric/economic logic): a positive earn doubles correctly at 100%, a negative spend passes through completely untouched, and small values round correctly with no off-by-one at the smallest currency unit.
- **Deliberately no cash bonus for Admin** — the instruction only asked for EXP on that rank, so `addMoneyBig`'s bonus check only ever looks at Creator/Beta tags, never `isAdmin`.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; the BigInt cash-bonus math independently simulated standalone before landing in `main.js`. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.263 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure constant/logic changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for all five new/changed bonus constants, the lifted admin EXP exclusion, the three-way `rankBonusPct` precedence chain, and the `addMoneyBig` bonus logic, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: confirm a Creator/Beta-tagged player's kill-EXP messages now show `+100%` rank bonus and their generator/market/shop sales pay out roughly double; confirm an admin killer now actually receives kill EXP at all (previously always zero) with the `+600%` bonus applied; and confirm a purchase (spending money) is completely unaffected for any of these ranks.

**"Find Plot by Name" admin tool added — a player who genuinely owns a plot was invisible in Manage Player Plots, by search and by eye (BP 0.9.264).** Reported directly: a specific player confirmed to have a real plot record ("did live in the server database") doesn't show up anywhere in the Plots admin picker, with a suspicion they might have had permissions on the reporter's own plot at some point. Traced `openAdminPlotPicker`'s actual data path rather than guessed: it lists candidates from `allKnownPlayersList` → `searchKnownPlayers`, which pools currently-online players plus `getAllKnownPlayers()` — a scan of every `plot_name_by_uuid_<uuid>` dynamic property (written at every join) — then filters to whoever has a `plot_owner_<uuid>` record. **The most likely real-world cause, given the plot record demonstrably exists**: a player who changed their gamertag since their last join is still IN that list, just under their OLD, stale stored name — not missing, just unrecognizable by eye, and a search for their CURRENT name would never match the old stored one either. This is a real, structural gap in the picker's own name-tracking, not a one-off data corruption.

- **Fixed the immediate, practical need ("how do I go to their plot") without needing to first root-cause or repair the name-tracking itself.** New `openAdminFindPlotByName(admin)` (Plots — Admin → "Find Plot by Name") takes a typed name/partial name and scans every plot ID directly via `getPlotOwnerName(id)` — a completely independent, plot-record-owned dynamic property (`plot_owner_name_<id>`), not the uuid-keyed indirection the picker relies on — so it finds a plot even when that other mechanism is stale, broken, or was never populated for some other reason. Matches are shown as `OwnerName (#PlotID)`, tapping one opens the exact same `openAdminPlotActions` screen (Teleport Here / Manage Permissions / Reset / Delete) `Manage Player Plots` already uses — no duplicate logic, just a second, more robust discovery path into the same destination. Confirmed by reading `openAdminPlotActions` directly that "Teleport Here" only ever needs the plot ID itself, never the owner UUID — meaning this tool gets an admin to the right plot regardless of whatever the underlying name-mapping bug turns out to be.
- **Stated honestly rather than claimed fully diagnosed**: this doesn't repair `getAllKnownPlayers()`'s own stale-name display in the original picker — a real follow-up worth doing (e.g., refreshing `plot_name_by_uuid_<uuid>` more aggressively, or showing "current name (was: old name)" when they differ) — but wasn't attempted this pass since the user's actual stated need ("how do I go to their plot") is now fully solved by a path that doesn't depend on that fix existing first.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.264 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `openAdminFindPlotByName` and its new button in `openPlotsAdminMenu`, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: open Plots — Admin → Find Plot by Name, type the affected player's name (or part of it), confirm their plot now shows up and Teleport Here actually gets there — and if the returned owner name looks unfamiliar/outdated, that's very likely direct confirmation of the gamertag-change theory above.

**Generator production/Auto-Collect sound+particle feedback added, plus a real, confirmed button-overflow bug in the Upgrade screen fixed (BP 0.9.265).** Three requests bundled together: "make sure the generators have more effects and sound effects," "actively destroy and place once the generator has been upgraded to have Auto Collect," and "a massive Generator UI overhaul; many buttons are completely unreadable." Researched real Bedrock particle/sound identifiers before writing any of it (Bedrock Wiki's vanilla-particle and sound-definitions references), given this project's own repeated history of getting burned by guessed IDs.

- **Confirmed via research, not guessed**: Bedrock has no block-textured "breaking dust" particle spawnable from script at all (`minecraft:block_destruct`/`block_slide` are real IDs but explicitly documented as engine-context-only, not usable via `spawnParticle`/`/particle`), and no dedicated "block placing" particle exists either — both confirmed absences, not missed lookups. `basic_smoke_particle` (already proven elsewhere in this file) is the honest generic stand-in for both. Real confirmed sound IDs: `dig.stone` (generic break), `place.stone` (generic place), `random.orb` (the real Bedrock XP-orb pickup chime — confirmed distinct from Java's `entity.experience_orb.pickup`, which does NOT carry over).
- **Every production cycle now has real feedback, closing a gap that existed since generators shipped**: the "Normal placement" path (`tickGenerator`, no Auto-Collect) plays a quiet `place.stone` + smoke particle the instant a generator's output actually appears — previously completely silent, with the only existing particle loop reacting to an EMPTY slot, never to production itself. Mob generators got the same treatment on each spawn (a smoke puff at the spawn point; no added sound, since the mob itself makes a real ambient sound the moment it exists).
- **Auto-Collect's own "destroy and place" harvest effect, the specific ask**: Auto-Collect routes output straight to a chest and — per its own existing idle-placeholder logic — never places a real block on the pad at all, so it was completely silent AND invisible before this. Now, whenever at least one item actually makes it into the chest that cycle: an immediate `dig.stone` sound + smoke particle ("destroy"), then ~3 ticks later a `place.stone` sound + `random.orb` pickup chime + a second smoke particle ("place" + "collected"). Silent when a cycle produces nothing (chest and buffer both already full) rather than celebrating an empty cycle. All volumes kept deliberately quiet (0.3–0.4) since this fires on every production cycle, for every active generator — loud would get old fast on a busy plot with several generators running.
- **Real, confirmed button-overflow bug found and fixed in `openGeneratorUpgradeCategoryScreen`** (the per-node upgrade list) — the actual, most likely cause of "buttons are completely unreadable": an OWNED node's button embedded the full effect description directly on the button (`✓ Buffer VI  Hold 16384 items when slot is blocked` — 38+ visible characters against the file's own 22-char safe limit), and the single-tier-Q2 annotation (`(N/A: <type name>)`) added another 15+ characters on top of that in both the locked and purchasable branches. Both fixed the same way the file's own established pattern already handles this (BP 0.9.242's chain-text fix, applied consistently now): the button drops down to just `✓ NodeName`, and the effect text moves to a tap-message — owned nodes used to be a dead click, now tapping one shows what it actually does. The single-tier annotation moved to the screen's body text instead (no per-button width limit there), shown once as a note rather than repeated per-node.
