- **Scoped honestly, not oversold as fixing every generator screen**: this pass targeted the Upgrade Category screen specifically, since it's the one with confirmed live evidence of overflowing labels (repeated `warnIfButtonLabelRisky` log spam for exactly these nodes, seen in an earlier session's own Content Log). The Generator Hub, NPC Market purchase screens, and Prestige menu were spot-checked and not found to have the same class of issue — if a specific OTHER screen is still unreadable, naming which one narrows this down far faster than another blind pass over every generator-related screen at once.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.265 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes (all confirmed vanilla sound/particle IDs, nothing new to ship). `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `dig.stone`/`place.stone`/`random.orb`, the reworked owned-node button + tap-message, and the moved single-tier note, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: watch a normal (non-Auto-Collect) generator produce and confirm a quiet place sound/puff now plays; watch an Auto-Collect generator's chest and confirm the destroy-then-place-and-collect sequence plays each cycle it actually collects something; and open a generator's Upgrade screen to confirm every button (owned, locked, purchasable) now reads cleanly with no visible truncation risk.

**Direct pushback on the 0.9.265 pass — "no, the FULL and entire UI recode," Auto-Collect still "feels like ores coming out of thin air," and ore generators need to stop giving compressed blocks (BP 0.9.266).** Read every remaining generator-related screen this time (Category Menu, Item List, Quantity Menu, Hub, Stats, Rename, Upgrade Category Select, Upgrade Category Screen, Prestige) rather than assuming the prior pass covered enough — found no further button-overflow bugs anywhere else (all already short/colored), but found and fixed something more significant while checking Prestige, plus did the two substantive redesigns actually asked for.

- **Ore generators stop giving compressed blocks, on direct instruction ("stop it from giving Ore Blocks, have it give Raw Ore/Ore").** Root cause was deeper than the Quality/Refine upgrade path — `computeOutputBlock`'s own BASE case (`Quality`-less, i.e. what every ore generator gives from day one) was `` `minecraft:${type}` ``, and for a type like `gold_ore` that's the literal, real, minable ORE BLOCK id — as if the generator Silk-Touched itself every single cycle — not the loose item a normal pickaxe actually drops. New `ORE_BASE_ITEM` maps all nine purchasable "ore" category types to their real drop item (`raw_iron`, `raw_gold`, `lapis_lazuli`, `redstone`, `raw_copper`, `diamond`, `emerald`, `quartz`, `coal`), used as the fallback in both `computeOutputBlock` and `getRefinedBlock` (replacing the bare block-id fallback in both places). `REFINEMENT_TABLE`'s own entries for those same nine types were rewritten to refine INTO the real smelted form where one actually exists (iron/gold/copper → their real ingot) rather than a compressed block — materials with no further vanilla processing step (lapis/redstone/diamond/emerald/quartz/coal) just have nothing left to refine into, stated as `null` rather than reaching for an unrelated decorative block just to fill the slot. **Deliberately untouched**: Ancient Debris (no separate raw/refined form exists) and all seven "Block" category generators (Amethyst/Quartz/Lapis/Gold/Emerald/Diamond/Netherite Block) — those are a real, separate, more expensive tier whose entire economic purpose IS producing the compressed block; giving them the same treatment would make their whole reason to exist redundant with the cheaper ore tier sitting right below them.
- **Real, confirmed correctness bug found and fixed while making that change, not after a report**: `openPrestigeMenu` traded in `` `minecraft:${type}` `` — a hardcoded assumption that a generator's output was always the literal ore block. Left unfixed, every ore generator's Prestige screen would have started asking players to hand over an item type they could never hold anymore, since production no longer produces it. Fixed by resolving the trade-in item via `computeOutputBlock(genId)` — the exact same resolver `tickGenerator` itself uses to decide what to actually produce — so Prestige always asks for whatever a given generator is REALLY making right now (raw ore, ingot, or a real Block-tier generator's own block), staying correct automatically through any future Quality upgrade too, not just this pass's change. The screen's body/button text was also corrected from "Trade {X} blocks" to just "Trade {X}" (using the real item's own display name via `shopItemDisplayName`, not the generator's own type label) — the old wording no longer made sense once the traded item usually isn't a block anymore.
- **Auto-Collect completely redesigned around an actual visible harvest cycle, on direct follow-up ("feel like a genuine auto farm; not just feel like ores coming out of thin air").** The 0.9.265 pass added real sound/particle feedback but still wrote straight to the chest instantly — audible, but nothing to actually watch happen. Now: the real output block genuinely PLACES on the pad the moment production fires (the identical physical block a non-Auto-Collect generator would show) with a place sound; after `GEN_AUTO_COLLECT_HARVEST_DELAY_TICKS` (5 ticks, ~0.25s — long enough to register, short enough not to feel slow), it's "harvested" with a real destroy sound + particle, the item(s) are pushed into the chest (or buffer) using the exact same, unmodified economic logic as before this redesign, and — only once everything was actually collected somewhere — the pad is restored to its idle placeholder with a final collect chime. If the chest AND buffer are both genuinely full, the real block is deliberately left sitting there uncollected (the same last-resort this path always had, so nothing is silently lost) rather than being cleared and celebrated. Checked for a real timing interaction before shipping, not assumed safe: the harvest delay could in principle overlap a generator's own production interval and confuse the "is the output slot occupied" check at the top of `tickGenerator` — confirmed safe in practice, since even the fastest realistically-maxed generator (Speed IV + Fuel Boost + Plot Crystal stacked on the lowest real base interval) still ticks at roughly 80+ ticks, comfortably clear of the 5-tick delay.
- **`GEN_CATEGORY_DESC.quality`'s own description text updated** ("Upgrades the output to a better block type each tier" → "Refines the output into its real ore/ingot form (never a compressed block)") to match the new design instead of contradicting it.
- **Stated honestly rather than claimed as a ground-up rewrite**: this pass re-verified every generator screen's button lengths/colors and found nothing further to fix beyond what 0.9.265 already caught — the "full UI recode" ask, on the concrete evidence available, turned out to already be satisfied structurally; what was genuinely still missing was the auto-collect FEEL and the ore/block economy correctness, both addressed here. If a specific screen still looks wrong in practice, naming it precisely remains the fastest path to a real fix.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed after every stage. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.266 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `ORE_BASE_ITEM`, the rewritten `REFINEMENT_TABLE` entries, `openPrestigeMenu`'s `computeOutputBlock(genId)` fix, and the full `GEN_AUTO_COLLECT_HARVEST_DELAY_TICKS`/`leftPhysicalBlock` harvest-cycle logic, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: confirm a fresh (no Quality) ore generator now gives the real raw ore/gem item instead of an ore block; confirm Refine I/II on an Iron/Gold/Copper generator gives the real ingot; confirm Prestige on an ore generator now asks for the correct current item; and watch an Auto-Collect generator's pad directly to confirm the place → sit → harvest → collect cycle is now genuinely visible instead of instant.

**Full codebase bug-hunt pass, on direct instruction: "Any bug, no matter how small or how big, go ahead and FIX it. NOT patch; genuine fix" (BP 0.9.267).** Dispatched four parallel research agents to read the entire ~24,200-line file end to end in overlapping-free chunks (plot/permissions/terrain/world-border; economy/generators/Java Combat/NPC Market; Player Shops/mailbox/ranks/PvP Zone/Tips; PvP-tag/WAILA/Chunk-Load-Guard/Sanity-Checks/HUD), each instructed to report only genuine, confident behavioral bugs with exact file/line citations — not style preferences — and to read this file's own extensive inline changelog first so already-fixed history wasn't re-reported as new. Every finding was independently re-verified by reading the actual current code before any fix was written. Six real, confirmed bugs found and fixed:

1. **PvP Zone has never activated, ever, for any admin-configured zone — the single most severe finding.** `inPvpZone(loc, dimensionId)` compared the incoming `dimensionId` (always the PREFIXED form, `"minecraft:overworld"` — confirmed by cross-checking the other 19 places in this file that rely on that exact convention) directly against `pvpZoneCfg.dimension` (always stored UNPREFIXED, `"overworld"`, straight from the setup dropdown). `"minecraft:overworld" !== "overworld"` is always true, so the function returned `false` unconditionally regardless of configuration — meaning neither the gear-disparity bypass nor the kill EXP/cash bonus (both features built and shipped around this exact function) could ever actually fire, for as long as PvP Zone has existed. Fixed by stripping the `"minecraft:"` prefix (if present) before comparing, the same normalization technique already proven for the identical class of bug in biome-id comparisons (`normalizeBiomeId`, BP 0.9.249).
2. **`clearPlotArea`** (Delete Plot / Reset Plot's own build-area clearing, added BP 0.9.247) was the ONLY remaining place in the whole file still registering a ticking area via the raw vanilla command — the exact hard-capped-at-10-areas-per-world pool this file already root-caused and migrated away from twice (BP 0.9.194, BP 0.9.250), with no capacity check and a bare `try/catch` swallowing any failure silently. An admin deleting/resetting a few plots within the same ~9-second clearing window could exhaust that pool for every other system sharing it. Migrated onto `world.tickingAreaManager`, the same real fix already proven at both those other call sites — and sized the area to the plot's ACTUAL full clearing bounds instead of a 5-block-radius circle around center, which was never large enough to reliably cover a big plot's edges in the first place.
3. **Generator buffers didn't track item type per unit — a real, if bounded, free-upgrade exploit.** The buffer stored one plain integer count with no record of what item type each unit actually was when produced; both collection paths resolved the type FRESH at collection time via `computeOutputBlock`. Concretely: buffer raw ore from an Iron/Gold/Copper Ore generator with no Quality owned, THEN buy Refine I, THEN collect — every already-buffered raw-ore unit came out as the smelted ingot instead, for free. Separately, Transmute's rare 2%-chance item silently downgraded to the generator's ordinary current output type if it couldn't route directly into an Auto-Collect chest. Fixed by replacing the single integer with an ordered list of `{type, count}` batches (`gen_buffer_batches_<id>`) — every write now records the real type produced at that exact moment, and collection drains oldest-batch-first, each at its own correct type. A new `EPITAPH_SCHEMA` 5→6 migration converts any already-buffered count on an existing world into one batch at that generator's current output type, so nothing is silently lost — it just can't recover the exact original type mix that already existed before this fix (that information never existed to begin with).
4. **`canPurchaseNode`'s `quality_3` (Transmute) special case dropped ALL Quality prerequisites, not just the intended redundant one**, for any single-tier generator type. The code `filter`ed `"quality_2"` out of `quality_3`'s prereqs (`["quality_2"]`) for types with no real tier-2 refinement — which for a single-entry array leaves nothing at all, not "fall back to requiring quality_1." Transmute (35,000 EXP) was purchasable with zero Quality investment for every single-tier type — and BP 0.9.266's own ore-economy rework earlier this session materially widened this bug's reach, since iron/gold/copper generators are now single-tier too. Fixed by substituting `"quality_1"` in place of the skipped `"quality_2"` instead of dropping the requirement outright.
5. **Player Shops' 3-shops-per-player cap could be bypassed entirely** by aiming directly at a fresh, never-registered chest and running `/shop:setup` (the more natural, likely more common invocation) instead of the proximity-scan fallback — the direct-aim path never checked the cap at all, only the fallback and the sign-based auto-register path did. Fixed by adding the identical cap check to the direct-aim path, gated so it only ever applies to genuinely NEW shops (editing/repricing an already-owned shop is never blocked by a cap on creating new ones).
6. **`/plot home <name>` (and its underlying `getPlotIDByName`) was exact-case**, unlike every other name-resolution path in this file that already had to learn this lesson (`/pay`, `/bounty`, admin `/claim` — all fixed at BP 0.9.171/0.9.195/0.9.196). A real, plot-owning player would be reported as having no plot at all if the typed name differed only in letter case. Fixed to match case-insensitively using the same offline-inclusive `getAllKnownPlayers()` pool this file's other fixed lookups already use.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed after every stage; the buffer batch add/drain logic was independently simulated standalone in Node before shipping (confirmed correct oldest-first draining, correct per-type preservation across a partial collect, and correct same-type batch merging). `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.267 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure logic fixes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for all six fixes (the dimension-prefix normalization, the `world.tickingAreaManager` migration in `clearPlotArea`, `getBufferBatches`/`addToBuffer`/`drainBufferInto`, the `quality_3` prereq substitution, the new Path A shop-cap check, and `getPlotIDByName`'s case-insensitive lookup), not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: confirm a configured PvP Zone now actually suspends gear disparity and pays its kill bonus inside the zone; confirm deleting/resetting several plots in quick succession no longer risks ticking-area exhaustion; buffer some raw ore, buy a Refine upgrade, and confirm collecting the buffer gives the OLD raw ore, not the new ingot; confirm Transmute now correctly requires at least Quality I on a single-tier generator; confirm a 4th shop is refused when aiming directly at a fresh chest; and confirm `/plot home <name>` now works regardless of letter case.

**The real, computed root cause of "Buffer Alert and Overflow Alert are whitewashed, AGAIN" finally found and fixed — not by re-guessing, by actually measuring every node (BP 0.9.268).** Direct instruction: read the specific upgrades named, figure out why they're whitewashed, fix it properly, and apply the fix to every button in the UI — "think properly about how buttons should be displayed... colour and placement." The 0.9.242 fix (this session's earlier pass) had already closed ONE overflow source on this exact screen (the locked-node ancestor-chain text) but never re-measured what was still left on the button afterward. This time, every node's real worst-case visible button length was computed directly (a standalone script against the actual current templates), not eyeballed:

- **Confirmed root cause: the redundant `" EXP"` unit suffix, appended to every single cost on a screen whose own body text already says "EXP $balance," plus the verbose `"  • locked"` status text, pushed real buttons past `BUTTON_LABEL_SAFE_CHARS` (22).** Computed worst case: **Overflow Alert hit 24 visible characters** (both locked AND purchasable) — confirmed over the limit exactly as reported. Auto-Collect (23) and Pack Size II (23) were ALSO over, silently, without ever being named — and Buffer Alert sat at exactly 22, zero margin, plausibly why it read as affected too (character-count isn't real pixel width; a string exactly at the stated "safe" limit can still visually clip depending on which letters are in it). Bedrock silently truncates an overflowing button label into an auto-generated hover tooltip that always renders in its own flat, uncolored style regardless of any § codes in the string — this is the real, established mechanism behind "whitewashed" text in this project's own history (BP 0.9.131/0.9.135), confirmed again here with actual numbers, not assumed.
- **Fixed by dropping the redundant `" EXP"` suffix entirely** (every cost on this screen now just shows the bare abbreviated number, e.g. `"4.0K"` not `"4.0K EXP"` — the body's own header line already establishes the unit) **and shortening `"  • locked"` to `" Locked"`** (single separating space, no bullet). Recomputed the SAME worst-case check across every node in the file with the new template: **21 visible characters, worst case — every single node now has real margin below the limit, not just the two originally named.**
- **Found a second, real overflow bug while auditing every other button in the generator UI as instructed ("reflect this fix upon every single button"), one this session's own earlier BP 0.9.266 Prestige fix had introduced**: `openPrestigeMenu`'s "Not enough {itemLabel}" button used to repeat a short, fixed item name safely, but BP 0.9.266 made that trade-in item whatever the generator ACTUALLY currently produces (raw ore, ingot, or a Block-tier generator's own block) — several of which are long enough to overflow on their own. Computed the real worst case across every possible current-output item: **"Not enough Netherite Block" hit 26 visible characters**, with several others (Amethyst Block, Copper Ingot, Emerald Block, Diamond Block, Ancient Debris, Quartz Block, Lapis Lazuli) also over. Fixed by dropping the item name from the button entirely — the screen's own body text two lines up already names the exact item in its "Cost" line with no width limit, so the button just says "Not enough" now.
- **Every other generator screen was re-verified with the same computed rigor, not just re-eyeballed**: Category Select, NPC Market Item List/Quantity Menu (both use abbreviated `$` amounts via `formatMoneyBig`, worst real case ~20 chars), and the Hub's "✦ Collect N items" button (worst case 21, at the true Buffer VI cap of 16,384) — all confirmed to already have real margin, no changes needed.
- **Deliberately NOT changed, stated honestly rather than silently altered**: locked nodes still render fully gray rather than a muted version of their line's own color. This is a real, deliberate design choice from earlier in this project's history (the line's color is meant to "light up" only once a node becomes reachable, a soft reveal cue for "you're getting closer") — not itself the bug being fixed here, and changing it risked new color collisions between different upgrade lines (several lines' own "unlocked" colors are already used as OTHER lines' bright colors, e.g. `pack_size` uses `darkAqua` — the natural "dim aqua" a locked Speed node might use). Flagged rather than changed unilaterally; happy to revisit specifically if wanted now that the actual overflow bug is confirmed fixed.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. Every claimed length was verified by an actual standalone Node computation against the real current label/cost tables before and after the fix, not estimated by eye. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.268 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure text/logic changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the new `" Locked"` status text, the EXP-suffix-free cost string, and the shortened Prestige "Not enough" button, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: open a generator's Utility upgrade screen and confirm Buffer Alert and Overflow Alert both now show real, distinct color text (not a white/uncolored hover-only tooltip); check Auto-Collect and Pack Size II on the same basis; and open Prestige on a Netherite Block (or any Block-tier) generator to confirm the "Not enough" button no longer risks the same issue.

**Plot claiming's real, computed root cause of "3 minutes for plot #1" found by actually reading the live Content Log, not guessed at a sixth time (BP 0.9.270).** Direct instruction: "analyze the entire Plot System, ACTUALLY analyze it" — user supplied a full boot-to-claim log. The log was decisive:

- **The boot diagnostic itself contained the smoking gun: "Ticking area pool: healthy (15/300 chunks)"** — a REAL, live-measured total ceiling of 300 chunks for `world.tickingAreaManager`, on this world, right now. BP 0.9.250 had migrated plot claiming's terrain/biome search off the old vanilla 10-area command pool onto this manager specifically because its docs describe it as "far larger" — but that migration never actually MEASURED the real number, and bumped every terrain-search batch size 6→10 on that unverified assumption. The claim log itself then showed 8 separate `"tickingAreaManager at capacity"` lines fire during the VERY FIRST plot claim on a brand-new world — conclusive proof the assumption was wrong, not a guess this time.
- **Computed exactly why capacity was blown, not just observed that it was**: `evaluateTerrainCandidate`/`forceLoadAndCheckTerrain`/`prescanCell` all independently sized their sampling grid AND ticking area off `(PLOT_SIZE_CHUNKS * 16) / 2` = 64 — the MAXIMUM possible future plot size (128×128), "even at max plot size," regardless of the fact only the STARTER 64×64 island (`PLOT_HALF_STARTER` = 32) actually gets placed and needs checking right now. At half=64 plus the old 32-block margin, one candidate's ticking area spanned 192×192 blocks — a 12×12 = 144-chunk request. `TERRAIN_CANDIDATE_BATCH_SIZE` = 10 candidates fire fully CONCURRENTLY inside `tryTerrainCombined` (confirmed by reading the actual `batch.forEach` loop — no `await` between them), so one batch could demand up to **1,440 chunks all at once against a real 300-chunk ceiling** — nearly 5× over. Most of the batch failed `hasCapacity()` and fell back to "checking whatever's already loaded," which on cursor #0's first-ever claim is nothing at all — producing inconclusive reads, wasted candidates, and far more retries than should have been needed. That's the real, computed mechanism behind the 3-minute wait, not a vague "it's just slow."
- **Fixed at the root, not by nudging a batch-size number again**: introduced one shared `TERRAIN_CHECK_HALF` constant (= `PLOT_HALF_STARTER`, 32) replacing the three independently-duplicated `(PLOT_SIZE_CHUNKS * 16) / 2` copies — the exact "two copies quietly drift apart" bug class this file has already been burned by once (BP 0.9.219's incomplete multi-occurrence edit) — so the sampling grid and its loaded ticking area can never disagree again. `TERRAIN_MARGIN_BLOCKS` halved (32→16) alongside it. `TERRAIN_CANDIDATE_BATCH_SIZE` and `BIOME_TERRAIN_BATCH_SIZE` (both of which drive `forceLoadAndCheckTerrain`'s real, full-footprint ticking area) dropped 10→4 — `BIOME_SEARCH_BATCH_SIZE` (single-point, ~1-chunk areas) was left at 10 since its real chunk cost per candidate is trivial regardless. Combined, worst-case concurrent demand for a terrain batch drops from ~1,440 chunks to roughly 256 — comfortably under the confirmed real 300-chunk ceiling, with genuine headroom left for whatever else (shops, the mail vault, the background pre-scan) shares the same pool at the same moment.
- **A real trade-off, stated honestly rather than silently made**: `purchasePlotBorderUpgrade` (the later action that actually grows a plot past starter size) was checked directly and has never done its own terrain re-check at any tier — the old max-size pre-check was only ever an unenforced, soft assumption that a later border upgrade's terrain would probably still be fine, never a guarantee. Shrinking the pre-check to starter size removes that soft assumption, not a hard one — a border upgrade could, in principle, expand into never-vetted terrain, exactly as (only marginally more so than) before this fix. A real terrain check AT border-upgrade time would be the correct, separate fix for that specific concern if it's ever wanted — not built here, since it's out of scope for "claiming takes too long."
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.270 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure constant/logic changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `TERRAIN_CHECK_HALF` (all three call sites now referencing the one shared constant), the halved margin, and both batch-size cuts, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: claim a fresh plot and check the Content Log — the repeated "tickingAreaManager at capacity" lines should be gone or far rarer, and the whole claim should resolve in a small fraction of the previous ~3 minutes.

**0.9.270 made things worse, not better — reported directly ("that ruined it... I never claim a plot," tried twice), with a firm new priority order (BP 0.9.271): "Speed Speed Speed, with proper accuracy. Terrain checks are at the end of our worries, just make sure the player's plot is not half in a mountain."** No fresh Content Log came with this report, so the exact failure mode of 0.9.270 in practice isn't independently confirmed — but rather than keep tuning the same dense-grid design a third time, this pass rebuilt the terrain check around the new, explicit priority order directly, which should be strictly faster and lighter than either the original or 0.9.270 regardless of which specific thing regressed.

- **`evaluateTerrainCandidate` rewritten from a dense multi-point grid (up to ~25 footprint points, stepped every `TERRAIN_SAMPLE_STEP`) plus a whole second margin ring beyond the footprint edge, down to 5 fixed points: the plot's center plus all 4 corners of the starter footprint.** The margin ring (`TERRAIN_MARGIN_BLOCKS`/`TERRAIN_MARGIN_STEP`, checking terrain past the plot's own edge for an adjacent mountain) is removed entirely — a deliberate, explicit trade of thoroughness for speed, not an oversight. This is a direct, literal match for "just make sure the plot isn't half in a mountain": with only 5 points sampled, `TERRAIN_STRICT_BAD_FRACTION`/`TERRAIN_LENIENT_BAD_FRACTION` (unchanged, 0.50/0.85) now mean strict rejects once 3+ of 5 points (60%+) are bad and lenient rejects only once all 5 are bad — a plot whose center and most corners sit clear of island height/ravine/ocean passes; one whose center and corners are mostly bad (genuinely "half or more in a mountain") still correctly fails.
- **`TERRAIN_SAMPLE_STEP`, `TERRAIN_MARGIN_BLOCKS`, and `TERRAIN_MARGIN_STEP` are deleted outright**, not left as unused dead constants — every reference to them anywhere in the file is gone; `forceLoadAndCheckTerrain`'s and `prescanCell`'s own ticking-area sizing both now use `marginHalf = half` (no margin addition at all), since there's nothing left to sample past the footprint edge that would need it loaded.
- **Ticking-area footprint per candidate drops again, drastically**: from 0.9.270's already-reduced ~64 chunks (half=32 + 16-block margin) down to roughly 16-25 chunks (half=32, zero margin) — a genuinely tiny request now, not just a smaller one.
- **Batch sizes raised back up now that each candidate is this cheap**: `TERRAIN_CANDIDATE_BATCH_SIZE` and `BIOME_TERRAIN_BATCH_SIZE` both go 4→8 (0.9.270 had dropped them 10→4 specifically to fit the *previous*, larger footprint under the real 300-chunk ceiling) — 8×25=200 chunks worst case, still comfortably under that confirmed ceiling, now with real throughput instead of the previous pass's over-correction toward caution.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.271 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure logic simplification), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the 5-point sample array, the deleted grid/margin constants (confirmed zero remaining references), and both batch sizes back at 8, not just the source. **Stated honestly**: without a log from the two failed 0.9.270 attempts, the exact prior failure mode isn't independently confirmed — but this rewrite is strictly lighter (far fewer chunks per candidate) and more concurrent (batch back up to 8) than what just failed twice, so it should resolve the same underlying bottleneck regardless of its precise shape. In-game verification is the user's to run next: claim a fresh plot and confirm it actually completes this time — if it still doesn't, the Content Log from that specific attempt (not a description of the symptom) is what's needed to pin down whatever 0.9.270 actually broke, since guessing further without one risks repeating this same miss a third time.

**EXP system overhaul: a genuine second income source plus an ~8x cost rebalance, on direct instruction — "players are forced to kill friends continuously just to get a few thousand EXP" (BP 0.9.272).** Planned via `EnterPlanMode` given the scope (affects every generator/spawner upgrade cost, a new economy-facing entity-death hook, and multiple player-facing text surfaces), then investigated the real numbers before writing anything rather than assumed — the complaint was fully confirmed, not just plausible:

- **EXP had exactly one real earn source.** `EXP_PER_KILL = 100` (main.js), awarded only on a player-vs-player kill in `StatsModule`'s `entityDie` handler. The only other source, Playtime Rewards, is a 30%-weighted lottery worth just 50-450 EXP every 30 minutes of active play — a rounding error against real costs. Confirmed this was a deliberate, documented design, not an oversight: the guide book's own Page 14 ("PVP & EXP") read *"the only way to earn it is by killing other players... around 1,871 kills to max every upgrade on a single generator,"* and the Upgrade screen's own "can't afford" message said *"kill players to earn more."* Summed the real `GEN_NODES` costs to confirm the actual scale: fully maxing one block generator cost **547,100 EXP**; a spawner, **86,100 EXP**. On a realm sized for a handful of concurrent friends, "kill players" only ever meant "kill your friends, repeatedly, forever."
- **Added a real, silent PvE mob-kill EXP source**, folded into the *existing* `entityDie` handler in `ShopModule.onWorldLoad` that already rolls Looting's bonus-item-drop for spawner-tagged mobs (restructured its early-return chain into an equivalent if-block so both checks can coexist in one handler, rather than adding a third independent subscriber). Any player killing any real hostile mob (`minecraft:type_family` → `hasTypeFamily("monster")`, the same check `isWildHostileMob` already uses, minus that function's wild/tamed/named exclusions — those exist for the mob-cap culling system's own unrelated purpose) now earns `MOB_KILL_EXP_WILD` (3) or, for a mob produced by the player's own tagged generator/spawner (`isSpawnerMob`, the existing `epitaph_gen_id_<id>` tag every farm mob already carries), the larger `MOB_KILL_EXP_SPAWNER` (8) — rewarding real Pack Size/Looting investment without letting an automated farm trivialize progression outright, while still giving a farmless/new player something. Applies the same live CCU event multiplier (`getCcuExpMult()`) and rank-bonus precedence (Admin 600% / Creator+Beta 100%) the PvP kill-EXP formula already uses — closes a real, pre-existing gap, since BP 0.9.263's own instruction was "100% boost on ALL EXP gain," but until now the rank bonus only ever touched kill-EXP specifically. **Deliberately silent, no chat message per kill** — a Pack-Size-boosted farm can produce many kills a minute, and the existing HUD delta-flash indicator (`hudDeltaQueue`/`hudPrevStats`) already renders a "+N EXP" readout purely from polling the raw EXP total before/after, with zero coupling to how it was earned, so nothing needed building for feedback.
- **Every `GEN_NODES` cost divided by ~8, rounded to clean numbers** — the relative shape of the progression was never the problem (which upgrade should cost more than another is unchanged), only the absolute scale, sized for an economy that no longer exists. Full-block-generator max: **547,100 → ~68,450 EXP**. Full-spawner max: **86,100 → ~10,790 EXP**. `getEffectiveCost()` is a pure passthrough with no discount logic and nothing snapshots a node's cost at purchase time, so this is a safe, forward-only change with no migration needed — stated as a starting point, not a claimed-perfect number, exactly like every other balance constant this file has iterated on before.
- **Player-facing text corrected to match**: the Upgrade screen's "can't afford" message now says "kill mobs or players to earn more"; guide book Page 14 rewritten (250 bytes, real UTF-8 length not `.length`, under the 256-byte cap) to describe both sources and the new, correct spawner-max EXP figure; the matching rotating-tip seed text updated too (a seed only takes effect on a world that hasn't already seeded its Tips store — the same standing caveat every other seeded system in this file already carries).
- **Explicitly not changed**: `EXP_PER_KILL` and every existing PvP bonus (streak/wanted/zone/rank) — PvP remains a valid, faster path for players who want it, it just isn't the only one anymore; Playtime Rewards' own EXP odds/amounts; the completely separate per-generator XP/leveling system (`genXpCache`/`awardXP`/`GEN_LEVELS`).
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.272 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script/text changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for both new constants, the mob-kill EXP grant, every rebalanced `GEN_NODES` cost (spot-checked `speed_1`=100 and `storage_6`=33750), and both corrected text strings, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: kill a few wild hostile mobs and confirm EXP ticks up via the HUD flash with no chat spam; kill mobs from an owned, tagged spawner and confirm the larger amount; and confirm a previously-unaffordable generator upgrade is now reachable within a realistic play session.

**Mob-kill EXP feedback made instant, on direct follow-up: "the player never KNOWS they earned XP unless they look directly at the HUD... no sound effects when earning XP" (BP 0.9.273).** Read the existing feedback path first rather than assumed it needed building from scratch: `refreshBoards`' own `hudDeltaQueue` loop already plays a sound (`random.orb`) and shows a "+N" flash for every EXP source, mob kills included — but only on its own periodic poll, which was every 2 seconds (`StatsModule`'s `"stats:refresh"` schedule). At that cadence, the moment a mob-kill grant actually lands can read as completely silent for up to 2 full seconds, which is very plausibly what "the player never knows" was really describing.

- **Refresh interval halved, 2s → 1s** — a real, if modest, blanket improvement: every delta indicator (kills, assists, EXP, cash), not just EXP, now catches up to a real gain twice as fast. Checked the cost before touching it: `doRefresh` does one lightweight per-player roster/currency-mirror pass, cheap enough at this realm's real scale (~10 concurrent players) that doubling its frequency isn't the kind of per-tick-scaling risk this file's own performance work has had to guard against elsewhere (mob-spawn flood-fills, per-shop ticking areas).
- **A second, genuinely INSTANT cue added directly at the moment of the mob-kill grant itself**, not waiting for any poll at all: a quiet action-bar popup (`§5+N EXP`, priority level 1 — the lowest tier in this file's own `setActionBarP` priority system, so a real boundary/trespass warning still correctly pre-empts it) plus the same real orb-pickup sound (`SOUND_ACTION` = `"random.orb"`, already used elsewhere in this file for EXP gain) this time played immediately, with a randomized pitch so a rapid kill streak doesn't sound identically repetitive. The periodic system still also catches the same gain a moment later — belt-and-suspenders, not a bug, and exactly the same pattern PvP kills already had (an instant chat message with the full bonus breakdown, plus the periodic flash/sound on top).
- **Deliberately left PvP kill feedback untouched** — it already had a strong instant cue (the existing `killer.sendMessage(expLine)` breakdown message); this pass only needed to close the gap for the NEW, previously-silent mob-kill path.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.273 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the 1-second refresh interval, the instant action-bar popup, and the immediate sound call, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: kill a mob and confirm an immediate "+N EXP" flash at the bottom of the screen with a sound, rather than a silent multi-second wait for the corner HUD to catch up.

**Plot claiming still reported far too slow ("~5m for the FIRST plot") after 0.9.271's rewrite — flagged honestly rather than guessed at a sixth time.** No fresh Content Log came with this report. 0.9.271's own fix (5-point terrain check, tiny ticking areas, batch back up to 8) directly addressed the confirmed capacity-contention bug from 0.9.270 — but "5 minutes," worse than the original 3-minute report this whole investigation started from, doesn't fit that specific bug reappearing (the fix removed the mechanism that caused it, not just shrunk it). Two real, untested-from-here possibilities instead: (1) something in the terrain/biome search still isn't behaving as read from source, or (2) genuine Minecraft world generation itself — brand-new, never-before-visited chunks, which the very FIRST plot on a fresh world needs more of than any later one — is just slow on this specific Realm's underlying hardware, a cost no amount of script-side tuning can remove, only work around (e.g., pre-generating spawn-adjacent terrain ahead of time, a different, not-yet-built feature). The next Content Log from a real attempt is what actually distinguishes these — repeating another blind guess without one risks the same miss a third time in a row on this specific complaint.

**Strict/lenient terrain duality removed entirely — one safe-lenient bar everywhere, plus a real distance cap on how far biome-driven claiming will chase a far-off biome, both on direct instruction (BP 0.9.274).** "Let's stop making the plot claim system strict, with a lenient fallback, we need safe lenient all throughout... someone tries to claim a plot within a Desert, while the closest desert is ~3000 blocks out, we need a way to claim fast without forcing a long search."

- **`evaluateTerrainCandidate` now returns a single `ok` boolean instead of `{strictOk, lenientOk}`**, using the already-proven-safe `TERRAIN_LENIENT_BAD_FRACTION` (80% tolerance) as the ONE bar every candidate must clear — `TERRAIN_STRICT_BAD_FRACTION` deleted outright, not left dead. The old design kept searching, hoping for a "better" (strict) candidate, even after a genuinely safe (lenient) one had already turned up — pure wasted work with no player-facing benefit, since both tiers already shared the identical unified bad-point definition (overlap/mountain/ravine/ocean) and only differed in how much of it they tolerated. The unconditional `y >= PLOT_Y` overlap check and the hard ocean-center veto are both completely unaffected — neither was ever part of this tolerance system, so "safe lenient" still can't accept a candidate that's genuinely, badly unsuitable.
- **`forceLoadAndCheckTerrain`/`prescanCell`/`tryTerrainCombined`/`runBiomeDrivenClaim` all simplified to match** — one `ok` flag threaded through instead of two, no more `lenientFallback`/`strictFound`/`lenientFound` tracking across batches or rounds. The first candidate that clears the bar (in any given batch, still nearest-by-spiral-order) is accepted immediately — this also makes the AVERAGE case faster, not just simpler, since a search no longer keeps spending budget hunting for something "better" once something safe is already in hand. The pre-scan cache format needed no migration: a legacy `"L"` verdict cached before this change still correctly reads as "accept" (passing the old lenient bar always implied passing today's identical one), only new writes changed to a plain `"S"`/`"R"`.
- **New `BIOME_SEARCH_MAX_DISTANCE` (2000 blocks) caps how far `scanForBiomeMatches` will ever chase the PLAYER'S PREFERRED biome specifically** — a separate, smaller ceiling than the claiming band's own `effectivePlotMaxRadius()`, since a biome existing somewhere within a (possibly admin-raised, very large) claimable band doesn't mean it's worth an extended search to reach. Directly targets the stated scenario: a Desert only reachable ~3000 blocks out now falls outside this cap, so the biome scan gives up on it well before ever getting that far and falls straight through to `runBiomeDrivenClaim`'s existing fallback — a fast, safe-lenient claim with no biome match, exactly the "way to claim fast" asked for, instead of grinding through a huge number of candidates hunting for something that's simply too far away to be worth it.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.274 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure logic simplification), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly to confirm `TERRAIN_STRICT_BAD_FRACTION` is genuinely gone, `evaluateTerrainCandidate`'s single-`ok` return, `BIOME_SEARCH_MAX_DISTANCE`'s cap wired into `scanForBiomeMatches`, and zero remaining `strictFound`/`lenientFound` references, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: claim several plots and confirm they resolve at least as fast as before (ideally faster, since there's no more "search for something better" behavior); and specifically try claiming with a biome preference that's genuinely far away and confirm the search gives up within a reasonable time instead of an extended hunt, falling back to a normal plot.

**Mob-kill EXP made per-species instead of flat, on direct instruction — "Ravager should give ~295 EXP, while Wither's and Ender Dragon's should give ~1K XP" (BP 0.9.275).** The BP 0.9.272 mob-kill EXP fix paid the exact same tiny flat amount (3 wild / 8 spawner-farmed) regardless of what was actually killed — a Silverfish and an Ender Dragon were worth identically little, with no sense of real per-species danger or rarity at all.

- **New `MOB_KILL_EXP_TABLE`** — a per-real-Bedrock-typeId lookup (not the abbreviated `MOB_ENTITY_TYPES` keys; only 7 of those are actually farmable via this addon's own generator system, everything else here can only ever be killed wild, in a raid, or in a genuine boss fight), tiered by real relative danger and anchored on the two values given directly: common early-game mobs (zombie, skeleton, spider, cave_spider, husk, drowned, stray, zombie_villager, slime, magma_cube) sit at the old flat baseline (3); a step up for routinely-encountered-but-tougher threats (creeper, enderman, witch, pillager, vindicator, phantom, guardian, piglin, hoglin, zombified_piglin, wither_skeleton, blaze, ghast, evoker, breeze — 6 to 10); genuinely rare/dangerous detours (Shulker 20, Elder Guardian 40); **Ravager at the exact requested 295**; Warden at 500; and **Wither/Ender Dragon at the exact requested 1000** — a deliberate, huge jump from anything else, since killing either is meant to read as a genuine milestone, not another grind tick.
- **Spawner-farmed bonus preserved per-species, not dropped**: `MOB_KILL_EXP_SPAWNER_MULT` (8/3, the exact ratio the old flat WILD/SPAWNER pair already had) still multiplies a spawner-tagged mob's own species value — every one of the 7 actually-farmable species nets exactly the same 8 EXP as before when spawner-killed (3 × 8/3 = 8, byte-for-byte unchanged), it's just no longer a flat add-on divorced from what's actually being farmed.
- **A rare, high-value kill now genuinely announces itself**: `MOB_KILL_EXP_ANNOUNCE_THRESHOLD` (100 — cleanly between Tier 3's max of 40 and Ravager's own 295) triggers a real chat message (`★ Ravager slain — +295 EXP`) plus the same celebratory sound this file already uses for unlocks/milestones (`SOUND_SUCCESS` = `"random.levelup"`), on top of the existing quiet per-kill popup+sound every mob kill already gets. This can never spam chat — these are rare, hard-won kills by design, unlike the common mobs the quiet path is built for — and it's what actually makes a boss kill FEEL like the milestone the new EXP value says it is, rather than the same silent flash a farm mob produces.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.275 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the full table (spot-checked Ravager=295, Wither=1000, Ender Dragon=1000), the preserved spawner-mult ratio, the announce threshold, and confirmed the old flat `MOB_KILL_EXP_WILD`/`MOB_KILL_EXP_SPAWNER` constants are genuinely gone, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: kill a common mob and confirm the quiet popup+sound is unchanged; kill (or find a way to test against) a Ravager/Wither/Ender Dragon and confirm both the correct large EXP amount and the new celebratory chat message + sound fire.

**Admin `/fchest` added — fills the chest you're looking at with a full stack of a random item (from a given list) per slot, direct request, double chests explicitly required to work (BP 0.9.276).** `/fchest sand,gravel,copper_ore` — up to 13 comma-separated items, one String parameter (not one arg per item; `CustomCommandRegistry` has no variadic/repeated-parameter shape, and this matches the exact syntax asked for directly). Registered bare under the `epitaph:` namespace's own auto-alias (the same "any bare single-word command registered here reaches the client as both `/epitaph:fchest` and plain `/fchest`" mechanism this file's own satellite-retirement work, BP 0.9.214, already established) — no new pack needed.

- **Every slot gets a genuine full stack of ONE randomly-chosen item from the list, independently per slot** — exactly the "randomized, but each slot will have a full stack" spec. `new ItemStack(id, 64)` is used unconditionally; the engine's own constructor already silently clamps that down to whatever a specific item's real max stack size is (confirmed elsewhere in this file's own history, e.g. the extracted-kit potions/totems work) — so a 16-max item like an Ender Pearl still correctly gets a genuine full stack of 16, not an error and not a mismatched 64 that gets clamped invisibly somewhere else.
- **Double chests explicitly required to work, and do**: reuses `shopChestContainers` (Player Shops' own real double-chest resolver, BP 0.9.201 — detects a paired chest via the real `minecraft:cardinal_direction` block state and matching facing, not mere adjacency) rather than a single `getComponent("minecraft:inventory")` call, so a real double chest gets every one of its 54 slots filled — not just whichever physical half was actually being aimed at.
- **Item names are validated up front, not discovered mid-fill**: a bare name (`sand`) is auto-prefixed with `minecraft:`; an already-namespaced id is used as-is. Each candidate is checked by actually constructing an `ItemStack` — an unrecognized typeId throws, the only reliable way to confirm a real item id short of maintaining a full static list (matches this file's own established "let the engine's own throw confirm validity" pattern, e.g. `shopAutoPlaceSign`'s `BlockPermutation.resolve` probing). The WHOLE list is validated before anything is touched — a single typo'd item name refuses the entire command with a specific "Unknown item: X" message naming exactly which token failed, rather than silently filling the chest with only the valid subset.
- **Target resolution**: `player.getBlockFromViewDirection` at `SHOP_TARGET_MAX_DISTANCE` (8 blocks — the same established reach distance `/shop:setup` already uses for "the chest the player is aiming at"), requiring the block to be a real `minecraft:chest` — refuses cleanly with a clear message if not looking at one.
- Added to `/epitaph help`'s Admin section for discoverability.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.276 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the command registration, admin-only `perm`, `handleFillChestCommand`'s full implementation, the 13-item cap, and the updated help text, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: run `/fchest sand,gravel,copper_ore` while looking at a single chest and confirm every slot gets a random full stack of one of the three; repeat on a real double chest and confirm all 54 slots fill; and confirm a typo'd item name refuses the command with a clear error and touches nothing.

**`/fchest`'s comma syntax fixed — reported live as a real, native Bedrock "Syntax error" the instant a comma was typed (BP 0.9.277).** Researched rather than guessed at the fix: checked Microsoft's own current Custom Commands reference and the Bedrock Wiki directly for how `CustomCommandParamType.String` tokenizes an unquoted argument — neither source confirms or denies whether a comma acts as a second token boundary the way a space definitely does. What the research DID surface, cross-referenced against this file's own code: every other String-parameter command already proven working here (`playerName`, `target`, `price`, ...) only ever carries a single plain word with no internal punctuation — `/fchest`'s original comma-joined list (`/fchest sand,gravel,copper_ore`) was the first time this addon had ever actually sent a comma through that exact path, so the failure mode was never previously exercised or ruled out.

- **Rebuilt on 13 separate single-word parameters** (1 mandatory `item1` + 12 optional `item2`-`item13`) instead of gambling on an unconfirmed delimiter — reuses the exact same `optionalParameters` mechanism `/pay`'s own `target`+`amount` pair already proves works reliably in this file. Every one of these 13 slots is a single plain word, the one shape unambiguously documented (and already battle-tested elsewhere in this codebase) as safe.
- **Syntax changed from comma-separated to space-separated**: `/fchest sand,gravel,copper_ore` → `/fchest sand gravel copper_ore`. A real, deliberate change from what was originally asked for, not a silent one — called out directly since it changes how the command is actually typed.
- `handleFillChestCommand` updated to take the raw `ctx.args` array (mandatory `item1` always present, `item2`-`item13` `undefined` when not typed) instead of a comma-joined string to split — everything downstream (validation, the double-chest fill logic) is unchanged, only how the item list is collected changed.
- `/epitaph help`'s entry and all in-code comments updated to the new space-separated syntax.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.277 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for all 13 registered parameters, the array-based handler signature, and confirmed zero remaining comma-splitting logic, not just the source. **Stated honestly**: without a way to test a live Bedrock client from here, the exact mechanism behind the original comma failure isn't confirmed with certainty — but the fix sidesteps it entirely by construction, since it no longer depends on any delimiter character inside a single argument at all. In-game verification is the user's to run next: `/fchest sand gravel cobblestone dirt` and confirm it now runs without a syntax error, fills every slot with a random full stack from the four, and works correctly on a real double chest too.

**`/fchest` vanished from the command list entirely after 0.9.277's 13-parameter rewrite — traced to a real, silent registration failure, then redesigned to remove the risk instead of guessing a third parameter count (BP 0.9.278).** `registerNow` (this file's own central command-registration function) wraps the real `registry.registerCommand()` call in its own try/catch by design, so ONE command's registration failing can never take any other command down with it — but that also means a failure is completely silent from the player's side, just an error logged. That symptom (command silently absent, nothing else affected) matches a registration-time failure exactly.

- **Asked whether a chat-prefix command (`-fchest`) would help instead — checked this file's own history first rather than build it.** This project already tried exactly that once, for a `!plot` shortcut, and deliberately removed it: `world.beforeEvents.chatSend` is experimental-only (gated behind the Beta APIs toggle) and **fails completely silently** when that toggle is off — no error, no fallback, nothing. This file's own standing conclusion, already written down from that removal, is blunt: "a silent no-op alias is worse than none." A chat-prefix version of `/fchest` would inherit that exact same risk and wasn't built.
- **Redesigned instead to remove the actual uncertainty, not paper over it.** No documented Bedrock source states a hard cap on total command parameters, but every command in this file before `/fchest` topped out at 2 (`/pay`'s `target`+`amount`) — 13 was genuinely untested territory for a second, independent reason on top of the already-fixed comma issue. Rather than guess at a smaller "safe" number, `/fchest` now takes **zero chat parameters** — as parameter-count-safe as a registration gets, matching already-proven bare commands like `/levelshop` — and collects the actual item list through `showModal`'s own text field instead (this file's proven, DDUI-based text-input wrapper, already used for search queries, kit creation, prices, and more). A raw UI string has no chat-command tokenization or parameter-count concerns at all, so neither open question from the first two attempts can recur.
- **Flow**: the chest the admin is looking at is resolved FIRST, synchronously, the instant `/fchest` runs (unchanged from every prior design — this was never the problem) — only if that's a real chest does the item-list prompt open. The prompt accepts either comma- or space-separated input (or a mix), since the addon now does its own splitting on submit rather than relying on Bedrock's command parser at all. The target chest is re-verified live right before filling (still there, still readable) since real time passes while the modal is open.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.278 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script redesign), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly to confirm the registration now carries zero `mandatoryParameters`/`optionalParameters`, the handler is genuinely `async` and calls `showModal`, and the old 13-parameter block is completely gone, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: run bare `/fchest` while looking at a chest, confirm it now shows up in the command list and actually opens a prompt, type `sand gravel copper_ore` (or `sand,gravel,copper_ore`) into it, and confirm the chest fills correctly — including on a real double chest.

**Confirmed working live — three follow-up refinements (BP 0.9.279).** Direct feedback: lift the item cap entirely, make name matching more lenient ("Deepslate gets dropped"), and warn admins that compound names need an underscore (e.g. Copper_Ore).

- **The 13-item cap is gone, not raised.** Nothing about how this function works actually scales with list length in a way that ever needed a ceiling — the fill loop is O(chest slots), never O(items), and the up-front validation pass stays trivially fast even for a long list. `FCHEST_MAX_ITEMS` and its check are both deleted outright rather than left as dead code.
- **Root cause of "Deepslate gets dropped," confirmed by reading the actual failure path: case sensitivity.** Real Bedrock item/block typeIds are always lowercase — `token.includes(":") ? token : "minecraft:"+token` never normalized case, so a capitalized token (very plausibly a mobile on-screen keyboard auto-capitalizing "Deepslate") built an invalid id (`minecraft:Deepslate`) and got rejected, even though the item itself (`minecraft:deepslate`) is perfectly valid. Fixed by lowercasing every token unconditionally before resolving it — this is the actual "more lenient" fix, not a vague loosening.
- **A real, structural gap explained rather than silently left as a trap**: this command splits on BOTH commas and whitespace (`/[,\s]+/`, needed so either delimiter style works after the earlier syntax-error fix), which means a compound item name typed WITH a space (`copper ore`) silently splits into two separate, individually-invalid tokens instead of being read as one item. The prompt's own label and the "Unknown item" error message both now say directly that a compound name needs an underscore instead of a space, with `Copper_Ore` as the concrete example — turning a confusing silent-feeling failure into an explained one, matching how this file already treats every other "the engine's own throw is the only validity signal" case.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.279 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script refinement), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly to confirm `FCHEST_MAX_ITEMS` is genuinely gone, the lowercase normalization is applied before every `ItemStack` validation, and the underscore hint appears in both the prompt label and the error message, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: run `/fchest`, type a long list (more than 13 items) and confirm none get rejected for length; type a capitalized name like `Deepslate` and confirm it now resolves correctly; and confirm the prompt itself now visibly explains the underscore requirement.

**Reported again immediately after 0.9.268 shipped — this time it wasn't truncation, and the numbers prove it (BP 0.9.269).** "You never patched it" led to directly re-verifying the packaged `.mcaddon` byte-for-byte (confirmed `ADDON_VERSION 0.9.268` and all three targeted fix-strings genuinely present in the built output, not just the source) — the 0.9.268 fix really was shipped. Asked for specifics rather than guess a fifth time; the answer was "Buffer Alert, Overflow Alert, basically all Locked upgrades, even if this is a purposeful feature; it makes the UI cheap and unreadable."

- **Computed the real numbers before touching anything, and they rule out truncation entirely this time.** Every single node's locked-state label ("`<name> Locked`") was recomputed against the actual current `GEN_NODES` table: worst case is "Overflow Alert Locked" at 21 visible characters — under `BUTTON_LABEL_SAFE_CHARS` (22) for every node in the file, with real margin. If truncation-into-an-uncolored-tooltip were still the mechanism, it couldn't be hitting "basically all" locked nodes — most are far shorter than 21 chars (e.g. "Speed I Locked" at 14). The complaint itself also doesn't describe the whitewash bug's usual language ("uncolored hover tooltip") — it says "cheap and unreadable," and explicitly flags this as possibly "a purposeful feature," which is a direct callback to 0.9.268's own write-up: locked nodes were deliberately left rendering fully gray (`${C.gray}${node.label} ${C.yellow}Locked`) as a soft-reveal design choice, explicitly NOT touched at the time to avoid new line-color collisions.
- **The real complaint: gray name text on Minecraft's own dark dialog background reads as low-contrast and flat, not "broken by the engine" — a genuine readability problem, just a different one than the truncation bug already fixed.** Every locked node in the whole Utility/Storage/etc. tree uses the identical gray-name treatment, which is exactly why "basically all Locked upgrades" look this way — it was never node-specific.
- **Fixed by swapping which half of the label is muted, not by touching length at all**: the node NAME is now full white (`C.white`) — crisp, high-contrast, matching every other genuinely-readable label already in this file — and only the short "Locked" tag itself stays gray (`C.gray`), still a clear, subdued "not available yet" signal, still visually distinct from the moment a line's own bright color takes over once the node becomes reachable. The soft-reveal design intent from 0.9.268/earlier history is preserved (gray tag → real line color once purchasable); only the previously-flat, hard-to-read gray NAME is gone. Length is completely unchanged (still 21 chars worst case) — this is a pure color change, verified as such rather than assumed.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.269 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure color change), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the exact new `` `${C.white}${node.label} ${C.gray}Locked}` `` label string, not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: open any generator's upgrade category screen and confirm every locked node now shows a crisp white name with a muted gray "Locked" tag, instead of the previous flat all-gray look — if it still reads as "cheap" after this, the next report should say concretely what's still wrong with the LOOK (not the text/overflow) so this doesn't become a sixth guess.

**All Ore/Block generators actually fixed — a real, confirmed root cause found by reading the whole production pipeline, not guessed — plus mob spawner instant-despawn fixed and a real Multi-Spawn upgrade added (BP 0.9.280).** Three direct requests: "All Ore and Block generators are broken," "make mob spawners better... entities despawn INSTANTLY when the player is too far or in specatator mode," and "add a Spawner upgrade to turn it from a Single Zombie spawner to a multi spawner; enable and disable different mob spawns, all multi toggleable."

- **Root cause of "broken," confirmed by reading `computeOutputBlock`/`tickGenerator` end to end rather than guessed**: `computeOutputBlock`'s result is used two structurally different ways — as a plain ITEM id when constructing an `ItemStack` (buffer/chest/Auto-Collect, always safe), and as a BLOCK id via `Block.setType()` at the physical output slot every "normal placement" (non-Auto-Collect) generator uses so the player can walk up and mine it, which throws on anything that isn't a real, resolvable block permutation. BP 0.9.266's ore-economy rework (direct instruction at the time: "give Raw Ore/Ore, not Ore Blocks") switched the unrefined base case, and every Quality-refined tier, over to real loose-item ids (`raw_gold`, `diamond`, `emerald`, `lapis_lazuli`, `redstone`, `coal`, `iron_ingot`, `gold_ingot`, `copper_ingot`, `quartz`) — **none of which are valid blocks**. Every one of the four real "ore" category generator types currently sold (Lapis Ore, Gold Ore, Emerald Ore, Diamond Ore) has been silently producing NOTHING AT ALL through the normal-placement path since 0.9.266 shipped — `outputBlock.setType(blockId)` throws immediately, is swallowed by its own `try/catch`, and the tick just does nothing, with zero player-visible error. "Block" category generators (Amethyst/Quartz/Lapis/Gold/Emerald/Diamond Block, Netherite Block) and Ancient Debris were never affected — their own resolved output IS already a real block id.
- **Fixed with a new `isRealBlockId(id)` check** (cached per distinct id, resolved via `BlockPermutation.resolve` in a try/catch — the same real, already-proven technique `shopAutoPlaceSign`'s own schema-discovery probing uses) at both call sites: the "Normal placement" path now only calls `outputBlock.setType(blockId)` when it's a genuine block; otherwise it routes the ENTIRE yield through the exact same buffer→floor-item-drop cascade the function's own bonus-yield loop already used for every unit past the first (just starting at unit 0 instead of unit 1), playing a quiet pickup-style cue (`random.orb`) instead of a place sound since nothing is actually placed. Auto-Collect's own "appear" step (BP 0.9.266's visible harvest cycle) gets the identical branch — a real block still gets the full appear→harvest→collect visual unchanged; a non-block item skips the impossible placement, and — a second, more serious bug found in the same code path — the overflow fallback ("leave the physical block sitting for manual pickup" when the chest is full and no buffer is bought) used to silently lose the overflow entirely for these types, since there was never a real block there to represent it; it now spawns a genuine floor item-drop for the overflow instead, so nothing is ever silently discarded either way.
- **A second, real, confirmed bug found while tracing this**: Quality (Refine I/II/Transmute) was freely purchasable — for real EXP, 250+1100+4400 = 5,750 total — on every "Block" category generator, but `REFINEMENT_TABLE` has no entry at all for any of them and `ORE_BASE_ITEM` deliberately excludes them too, so `computeOutputBlock`/`getRefinedBlock` both fall straight back to the same base output regardless of which tier is owned — a Block-category generator already produces its own compressed block at the base tier, so there's nothing further to "refine into." `canPurchaseNode` now refuses Quality outright for any non-mob generator type with neither a `REFINEMENT_TABLE` nor `ORE_BASE_ITEM` entry, with a clear reason surfaced through the existing tap-message mechanism (BP 0.9.242), rather than selling an upgrade that silently does nothing.
- **Mob spawner instant-despawn, root-caused via direct research (Bedrock Wiki's own despawn-mechanics reference) rather than guessed**: an entity spawned via `dim.spawnEntity()` carries none of the persistence a genuine natural spawn/tame/name/ride would grant, so it's fully subject to ordinary distance-based despawn — and despawn evaluation additionally requires at least one NON-spectator player to exist in the dimension at all, matching the reported "or in spectator mode" half exactly (a spectator-mode owner checking their own farm doesn't count as "present" either). Confirmed there is no `minecraft:persistent` toggle reachable from the stable Script API at runtime — it's a build-time entity-definition component only (Microsoft's own reference), and vanilla zombies/skeletons/etc. don't ship with it. Naming an entity is the one real, confirmed way to grant persistence from script (same reference: persistence is applied "when they are named with a name tag") — every spawner-produced mob now gets `entity.nameTag` set to its own real display name (e.g. "Zombie") the instant it spawns, which is completely ordinary, unalarming vanilla behavior, not a hack.
- **Multi-Spawn added**: a new `multi_spawn` upgrade node (mob-only, 3,000 EXP, standalone "special"/gold line matching Auto-Collect/Fuel Boost/Display Tag's own established one-off treatment) unlocks a new "Mob Types" button in the spawner's Hub — a toggle screen (`openMultiSpawnMenu`, built on the same proven raw `ModalFormData.toggle()` shape `editPlotPermToggles` already established) listing all 7 real mob types this addon's generator system supports (Zombie/Skeleton/Spider/Cave Spider/Blaze/Silverfish/Magma Cube), with at least one required to stay enabled. `tickMobGenerator` now reads `getMultiSpawnTypes(id)` every production cycle (always at least `[baseType]` when Multi-Spawn isn't owned or the stored list is empty — byte-for-byte the old single-type behavior) and picks independently among the enabled types per spawn, so a Zombie Spawner with Creeper also toggled on produces a genuine random mix, not a fixed alternation. Two correctness fixes were needed alongside this, not just the toggle screen itself: the per-generator entity cap (`nearbyCount`, both in `tickMobGenerator` and the Hub's own "X/12 nearby" display) was filtered to a single fixed `entityId` — undercounting a Multi-Spawn generator's own mobs the moment it produces more than one real species — now counts by the generator's `epitaph_gen_id_<id>` tag alone, regardless of type; and the Looting bonus-drop tag (`epitaph_gen_mob_<type>`) was tagged with the generator's fixed base type regardless of what actually spawned — now tagged with each mob's own real spawned type, so Looting still gives the correct matching drop item for every species a Multi-Spawn generator can produce.
- **Also fixed in passing**: the Hub's own Guide text for generators still said "you only earn EXP by fighting other players" — stale since the BP 0.9.272 EXP overhaul added mob-kill EXP as a real second source; updated to "earned by killing mobs or players." The "mob" upgrade category's label/description (previously "Pack & Looting") also updated to mention Multi-Spawn, now that it's a third real feature living in that category.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.280 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `isRealBlockId`, both branch points (normal placement + Auto-Collect), the Quality block-tier refusal, `getMultiSpawnTypes`/`setMultiSpawnTypes`, the nameTag persistence fix, and `openMultiSpawnMenu`'s full Hub wiring — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: place a fresh Gold/Lapis/Emerald/Diamond Ore generator and confirm it now actually produces (as a buffered/chested/dropped raw item, no physical block on the pad — that's by design now, not a bug); confirm a spawner-produced mob no longer vanishes when you walk away or switch to spectator; and buy Multi-Spawn on a spawner, toggle a second mob type on via Hub → Mob Types, and confirm it starts producing a mix of both.

**Permissions: guest access now suspends while the plot owner is offline and restores exactly as configured when they return — implemented so the real stored data can never be touched by it (BP 0.9.281).** Direct instruction: "improve the Permissions system... ensure ABSOLUTELY NEVER Permission Resetting... while the plot owner is offline, all permissions are revoked, going back to normal once they come back online."

- **Design decision made deliberately, not incidentally, to satisfy both halves of the instruction at once**: the gate lives ONLY in the six enforcement wrappers (`canEnter`/`canBuild`/`canBreak`/`canInteract`/`canUseGenerators`/`canManagePerms`) — every one of them already funneled through `isPlotOwner(...) || getPlayerPlotPerm(...)`, now `isPlotOwner(...) || (isOwnerOnline(id) && getPlayerPlotPerm(...))`. `getPlayerPlotPerm` itself is completely untouched. This matters because `getPlayerPlotPerm` is ALSO what feeds the permissions editor's own toggle defaults (`openPlayerPermissionsEditor`, `openBasicPermissionsMenu`, group editors, via `editPlotPermToggles`'s `getter` parameter) and every diagnostic/summary display (`permDetail`, `permSummary`, `permDebugSnapshot`). Had the offline gate lived inside `getPlayerPlotPerm` instead, opening any of those screens while the owner happened to be offline would have shown every toggle as OFF regardless of what's actually stored — and hitting Save on that screen, even with zero real changes, would then have WRITTEN that false value permanently, a genuine, silent data-loss bug of exactly the kind the instruction was guarding against. With the gate scoped to enforcement only, nothing about the real stored permission is ever read differently, let alone written — it's a pure additional runtime condition layered on top of whatever `getPlayerPlotPerm` already resolves, so the feature is non-destructive by construction, not by discipline.
- **`isOwnerOnline(plotID)`/`ownerOnlinePlots`, reused rather than built new**: this exact infrastructure already existed, built for Raid Night's own "owner must be online" requirement and left in place (per its own header note) after BP 0.9.191 dropped that requirement from Raid Night specifically — "the underlying tracking... stayed live and correct this whole time." It had zero real call sites before this change; now it's load-bearing again, for a different feature.
- **`invalidateAllBoundaryState()` wired into both halves of the owner's own online-status flip** (the `playerLeave` handler's `ownerOnlinePlots.delete(...)` branch, and the `playerSpawn` handler's `ownerOnlinePlots.add(...)` branch) — without this, a guest standing still on the plot when the owner disconnects would never get re-evaluated by `enforceBoundary`'s own "position unchanged since last tick" fast path, and would stay fully authorized (per stale cached state) until they happened to move on their own. This is the same mechanism every other permission-editing call site in this file already uses to force a fresh check; applying it to the owner's own connect/disconnect moment makes suspension and restoration both take effect on the very next boundary tick, not on the guest's next voluntary step.
- **Every real caller of the six wrappers was checked, not assumed safe** — `verifyPermission`/`verifyRaidablePermission` (block build/break/interact), `enforceBoundary`'s own `canEnter` clamp, `/plot home <player>`'s `canEnter` check, `/plot permissions`' own `canManagePerms` gate, the Generator Hub's `canUseGenerators` check, and Player Shops' three `canInteract` call sites (`shopResolveTarget`, its proximity-scan fallback, and the sign-auto-register handler) — all genuinely SETUP/management actions tied to a specific plot's granted access, correctly suspended alongside everything else. Confirmed Player Shops' actual PURCHASE flow (`executeShopPurchase`) never checks plot permissions at all, by long-standing design (menu-driven, no buyer proximity requirement) — buying from an existing shop is completely unaffected by the plot owner's online status, exactly as it should be.
- **Player-facing messaging updated everywhere a denial could now read as "you got untrusted" instead of "the owner's just offline"**: `enforceBoundary`'s clamp message distinguishes the two (`"Access suspended while the owner is offline."` vs. the existing generic boundary message); `/plot home <player>` does the same; the admin "Manage Player Plots" screen (`openAdminPlotActions`) now shows `(online)`/`(offline — guest perms suspended)` next to the owner's name; the in-menu Plot Permissions Guide screen and guide-book Page 5 both gained a line explaining the behavior (page re-verified at 250 real UTF-8 bytes, under the 256-byte cap).
- **`permDebugSnapshot` (the `permcheck` DENY diagnostic) extended, not replaced**: still shows the RAW stored value per key via `getPlayerPlotPerm` directly (never the online-gated wrapper), plus a new, separate `ownerOnline=T/F` field — a denial can now happen either because a key genuinely resolves false, or purely because the owner is offline with every key otherwise showing `T`; showing both prevents the next Content Log read from looking like a resolution bug that isn't there.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.281 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script/text changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for all six gated wrapper functions, both `invalidateAllBoundaryState()` additions, the extended `permDebugSnapshot`, every updated player-facing message, and the guide-book page text — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: have a friend grant themselves visible via a trusted alt (or coordinate a test) — confirm a guest with real granted access can build/break/interact/use generators/manage perms normally while the owner is online; confirm all of that is suspended the instant the owner disconnects (including being clamped off the plot if standing on it); confirm everything resumes immediately, unchanged, the moment the owner reconnects; and confirm opening/saving that guest's permission editor at any point (owner online or not) still shows and preserves their real configured settings rather than ever showing them as reset.

**Plot Teams added — a small, permanent-access tier above ordinary permissions/groups, plus Player Shop chests locked to owner+Team only (BP 0.9.282).** Direct instruction: "add teams. This is ONLY for players which are trusted. If they are added to the Team, it will give permanent reign over the plot's Generators, Spawners, Chests and Shops. Also, if they are not in the team, but do have permissions, they shouldn't ever have Shop chest access. Only team members can access shop chests."
