- **"Permanent" is the load-bearing word, read literally against the very system built one version earlier**: BP 0.9.281 made every ordinary permission (Basic, per-player override, group) suspend automatically the instant the plot owner goes offline. Team is the deliberate exception — membership grants an unconditional bypass of BOTH the offline-suspension gate AND whatever the entry/interact/generators toggles happen to say, for exactly three of the six permission keys: `entry` (a Team member has to be able to physically reach the generators/chests to use them), `interact` (chests), and `generators` (block/mob generators, i.e. spawners — this addon has never had a separate permission key for "spawners," they're just generators with `gen_is_mob_<id>` set, so `generators` already covers both). Deliberately NOT extended to `build`/`break`/`trustmgmt` — the instruction named Generators/Spawners/Chests/Shops specifically, not general building or permission management, so Team stays a narrow, purpose-built escalation rather than a second ownership tier.
- **Shop-chest exclusivity implemented as a genuinely separate, stricter gate, not a stretched version of `canInteract`**: a new `canAccessShopChest(player, id)` (`isPlotOwner || isTeamMember`, nothing else — no Basic, no per-player override, no group, regardless of what any of those say) is now what governs setting up, restocking, or physically opening a registered Player Shop's own chest. The general container-interact handler (`world.beforeEvents.playerInteractWithBlock`, the same one BP 0.9.225 already fixed to scope "interact" to real containers only) now routes through a new `verifyContainerPermission(player, loc, dim)`: it checks `shopIdAtChest` first — if the block IS a registered shop's own chest, `canAccessShopChest` decides, full stop; every other container (an ordinary chest, barrel, shulker box, the mail vault, etc.) falls through to the exact same `verifyRaidablePermission(..., "interact")` path as before, completely unchanged. The three shop SETUP call sites that used to gate on plain `canInteract` (`shopResolveTarget`'s direct-aim path, its proximity-scan fallback, and the sign-auto-register handler) were all switched to `canAccessShopChest` too — a player with full Basic "Containers & Interact" granted, even while the owner is online, can no longer set up, restock, or open a shop chest unless they're the owner or on the Team. Confirmed, not assumed, that this doesn't touch buying: Player Shops' actual purchase flow (`executeShopPurchase`) has never checked plot permissions at all (menu-driven, no proximity requirement) — completely unaffected either way.
- **"ONLY for players which are trusted" enforced structurally, at the picker level, not just by a runtime check that could be bypassed by calling the function directly**: `isPlotPlayerTrusted(id, uuid)` (`hasPlayerPlotPermOverrides || getPlayerGroup !== null`) defines eligibility — a player needs a real, pre-existing personal override OR group membership before they can ever be promoted. `getTrustedPlayerCandidates(id)` (the Team "Add Member" picker's own candidate pool) unions the permission roster with every group's membership list, owner excluded, de-duplicated — deliberately NOT `allKnownPlayersList` (every known player, trusted or not), which is what Groups/Players search from. The same `isPlotPlayerTrusted` check is re-validated a second time at the point of adding (both the dedicated picker and the quick-toggle button on a player's own permissions screen), so a direct call can never add someone who doesn't qualify either.
- **Storage mirrors `plot_player_group_<id>_<uuid>`'s own flat, enumerable-by-prefix style exactly** (`plot_team_<id>_<uuid> = true`) — `getTeamMembers` derives the member list live via `World.getDynamicPropertyIds()`, the same technique `getGroupMembers`/`getAllKnownPlayers` already established, so there's exactly one place membership can ever be recorded, never a separate roster array that could drift from it. `addToTeam`/`removeFromTeam` both call `invalidateAllBoundaryState()`, the same mechanism every other permission-editing call site in this file already uses, so a Team add/remove takes effect on the very next boundary tick rather than waiting on the affected player's own next move.
- **UI**: a new "Team" entry on the main Plot Permissions screen (`openPlotPermissionsMenu`, alongside Basic/Groups/Players/Guide) opens `openPlotTeamMenu` — a member list (tap to remove, with a confirm dialog explaining exactly what's lost) plus an "Add Member" button routing to `openPlotTeamAddMember` (the trusted-only picker) and its own Guide screen. A specific player's own permissions screen (`openPlayerPermissionsMenu`) also gained a quick "+ Add to Team" / "★ Remove from Team" toggle button, grayed out with a clear refusal message if they don't yet qualify. `/plot info` and the admin "Manage Player Plots" screen (`openAdminPlotActions`) both gained a Team line for visibility, matching how both already show Groups.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.282 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script/text changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `isTeamMember`/`addToTeam`/`getTeamMembers`/`isPlotPlayerTrusted`/`getTrustedPlayerCandidates`, the Team bypass in `canEnter`/`canInteract`/`canUseGenerators` (and its absence from `canBuild`), `canAccessShopChest`/`verifyContainerPermission` and all four of their call sites, and the full `openPlotTeamMenu`/`openPlotTeamAddMember` UI wiring — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: grant a friend a personal permission override or group membership, add them to the Team, confirm they can use generators/chests even after the owner disconnects; confirm a DIFFERENT friend with full "Containers & Interact" granted (but not on the Team) can still open ordinary chests but is refused at a registered shop's own chest, both for setup and for opening an existing one; and confirm a player with no override or group at all can't be added to the Team until they get one.

**Two real, confirmed generator bugs fixed: leftover idle-placeholder bedrock on pickup, and the "ore" category's own missing mining visual since BP 0.9.280 (BP 0.9.283).** Direct instruction: "delete all Bedrock when a generator spawns it, currently when I pickup a gen, it leaves the bedrock behind indefinitely. Also, the Visual Breaking of the Ore never happens, it literally visually pulls the ores out of thin air."

- **Bedrock left behind forever, confirmed by reading `removeGenerator`/`cleanupOrphanedGenerator` directly**: the ambient idle-placeholder loop (BP 0.9.125) sets `minecraft:bedrock` at a generator's OUTPUT slot (y+1) whenever it's empty between production cycles — a real, working design, but neither `removeGenerator` (the in-Hub "Remove Generator?" flow, the actual "pickup a gen" path) nor `cleanupOrphanedGenerator` (non-standard destruction — commands/WorldEdit/admin edits) ever cleared THAT block; both only ever touched the generator's own machine block one level below. Once the generator was gone, nothing was left tracking that bedrock at all, so it sat there permanently. Fixed by clearing it in both functions — but only when it's genuinely still the bedrock placeholder (never touches a real, uncollected product a player might still want to grab first). Confirmed no race with the ambient loop re-placing it: both cleanup functions already wipe `gen_active_<id>` in the same synchronous call (JS's single-threaded execution guarantees nothing else, including that loop's own `system.runInterval`, can interleave mid-function), and the loop's own very first check (`if (!gw(\`gen_active_${id}\`)) continue;`) already skips a generator the instant that flag is gone.
- **The real root cause of "pulls ores out of thin air," and why it's a direct, honest consequence of BP 0.9.280's own fix, not a new regression**: that pass correctly stopped `outputBlock.setType(blockId)` from throwing on a loose item id with no block form (every "ore" category generator's raw/refined output since 0.9.266's rework) — but the replacement routed production straight into an instant, invisible buffer/floor-drop credit, with nothing physical ever appearing at all. Fixed by restoring a real, visible appear→harvest→collect cycle — the same one Auto-Collect's own branch already uses — for both the normal-placement AND Auto-Collect paths: the generator's own real, UNREFINED ore block (`minecraft:${type}` — e.g. `minecraft:gold_ore` — always a genuine placeable/minable vanilla block at every Quality tier, since real ore blocks exist regardless of what refined item a mined one would represent) appears on the pad with a place sound, sits for the same short `GEN_AUTO_COLLECT_HARVEST_DELAY_TICKS` window Auto-Collect already uses, then gets a real destroy sound+particle before the CORRECT item (raw ore, ingot, or Transmute's rare item — whatever Quality actually resolved) is credited via the exact buffer→floor-drop cascade 0.9.280 already built. This deliberately decouples "what's shown breaking" (always the base ore block, since that's the one thing guaranteed to have a real block form regardless of Quality tier) from "what's actually given" (the real resolved item) — the same trick Auto-Collect's own redesign already relies on, now applied consistently everywhere instead of only for generators whose own output happens to already be a real block.
- **Stated honestly, not hidden**: a player who manually mines the visible ore block themselves inside that short (~0.25s) window gets whatever vanilla's own break-drop naturally gives — correct for the unrefined base tier, technically short of a Quality-refined tier's real value. This is the same small, already-accepted timing risk Auto-Collect's identical design has carried since 0.9.266; not a new gap introduced by this fix. A generator type with no real block form even for its own base ore (none currently sold have this problem — every "ore" category `SHOP_TIERS` entry's own type is a genuine vanilla ore block) still falls back to the original thin-air-but-honest cascade rather than silently doing nothing.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.283 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the bedrock-clearing fix in both `removeGenerator` and `cleanupOrphanedGenerator`, the new `oreBlockId` display-block logic and its appear/harvest steps in the normal-placement path, and the matching `displayBlockId` fallback in the Auto-Collect path — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: pick up a Gold/Lapis/Emerald/Diamond Ore generator (or spawner) and confirm no bedrock is left floating where the output slot used to be; and watch a non-Auto-Collect ore generator produce and confirm a real ore block now visibly appears and gets "mined" (place sound → short wait → break sound/particle) before the correct item shows up in your inventory/buffer, instead of the item just silently appearing.

**Two real, confirmed unsafe-teleport bugs found and fixed — both trespass-adjacent code paths that could genuinely kill a player, not just clamp them oddly (BP 0.9.284).** Direct instruction: "ensure players never get teleported anywhere apart from their plot when trespassed, as right now, some people get teleported somewhere and die."

- **`teleportToPlot` (the "has a plot" branch both trespass paths already used) was confirmed already safe, not touched** — it routes through `suspendPlayer`/`releaseSuspendedPlayer`, which levitates the player, waits for the real chunk to load, and scans downward for genuine solid, non-air/water/lava ground before ever landing them (the same vetted path `/plot home` itself uses). The bug was never in this branch.
- **Two separate PLOTLESS fallbacks, both real, both fixed**: `handleTrespass`'s own 3-strikes "kicked for repeat trespassing" branch did a raw `player.teleport({x: spawnOriginX+0.5, y: PLOT_Y+5, z: spawnOriginZ+0.5})` with NO ground check at all and no dimension pinned. Separately, and likely the MORE frequently hit of the two (it fires on every boundary tick for a plotless player standing on someone else's claimed plot, not gated behind 3 strikes at all), `enforceBoundary`'s own general clamp had the identical pattern at `PLOT_Y+1`. Real generated terrain at `spawnOriginX/Z` has no guarantee of being open air at either assumed height — it could be well underground (instant suffocation) or hanging over open water/a ravine/the void (a long, damaging fall), entirely dependent on this world's actual terrain shape at that exact spot. Both fully explain "teleported somewhere and die."
- **Fixed by extracting a single shared `teleportToSafeWorldSpawn(player, dim)` helper** — factored out of `releaseSuspendedPlayer`'s own already-correct last-resort branch (a real, sanity-checked `world.getDefaultSpawnLocation()` attempt first — same guard against the confirmed BP 0.9.251 garbage-coordinate incident — falling back to a genuine `findSafeLandingY` scan at `spawnOriginX/Z`, never a blind assumed-safe height) rather than leaving three separate copies of similar-but-not-identical logic to drift apart. Both plotless fallbacks (`handleTrespass` and `enforceBoundary`) now call this one function — a trespasser can now only ever land on their own plot (the common case, already safe) or a confirmed-safe spot near world spawn (the rare plotless case), never blind air over unknown terrain.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.284 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `teleportToSafeWorldSpawn`'s definition and both of its new call sites, and confirmed the two old raw, unchecked teleport strings are genuinely gone from the built output — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next (hardest to trigger deliberately without a plotless test account): have a genuinely plotless player trespass on a claimed plot (both by lingering for 3 warnings, and by simply standing on it without permission) and confirm they land safely near spawn both times, on solid ground, never mid-air or underground.

**Combat-log warning/ban system added, built on top of the existing permanent strike counter rather than a new parallel one (BP 0.9.285).** Direct instruction: "add a warning system; each warn gives a permanent PvP debuff. You get warnings by Combat-Logging. 5 strikes and it bans them, permanently and continuously kicking them from the realm every time they join."

- **Reused, not duplicated**: `combatlog_count_<uuid>` already existed (BP 0.9.167) — a real, permanent, world-dynamic-property counter incremented once every time a player disconnects while PvP-tagged. This IS the warning count the instruction describes, so every new mechanic below reads directly off it (`getCombatLogStrikes`/`isCombatLogBanned`) rather than inventing a second, competing counter.
- **Permanent PvP debuff, scoped strictly to real player-vs-player damage**: `combatLogPvpDamageMult(uuid)` reduces damage DEALT by 15% per strike (capped at 4 strikes short of the ban threshold — a maxed-out 60% reduction), injected directly into `resolveJavaCombatHit` — the one place this addon's own Java Combat module actually computes melee/ranged damage — gated on `victim.typeId === "minecraft:player"` specifically, so a combat-logger's ability to farm mobs/generators is completely untouched; only their PvP effectiveness suffers, matching "PvP debuff" literally rather than a blanket Weakness effect that would nerf PvE too.
- **The 5th strike bans, and "permanently" is enforced by construction, not by a flag an admin could accidentally undo**: the moment `combatlog_count` crosses `PVP_COMBATLOG_BAN_STRIKES` (5), the existing Soft-Ban flag is set for panel visibility, but actual enforcement (`isCombatLogBanned`) reads the live strike count directly — there is no separate on/off toggle anywhere that lifts a combat-log ban, only the count itself, which this file only ever increments. The Soft-Ban disorientation loop (Blindness/Slowness/Weakness/Nausea, reapplied every 5s — already proven working for manual admin Soft-Bans) now also covers `isCombatLogBanned` unconditionally, independent of whatever the shared flag currently says.
- **"Continuously kicking them... every time they join"**: a new `playerSpawn` subscriber (gated on `initialSpawn` — a real new connection, not a mid-session respawn) fires on every single future join for a banned player, messages them plainly, and attempts a real `player.runCommand("kick @s ...")`. **Stated honestly, not oversold**: this addon's own long-standing, repeatedly-documented conclusion (see Soft-Ban's own Guide text) is that there is no true kick/ban API in stable `@minecraft/server` — the one that does exist (`@minecraft/server-admin`) is Bedrock-Dedicated-Server-only and unavailable on a Realm. A script-issued `/kick` (run as the server, never typed by the player) is the one remaining real removal path actually reachable from here, researched directly rather than assumed working — but whether it actually succeeds on a Realm specifically was NOT independently confirmed (no live client reachable from this environment to test against). The disorientation loop is the layer that's unconditionally guaranteed to take effect regardless of whether the kick command itself works, so a banned player is never fully "un-punished" even in the worst case where `/kick` silently fails.
- **Admin visibility**: the Ranks & Moderation panel's per-player row now shows a distinct `[Combat-Ban: N]` tag (not the generic `[Soft-Banned]` label) whenever `isCombatLogBanned` is true, so an admin can immediately tell a combat-log auto-ban apart from a manual, freely-toggleable Soft-Ban; Staff Tools' "Player Info" screen gained a "Combat-log strikes" line with a `(BANNED)` marker at threshold; the Mute/Soft-Ban Guide screen gained a full explanation of the new system, including the exact debuff percentage and strike cap, computed from the real constants rather than hardcoded into the string.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.285 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `getCombatLogStrikes`/`isCombatLogBanned`/`combatLogPvpDamageMult`, the damage-multiplier injection in `resolveJavaCombatHit`, the auto-ban trigger at strike 5, the join-time kick-attempt subscriber, the extended Soft-Ban enforcement loop, and every admin-facing display update — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: combat-log a few times (or manually bump `combatlog_count_<uuid>` for testing) and confirm PvP damage dealt visibly drops each strike; confirm the 5th strike triggers the ban message and shows `[Combat-Ban: 5]` in the Ranks panel; and confirm a banned player gets the ban message plus a kick attempt on every subsequent join (and, if the kick itself doesn't take effect, that the disorientation debuffs are still clearly active).

**"Edit Held Item" admin menu added, plus real bugs found and fixed in `/giveadv` while reviewing it for the same feature (BP 0.9.286).** Direct instruction: "add a menu to the admin menu to edit any item I am holding — for example, if I have a shulker box with a lot of good stuff in it, I can edit it to give it Keep On Death. Only when I am holding it. Also, improve and patch the /giveadv command."

- **Why this needed to be a genuinely new tool, not just another `/giveadv` option**: `/giveadv` (and `buildGiveItem` underneath it) only ever CONSTRUCTS a brand-new `ItemStack` from a typeId — it has no way to touch an item that already exists, which is exactly the shulker-box case in the request: there's no way to hand `/giveadv` "the exact items already inside this specific box," since it would have to be told to build a fresh shulker box and somehow describe its entire contents from scratch. `openEditHeldItemMenu` (new, reachable from Control Panel → Give → "Edit Held Item") instead reads `player.selectedSlotIndex` directly out of the admin's own inventory container and edits that EXACT stack in place — a shulker's real contents, an item's real enchantments, its stack size, all survive completely untouched; only the specific property picked in the menu changes. Offers Keep On Death (the literal example given), Rename, and Edit Lore, plus its own in-menu Guide explaining the distinction from `/giveadv`.
- **Handled the one real Bedrock gotcha this depends on**: `Container.getItem()` returns a detached snapshot, not a live reference — mutating the returned object does nothing on its own. Every edit here follows the established "read, mutate the local copy, write back via `container.setItem(slot, ...)`" idiom this file already uses for shop chest restocking, and re-reads the live slot immediately before acting (a dialog awaits real player input, so the held item could genuinely have changed by the time the admin makes a choice). Keep On Death specifically gets a real read-back verification after the write — the same lesson this file's own `buildBall`/`buildPearl` history already learned for this exact property (a write can silently not take with no exception at all) — rather than trusting the assignment blindly.
- **Three real, confirmed bugs found in `/giveadv` while reviewing its own item-editing code as the natural point of comparison, not guessed at**:
  1. `buildGiveItem` already computed `nameApplied`/`keepOnDeathApplied` — genuine read-back checks, exactly the same discipline just reused above — but `executeGiveAdv` only ever destructured `stack`/`warnings` from its return value, silently discarding both flags. An admin requesting Keep On Death (or a custom name) that failed to actually apply got zero warning about it, ever, since 0.9.something whenever this was originally written. Wired up both flags to push a real warning when the write didn't take.
  2. Enchantments only ever warned if `comp.addEnchantment(...)` threw an exception — never checked whether the enchantment actually landed at the requested level otherwise, the same class of silent-failure risk as Keep On Death. Added a genuine read-back (`comp.getEnchantment(id)`, confirmed via Microsoft's current reference to accept a plain string id directly, no need to reconstruct an `EnchantmentType` for the read side) that warns with the actual resulting level whenever it doesn't match what was requested.
  3. The final summary message (`"Gave Nx <item> to N player(s)"`) was unconditionally green, even when every single target failed (e.g. every attempt threw on a bad item id) and `given` was 0 — reading as a cheerful success report for a total failure. Now red and worded as a failure whenever nothing was actually given.
- **One more real gap fixed**: the admin-menu text-entry path (`giveAdvMenuFlow`, used when giving via the UI rather than the `/epitaph:giveadv` command's own client-validated `ItemType` parameter) never validated the typed item id at all — a typo fell all the way through to `buildGiveItem`'s `ItemStack` constructor, throwing per target and surfacing as a raw, unfriendly JS error string. Now validated up front (the same validate-by-construction technique already proven for `/fchest`, including that command's own lowercase-before-resolving lenience fix, since real Bedrock typeIds are always lowercase) with a clear "Unknown item" message naming exactly what was tried.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.286 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `openEditHeldItemMenu`'s full implementation and its new Give-menu button, the keepOnDeath read-back check, both previously-dead `nameApplied`/`keepOnDeathApplied` flags now wired into real warnings, the enchantment read-back check, the fixed all-red-on-total-failure summary line, and the new up-front item-id validation in `giveAdvMenuFlow` — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: hold a shulker box with real contents, open Control Panel → Give → Edit Held Item, toggle Keep On Death, and confirm the box (with its contents intact) now actually survives death; try `/epitaph:giveadv` with a deliberately bad item id via both the command and the admin-menu text-entry path and confirm each now gives a clean "Unknown item" message instead of a raw error or a falsely-green success line.

**Real, confirmed root cause found for "Sweeping Edge does zero damage" (BP 0.9.287).** Traced through the actual damage path rather than guessed: `resolveJavaCombatHit`'s sweep block calls `nearby.applyDamage(sweepDamage, { cause: EntityDamageCause.entityAttack, damagingEntity: attacker })` for every entity caught in the splash — but that call itself fires a genuine `entityHurt` event, and `initJavaCombat`'s own `world.beforeEvents.entityHurt` listener (registered by this exact module) matches its own trigger condition against it (cause `entityAttack`, attacker is a player) with nothing to tell it "this is our own already-computed damage, leave it alone." The main hit's own final `applyDamage` call, a few lines further down the same function, is protected against exactly this by briefly tagging the attacker with `JAVA_COMBAT_REENTRY_TAG` first (the before-event handler steps aside when it sees that tag) — but the sweep block's own `applyDamage` call, added earlier in this file's history, was never given the same protection.

- **What that gap actually did**: every sweep hit got silently cancelled and rerouted into a brand-new, independent `resolveJavaCombatHit(attacker, nearby)` call, recomputing the "hit" from scratch — including the anti-reach distance check (`JAVA_COMBAT_ANTI_REACH_DISTANCE_SQ`, a short melee-range threshold). A sweep-caught entity is, by design, routinely farther from the attacker than that threshold allows (that's the entire point of a sweep — hitting things beyond direct melee range), so the recomputed hit almost always failed the reach check and dealt nothing at all. The real, correctly-computed `sweepDamage` (a random 5–7.5, per Blurry's own ported flat numbers) never actually survived to land on anyone — it was always being thrown away and replaced with a doomed, out-of-range re-hit.
- **Fixed by giving the sweep's own `applyDamage` call the exact same `JAVA_COMBAT_REENTRY_TAG` guard the main hit already uses** — tag added immediately before, removed in a `finally` block immediately after, per swept entity. The before-event handler now correctly recognizes every sweep hit as this module's own already-resolved damage and lets it through unmolested, instead of re-litigating it as a fresh, out-of-range attack.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.287 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 131 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script fix), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the reentry-tag guard now wrapping the sweep's own `applyDamage` call — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: land a slow, deliberate, fully-charged sword swing against two or more entities standing close together and confirm the non-primary target(s) now actually take real damage (roughly 5–7.5) instead of the sweep particle/sound playing with zero effect.

**A new, extremely rare item was quietly added around this point (BP/RP 0.9.288–0.9.291).** What it is and what it does are intentionally left undocumented here — it is meant to be found and discovered in-game, not read about. It can occasionally be earned as a reward for very difficult kills.

**Generator output made hopper-compatible — hoppers and hopper minecarts can now pull directly from a generator, before Buffer even gets a look, on direct request: "make it possible to have a hopper pull items from generators, even when it has Buffer upgrades. Only using buffer if it cannot output the items into the hopper... works for all means of hopper, hopper minecarts and etc." (BP 0.9.292).**

- **Why this can't work as real vanilla hopper suction.** A real hopper only ever pulls from a genuine container block (or catches free-floating item entities) directly above itself — a generator's output pad holds a plain block (no inventory) or nothing between cycles, so there's nothing for vanilla's own suction physics to actually grab. Implemented instead as a direct script-side push, checked at the exact same "directly under the generator" position (`y-1`) Auto-Collect's own paid chest lookup already uses — the natural, already-established build spot for this addon's own generators, just extended to recognize a hopper as a second valid thing to find there, alongside (not instead of) a chest.
- **`findGeneratorHopperTarget(dim, x, y, z)`** (new, right before `tickGenerator`) checks that position for a real `minecraft:hopper` block first, then — since a hopper minecart needs no placed hopper block at all, just to be sitting on a short rail directly beneath the generator — for a `minecraft:hopper_minecart` entity at the same spot (confirmed against this file's own already-established `hopper_minecart` identifier fragment, reused from the existing minecart item-texture table, rather than guessed fresh). Returns the live `Container` from whichever is found, or `null`.
- **Deliberately NOT gated behind the `auto_collect` node**, unlike the paid chest-routing path it sits right next to — a hopper (the item itself, correct placement, optionally a minecart+rail) is a real, separate, effort-requiring vanilla build, not a shortcut around Auto-Collect's own EXP cost. It's checked in `tickGenerator` immediately after the output item is resolved (`blockId = computeOutputBlock(id)`), **before** the Transmute roll, the "output slot occupied" buffer check, the Auto-Collect chest branch, and Normal Placement — genuinely first priority, which is what makes "even when it has Buffer upgrades" and "only using buffer if it cannot output into the hopper" literally true: `yieldCount` is reduced by however much the hopper actually absorbed (stopping the instant it reports full) before any of the existing downstream logic ever runs, so Buffer — and everything after it — only ever sees whatever's left over. A generator with no hopper present is completely unaffected: `hopperTarget` is `null` and every check around it short-circuits, so this is purely additive over the existing, already-hardened production pipeline (BP 0.9.280/0.9.283's own recent fixes included).
- **Full absorption returns early** (mirroring the Auto-Collect chest branch's own established shape) — nothing left to buffer, chest-route, or physically place, so the pad is never touched and no wasted appear/harvest visual cycle plays for zero net production. A partial absorption (hopper filled up mid-push) leaves the genuine remainder in `yieldCount` and falls straight through into the existing cascade completely unchanged, operating on just what's left.
- **Transmute's own rare-item roll (2% chance, `quality_3`) gets the identical priority** — it's generator output too, so it now tries the same `hopperTarget` first, ahead of its existing chest→buffer→floor-drop cascade, all of which are otherwise untouched.
- **Quiet feedback only, not a full visual cycle**: a soft pickup-style chime (`random.orb`) plays once per tick that the hopper actually absorbs something — no block ever appears on the pad for hopper-routed output, so there's nothing to "harvest," unlike the Auto-Collect/Normal-Placement paths' own appear→harvest→collect cycles.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to match manifest. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.292 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 132 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script addition), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `findGeneratorHopperTarget`'s full implementation, its wiring into both `tickGenerator`'s main yield path and the Transmute cascade, and the real `minecraft:hopper`/`minecraft:hopper_minecart` identifiers — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: dig one block under a generator and place a hopper there, confirm production starts flowing directly into it (with the quiet pickup chime) instead of appearing on the pad; swap the hopper for a hopper minecart on a short rail in the same spot and confirm the same; fill the hopper completely and confirm any further production correctly falls back to Buffer (if purchased) or the normal pad-placement behavior; and confirm a generator with neither a hopper nor Auto-Collect still behaves exactly as before.

**A couple of display and interaction bugs affecting that same undocumented rare item were found and fixed (BP/RP 0.9.293–0.9.295)** — an icon that was not rendering correctly, and an interaction that could fire more than once per click. Bugfixes only; no gameplay details are revealed here on purpose.

**Note found while starting the next pass**: `ADDON_VERSION`/the BP manifest were already at 0.9.298 before this entry's own change (0.9.299) — three versions (0.9.296–0.9.298) exist in the shipped code with no corresponding changelog entries here. Not reverse-engineered or guessed at; flagged honestly rather than silently skipped over.

**Offline plot lockdown: entry no longer suspends while the owner is offline — combat is blocked entirely instead, on direct instruction: "when players are offline; people aren't kicked out of the plot. Instead, they cannot deal damage to ANYTHING, all block breaks and interacts are blocked aswell" (BP 0.9.299).** Read the real BP 0.9.281/0.9.282 enforcement code directly before changing anything — `canEnter`/`canBuild`/`canBreak`/`canInteract`/`canUseGenerators`/`canManagePerms` all gate on `isOwnerOnline(id)` today, and it's specifically `canEnter` failing that makes `enforceBoundary` clamp/kick a guest the moment the owner disconnects (`handleTrespass`'s own 3-strike teleport-away). "Not kicked out" meant removing that ONE gate; "breaks and interacts blocked" was already true and needed no change (`canBuild`/`canBreak`/`canInteract`/`canUseGenerators`/`canManagePerms` are byte-for-byte untouched); "can't deal damage to ANYTHING" was the one genuinely new piece, since general PVP/mob combat has never been one of this file's 6 stored permission keys at all (per the item-frame protection gate's own long-standing header comment) — there was no existing wrapper to extend, only a standalone lockdown to add.

- **`canEnter` reverted to `isPlotOwner(player, id) || isTeamMember(id, player.id) || getPlayerPlotPerm(id, player.id, "entry")`** — the `isOwnerOnline(id) &&` gate is gone. Entry now resolves exactly the same regardless of whether the owner is connected, so `enforceBoundary` never has a reason to clamp/kick someone purely because the owner logged off; a genuinely denied "entry" permission (Basic Entry off, no override, not Team) still clamps exactly as before, unrelated to online status.
- **New `isOfflinePlotLockdown(attacker, loc, victim)`** (right after `isOwnerOnline`) — `id = getPlotAtBlock(loc)`; returns false for unclaimed ground, an admin bypass (`isPlotProtectionExempt`), the plot owner, or a Team member (Team's own "permanent reign" already established at BP 0.9.282 extends naturally to combat too); also returns false for a legitimate Raid Night spawner-mob kill (`isRaidNightActive() && isSpawnerMob(victim)`, BP 0.9.191's own deliberate "works regardless of online status" exception — this new lockdown must not silently take that back the moment a plot's owner happens to be offline mid-raid); otherwise returns `!isOwnerOnline(id)`. Checked against the **victim's** location, not the attacker's own — same convention `canBreak`/`canInteract` already use for the block being acted on, and the same one the pre-existing item-frame and raid-night combat gates already use for an entity being hit.
- **New `world.beforeEvents.entityHurt` handler** (right after the raid-night mob-combat gate, same `onWorldLoad`) — for any player-dealt hit on any victim not already covered by the item-frame gate, cancels the hit outright when `isOfflinePlotLockdown` says so, with a plain action-bar warning (`setActionBarP`, priority 2) and error sound — deliberately **not** `handleTrespass`: no strike counter, no teleport, nothing that could ever eject anyone, matching "not kicked out" literally. Item frames/armor stands/paintings are skipped here since they already inherit the same lockdown for free (their own gate checks `canInteract`, which stays `isOwnerOnline`-gated, unchanged).
- **`isCombatBlockedByOtherSystems` (the function Java Combat's own before-event handler consults before applying its custom damage) extended with the identical check** — Java Combat intercepts every player-attack hit and reapplies damage manually via `applyDamage`, so without this it would keep dealing damage on top of/instead of the new handler's cancel; now it steps aside (no custom damage applied) whenever the offline lockdown applies, and the new dedicated handler above is what actually cancels the underlying event.
- **Three now-stale "owner offline" messages fixed for accuracy**, since none of them can be reached for that reason anymore: `enforceBoundary`'s clamp message (was a special-cased "Access suspended while the owner is offline" branch — now always the plain `BOUNDARY_MESSAGE`, since a clamp here is always a genuine stored-permission denial now); `/plot home <name>`'s denial message (same fix, one plain "no entry permission" message instead of the old online/offline branch); the admin "Manage Player Plots" panel's owner-status line and the in-menu Plot Permissions Guide text (both reworded to state the real current split: entry is unaffected by online status, everything else — build/break/interact/generators/managing perms, and now combat — still pauses). Guide book page 5 rewritten to match and re-verified at 241 real UTF-8 bytes (not `.length`) against the 256-byte cap.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to 0.9.299. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.299 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 133 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script/text changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `isOfflinePlotLockdown`'s definition, the reverted `canEnter`, the new entityHurt handler, the `isCombatBlockedByOtherSystems` extension, and confirmed both old stale offline-branch messages are genuinely gone from the built output — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: have the plot owner disconnect, confirm a guest already standing on the plot is no longer clamped/kicked; confirm that same guest can no longer break blocks, open containers, use generators, or deal any damage (to a player, a mob, or an item frame) on that plot; confirm a Team member is completely unaffected by all of the above; and confirm everything returns to normal the instant the owner reconnects.

**Stats HUD board "sometimes becomes totally smaller, hard to read" — the REAL, structural root cause found, not another `font_scale_factor` guess (BP/RP 0.9.300).** This exact symptom had already been chased twice (0.9.254, 0.9.262) and both times concluded it was GUI Scale — a genuine, confirmed, unfixable-from-the-resource-pack platform behavior. Rather than re-tune the number a third time, the user pointed at two concrete triggers — "the Wanted notification and large sums like 100K" — which led to reading the board's actual JSON UI capture logic instead of the font size at all, and it turned up a real, structural bug fully independent of GUI Scale.

- **Root cause**: the board uses the established "invisible title" trick — `main.js` writes stats text via `setTitle()` with all-zero durations, and `epitaph_hud.json`'s `board_data_control` captures that shared title channel into `#preserved_text` for `board_text` to render. Its capture condition was a **blacklist** — "capture anything that ISN'T WAILA- or Combat-marked" — but every *other* real, unmarked on-screen title this addon fires (the Milestone flash at `fireMilestone`, the PvP "§c⚔ Combat!" tag notice fired on every single fresh hit via `applyPvpTag`, plot trespass notices in `noticeEntry`/`handleTrespass`, a certain undocumented rare item's own drop flash) got captured by that same blacklist as "new board content," instantly replacing the real multi-line stats with that flash's own short text. Since `board_panel`'s height is content-based (`"100%c"`), the panel visibly shrank to fit whatever short line just hijacked it — exactly "totally smaller." Worse, `refreshBoards()` only re-sends when the player's own computed stats text changes from its cached `lastBoardText`; if nothing changed right after a flash fired, the hijacked text just stayed displayed indefinitely — exactly "sometimes," not a one-frame flicker. The user's two named triggers line up exactly: PvP tagging fires on every fresh hit (constant during any fight involving a Wanted player, who by definition is being hunted), and Milestone flashes fire on large kill/cash thresholds. `waila_panel` never had this bug — it was already built the other way around, as a whitelist ("only capture text that IS `$waila_marker`-tagged"), immune to every other title writer by construction.
- **Fixed by flipping `board_text` to the same proven whitelist pattern**, not by patching each individual flash with its own exclusion marker (which would need updating again for every future title anywhere in the file — the exact fragile-blacklist shape that caused this bug in the first place). New `$board_marker: "epitaphhud."` (a plain ASCII literal, matching this project's own established post-0.9.208 practice of avoiding typed Private-Use-Area characters for markers) replaces the old `$waila_marker`/`$combat_marker` exclusion pair on `board_text`; its visibility condition and text-extraction binding both now check for `$board_marker`'s presence instead of the two old markers' absence.
- **`main.js`**: new `const BOARD_MARKER = "epitaphhud."` (must match the JSON literal exactly). `refreshBoards()`'s own `setTitle()` call now sends `BOARD_MARKER + text` instead of bare `text`; `clearBoards()`'s clear call now sends `BOARD_MARKER` alone instead of a bare `""` — a marker-less empty string would no longer be recognized as legitimate board content under the new whitelist logic either, so clearing would have silently stopped working without this. No other `setTitle()` call site anywhere in the file needed to change — every one of them (Milestone, Combat tag, plot notices, that same undocumented rare item’s drop flash, WAILA, Java Combat) is now simply invisible to the board by construction, present and future, with zero per-event bookkeeping required going forward.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; `epitaph_hud.json` re-validated as JSON with comments stripped. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to 0.9.300. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.300 (header + both modules); RP manifest bumped 0.9.33→0.9.34 (header) / 0.9.29→0.9.30 (module) — the marker/binding rework is a real RP change — and the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 133 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure logic rework), and the packaged build re-checked directly for `BOARD_MARKER`'s definition, both of its call sites, and the whitelist binding in the packaged `epitaph_hud.json` — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: fight (or get tagged in combat near) a Wanted player and confirm the corner board keeps showing normal stats instead of shrinking to "Combat!"; trigger a Milestone and confirm the board is undisturbed while the big celebratory title plays in the center of the screen; and generally watch for whether the board ever again shrinks/shows the wrong content after this build — the separate, still-real GUI-Scale-driven overall size variance (0.9.254/0.9.262) is unrelated and unaffected by this fix, since that's a genuine platform limitation with no resource-pack-side override.

**Search UIs and Market/Shop UIs both reported slow — "a few seconds to just load panels" — two real, separate, confirmed bottlenecks found by reading the actual code, not guessed (BP 0.9.301).** Two genuinely different systems, investigated independently rather than assumed to share one cause.

- **Search UIs**: `getAllKnownPlayers()` — the function behind every search-driven screen in the file (Plot Permissions Players/Search, Team Add Member, the Admin Plot picker, Ranks grant/revoke, Mute/Soft-Ban toggles, Staff Tools' pickers) — did a full `world.getDynamicPropertyIds()` scan (a real, synchronous walk of every dynamic-property key in the ENTIRE world, not just this addon's own) on every single call, with zero caching, on the explicit original assumption that this was "never a hot path." That assumption didn't hold at this realm's real, stated scale (~100 players, each with plots/generators/shops/permissions of their own contributing to the total key count) — the full-world key count is large enough that this scan measurably adds up on every one of those screen opens. Fixed with a `KNOWN_PLAYERS_CACHE_TICKS` (30s) cache, since the underlying data changes rarely (only on a genuinely new player's first-ever join, or a rename) — a short, self-correcting staleness window (a brand-new joiner might not show in search for a few seconds, and they're still reachable via `searchKnownPlayers`'s always-fresh online-player half regardless) in exchange for skipping the expensive full-world scan on every screen open. Proactively invalidated (`invalidateKnownPlayersCache()`) the instant a real join/rename actually happens, in the same `playerSpawn` handler that already writes `plot_name_by_uuid_<uuid>` — not left to blindly expire.
- **Market/Shop UIs**: `withLocationLoaded` — the shared force-load helper `shopComputeStocksFor` (and therefore `openSellerShopsMenu`, and every other shop chest read) routes through — used to remove its ticking area the INSTANT its callback finished, on every single call. Player Shops is explicitly menu-driven with no proximity requirement, so a buyer browsing a seller's shop list is rarely standing anywhere near that seller's actual chest — meaning browsing INTO a seller's shop list genuinely force-loaded their chest's chunk (a real wait when it wasn't already loaded), then immediately tore it back down again. Re-opening that SAME seller's list even a few seconds later — a buyer comparing one item, going Back, checking another — paid the exact same real chunk-load cost all over again, every time, not a one-off warm-up. Fixed with a short keep-alive (`SHOP_TICKING_KEEPALIVE_TICKS`, 30s) instead of instant teardown: an area now stays alive briefly after its last real use, so realistic back-and-forth browsing reuses the already-loaded chunk instantly. Still bounded and self-cleaning (`sweepIdleTickingAreas`, a new periodic sweep wired into the world-load bootstrap alongside this session's other background hygiene passes) — explicitly **not** a reversion to BP 0.9.194's permanent-ticking-area design (already root-caused as a real, ongoing "lag out and about" source and removed at BP 0.9.261): an idle area still gets released, just not the instant its callback returns, and only for whatever locations were genuinely touched recently, never every shop that exists. `withLocationLoaded` is shared (not shop-exclusive despite its constant's name) — RTP uses the identical helper for its own random-teleport force-loads, and just as harmlessly idles out on the same schedule with nothing left to reuse it.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to 0.9.301. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.301 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes, pure script logic. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 133 entries (unchanged — no new assets), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the known-players cache and its invalidation call, `SHOP_TICKING_KEEPALIVE_TICKS`/`shopTickingAreaLastUse`, confirmed the old immediate-teardown `finally` block is genuinely gone from `withLocationLoaded`, and confirmed `sweepIdleTickingAreas`/`initTickingAreaKeepAlive` are present and wired into the bootstrap — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: open a search-driven screen (Plot Permissions → Players, or a Rank grant picker) a couple of times in quick succession and see whether it opens noticeably faster after the first time; and browse into the same seller's Player Shop list more than once within thirty seconds or so and confirm the second (and later) opens are near-instant instead of re-paying the same multi-second wait every time.

**Anti-dupe request researched, then explicitly dropped by direct instruction — recorded here so the reasoning isn't lost, not shipped as code.** Asked for a system covering both stackable items (Enchanted Golden Apple named directly) and non-stackable ones. Investigated the real mechanism before writing anything: `ItemStack.setDynamicProperty`/`getDynamicProperty` exist and are stable well within this addon's pinned `@minecraft/server` 2.8.0 (added at 1.9.0, confirmed directly against Microsoft's own changelog) — but Microsoft's own current `ItemStack` reference states plainly that `isStackable` is false the instant an item "contains any custom data or properties," and `setDynamicProperty`'s own docs add "this function only works with non-stackable items." Cross-checked against real community developer discussion, which confirmed the same conclusion independently: the accepted technique for tagging a stackable item is to first give it lore/a name (forcing it non-stackable) and only then set the dynamic property — there is no way to tag a stackable item's per-instance identity without permanently ending its ability to stack, full stop, not a guessable workaround. Presented this tradeoff directly (mathematically-sound per-item tracking via a visible "serial number," accepting that e.g. 6 Golden Apples given at once would occupy 6 separate slots forever and never merge again) alongside two weaker alternatives (skip stackable items entirely; track stackable items by realm-wide total-issued count only, with an explicit false-negative risk since consumption can't be tracked exhaustively). **Direct instruction after seeing the real tradeoff: "Forget it; we will skip this dupe protection."** Nothing was implemented — the discussion never left the research/design stage, so there is no code to revert.

**Generator/spawner upgrade screens: hidden buttons for upgrades a specific generator can never actually use — on direct report: "some Gens have button upgrades like Refine II on gold gen, but notes Gold Gen can't use Refine II — this should mean the button would not appear... same for all gens and spawners" (BP 0.9.302).** Read the real upgrade-node system end to end rather than patching the one named example — confirmed this was a genuine, broader gap affecting every generator, not just Gold.

- **Root cause, two distinct cases, both previously only refused-on-tap rather than hidden**: (1) `quality_1`/`quality_2`/`quality_3` (Refine I/II/Transmute) have `blockOnly: true` in `GEN_NODES` — which only ever means "hidden for mob generators/spawners," a completely different axis from "Block-CATEGORY generator" (Diamond Block Gen, Amethyst Block Gen, etc., which are non-mob but have no `REFINEMENT_TABLE`/`ORE_BASE_ITEM` entry at all). `canPurchaseNode` already refused a tap on these (BP 0.9.280), but the button lists (`openGeneratorUpgradeCategorySelect`'s category counts, `openGeneratorUpgradeCategoryScreen`'s node list) only ever filtered by `blockOnly`/`mobOnly`, so the button — and the whole "Quality" category — still rendered, just always failing once tapped. (2) The exact reported case: `quality_2` (Refine II) specifically needs `REFINEMENT_TABLE[type][1]` to be non-null to mean anything — Gold (and every other now-single-tier Ore type, since BP 0.9.266's ore-economy rework) refines straight to its real ingot at tier 1 and has nothing left for tier 2. This had no refusal-on-tap at all before now (a real, previously-unnoticed "dead purchase" bug — a player COULD spend the EXP and get literally nothing) and was only ever surfaced as a body-text note ("Gold Gen has no Premium tier"), with the button still sitting right there next to the note explaining it couldn't be used.
- **Fixed with one shared, single-source-of-truth check** instead of patching each screen separately (avoiding the exact "two copies quietly drift apart" bug class this file has already been burned by before, e.g. BP 0.9.219): new `generatorNodeStructuralReason(genId, nodeId)` returns the refusal reason (or `null` if structurally fine) for whether a node could EVER be bought on this specific generator, independent of current progress — covering `blockOnly`/`mobOnly`, the Block-category Quality refusal, and the new Refine-II-needs-a-real-tier-2 check all in one place. `nodeAppliesToGenerator()` is the boolean wrapper both button-rendering screens now call (replacing their old bare `blockOnly`/`mobOnly` checks); `canPurchaseNode` calls the same underlying function for its own purchase-attempt refusal, so the two can never disagree about which nodes are structurally dead, only how to react to it (hide vs. refuse-with-a-message).
- **A related, previously-existing inconsistency fixed in the same pass**: `getPrereqChain` (the "Unlock path: A → B → C" message builder) walked the STATIC `prereqs` array, ignorant of `canPurchaseNode`'s own dynamic quality_2→quality_1 substitution for single-tier types — meaning Transmute's own unlock-path message on a single-tier generator would still name "Refine II" even after this pass hid its button entirely. `getPrereqChain` now takes `genId` and splices straight through any structurally-inapplicable ancestor during its walk (using the same `nodeAppliesToGenerator` check), so the displayed chain can never mention a node that isn't actually there to unlock.
- **Category-level cleanup**: a category that ends up with zero applicable nodes for a given generator (e.g. "Quality" for any Block-category generator, once Refine I/II/Transmute are all correctly excluded) is now dropped from the category-select screen entirely too, rather than leading into a technically-correct-but-empty node list. The now-redundant body-text note ("Gold Gen has no Premium tier — Refine II won't add anything extra") was removed along with its `showSingleTierNote`/`hasTier2`/`refineTbl` local computation — once the button itself is gone, there's nothing left to explain.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to 0.9.302. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.302 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes, pure script logic. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 133 entries (unchanged — no new assets), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `generatorNodeStructuralReason`/`nodeAppliesToGenerator`, `getPrereqChain`'s new 3-argument signature, both screens' updated filters, the empty-category drop, and confirmed the old `showSingleTierNote` body-line is genuinely gone — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: open a Gold (or Iron/Copper) Ore Generator's Quality upgrades and confirm Refine II no longer appears at all (Refine I and Transmute still do); open a Block-category generator (e.g. Diamond Block Gen) and confirm the "Quality" category itself no longer appears in the category list; and spot-check a spawner and an ordinary multi-tier Ore generator (e.g. Diamond Ore, which has real Refine II headroom) to confirm nothing that SHOULD still show got accidentally hidden too.

**Permissions/Team/Groups redesigned for "perfect harmony" — on direct instruction: "TEAM is literal team, players on this should have permissions on each other's plot, full permissions... if someone has all perms in a group OR is Team, this trumps personal player perms... if this seems like a bad idea, put some thought into the entire permission, team and groups system" (BP 0.9.303).** Read the full existing chain (`getPlayerPlotPerm`'s override>group>Basic resolution, all 6 `canX` wrappers, Team's storage model) directly before changing anything, then pushed back on one specific piece with concrete reasoning rather than implementing the request verbatim — the user explicitly invited that. Three real decisions were put to the user directly (`AskUserQuestion`) rather than guessed, since each has a genuine, non-obvious tradeoff:

1. **Should a full-permission Group override a personal restriction?** Flagged the real paradox first: inverting "more specific wins" (the standard access-control rule this whole system was explicitly modeled on, per its own header) means an owner loses the ability to restrict one specific problem member of an otherwise fully-trusted group without removing them entirely. **Decided anyway: yes, implement as originally asked**, scoped narrowly — a group only gets this absolute-override power when it grants literally all 6 keys (`isFullPermissionGroup`), never a partial group.
2. **Should Team's "full permissions" include Manage Permissions (trustmgmt)?** **Decided: no — 5 of 6.** Team gets build/break/entry/interact/generators unconditionally; editing the plot's own permission structure (Groups, Basic, other players' overrides) stays owner-only even for Team members.
3. **Should adding someone to your Team automatically add you to theirs (mutual)?** **Decided: yes.** This is the literal "broken" part reported — Team was stored per-plot (`plot_team_<id>_<uuid>`) and was purely one-directional: the owner of plot A adding player B to A's Team gave B access to A's plot, but gave A nothing on B's plot unless B separately did the identical thing back.

- **`getPlayerPlotPerm` resolution order changed to: full-permission Group > personal override > group's own toggle > Basic.** New `isFullPermissionGroup(id, name)` checks all 6 `PLOT_PERM_KEYS` are true for that group; `getPlayerPlotPerm` checks this FIRST and returns `true` unconditionally when it holds, before ever reading a personal override — a group with anything less than all 6 is completely unaffected and still respects the original order exactly as before. This automatically propagates to all 6 `canX` wrapper functions (`canBuild`/`canBreak`/`canInteract`/`canUseGenerators`/`canManagePerms`/the `canEnter`-underlying `entry` key) with zero changes needed to any of them individually, since they all route through this one shared function — the single-source-of-truth approach this project has repeatedly favored (e.g. the generator-node fix immediately above this entry) to avoid two copies of the same rule quietly drifting apart.
- **Team's unconditional bypass (already existing for `entry`/`interact`/`generators` + shop-chest access) extended to `canBuild`/`canBreak` too** — `isTeamMember(id, player.id)` added to both, matching the exact pattern the other three already used. `canManagePerms` deliberately untouched, per the user's own "5 of 6" decision.
- **Team reciprocity**: `addToTeam`/`removeFromTeam` (the raw, single-direction dynamic-property writes) are kept as internal primitives, but are no longer the public API — new `addToTeamMutual`/`removeFromTeamMutual` are what the UI now calls, and both write BOTH directions in one call: adding player B to plot A's Team also resolves B's own plot (`getOwnedPlotByUUID`) and adds plot A's owner to B's plot's Team too, if B has one. Removal is symmetric — ending a Team relationship ends it on both sides at once, never a "remove from just my side" half-measure. The reciprocal half deliberately bypasses `isPlotPlayerTrusted`'s "already trusted" gate (that gate exists to stop a stranger being promoted via the picker; the reciprocal side isn't a separate trust decision, it's the automatic consequence of the one decision that already passed the gate — requiring prior trust on BOTH sides would make reciprocity impossible for two players teaming up for the first time).
- **One-time migration for existing data**: `EPITAPH_SCHEMA` 6→7 walks every claimed plot's existing Team roster and writes the missing reciprocal half directly for every relationship that already existed under the old one-directional model — an existing Team pairing becomes genuinely mutual on this world's next load, not just for new adds going forward.
- **UI/diagnostics updated to stay honest about the new precedence, not just the underlying logic**: `openPlayerPermissionsMenu`'s header line and `permDetail`'s per-key breakdown both used to be able to say "Has personal overrides on this plot" / show a blank (implicitly "your own override") tag for a player whose overrides are now completely ignored because they're in a full-permission group — both fixed to check `isFullPermissionGroup` first and attribute the real reason. `groupButtonLabel` (the Groups list) and `openPlotGroupDetail` (a specific group's own screen) both gained a `⚡ Full Access` flag so an owner can tell at a glance which groups carry this absolute-override power. `permDebugSnapshot` (the `permcheck` DENY diagnostic) gained a `team=T/F` field, since Team now affects build/break too. The Team menu, Add Member flow, and the per-player quick-toggle button all have new messaging describing the mutual relationship (and honestly note when the reciprocal half can't apply yet because the other player has no plot).
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to 0.9.303. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.303 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 133 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script/text changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `isFullPermissionGroup`, the reordered `getPlayerPlotPerm`, both `canBuild`/`canBreak` now including the Team check, `canManagePerms` confirmed UNCHANGED, `addToTeamMutual`/`removeFromTeamMutual`, the schema 6→7 migration, and every updated UI/diagnostic string — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: create a group with all 6 toggles on, put a player in it who ALSO has a personal `build:false` override, and confirm they can still build (with the screen correctly explaining why); add a player to your Team and confirm you now show up on THEIR Team automatically (assuming they have a plot) with full build/break/interact/generators access to each other's plots; and confirm Manage Permissions still refuses a Team member who isn't the owner.

**Damage Entities toggle added — a real, previously-unfilled protection gap, on direct instruction: "enable and disable a player's ability to hit any animal, villager, pet or generator mob. Generator mobs should be damageable during raid nights, have Spawners permission for Mob Entities separate from general animals and pets" (BP 0.9.304).** Read the full existing combat-protection surface first (item-frame gate, the raid-night-only mob-combat gate, the offline lockdown, Java Combat's own step-aside function) before touching anything — confirmed a real, previously-undocumented gap: outside of raid night and the owner-offline lockdown, general mob/animal/villager combat had **never** been gated by anything at all (the item-frame gate's own long-standing header said so directly: "general combat was never one of the 6 stored permission keys"). Any visitor with nothing but default Entry could freely kill an owner's pets, farm animals, villagers, and generator mobs at any time the owner was online — exactly the gap reported.

- **New 7th permission key: `damage_entities`** (`PLOT_PERM_DEFAULTS`/`PLOT_PERM_KEYS`), defaulting to `false` like every other guest-facing key. Governs hitting animals, villagers, and tamed pets specifically. New `canDamageEntities(player, id)` wrapper, matching the existing 6 exactly (owner/Team bypass, `isOwnerOnline`-gated otherwise).
- **Generator-mob damage deliberately reuses the EXISTING `generators` key instead of an 8th one** — matching the direct instruction to keep it "separate from general animals and pets" (a *different* key, not a new *third* one): a player not trusted to use a generator's own Hub has no clearer reason to be trusted to kill its mobs either.
- **One shared decision function, `canDamageEntity(attacker, victim, id)`**, used by both the new dedicated `entityHurt` handler and `isCombatBlockedByOtherSystems` (Java Combat's own step-aside check) — so the two can never quietly disagree: checks `isPlotProtectionExempt` first, then a spawner-tagged mob (`isSpawnerMob`) is gated by `generators` UNLESS Raid Night is active (in which case it's always fair game — the exact "generator mobs should be damageable during raid nights" requirement, preserving BP 0.9.191's own established exception byte-for-byte), then a genuine wild hostile mob that ISN'T spawner-produced (`isUnprotectedWildHostile`, checking `hasTypeFamily("monster")`) is left completely unrestricted — not part of what was asked, and this addon has never treated ordinary wild-mob combat as something plot protection covers — and everything else (animals/villagers/tamed pets) falls to the new `damage_entities` key.
- **The old raid-night-only mob-combat gate (BP 0.9.191) is retired, not duplicated** — its entire job (protect non-spawner mobs from unpermitted raiders, using `canInteract` as a stand-in for "legitimate guest" purely because no dedicated combat key existed yet) is now fully subsumed by the new unified handler, which runs unconditionally (not gated on `isRaidNightActive()` at all) since `canDamageEntity` already knows about the raid-night carve-out internally. `isCombatBlockedByOtherSystems` had its own **inline duplicate** of that exact raid-night logic — replaced with a call to the same shared function, closing a real "two copies could drift apart" risk this project has been burned by before (e.g. BP 0.9.219).
- **UI**: `editPlotPermToggles` (the shared Basic/per-player/per-group editor) gained a 7th toggle ("Hit Animals/Villagers/Pets"); `PLOT_PERM_LABELS` gained a matching entry. Team's own scope extended consistently with its already-established rule ("everything except Manage Permissions") — now 6 of 7 keys, not 5 of 6 — with every Team-related UI string (menu body, Guide text, both add-confirmation messages) updated to name the new permission. `/plot info`'s own permission-line display was found to hold a **third, independently-hardcoded copy** of the 6-key list (separate from `PLOT_PERM_KEYS` and `editPlotPermToggles`'s own toggle calls) — fixed to read `PLOT_PERM_KEYS` directly instead, so it can't silently drift out of sync again the way it would have here.
- **Migration for existing data**: `EPITAPH_SCHEMA` 7→8 backfills `damage_entities: true` for every existing group that already had all 6 ORIGINAL keys on — without this, `isFullPermissionGroup` (BP 0.9.303, checks ALL of `PLOT_PERM_KEYS`) would have silently downgraded every already-established "full permission" group the instant this 7th key existed, since a brand-new key defaults to false and a group missing even one of seven no longer qualifies.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to 0.9.304. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.304 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 133 entries (unchanged — no new assets, pure script/text changes), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for the new key in `PLOT_PERM_DEFAULTS`/`PLOT_PERM_KEYS`, `canDamageEntities`/`isUnprotectedWildHostile`/`canDamageEntity`, the unified `entityHurt` handler, `isCombatBlockedByOtherSystems`'s updated call, the 7th toggle in `editPlotPermToggles`, and the schema 7→8 migration — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: as a non-permitted guest, confirm hitting the owner's animals/villagers/pets is now blocked (with a trespass warning) while the owner is online; confirm a granted `damage_entities` permission (or Basic/group toggle) actually allows it; confirm generator mobs still require `generators` permission normally but are freely killable by anyone during an active Raid Night; and confirm a genuine wild (non-spawner) hostile mob remains unrestricted throughout.

**Java-style saturation/healing added — the one confirmed-missing Java mechanic, layered on top of Bedrock's own native hunger/exhaustion/starvation left otherwise untouched (BP 0.9.305).** On direct instruction: "Research Minecraft Java saturation and healing, as we need to add it to our addon." Researched properly before writing anything (Minecraft Wiki for the real Java mechanic, Microsoft Learn for the actual Script API surface) rather than assumed — confirmed Bedrock's own native regen is hardcoded at a flat 1 HP / 80 ticks (4s) regardless of hunger/saturation level, while real Java has two tiers: a fast "saturation boost" (1 HP / 10 ticks / 0.5s, costing 1.5 saturation per heal) whenever hunger is genuinely full (20) AND saturation > 0, plus the same baseline slow tier Bedrock already has (1 HP / 80 ticks, hunger ≥ 18, costing 6.0 exhaustion per heal in real Java). The fast tier — Bedrock's one confirmed, concrete gap — is the only thing this pass adds; scope was explicitly confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before writing code ("Just the two healing tiers," not a full per-action exhaustion re-tune).

- **Confirmed technically sound before implementing, not guessed**: `world.gameRules.naturalRegeneration` is a real, stable, plain settable boolean (Microsoft's own `GameRules` class reference, no experimental caution on this specific property) that, per its own documented scope, only ever gates the ENGINE's own health-regen-from-hunger — hunger depletion and starvation damage stay fully active regardless of its value, confirmed directly rather than assumed. `minecraft:player.hunger`/`.saturation`/`.exhaustion` are real, stable `EntityComponentTypes` entries (confirmed against Microsoft's current enum reference, exact identifiers), all extending the same `EntityAttributeComponent` base `minecraft:health` already uses in this file (`wailaReadHealth`) — meaning `currentValue`/`effectiveMax` reads and a genuine `setCurrentValue()` write are all real and available, not just read-only despite the API's own "read the hunger of a player" description text.
- **New `JAVA-STYLE SATURATION/HEALING MODULE` section** (`main.js`, right after the Java Combat module): `world.gameRules.naturalRegeneration = false` is set once at world load inside `initJavaRegen()`, handing 100% of "hunger → healing" over to this module. `javaRegenTick(player)` (driven by a `system.runInterval` every `JAVA_REGEN_CHECK_INTERVAL_TICKS` = 5 ticks, iterating only `world.getPlayers()`) reads real health/hunger/saturation, skips entirely below `JAVA_REGEN_MIN_HUNGER_FOR_SLOW` (18, matching real Java exactly), and otherwise picks the fast tier (hunger at `effectiveMax` and saturation > 0) or the slow tier per-check, healing 1 HP once a per-player elapsed-ticks counter (`javaRegenElapsed`) reaches whichever tier's real interval — the remainder is carried forward rather than reset to 0, so the 5-tick check cadence doesn't drift the long-run timing away from the real 10-tick/80-tick intervals. `world.afterEvents.playerLeave` cleans up `javaRegenElapsed` per player, matching this file's own established per-Map-cleanup discipline (the exact class of leak already caught once for `genMenuDebounce` at BP 0.9.256).
- **Honesty note, stated directly in the code's own header comment, not just here**: the slow tier's exhaustion cost is applied by writing to the real `minecraft:player.exhaustion` component, on the reasoned assumption that Bedrock's native engine reads/drains that same live, shared attribute for its own hunger-depletion logic (a real game value the engine itself maintains, not a script-only counter) — this specific write-back link was **not** independently confirmed via a live test, unlike every other claim in this entry. Worst case if the assumption doesn't hold: slow-tier healing simply costs nothing extra — a benign gap, not a broken mechanic.
- **Deliberately not built this pass, per the confirmed scope**: no re-implementation of hunger depletion, no per-action exhaustion costs (sprinting/jumping/mining/etc.), no changes to starvation damage, and no attempt to pin down the exact per-food `saturationModifier` tier values (Bedrock's own native eating-restores-saturation behavior is left completely untouched and is reportedly already consistent with Java's own values).
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to 0.9.305. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.305 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes, pure script addition. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 133 entries (unchanged — no new assets), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `initJavaRegen`/`javaRegenTick`, the `world.gameRules.naturalRegeneration = false` call, all three real component identifiers, and `initJavaRegen()`'s wiring into the bootstrap right after `initJavaCombat()` — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next (no local Bedrock harness to exercise this from here): eat until hunger is full, stay fed, and confirm healing now visibly speeds up once saturation is present (vs. the old flat slow rate); let saturation run out while hunger stays ≥18 and confirm healing continues at the slower rate instead of stopping; and confirm hunger still depletes and starvation damage still applies exactly as before, completely unaffected by this change.

**`/tpa accept` grants the teleported sender 120 seconds of Entry-permission-kick immunity (BP 0.9.306)**, on direct request. Root-caused what the actual "kick" mechanism is before writing anything: `enforceBoundary`'s own hard clamp (the function silently pushes a denied player back toward their own plot bounds, with a message/sound/particle, every 4-tick check) is the only place a lack of Entry permission ever ejects anyone — accepting a TPA request lands the sender directly on the target's plot, which very plausibly has no Entry permission granted to them at all, immediately clamping them right back out the moment they arrive.

- **Reused the file's own existing grace primitive instead of building a second one.** `enforceBoundary` already has `exemptPlayer(player, seconds)`/`state.exemptUntil` — a short automatic grace window it already grants after any large jumpDist (a real teleport), which fully suspends the clamp/kick check for its duration. Critically, this exact mechanism already has the "stands still through expiry, never re-checked" staleness bug closed for it (`justExitedGrace`, from the BP 0.9.299-era permission-offline work) — a brand-new, separately-tracked immunity map would have reintroduced that exact already-fixed bug class for no reason, since nothing would force a re-check once a second, parallel timer expired while the player stood still. `handleTpaAccept` now calls `exemptPlayer(sender, TPA_ENTRY_IMMUNITY_SECONDS)` (120) immediately after the real teleport succeeds — the sender gets the identical, already-correct grace behavior every other teleport-triggered grace already relies on, just held open far longer than the automatic short window.
- **Scope confirmed by reading what `authorized`/grace actually gates inside `enforceBoundary`**: only the clamp/kick itself. `verifyPermission`/`verifyRaidablePermission`/`canInteract`/`canManagePerms`/`canDamageEntity` are all separate functions, never touched by this grace state — an immune visitor can walk freely around any plot for 120 seconds without being ejected, but still can't build, break, open containers, use generators, or damage anything they weren't already permitted to. Raid-night's own entry bypass and every other existing `enforceBoundary` branch are completely unaffected; this is purely an additional grace-grant at one call site, not a change to the clamp logic itself.
- **Player-facing**: the existing accept message now notes the immunity (`"...teleported to X. (120s entry immunity)"`) so the sender knows the window exists and roughly how long it lasts.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to 0.9.306. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.306 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes, pure script change. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 133 entries (unchanged — no new assets), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `TPA_ENTRY_IMMUNITY_SECONDS` and the `exemptPlayer(sender, TPA_ENTRY_IMMUNITY_SECONDS)` call in `handleTpaAccept` — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: send and accept a `/tpa` request onto a plot where the sender has no Entry permission, confirm they're no longer clamped/kicked for the ~120 seconds after arriving, and confirm they still can't build/break/interact/use generators there during that window; also confirm normal boundary enforcement resumes correctly once the 120 seconds elapse.

**Plot boundary enforcement rebuilt around World Border's own real technique — no more cross-map teleport on denial, a genuine local physical stop instead (BP 0.9.307).** On direct instruction: "stop the teleport border from plots, and turn it into something like World Border, they get strictly stopped from entering." Read `enforceBoundary`'s actual denial branch before touching anything, and confirmed the literal problem: on a denied entry, it computed `clampX`/`clampZ` against the bounds of the player's OWN plot (`refID = getOwnedPlot(player)`) — not the plot they were actually standing on — then `player.teleport()`ed them there directly. Since plots sit at real, often-distant coordinates on the claim grid (not adjacent parcels), a player denied entry while standing far from their own plot could be warped clean across the map in one tick. That's a teleport, not a border.

- **Rebuilt on `enforceWorldBorderVelocity`'s own proven technique** (already shipped and working for the actual world edge) instead of inventing a new mechanism: `clearVelocity()` + a directional `applyKnockback()` impulse, using the exact same horizontal/vertical strength values (`BOUNDARY_PUSH_HORIZONTAL` = 0.6, `BOUNDARY_PUSH_VERTICAL` = 0.1) the world border already uses, for a consistent "hit an invisible wall" feel between the two systems. The push direction is computed from the DENIED plot's own bounds (`getPlotBoundsBlocks(plotID)` — the plot the player is actually standing in, not their own) — whichever of the four edges (west/east/north/south) is nearest gets pushed away from, stopping the player right where they tried to cross rather than moving them anywhere else.
