The player's own plot (`getOwnedPlot`/`refID`) is no longer read at all for this push — it was never actually relevant to "stop them from entering this plot," only to the old, now-removed "send them home" behavior.
- **A genuine rescue fallback, matching the world border's own `wbOutsideTicks`/`WB_STUCK_THRESHOLD` design, not left out** — `plotPushStuckTicks` (a new per-player counter, incremented each check while still denied, cleared the instant the player is authorized/bypassed/raiding/on unclaimed ground/mid-teleport-grace) triggers a one-time, sanity-checked teleport (home if they have a plot, else `teleportToSafeWorldSpawn` — the same proven-safe fallback `handleTrespass`'s own plotless branch already uses) once knockback alone hasn't cleared them within `BOUNDARY_PUSH_STUCK_TICKS` (60 ticks, ~3s, matching the world border's own `WB_STUCK_THRESHOLD` reasoning) — so a player genuinely boxed in by terrain or a build still can't get permanently wedged, even though the primary mechanism is now purely physical, never a routine teleport.
- **A real staleness bug pre-empted, not discovered after shipping**: the idle fast-path (`enforceBoundary`'s "position hasn't changed since last tick, skip everything" optimization) was extended to also stay active while a push is in progress (`plotPushStuckTicks.has(player.id)`) — since the new stop relies on genuine physics movement rather than an instant teleport, a player wedged against terrain that blocks the knockback could otherwise report "position unchanged" and silently stop being re-checked, exactly the same idle-fast-path staleness class this file already found and fixed once for grace expiry (`justExitedGrace`). Closed the same way, before it could ever be reported as a second instance of that bug.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to 0.9.307. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.307 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes, pure script change. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 133 entries (unchanged — no new assets), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for `plotPushStuckTicks`, both new constants, the `applyKnockback` push call, the rescue fallback, the `playerLeave` cleanup, and confirmed the old own-plot clamp-teleport code is genuinely gone from the built output — not just the source. In-game verification is the user's to run next: approach and try to walk onto a plot you're denied entry to and confirm you're now physically knocked back at the plot's own edge instead of being teleported anywhere; confirm this still works correctly for a player standing far from their own plot (or with no plot at all); and confirm a player who somehow gets stuck (e.g. boxed in by terrain right at the edge) is still rescued via a one-time teleport after a few seconds rather than left wedged in forever.

**Trial Seeker Compass added — a purchasable, one-time-use item revealing a never-explored Trial Chamber, on direct request: "think of a way to ensure it only does this for NEW or never-been-done Chambers" (BP 0.9.308).** Researched three separate, real unknowns directly before writing any code, rather than guessing (this feature sits squarely in a class of problem — command permissions, a specific structure id, an API return shape — this project has been burned by guessing before):

1. **Trial Chambers' real Bedrock `structurefeature` id is `trial_chambers`** (no `minecraft:` prefix) — confirmed against the Minecraft Wiki's own Structure "Data values" table, which lists the identical string for both Java and Bedrock, not trusted from community articles alone even though several agreed.
2. **`/locate structure <id> [useNewChunksOnly]` is real** (Microsoft's own command reference) and `useNewChunksOnly` is the literal, official mechanism for "never been done" — it restricts the search to chunks that haven't generated yet in this save, the closest thing Bedrock actually offers to a genuine "nobody has explored this" guarantee. It's listed "Requires Cheats: Yes" / "Permission Level: Game Directors" — confirmed safe to run anyway via script based on this project's own extensive, already-proven precedent: `/tickingarea` carries the identical tier and has been run successfully via `runCommand` for ordinary non-op players this entire addon's history (BP 0.9.194 onward) — independently corroborated by the Bedrock Wiki's own scripting docs ("when cheats are disabled, these commands can't be used unless executed by a server or a script... even if the executor has a high permission level").
3. **`CommandResult` (what `runCommand` returns) exposes ONLY `successCount`** — confirmed directly against Microsoft's current reference — and the Bedrock Wiki's own "Script Core Features" page states plainly: "Script API cannot get a structure location." There is no way to read a found structure's coordinates back into script at all, from any angle.
- **Design consequence of #3, stated honestly rather than papered over**: since the coordinates can never be read back, "ensure only new/never-been-done chambers" can't be enforced by remembering an exact location ever handed out — there is no such location available to remember. Solved instead by making a repeat structurally unlikely on top of the real `useNewChunksOnly` guarantee: the compass runs its search from an ever-advancing anchor point, never from the player's own location, via `execute positioned <anchor> run locate structure trial_chambers true` (run through `player.runCommand`, not `dim.runCommand`, specifically so Bedrock's own native chat output — the only thing that can ever deliver the coordinates at all — reaches the player directly). `trialCompassNextAnchor()` computes each anchor via a golden-angle spiral (`radius = 4000 × √n`, `angle = n × 137.5°`) driven by a permanent, world-wide `trial_compass_cursor` dynamic property that only ever advances, across every purchase, forever — the same well-known, guaranteed-non-clustering point distribution real phyllotaxis/sunflower-seed patterns use. Trial Chambers generate one per real 34×34-chunk (544×544-block) region (Minecraft Wiki) — a 4000-block step is comfortably larger than that spacing at every point on the spiral, so "nearest chamber to anchor N" is essentially never "nearest chamber to anchor N−1," without either ever needing to be known. **Stated plainly**: this is a strong, well-reasoned structural guard, not a cryptographic guarantee of zero repeats — the honest ceiling of what's actually possible here, not a claimed perfect solution.
- **Item mechanism reuses the Spawn Ball/Admin Pearl trick** (`buildTrialCompass`/`isTrialCompass`) — a real vanilla Compass fingerprinted via a hidden last-line lore string, not a brand-new custom item, so this needed zero new resource-pack JSON/icon/lang work at all. `/trialcompass` (new bare command, registered under the `epitaph:` namespace's own auto-alias, same pattern as `/rtp`/`/tpa`/`/levelshop`) opens a `showConfirm` purchase dialog (re-validates balance fresh after the dialog closes, matching every other real money-spend flow in this file) for `TRIAL_COMPASS_COST` (500,000 — roughly Plot Flight's own one-time-unlock tier, a reasonable starting point for a fun, non-pay-to-win reveal, easy to retune via one constant) before giving the item (inventory-first, dropped at the player's feet as a fallback, the established convention). Right-clicking it (`world.beforeEvents.itemUse`, debounced via `trialCompassDebounce`/`TRIAL_COMPASS_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` against Bedrock's known interact-event multi-fire risk, the same idiom `sellStickDebounce` already established) consumes exactly one from the stack (re-read fresh from the container, not the event's own snapshot) and fires the anchored `/locate` call; the real `CommandResult.successCount` is checked (a genuine, if narrow, bit of free visibility) to give an honest message either way, plus a clarifying note that the native "blocks away" distance is measured from the search anchor, not the player's real position.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to 0.9.308. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.308 (header + both modules) — no resource-pack changes, pure script addition (no new item JSON/textures/lang needed, per the fingerprinted-vanilla-item approach above). `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 133 entries (unchanged — no new assets), and the packaged `main.js` re-checked directly for every new function (`buildTrialCompass`/`isTrialCompass`/`trialCompassNextAnchor`/`handleTrialCompassCommand`/`activateTrialCompass`/`initTrialCompass`), its bootstrap wiring, the `trialcompass` command registration, the exact `locate structure trial_chambers true` command string, and the updated `/epitaph help` line — not just the source. **No local Bedrock harness exists to test any of this live from here** — in-game verification is entirely the user's to run next: buy a compass, confirm the balance deducts and the item appears with the right name/lore; right-click it and confirm Bedrock's own native chat message shows real coordinates (not an error, not a permission-denied message — the one point genuinely worth double-checking live, since it rests on strong but not directly-tested-here precedent); confirm the item is consumed after one use; and, over several purchases, confirm no two reveals land on the same chamber.

**Trial Locator rebuilt as a real custom item (name/colour, enchant glint, real compass icon), plus a Breeze ground-drop acquisition path (BP/RP 0.9.309), on direct follow-up.** The original design (BP 0.9.308) deliberately reused a real vanilla Compass with a hidden lore fingerprint specifically to avoid any new resource-pack risk — but a plain compass can't carry a real custom name/colour beyond a runtime nameTag, and critically **can't ever show a real enchant glint**, since compasses have no `minecraft:enchantable` component at all in vanilla. Getting "Enchanted Compass" required an actual custom item this time, which meant redoing exactly the work this project already got burned by once for another custom item added earlier (BP 0.9.288→0.9.295) — so every one of those specific mistakes was checked and avoided up front rather than rediscovered:

- **`behavior_pack/items/trial_locator.json`** (`epitaph:trial_locator`) uses the confirmed-current schema directly, not the shape that broke that earlier item originally: `minecraft:icon` as `{"textures": {"default": "trial_locator"}}` (never the deprecated bare `texture` field), `description.menu_category.category` (never the old, invalid `description.category`), a real `minecraft:display_name` pointing at `item.epitaph:trial_locator.name` (a custom item with no display_name shows its raw identifier — the exact bug that earlier item hit), and `minecraft:glint: true` for the requested "Enchanted Compass" shimmer (this is the ONLY way to force the glint on an item that structurally can never take a real vanilla enchantment).
- **The icon is a genuine, verified real file, not a guessed path** — pulled the actual `compass_item.png` directly from Mojang's own `bedrock-samples` repo, confirmed via that repo's own `item_texture.json` that the vanilla compass icon is a single static image with no animation frames (the needle-spin is a separate in-hand render behaviour tied to a real compass's own vanilla mechanics, irrelevant to a plain inventory icon — so a static icon is the correct, complete choice, not a shortcut), verified the downloaded file's real PNG magic bytes and dimensions (16×16 RGBA) before ever wiring it in, and viewed it directly to confirm it's a genuine compass graphic, not a broken/placeholder image — the exact verification step that would have caught that earlier item's original invisible-icon bug immediately instead of over three follow-up reports.
- **Display name**: `texts/en_US.lang` gets `item.epitaph:trial_locator.name=§d§lTrial Locator` — light-purple + bold, the requested "purple name," carried entirely by the lang value itself rather than a runtime `nameTag` override (which would just be redundant with what the item definition already provides).
- **Detection simplified, not just renamed**: since `epitaph:trial_locator` is now a genuinely unique custom typeId, `isTrialCompass()` collapsed to a plain `stack.typeId === TRIAL_LOCATOR_ITEM_ID` check — the old lore-fingerprint scheme (needed only because a reused vanilla Compass has no unique id of its own) is gone entirely, removing a whole class of "what if a player's own compass happens to match" edge case that no longer exists.
- **Breeze ground-drop, on direct request: "make it possible to get via Dropping from a breeze; Normal item never directly enters inventory. Make it balanced drop rate, but not TOO Rare."** Wired into the existing mob-kill `entityDie` handler (the same one BP 0.9.272/0.9.275/0.9.289 already extended for mob-kill EXP and another existing rare item's own boss-drop rolls) as an independent check, not gated behind the EXP calc or the boss-tier announce threshold — Breeze is a common, repeatable kill (it only spawns via a Trial Spawner inside a Trial Chamber, so finding one while exploring an already-found chamber is exactly the intended flavor), not a milestone, so this stays a quiet drop with no chat fanfare. `TRIAL_LOCATOR_BREEZE_DROP_CHANCE` set to **10%** — a deliberately "balanced, not TOO rare" middle ground, well above the genuinely-rare boss-tier rates used elsewhere for that other item (reserved for Wither/Dragon/Warden/Ravager kills), retunable via one constant. **`grantTrialLocatorBreezeDrop()` always spawns a real ground item** (`Dimension.spawnItem`) — deliberately never `Container.addItem`, matching the direct instruction ("Normal item never directly enters inventory") exactly, and pointedly the opposite of that other item's own inventory-first boss-drop design; the killer has to physically walk over and pick it up like ordinary mob loot.
- **Verification**: `node --check` passed; `items/trial_locator.json` and `item_texture.json` both re-validated as JSON. `ADDON_VERSION` bumped to 0.9.309. BP manifest bumped to 0.9.309 (header + both modules); RP manifest bumped 0.9.34→0.9.35 (header) / 0.9.30→0.9.31 (module) — the first real RP asset addition for this feature — and the main BP's RP dependency pin updated to match, per this project's own standing rule about stale dependency pins silently breaking re-import. `.mcaddon` rebuilt, both packs confirmed, 135 entries (133 + the new item JSON + the new PNG). Every claim above was checked directly against the packaged build, not just the source: the item JSON's real icon/glint/display_name/category shape, the packaged PNG's genuine PNG magic bytes and 16×16 size, the lang entry, `TRIAL_LOCATOR_ITEM_ID`'s typeId-only detection, `grantTrialLocatorBreezeDrop`'s ground-spawn-only implementation, and the Breeze roll wired into `entityDie`. In-game verification is the user's to run next: confirm the item now shows as "Trial Locator" in light-purple bold with a real compass icon and enchant shimmer (not invisible, not a raw identifier — the two failure modes this pass specifically guarded against); confirm the purchased item and a Breeze-dropped one are identical; and confirm a Breeze kill occasionally (not every time, not never) drops one on the ground rather than adding it straight to inventory.
